# Big Deck Optimization — Design

A Slay the Spire 2 mod that removes lag when playing with huge decks (200+ cards).

Supported and tested game versions: **v0.107.1** (public) and **v0.113.1** (beta); the former betas v0.111.0 and v0.113.0 keep their code paths, untested ("Technical choices"). One DLL built against v0.107.1 (Godot 4, C# / .NET 9, game logic in `sts2.dll`; another version's code through fingerprints, per-version mirrors and reflection: "Implemented patches").

## Goal

The original v0.1 brief: no visible frame drop or lag during combat with 1,000 cards (comfort 500, no collapse at 2,000+). Replaced by the **V1 goal** (set in Step P, accepted with v1.0.0 after Step V): at 10,000+ cards per player in co-op with 189 relics, every phase at a player's pace above 120 fps on the 1% low, combat start one short freeze.

- In scope: combat (draw/shuffle/end of turn), deck/draw/discard pile views, combat start, out-of-combat screens listing the deck (rewards, shop, campfire upgrade/remove), **co-op** (the original lag report is a 4-player co-op run).
- The game's own memory leaks were out of scope in the brief; two that grow with the deck are now fixed without changing state (`PooledCardRelease`: finished combats kept with BaseLib; `SyncedCardRelease`: co-op decks kept at every room). The mod itself must not leak (checked before each release: `docs/RELEASE.md`).

## Hard constraints

1. **No patch may change game state.** Up to v0.3.0 the manifest said `affects_gameplay: false` (one co-op player with the mod, the others without). Since v0.4.0 it says `true`: the game only lets players with the same gameplay mods (id and version) join, because a player without the mod falls tens of seconds behind at every combat start and the game drops the actions that reach a player still starting a combat (Step J, co-op). Results must still be bit-identical with vanilla: peers can have different switches, a patch can turn itself off after an error, and `bdopt off` must give the unmodified game.
2. **Bit-identical.** Every patch on a logic path (hook dispatch, per-card recalculation, checksum, serialization) must give exactly the vanilla result, and is checked against vanilla: in the bench (`bdperf`) and, for the checksum, live (`SnapshotVerifier`).
3. **Never crash.**
   - At startup, each patch is applied in isolation; a missing target only disables that patch (logged).
   - At runtime, an exception inside a patch disables it for the session and falls back to vanilla.
   - An optimization backs off when another mod patches its target (`PatchSafety`).

## Optimization categories

- **(a) Invisible — on by default.** Same results and the same screen; each option can be switched off (`bdopt`, BaseLib menu).
- **(b) Visual concessions.** Shipped as options marked "Visual" (no deck-size "Auto" mode, as first planned): `fast_combat_start` (on, solo from a relic threshold, always on in co-op), `instant_combats` (off by default), `card_flights`, `relic_flashes`. Results stay the game's; only animation pacing or stacked effects change, on the local screen.
- **(c) Gameplay logic changes — never.**

### 16,800 cards + 736 relics (2026-10-23, `solo-16k`, `solo-16k-trace`)

Double the reference size (its cards 18x, relics 2x), release-like timings: idle combat smooth (no frame over 100 ms in
10 s), combat start 10.2 s, plays and end of turn at ~8 fps (frames 110-190 ms), ~30% of action time in GC pauses
(`gcstat`: e.g. 4 plays 7.6 s with 2.3 s of GC; mostly gen1 collections, ~50 ms each). Trace: Limitless'
`RecalculateCardValues` patch re-serializes every stacked enchantment after each action (`MultiEnchantment.SetSerializableCards`,
~13% of the main thread here: ~13,000 enchanted cards since the reference run's 735 are copied 18x), with BaseLib's
extended save data (`ConditionalWeakTable` entry per `ToSerializable`) on top; per-frame rich-text effects (`RichTextGold`/`RichTextSine`) ~16%.

Tried and dropped: skipping BaseLib's empty extended-data entries (identical checksum and save packet, but no clear gain
in an A/B over 4 turns: plays 7.6/8.0 s off vs 5.2/6.1 s on with different cards, end of turn 5.5/4.7 vs 6.3/5.0 s).
The table cost in the trace is mostly GC triggered there. A real fix would be in Limitless (re-serialize only changed
cards); its serialized state is checksummed, so it stays untouched here.

## Technical choices

- Harmony runtime patching (`0Harmony.dll` ships with the game). C# .NET 9 class library referencing `sts2.dll`, `GodotSharp.dll`, `0Harmony.dll` from the game folder (not copied).
- Decompile with ILSpy (`ilspycmd`) into `/decompiled` — **gitignored, never committed** (MegaCrit and other mod authors' code). The public repo contains only our patches.
- No dependency. BaseLib is optional (v0.2.3; a manifest dependency + Workshop required item in v0.2.1-v0.2.2, dropped because some players report BaseLib broken and the game then refused to load this mod too): it only hosts the settings menu, in a separate `BigDeckOptimization.Menu.dll` loaded once BaseLib is loaded. Config files must go to `%APPDATA%/SlayTheSpire2/mod_configs` (BaseLib: `BigDeckOptimization.cfg`), **never as `.json` in the mod folder** (see modding facts).
- Compatibility: **must** work with Limitless and BetterSpire2; best effort with SpeedX, CampfireTrade, BaseLib. Patches are prefixes/postfixes/finalizers that only skip the original inside well-defined read-only scopes.
- Language: the settings menu in English and the game's 13 other languages (`Localization/<game language>.json`, embedded, English fallback); docs, logs and console texts in English.
- Game versions (2026-11-17): tested and supported, v0.111.1 (Steam's public branch since 2026-11-17: v0.111.0's code with a data hotfix, "Game public v0.111.1" below) and v0.113.1 (`public-beta`). v0.107.1, the public version until then, and v0.111.0, the beta of late October (the beta moved to v0.113.0 on 2026-11-12, then v0.113.1), are no longer distributed: the DLL keeps their code paths (mirrors, equivalent fingerprints, per-version walks), but they are no longer tested: the release checks and test batteries run on v0.111.1 and v0.113.1, and `tools/versions.json` keeps v0.111.0 under `former_betas`, not among the verified `betas`. `HookSkipTable.cs` holds v0.111.1's and v0.113.1's rows only (`classify.py` runs on those two; v0.111.0's rows were dropped on 2026-11-16, v0.107.1's on 2026-11-17). HookSkip matches a hook against every row of its name whatever the version, and an exact match is the same code, defaults and helpers, so the filtering stays as safe as on the classified version, untested: v0.111.0's hooks, defaults and helpers are v0.111.1's, all 148 filtered; v0.107.1 keeps the 62 of its 147 hooks that use no helper and equal a kept row, its helper profile (the choice-context constructor inlines the owner lookup) matching no kept one: its other 85 hooks run the game's code. v0.113.0, replaced by its hotfix v0.113.1, isn't distributed either: `former_betas` too (2026-11-16). The findings and release checks below that name v0.107.1 or v0.111.0 as tested are history.

## Game modding facts (v0.107.1)

- Mods load from `<game dir>/mods/` (recursive) and Steam Workshop (app 2868840). Each mod: `<Id>.json` manifest + `<Id>.dll` (+ optional `.pck`).
- **Every `.json` file in a mod folder is read as a manifest** → the build disables `deps.json` generation.
- Manifest fields: `id`, `name`, `author`, `description`, `version`, `has_pck`, `has_dll`, `dependencies` (`[{id, min_version}]`), `affects_gameplay` (**defaults to `true`** — must be set explicitly), `min_game_version`.
- Entry point: a class with `[ModInitializer("MethodName")]` (static method); without it the game calls `Harmony.PatchAll` on the assembly.
- Multiplayer: only mods with `affects_gameplay: true` are included in the gameplay mod list compared between players (`ModManager`).
- Console commands: subclass `AbstractConsoleCmd`; the dev console auto-discovers subtypes in mods. Debug commands are allowed whenever the game runs modded. `IsNetworked` commands go through the action queue in multiplayer and run on **every** peer (`ConsoleCmdGameAction`), so they must be deterministic and every peer needs the command.
- Console key: `` ` ``, `'`, `*` or `^` (AZERTY: `*` or `^`).
- Logs: `%APPDATA%/SlayTheSpire2/logs/` — **only the last few sessions are kept**; copy them right after a session worth analyzing.
- Run history: `%APPDATA%/SlayTheSpire2/steam/<steamid>/modded/profileN/saves/history/*.run` (JSON, full decks and relics per player).
- Game beta v0.111.0 (`public-beta` branch, 2026-10-26) changes met so far (decompiled in `decompiled/sts2-beta/`): `AbstractModel.ModifyDamage*` and `Hook.ModifyDamageInternal` take a `CardPlay? cardPlay`; `CardPile.RemoveInternal` calls a new `InvokeCardRemoved`; `SavedPropertiesTypeCache` merged into `ModelIdSerializationCache` (same `GetJsonPropertiesForType`); `Mod.assembly` became `Mod.assemblies`; `CardSelectCmd.PushSelector(selector, localOnly = false)` (a local-only selector answers only the local player's choices, synchronized like a human's; the global one answers every player's choices locally); `Rng(ulong seed)` instead of `Rng(uint, int)`; `StartRunLobby.Players` holds `StartRunLobbyPlayer`; `CombatManager.StartTurn` takes a `CombatTurnState`; `CombatManager.SetupPlayerTurn` takes a `CombatTurnState` (state and cancellation token from it); `Hook.BeforeTurnEnd`/`AfterTurnEnd` renamed `BeforeSideTurnEnd`/`AfterSideTurnEnd`; new hook `BeforeCombatRewardOffered`; `Hook.AfterBlockBroken` passes a choice context and the breaker; `HookPlayerChoiceContext`'s constructor finds the owner through a new `GetOwner` (no `combatState.Players[0]` throw for a null state) and `AfterDeath` uses `NonCombat` actions outside combat; a hook added near the top of `Hook` renumbered every compiler-generated `<Hook>d__N` below it (same code: fingerprints ignore that numbering since v0.7.0). The game sets every player's `PlayerCombatState.Phase` to `None` outside their turn.

## Findings

### Phase 0 (2026-10-21): the co-op checksum

- Singleplayer with a 791-card deck: no lag except at combat start. The co-op run (4 players, 791-card deck incl. 736 Squeeze / 735 enchanted, a teammate with 189 relics) lagged for **every** player during the whole combat.
- The post-action checksum (`ChecksumTracker`, **multiplayer only**) snapshots the full state (`NetFullCombatState.FromRun`) on every peer after every action. Measured: **92 ms and 64 MB allocated per checksum** with 802 combat cards; serialize + hash is only 0.6 ms.
- Root cause: `CombatState.IterateHookListeners()` allocates a fresh list of all listeners (every card, enchantment, relic, power, mod subscriber…) on each call. The snapshot calls it 3× per card (keywords, energy cost, late energy cost): O(cards²).
- Fix: `ListenerCache` (snapshot scope) + hook filter (now `HookFilter`) → **94 ms → 3.8 ms per checksum (x24.8), bit-identical**.
- Each peer computes its own checksum: a player only gets the gain if they have the mod.

### Phase 2 (2026-10-21): dotnet-trace, singleplayer, 935 cards + 368 relics

- Combat setup itself is cheap: `SetUpCombat` 66 ms (card cloning 38 ms).
- **After every action**, `CombatStateTracker.CallCombatStateChangedDeferred` takes 80–100 ms (356 ms at combat start): `RecalculateCardValues` (every enchantment, incl. Limitless multi-enchantments) + hand refresh (`NCard.UpdateVisuals`, `CanPlay` ×5 per card). Every hook call rebuilds the listener list → O(cards²) again. Fix: `ListenerCache` refresh scopes. **Not measured in game yet.**
- Relic bar: every `NButton` gets every input event via `_Input` (only used for drag-cancel while pressed): 368 Godot→C# calls per mouse move. Fix: `IdleButtonInput`. **Not measured in game yet.**
- `CombatState.Players` allocates a list (LINQ) on each call; `IterateHookListeners` calls it every time (720 ms of the trace). Covered inside cache scopes only.
- **Measured in game (2026-10-21, singleplayer, 2830 combat cards + 370 relics)**, all identical to vanilla:
  - checksum: vanilla 1723 ms → cache 422 ms → cache+filter **40 ms** (x42.8). Relics make vanilla far worse (1.7 s vs 94 ms with 2 relics).
  - post-action refresh (`bdperf refresh`): 80 ms → **21 ms** (x3.9).
  - relic bar input: 2333 ms → 46 ms of a 60 s trace.
- Remaining in the refresh (second trace): Limitless `MultiEnchantment.RecalculateValues` over every card (~27%), `CanPlay` → BaseLib's `HasEnoughResourcesFor` patch and `Hook.ShouldPlay` still walk the whole (cached) listener list per hand card. Relic hover tooltips (vanilla, created on hover) are the rest of the relic bar cost.
- Third trace + `bdperf combatstart` (945 cards + 368 relics): refresh 21.7 → 6.1 ms; enemy intents (`RunState.IterateHookListeners`, which also walks the whole permanent deck) now covered. Combat start is ~5.9 s wall clock but almost all of it is relic animations/waits (AfterRoomEntered ~2.9 s ≈ 6 StoneCracker × 0.5 s, BeforeCombatStart ~0.7 s, first turn ~1.6 s); assets + scene + SetUpCombat ≈ 100–170 ms; only one long frame (99–208 ms, first render of the room = the "black screen"). Shortening waits would change timing, which SpeedX itself flags as a co-op desync risk → only as an opt-in (b) concession.
- Not addressed: `RichTextSine` / `RichTextGold` custom text effects (~10% of the main thread, per character per frame, mostly Godot→C# wrapper creation, unavoidable from C#).

### Step D (2026-10-22): card grids (deck view), singleplayer, 945-card deck + 370 relics, Limitless

- `NCardGrid` is already virtualized by the game (sliding window of ~6 rows of pooled holders), on every grid screen (deck view, piles, card selection). No virtualization needed.
- 4 traces of the same gestures (scroll, each sort twice, inspect). Before: scrolling 45 ms per row (max 275 ms), half of it Limitless' `UpdateEnchantmentVisuals` (`Node.Duplicate` of every tab on every render, 9.1 s of 90 s); a sort click 30–114 ms (`List.IndexOf` tie-break per comparison, culture string compare, cost).
- After `EnchantmentTabReuse` + `GridSortCache`: duplicates 9.1 s → 0.2 s, sort ≤ 4 ms (bench 24–39 ms → 2.4 ms, identical orders); scrolling 34 ms per row.
- Remaining scroll cost is vanilla per-card rendering (~7 ms per card: title/type/description autosize, energy/star cost visuals), the same for any deck size.
- `ListenerCache` was dropping its cache on every `CardModel.Enchantment` set, including Limitless' ownerless enchantment storage cards rebuilt while rendering every enchanted card: now only owned cards invalidate. Combat refresh with Limitless: 23.6 → 2.0 ms (x11.7, was x3.9).
- Tried and removed: listener scopes around `NCardGrid.AssignCardsToRow`/`InitGrid` (BaseLib's type plaque walks every run listener per rendered card): no measurable gain, materializing the list costs as much as the walks it saves.
- Vanilla, deck-size independent: `NodePool.Free` → `DisconnectIncomingAndOutgoingSignals` walks every node of every freed card and lists all its signals (Godot dictionaries), 60–340 ms on each grid init (open, sort); the actual disconnects are ~6 ms. `PoolSignalScan`: 12.3 s → 5.9 s over comparable gestures (avg 118 → 47 ms per grid init, worst ~280 ms). What remains is reading connections that do exist (engine/internal ones) only for `DisconnectSignal` to skip them: unavoidable without changing behavior.
- Harmony on `NodePool<NCard>` methods does not reach the shared code reference-type instantiations run (`NodePool`1[__Canon]`): patch non-generic entry points instead.
- Logs: a singleplayer session log is ~34 KB, nothing repeated at volume: no log reduction needed.

### Step G (2026-10-23): unattended 8,425-card sessions (`AutoTest`), singleplayer, 368 relics, all Workshop mods of the reference setup

Scenario: reference run imported once with relics, then its cards 8 more times (8,425 deck cards ≈ the friends' 8,274-card co-op combat), `fight CULTISTS_NORMAL` with 999,999-HP enemies. Measured with `PerfProbe` (per-method times + long frames) and dotnet-trace.

- Combat start (vanilla, mod off): ~31 s. Relic effects play one after the other with their animation waits (`AfterRoomEntered` ~6 s, `BeforeCombatStart`, first turn), each costing more because frames are slow. With 3,312 relics (relics imported 9×) it was 140 s. `FastCombatStart` (Instant-mode waits until the first play phase, 50+ relics): 23.7 → 10.4 s; same relic results (Vulnerable 7 / Weak 6 / block 37 / player powers identical with fast, normal and mod off).
- First turn: one 6 s frame = `SetupPlayerTurn`'s Innate filter on the draw pile: `c.Keywords` → `Hook.ModifyKeywordsInCombat` → full listener walk per card (O(n²)). In a refresh scope (`ExtraScopes`): 6,497 → 12 ms, same cards (checked against vanilla in Debug).
- Every frame in combat ~100–300 ms: BetterSpire2's `DamageTracker.Recalculate` (4×/s, `Hook.ModifyDamage` per enemy/intent outside any scope) was 22% of all CPU. In a refresh scope + listener lists reused across refresh scopes while nothing changed.
- `CardModel.Pile` (= `_owner.Piles.FirstOrDefault(p => p.Cards.Contains(this))`) is read through `CardModel.CombatState` everywhere: ~780k calls during one combat start, ~5 µs each with 8,000 cards. `PileIndex` answers in O(1). First version updated the index in postfixes and was caught by the exhaustive check: `AddInternal`/`RemoveInternal` raise `CardAdded`/`ContentsChanged` (whose listeners read `card.Pile`) before postfixes run → updated in prefixes, undone by a finalizer if the original throws.
- Combat end: `NetCombatCardDb.OnPileContentsChanged` re-walked a whole pile per removed card while `Player.AfterCombatEnd` clears the piles: 597 → 1 ms with `CardDbScan`; end-of-combat hitch 1,225 → 545 ms. Pile counters re-fitted their label 8,500 times (`PileCounterBatch`).
- Remaining (not addressed): per-frame rich-text effects (`RichTextGold`/`RichTextSine` custom FX, ~20% of idle CPU, mostly Godot→C# call overhead), hooks during actions outside scopes (each walks every listener), `SaveRun` ~100–160 ms after combat, rewards generation ~100 ms, ~40 ms GC per frame with 8k cards.
- `bdperf refresh`/`checksum` run vanilla first: at 8k cards they take minutes (the game looks frozen). Use them at ≤3k cards.
- With the scopes above, BetterSpire2's recalculation was dominated by `Creature.IsHittable` → `Hook.ShouldAllowHitting` (every listener per call): now filtered by `HookFilter`; filtered lists are kept while their (reused) source list is the same. `PatchSafety.IsPatchedByOthers` (`Harmony.GetPatchInfo`) was 1.3 s per 20 s of idle when called per scope: results cached 2 s.
- Release-like numbers (bench re-checks off, Normal speed, 8,425 cards + 368 relics): combat start 31 s (vanilla) → 4.4 s; idle combat frames no longer over 100 ms; card play ~0.46 s wall (36 ms CPU); end of turn ~0.3 s; combat end hitch ~1.2 s → 263 ms. Apotheosis on 8,400 cards: 0.4–0.7 s.
- Journey (`tools/autotest/scenarios/journey-8k.txt`, 8,425 cards, 368 relics, Workshop mods): combat, shop (~0.75 s hitch on entering), rest site (~0.2 s), elite start 2.5 s, The Kin boss start 3.7 s, boss killed → end-of-combat hitch 192 ms. Rooms (`rooms-8k`): treasure/event/map instant, deck view opens in ~50 ms.
- Not worth it: `CombatState.IterateHookListeners` outside scopes (each hook during actions walks every listener) is ~45% `Contains` per model, ~20% BaseLib subscribers, ~10% list growth: a mirror would gain ~10% at the heart of game logic. Only an action-wide verified cache would really help (future work, high care). After-combat save ~300 ms (240 ms Steam Cloud write) and card rewards ~80 ms are inherent.
- ~10,300 cards (`solo-10k`): combat start 4.9 s, card plays 0.3–0.5 s (Apotheosis on 10,300 cards 1.5 s), end turn → next turn 2.1 s, combat end hitch 0.36 s.
- Co-op turn transition (`coopturn`, host traced, ~8,400 combat cards, Workshop mods): ~9.6–9.9 s from ending the turn to playing again, the CPU busy all along. Biggest single cost ~1.6 s: **Limitless `MultiEnchantment.SetSerializableCards`**, called not only by `RecalculateValues`/`OnEnchant` but inside pure calculations (`EnchantBlockAdditive`/`Multiplicative`, damage equivalents): every block/damage computation re-serializes all inner cards (`CardModel.ToSerializable`, through BaseLib's extended save data). Not changed here: the serialized list is checksummed in co-op, so skipping refreshes could diverge from peers running Limitless without this mod; an upstream fix in Limitless (serialize only when the inner cards change) would remove it. Then hook walks during actions ~2.1 s, `QueueFreeSafely` ~0.6 s, `MegaLabel.AdjustFontSize` ~0.4 s, the Debug probe ~0.7 s (not in Release). Both instances share one PC (CPU/GPU), so this is pessimistic for real co-op.
- Local co-op (2 instances, ~3,750 cards each): two combats, every turn's state identical on both peers, 30 checksums (4 verified vs vanilla), 0 mismatch, no divergence. But `SnapshotVerifier`'s vanilla rebuild took ~9 s per verification at that size (first 3 actions + 1 in 25 froze ~9 s): verifications are now budgeted to ≤1% of play time (the first two extra checks only while a check costs < 250 ms), and skipped entirely above 1,000 combat cards or after a check over 1 s (user's choice 2026-10-23: no multi-second freeze; the earlier sessions never found a mismatch).

### Step J (2026-10-23): the 59 pile-scanning cards (`pile-cards-on/off/small/trace`)

- Scenario: 8,425 cards + 368 relics, No Draw applied (`NO_DRAW_POWER`; the earlier `NO_DRAW` id didn't exist), each card made free (`playcard X free`, X costs keep `setenergy 5`) and played alone; Grand Finale (needs an empty draw pile) and Regret (unplayable curse) left out. `CARDPLAY` lines log every play: exactly the 59 scenario cards manually, plus 12 auto-plays from Beat Down, Cascade, Catastrophe, Havoc and Uproar, identical in every session.
- Sum of per-card times: mod off 65.2 s, v0.3.0 patches 28.6 s, now 17.0 s; same cards with only the relics (`pile-cards-small`): 6.9 s. CHECKSUM/STATE/CARDPLAY lines identical on/off. Almost every card cost ~400 ms more at 8,400 cards than with a small deck: a per-action cost, not per-card scans.
- Trace of those 42 s (v0.3.0 patches): Limitless `SetSerializableCards` 17.9 s, almost all in `ConditionalWeakTable.AddOrUpdate` from BaseLib's extended save data postfixes (~7,000 multi-enchanted cards × inner cards × 2 entries per `RecalculateCardValues`; dependent handles also weigh on every GC). `LimitlessReserialize`: 6.3 million lists kept, 0 rebuilt, 0 Debug mismatch. Its remaining cost (~5 s per session) is the comparison itself, mostly BaseLib's save getters, which must run to stay exact.
- `FastListeners` filters walking every pile entry with a type lookup each: 4,663 uncached walks (filters tracking ExecutionFinished subscribers) + ~1,000 rebuilds per session → shared `PileSnapshot`. Tracking subscriptions instead was rejected here (the event accessors could be inlined, and a missed change would drop an `InvokeExecutionFinished`); Step K does it with safety nets.
- Inside ListenerCache scopes, hooks materialized every listener (~30,000) after each state change, e.g. for BetterSpire2's `IsHittable` 4×/s: `HookSkip` now also filters there.
- `HookFilter` built its per-hook lists by materializing every listener (~30,000) once per state version, then filtering them, mostly for the few hand cards refreshed after each action: now each hook's list comes from FastListeners' filtered walk (listeners outside piles + the cached pile entries overriding that hook), same objects and order, cached per scope and version (Debug: 1 build in 32 compared with the materialized way). Tried and dropped: letting the first calls after a change go to HookSkip's walk instead (the cost only moved).
- Allocations (`run.ps1 -TraceProfile gc-verbose`, AllocationTick stacks read with a TraceEvent tool): 2.4 GB per 10 s of turns, 78% from `LimitlessReserialize`'s own comparison (a new `ExtendedSaveData` and its dictionaries per inner card). Now one reused data object (dictionaries emptied, then BaseLib's registered getters run into it, as its constructor does) and lookups by registered id: 0.75 GB per 10 s, 14 GCs instead of 49, GC pauses ~100 ms per turn instead of ~280. What remains of it is BaseLib's own getter (`DirectModifiers(card).Select(...).ToList()`), left as is.
- 59 cards: 28.6 s (v0.3.0) -> 14.0 s. 5 turns + combat end, all on (`hookskip-on`) vs all off (`turns-off`): identical. Turn ~3.0 s (v0.3.0) -> ~1.6 s, plays 83–108 fps (45–55), end of turn 53–60 fps (20–25).
- Audit fixes: inside `CardPile.RandomizeOrderInternal` the game hands the pile's own list to `ModifyShuffleOrder` listeners, which reorder it in place with no bump, so FastListeners' caches (segments since v0.3.0, snapshots now) are bypassed there (`shuffle-on/off`: 6 turns with reshuffles, identical); `ListenerCache.RunVanilla` (the snapshot verifier's and the bench's vanilla reference) now also turns HookSkip off, which now runs inside the checksum scope; the deck snapshot no longer keeps the deck alive after a run; `LimitlessReserialize` treats empty collections as equal only for arrays and `System.Collections.Generic` types. Regression set: `pile-cards-on/off`, `hookskip-on`/`turns-off`, `shuffle-on/off`, `listeners-8k` (identical listeners), `hover-tips-8k`, `fast-start-check` (PileIndex checking every answer).
- Left: keepSubscribed walks (cache misses reading each entry's subscriber field), UI (`NCard.UpdateVisuals`, `NHandCardHolder.UpdateCard`, orb visuals' `ModifyOrbValue`), materialization for hooks HookSkip doesn't cover inside scopes, BaseLib's getter allocations.

### Step K (2026-10-24): copying thousands of cards (`clone-*`, `reflections-*`, `coopclone`)

- Campfire clone with Limitless' stacked Clone (`Patch_OnSelect.Replacement`: one `CardPileCmd.Add` per Clone stack) and Bing Bong (each relic copies every card added to the deck, `clonedBy` stops the recursion): the reference run imported twice = 1,880 cards with 1,520 Clone stacks and 4 Bing Bongs, so 7,600 adds (1,880 -> 9,480 cards); at 16,840 cards, 68,400 adds (-> 85,240). The user's trace (v0.4.0): >90 s, 94% in `CardPileCmd.Add`, of which FastListeners' deck walks rebuilt the whole deck snapshot and segments after every add (each `AddInternal` bumps `PileVersion`): O(n²).
- FastListeners: an add that appends a card at the end of a pile with nothing else changing between its two bumps is journaled (`_moves`, named `_appends` then: consecutive versions, cleared by any other change); a snapshot whose piles only got appends to their last pile since its version appends the new entries (`PileSnapshot.CatchUp`, same `Generation`), and each segment extends itself from the snapshot entries it hasn't consumed. Enchantment/affliction setters no longer bump for a card in no pile (`PileIndex.InNoPile`): a fresh clone gets its enchantment before it's added, which used to invalidate everything.
- Filters keeping ExecutionFinished subscribers (AfterCardChangedPiles, ModifyCardBeingAddedToDeck and most "after" hooks) are now cached too: `SubscriptionVersion` is bumped around `add_/remove_ExecutionFinished` (fingerprinted with `AfterCloned`, the only other writer, on fresh clones) and around the game's only subscriber's own methods (`NPlayerHand.AfterCardsSelected` / `OnSelectModeSourceFinished`), whatever the JIT did with the accessors there (Harmony 2.4 disables inlining of patched methods; the game isn't ReadyToRun; tier-0 code never inlines). Every build checks a cached hit of these filters against the live subscriber fields at most once every 2 s (one walk: constant cost); a difference stops caching them for the session.
- `RelicFlashMerge`: the screen went black during the clone (and the game crashed at 16k -> 85k): ~9,000 `NVfxParticleSystem` (GPU particles) over the relic bar, one per Bing Bong flash in the same frame, overflowing D3D12's per-frame descriptor heap ("Cannot bind uniform set because there's not enough room in the current frame's RESOURCES descriptor heap"). Now 27,126 -> 8,898 nodes, 0 renderer errors.
- Card-add previews are left exactly vanilla: the game caps them at ~51 on screen (`CardCmd.PreviewInternal`, a flying card keeps its place until it lands); in vanilla the clone freezes the game, then one wave of 51 flies (`clone-visual-vanilla`: all optimizations off except the flash merge, previews sampled 4×/s). A queue replaying the dropped previews (a stream of up to ~1,000) was tried and removed at the user's request.
- Second trace (16,840 -> 85,240): `List.IndexOf` 11 s = `RunState.ContainsCard` (`_allCards.Contains`; the card just created is last: now `LastIndexOf`, same comparer, same answer) + `CardPile.AddInternal`'s duplicate check (`Cards.Contains`; a PileIndex transpiler answers false when the index counted this add as the card's only pile, i.e. it was in no pile, which is also every combat move since the game removes the card first; 1 in `VerifyEvery` checked); `PatchSafety.IsPatchedByOthers` took a lock per hook call (now lock-free reads); the journaled-append `ConditionalWeakTable` (replaced by PileIndex). Pitfall: Harmony re-runs transpilers each time a method is re-patched, so the transpiler's "replaced once" flag must be local.
- Results: 16,840 -> 85,240 cards 33.9 s (first fix) -> 13.8 s (release-like: `bdperf probe off`, `bdperf segments verify off`), 89 fps afterwards; 1,880 -> 9,480: 15.7 s -> 1.7 s; three clones in a row from 1,880: 9,480 (1.7 s), 55,080 (16.5 s), 328,680 cards (6.6 min, then 122 fps at the campfire; a fourth is out of reach). Reflections/Shatter (copies the deck card by card with a 0.1 s / 0.2 s wait each, none at Instant): Instant 31.1 s -> 4.8 s (1,880 -> 11,285); Fast ~195 s both ways (the waits), 144 -> 180 fps during it, frames over 33 ms 206 -> 26.
- Checks: `clone-on/off` and `reflections-instant/-off`, `reflections-fast/-off` CHECKSUM identical; `bdperf segments verify all` (every cached walk compared) 0 mismatch in `clone-on` and `pile-cards-verify` (49,520 hits); `pile-cards-on` vs `pile-cards-off` identical, 14.7 s (v0.4.0) -> 13.3 s. Throttled subscriber check cost: 8 checks / 22 ms over the 14 s clone, 13 / 2.2 ms over the 59 cards.
- Co-op (`coopclone`, Workshop mods, host Necrobinder 945 cards + 2 Bing Bongs, client Ironclad): a 4-round combat (enemies `immortal until 4`, a round-limited immortality both peers apply identically), Shatter for both, the host's campfire clone (3,783 -> 12,903): same enemy state every round, same CHECKSUM after the combat, after Shatter and at the end, `bdperf verify` 0 mismatch, no divergence. Test pitfalls: the game's `kill all` console command makes a checksum on the host only, so every later checksum is misnumbered and the next comparison reports a divergence, mod on or off (`coopclonevan`, mod off: same report); a single action freezing the host longer than the network timeout (Shatter at Instant speed from 9,480 cards: 35 s) disconnects the client, as in vanilla (where the freeze is longer); an interrupted `coop.ps1`/`run.ps1` leaves its `.inprogress` marker, `mods_STEAMTEST` and possibly a changed cfg (restore from the backups, check `Enabled`).

### Step L (2026-10-24): card grids at ~32,000 cards, menu translations

- The user reported that scrolling the draw pile in combat still lagged a lot at ~32,000 cards (`try.ps1 -Scenario try-32k`). `pilescroll` (AutoTest) drives the combat pile view as a held mouse wheel; scenario `pile-scroll-32k` (31,808 cards, 736 relics). Before: open ~200 ms, a new row of cards ~33 ms (95th percentile 38 ms), jumps of more than two screens (dragging the scrollbar, which crosses many rows per pixel at this size) redraw every row each frame: 160–180 ms per frame.
- Trace: 87% of `NCardGrid.AssignCardsToRow` in BaseLib's `AddSubtypesToTypePlaquePatch.TryModifyPlaqueText`, a step BaseLib's transpiler adds to every `NCard.UpdateTypePlaque`: it walks every hook listener of the combat (all cards of every pile) to collect the type-line texts of those implementing `ICardTypeTextModifier` (~5.6 ms per card shown at this size). No loaded type implements it in the user's setup (nor in any decompiled mod), so the walk can't find anything and BaseLib returns the original text.
- `TypePlaqueSkip`: returns that original text without the walk while no loaded concrete type implements the interface (checked once, rechecked when an assembly referencing BaseLib loads); cards implementing `ICustomTypeTextCard`, another mod patching the method and a changed body (fingerprint) leave BaseLib's code as is. Option `card_type_line`. `bdperf plaque [n]`: same `LocString` object for every card, 5.6 ms -> 0.002 ms per card.
- Results (`pile-scroll-32k` vs `-off`, CHECKSUM identical before and after scrolling): open 170–290 -> 26–137 ms; scrolling 95th percentile 38.6 -> 4.8 ms per frame; 200 px per frame 20 -> 4.7 ms; scrollbar-like jumps 160 -> 23 ms per frame. Turns are unchanged (`turns-32k-on/off`, identical CHECKSUM/STATE): the walk only ran for grids there.
- Turns at 32k (`turns-32k-trace`, 5 turns, 42 s of game code): `CombatStateChanged` 27 s, of which Limitless' `RecalculateValues` with `LimitlessReserialize`'s comparison 12 s and the hand refresh 12 s; in the hand refresh, the energy cost color (`NCard.UpdateEnergyCostColor` -> `CardCostHelper.TryModifyEnergyCostWithHooks`) 8.3 s: a private copy of the cost hooks' loops over every combat listener (twice), which HookFilter's `Hook.ModifyEnergyCostInCombat` mirror didn't cover; the star cost color 1.6 s likewise. Both now mirrored over the filtered listeners inside ListenerCache scopes (Debug, with bench checks: 1 call in 64 compared with the game's helper run without caches, `turns-32k-checks`: 143 compared, 0 mismatch). 5 turns 38.7 -> 31.8 s, plays ~28 -> ~35 fps, CHECKSUM/STATE identical to `turns-32k-off`.
- `LimitlessReserialize`: BaseLib's table lookups (`ConditionalWeakTable.TryGetValue` for each inner card and its enchantment, ~5 s of the 12 s; enchantments have no registered getter, so their comparison was almost only that lookup) are now done once per stored list and object: a `StoredData` per kept list (its own ConditionalWeakTable, one lookup per call) holds the data object found for each slot while the slot holds the same object. BaseLib sets an object's entry only in the postfix that creates it, so a found entry stays that object's; contents are still compared every time. `turns-32k-checks`: 17.3 M lists kept, every 4,096th re-serialized and compared byte for byte, 0 mismatch. 5 turns 31.8 -> 28.5 s (38.7 s before Step L's turn fixes), plays ~35 -> ~39 fps.
- Allocations (`turns-32k-trace -TraceProfile gc-verbose`, 20 s of turns): 2.27 GB, 963 MB (42%) from BaseLib's "BaseLibCardModifiers" card save getter (`DirectModifiers(card).Select(...).ToList()`), run by `LimitlessReserialize`'s expected data for every inner card at every comparison. With that save as the only one registered for cards, its lambda and RegisterSave's wrapper unchanged (fingerprints; the wrapper's taken on its generic definition, the closed method's differs) and no concrete CardModifier type loaded (`BaseLibTypes.NoneDefined`, rechecked when an assembly loads; the list is a NotNullSpireField of CardModifier instances, empty by default), the result is known: an empty list under its id, without running the getter. `turns-32k-checks`: 17.6 M getter calls skipped, 0 mismatch in the byte comparisons (which run the real getter). 5 turns 28.5 -> 25.2 s, GC pauses per turn ~1.0 -> ~0.5 s, plays ~39 -> ~44 fps; CHECKSUM/STATE identical.
- Then the data comparison itself, when that expected data is known without building it (no registered save: every stored dictionary must be empty; the empty card modifier save alone: the stored data must hold exactly an empty list of the same type under its id and nothing else; the same answer as the full comparison, case by case): no scratch data cleared and filled, no id walk. 5 turns 25.2 -> 21.3 s (38.7 s in v0.5.0), plays ~44 -> ~52 fps, end of turn ~26 -> ~30 fps; CHECKSUM/STATE identical, 0 byte-comparison mismatch.
- Regression set after these changes (2026-10-24): `pile-cards-on/off` identical (131 lines), 59 cards 62.8 s off -> 11.3 s on (13.3 s in v0.5.0); `shuffle-on/off`, `clone-on/off` identical; `listeners-8k` identical listeners; `hover-tips-8k` 12/12 TIPS OK; `fast-start-check` identical first-turn filter, PileIndex 228,287 answers verified.
- Co-op (`coop.ps1 -Name coopclone -WithMods`, Limitless/BaseLib loaded, both windows hidden and muted): same CHECKSUM on both peers after the combat, after Shatter and at the end, `bdperf verify` 0 mismatch, no divergence (one end-of-turn action rejected 136 ms after the combat ended, identically on both, as the game does at a combat end).
- Conflicts with other mods: `PatchSafety.IsPatchedByOthers` (each back-off from a method another mod patches) records the calling patch (`[CallerFilePath]`: the patch's file name, which is its `Name`) with the other mods' names (the loaded mod's display name, as the game's mods screen shows it: the mod whose assemblies, or whose folder, hold the patch method, else the mod whose manifest id is a dot-separated part of the Harmony id, else the Harmony id; one mod's several Harmony ids read as that mod once: "Re-audit of the fourth pass", third pass); `Settings.Conflict` turns that into the tooltip's orange "Partly inactive: what this optimization speeds up is also changed by <mods> (normal game for that part)." (translated, the switch stays clickable), `bdopt` adds " (partly vanilla because of <mods>)", the log says it once per method. Known once the patch has checked that method (when it first runs). `conflict-check`: a bool prefix (`fakeconflict ... boolprefix`) under another Harmony id on the cost color helper -> hook_filter reports it, other options don't (a plain postfix is judged by the shape rules at tolerant sites: "Foreign patches judged by their shape"). Since 2026-11-15 they are known from the main menu on: see "Back-offs visible before they happen".
- Settings menu translations: `Localization/<game language code>.json` (fra, deu, spa, esp, ita, ptb, pol, rus, tur, jpn, kor, zhs, tha: every language the game ships), embedded in the DLL (the game treats every .json in a mod folder as a manifest), read by `Config/Translations.cs`; English stays in the code and is the fallback for any other language (e.g. zht) or missing key. The game has one code per language (Steam only reports "english", "french"...; regional variants map to the same code), Spanish has two (spa, esp). `menu-languages` (AutoTest `language <code>`, session only) captures the menu in each language and logs the texts: 13 translated, zht in English. `package.py` checks every file has the same keys, no empty text, and is embedded.

- **Correction (2026-10-27): the turn times above are inflated.** Until v0.6.2 `FastCombatStart`'s window never closed in solo, so every animation wait of every combat (enemy turns, attacks) was skipped with the mod on and kept with it off: "5 turns 38.7 -> 21.3 s" compared vanilla with animations to the mod without them. With v0.6.2's fix, `turns-32k-on` on public v0.107.1 takes 35.1 s (end of turn 4.4–5.2 s at 134–146 fps, plays 46–55 fps); the per-frame numbers (plays fps, pile scroll, GC) are unaffected. The vanilla side was not re-measured: compare with a new `turns-32k-off` before quoting turn times again.

### Update reactions (2026-10-27, `tools/autotest/mutations.ps1`, public copy, all Workshop mods)

Each row: a modified `sts2.dll` (or Limitless / BaseLib in `<copy>/mods`) written by `tools/mutate`, then `release-checks` (combat + 13 vanilla-vs-optimized comparisons). Every session ran to its end with no error from this mod, every comparison identical, and exactly these parts went vanilla:

| Mutation | What changed | Left vanilla |
|---|---|---|
| renumber-hooks | every `<Hook>d__N` renumbered (a member added above) | nothing (v0.6.4: every hook state machine's fingerprint changed) |
| new-hook | new `AbstractModel` virtual method | nothing |
| unrelated-body | `Log.Info` changed | nothing |
| energy-hook | `Hook.ModifyEnergyCostInCombat` | that hook in HookFilter and HookSkip |
| default-hitting | `AbstractModel.ShouldAllowHitting` default no longer plain `return true` | that hook in HookFilter and HookSkip |
| default-task | `AbstractModel.AfterCardPlayed` default | HookSkip's AfterCardPlayed |
| helper-push | `PlayerChoiceContext.PushModel` | the 83 HookSkip hooks using choice contexts (63 still filtered) |
| pile-remove | `CardPile.RemoveInternal` | PileIndex, FastListeners, HookSkip (needs FastListeners) |
| pile-writer | new `CardPile.ReverseInternal` changing `_cards` | ListenerCache, PileIndex, FastListeners, HookSkip |
| rename-method | `CardPile.RemoveInternal` renamed | ListenerCache, PileIndex, FastListeners, HookSkip |
| rename-field | `CombatState._allies` renamed | FastListeners, HookSkip |
| internal-member | `CardEnergyCost.GetWithModifiers` made internal | HookFilter's cost colors only |
| signature | `AbstractModel.ShouldPlay` gains a parameter | HookFilter's ShouldPlay only (HookSkip's: hook code changed) |
| limitless-body / limitless-tabs | Limitless `SetSerializableCards` / `UpdateEnchantmentVisuals` | LimitlessReserialize / EnchantmentTabReuse |
| baselib-plaque | BaseLib `TryModifyPlaqueText` | TypePlaqueSkip |

Found while building this: the test runner (AutoTest) itself called game members directly and was skipped by the load check on modified games (now through reflection; `run.ps1` closes the game when it can't load); making the `CardModel.EnergyCost` property internal breaks the unmodified game itself (its serialization reads public properties), so it isn't a usable test. Fallback test: `guard-async-on` (pile cache throws once) vs `guard-async-ref`: same checksums, FastListeners and HookSkip off afterwards.

### v0.7.0 audit (2026-10-27): crashes, change detection, performance

Every place this mod's code runs inside the game was reviewed for what an exception there would do:

- Harmony prefixes/postfixes on game methods: each catches its own errors and falls back (added for the per-card-move prefixes of PileIndex and CardDbScan, which relied on `PatchGuard` alone: the move that threw would still have failed once).
- Lazily run code (iterators run by the caller, async hooks): FastListeners' pile-entry cache and mod-subscriber snapshot, and HookSkip's per-call checks, fall back to the vanilla walk on an error (an exception in an async state machine never reaches `PatchGuard`). The subscriber walk's reflected fields are resolved and type-checked at load: a renamed field used to be found null only inside every walk.
- Transpilers: Harmony reruns them whenever another mod patches the same method later, so they never throw (HookSkip's no longer assumes its table entry exists).
- Event handlers: the `AssemblyLoad` handlers run inside other assemblies' loads and never throw (unknown = conservative answer); the menu DLL is load-checked like a patch before BaseLib's screen can run its code, and its text update can't break that screen.
- Missing members at load: the patch is skipped (update), never left half-applied.

Assumptions that were not checked and now are: which piles CardDbScan's database walks (no longer derived from `IsCombatPile`; `StartCombat` fingerprinted), `OrbQueue._orbs` writers and `TryEnqueue` adding before its first await (ListenerCache), card enchantment/affliction writers (ListenerCache, FastListeners), other mods' patches on a hook's state machine (HookSkip). `tools/fpdiff deps <dll> <data dir>` lists, per patch, the game/mod methods used and whether they're pinned; the unpinned ones left are plain getters, invalidation entry points (their writers checked) and UI-only code.

Performance: combat unchanged vs v0.6.4 (`fps-8k`, 2 alternating runs each: plays ~140 fps, end of turn ~221 fps both). Load (the mod's `Init`, Debug, test tooling ~115 ms included): v0.6.4 961 ms, v0.7.0 351 ms. Profile of the first v0.7.0 build (832 ms): `PatchGuard` comparing every patch of the mod before and after each Apply 217 ms, guard finalizers added afterwards (one more rebuild of each patched method) 138 ms, HookSkip's ~146 transpilers ~200 ms, fingerprints 19 ms, field-writer scans 8 ms. Changes:
- each patch applies with its own Harmony id (`thekiwi7.BigDeckOptimization.<patch>`): what it installed is what that id owns (`PatchGuard.Owned`), no global comparison; `PatchGuard.IsOurs` recognizes every id of this mod (other-mod checks, fault removal);
- the guard finalizer is added in the same Harmony call as the patch (`PatchGuard.Patch`): one rebuild per method; none on async state machines (it could never fire there);
- HookSkip checks every hook at load but transpiles them 8 per frame afterwards (`PatchOverFrames`, main thread): hooks only run in a run, seconds later, and one not patched yet runs vanilla; the summary line comes when the last one is patched;
- member scans cached across the field-writer checks.
Per-patch load times are in the log (`Patch X applied (N ms)`).

### Step O (2026-10-29/30): the end of a 5-player run (`party5-*`, `party5coop`)

The user's 5-player co-op run (Limitless chapter 4, floors 39-45) lagged at the end. Its game log stopped at floor 40: the log is capped at 64 MB and 80% of it was the game's own errors for controller actions missing from the InputMap (an old `controller_mapping` in `settings.save` with v0.10x names: `controller_d_pad_up`, `controller_r_stick_*`...; 9 errors with C# backtraces per mouse event; resetting the controls fixes it, not this mod). The run history explains the end: one Necrobinder's Oblivions carried Limitless's Clone enchantment, doubled at each campfire (512 at floor 39, 1,024 at 41, 2,048 at 44): 4,112 cards, each multi-enchanted (Swift 3 + Clone) and upgraded +10; ~480 relics in all.

Reproduced on the public copy (`party5.run` in the shared folder: that Necrobinder's deck and relics, the other players' 400 relics, enemies 999,999 HP and immortal):

- **Hex** (Spectral Knight, Knights elite, floor 42 of the run): `HexPower.AfterApplied` afflicts every card one by one, and each `AfflictionModel.Amount` set calls `RecalculateCardValues` over every card (O(n²), Limitless's `MultiEnchantment.RecalculateValues` 17 million times), plus a pile-entry rebuild per card for `ShouldAfflict`. End of turn 1 at 4,138 combat cards: vanilla **310 s** (270 s of GC, 2 GB heap), v0.7.0 10.3 s, + `LimitlessRecalcSkip` 3.8 s, + `FastListeners` affliction version **2.3 s** (beta copy 2.3 s). Removing Hex (`ClearAfflictionInternal` per card) takes the same path.
- **Plays** (solo, 80 Oblivions per turn, `party5-on`): ~0.2 s per play is the game's animation; fps ~130 (vanilla 9 fps, 223 s for the same 80 plays). 1% low 38 -> ~50 fps with `TextPoolReuse`.
- **Co-op** (`party5coop`, 2 instances on one PC, host with the big deck, Soul Nexus elite, 40 plays per turn): the checksum after every action (`FromRun`) was 57% of all allocations while cards were played (539 of 947 MB in 13 s), the hand's card texts (SmartFormat via Limitless's stacked enchantment lines) another 156 MB, then this mod's own `HookFilter` closures, `Player.Piles` iterators (`PileIndex`) and doubling listener lists (`ListenerCache`). Host while playing, before -> after: 1% low 9-10 -> 25 fps, frames over 100 ms 9 / 14 -> 0 / 0, GC pauses per 13 s turn 1.2 / 1.5 s -> 0.18 / 0.2 s, gen1 collections 20 -> 2-3 per turn, heap 500-760 -> ~300 MB. Checksum allocations 539 -> 255 MB per window before `TextPoolReuse` (the rest is mostly `PerfProbe`'s Debug-only `__originalMethod` stubs).

Equivalence: `party5-off` (bdopt off, 15 min) vs `party5-on` and `party5-verify` (every cached FastListeners walk compared with a fresh one: 0 mismatch): CHECKSUM/STATE identical (190 lines); `release-checks`, `ab-2k`, `pile-cards-2k`, `shuffle` identical; `party5coop`, `coopclone -WithMods`: no divergence; sampled checks of the new patches 0 mismatch (RecalcSkip ~26,000 full comparisons, SnapshotCardReuse byte comparisons). Beta copy: every patch applies (HookSkip 147/147).

Release checks v0.8.0 (public copy): `turns-off` vs `hookskip-on` and `hookskip-off` vs `hookskip-on` (8,400 cards, 54 lines), `pile-cards-off/on` (131 lines) identical, 0 mismatch in every sampled check; `coop`, `coopfast`: enemy states identical on both peers, no divergence; `settings.ps1`, `baselib-optional.ps1`, `limitless-switches.ps1`, `coop-required.ps1` as expected (26 patches with BaseLib + Limitless; without Limitless its two options greyed, `coop_checksum` still available). `memory-on` grows ~3 MB per turn over 15 turns (169 -> 217 MB live), as v0.7.0 does on the same build setup (163 -> 209 MB, tag rebuilt and run the same day): not from this release; `memory-off` (hook_skip off) starts higher and ends at the same size (216 -> 223 MB), so it looks like combat state growing, not a cache. Beta copy: `release-checks`, `ab-2k`, `pile-cards-2k`, `shuffle` identical, `coopclone`/`coopfast` no divergence; `bdperf refresh` at 959 cards is slower with the refresh scope than without on the beta (1.15 -> 1.55 ms; v0.7.0 1.35 -> 1.80 ms, same build day): an existing small-size cost of ListenerCache's scope there, not new. Release build in game: `Initialized v0.8.0` (316 ms), every patch applied.

Left: the remaining 2.3 s of Hex is mostly `RecalculateCardValues`' loop itself (~0.35 ms per affliction at 4,100 cards: reading each memo's inputs is memory-bound) and the game's own per-card work (keywords, affliction bookkeeping). The hand's UI refresh after each action (`NCard.UpdateVisuals` of 10 Limitless cards) is the next co-op cost.

### Step P (2026-10-31 to 11-01): v0.9.0, audit and the 10,000-card co-op goal

Goal given by the user for v1: 10,000 cards in co-op, no visible lag in combat, always above 120 fps, judged on the 1% low. Numbers below: Debug builds (same optimization level as Release), public copy, all Workshop mods unless said; co-op = two instances on one PC, the host's numbers.

**The finalizer bug (in v0.7.0 and v0.8.0).** `PatchGuard.Patch` added its guard finalizer in the same `PatchProcessor` call as the patch; a processor holds one finalizer, so on a method first patched with a finalizer the guard replaced the patch's own. Lost until now: `ExitSnapshot` on `FromRun`, `ExitRefresh` on `RecalculateCardValues`, `UpdateCard`, `UpdateVisuals` and `DamageTracker.Recalculate`, `SetCardsFinalizer` (`GridSortCache` never sorted from its cache, its counter stayed 0), `BumpSubscriptionsFinally`, `BaseLibHooks.Finalizer`. Fixed: the guard goes through a second processor when the patch has a finalizer; `CheckInstalled` (BENCH) compares what Harmony holds with what was asked and logs `PatchGuard: MISMATCH`. This was found by asking for proof that each optimization runs, not by any A/B: an optimization silently off gives identical results.

**Effects, not "applied"** (user, 2026-10-31: tests only proved that patches were installed). Every optimization has a BENCH counter of what it really did (`effects` command, `tools/autotest/effects.py`); `effects-check` runs them all: public 34/35 (`ReplayStateSkip` needs co-op: 65-83 there), beta 35/35. Mutations compare with a session on the unmodified game: only the effects the mutation may take away may drop to zero, and CHECKSUM/STATE/CARDPLAY lines stay identical. 28/28 as expected on the final code. No effect counter: `ModImage`, `SnapshotVerifier`.

**Disabling only what changed.** A fingerprint now only disables what relies on that code: `FastListeners` has three layers (walks, cached pile entries, moves), `PileIndex` and others check tracked methods by their effect (`_selfCheck`) when a body changed instead of switching off, `CardDbScan` no longer depends on `AddInternal`'s body, `SnapshotCardReuse` no longer needs `LimitlessReserialize` applied (it reads `ComparisonReady`; `LimitlessRecalcSkip` always needed it, its memo is filled by LimitlessReserialize's prefix, and `LimitlessHookSkip` needs it again since the 2026-11-13 audit), `SnapshotCardReuse` has parts (`PatchPart`, `RequirePartResolvable`). Limit: `RequireResolvable` still takes a whole class outside marked parts. `InlineGuard`: a patched method the JIT inlines at a later tier silently loses its patch; every patched original gets the no-inlining flag. Checked on a whole session (`tools/profiling/inlines`): 73,038 inlinings, 5 of patched methods, none of ours really.

**New optimizations** (patch table): `CardFlightLimit`, `GcPacing`, `LimitlessHookSkip`, `ReplayStateSkip`, `ThemeColorSkip`, BaseLib's downgrade hook filtered (Dampen on a whole deck: Knights' end of turn at 8,425 cards, worst frame 1,326 -> 64 ms; apply at 1,880 cards 528 -> 78 ms). The downgrade filter has no in-game implementer to test against (no installed mod implements `IAfterCardDowngraded`): same mechanism as the heal and hand size filters.

**Before / after (v0.8.0 vs v0.9.0, same scenarios, same day)**

| Scenario | Moment | v0.8.0 | v0.9.0 |
|---|---|---|---|
| `hookskip-on` (8,400 cards) | plays: avg / 1% low / worst | 130-178 fps / 9-31 / 32-110 ms | 185-234 fps / 17-79 / 13-61 ms |
| | end of turn | 219-223 fps / 58-66 / 53-82 ms | 236-240 fps / 125-156 / 9-24 ms |
| | GC pauses per turn | 33-118 ms | 0 |
| `turns-32k-on` (~32,000 cards) | plays | 55-76 fps / 5-9 / 184-239 ms | 133-199 fps / 11-45 / 22-129 ms |
| | end of turn | 150-164 fps / 15-18 / 133-384 ms | 227-236 fps / 105-132 / 16-83 ms |
| | one turn, GC pauses | 5.4-7.1 s, 213-438 ms | 3.4-4.5 s, 0 |
| `apotheosis-32k` | first Apotheosis | 1,021 ms | 295 ms |
| `coop10k -WithMods` (Limitless) | plays: 1% low / worst | 5-6 fps / 207-375 ms | 7-34 fps / 55-191 ms |
| | end of turn | 4-6 fps / 306-384 ms | 60-73 fps / 47-193 ms |
| `coop10kplain` (no other mod) | plays: 1% low / worst | 22-28 fps / 69-83 ms | 78-101 fps / 26-122 ms (one 618 ms GC) |

Co-op caution: v0.8.0's test tooling has no `uncap` command, so its co-op sessions ran at the game's 60 fps cap with vsync (log: `max fps 60`, `vsync Adaptive`) and v0.9.0's uncapped: averages left out, its 1% lows understated; worst frames are the fair comparison there. `reshuffle-16k`: v0.8.0's tooling has no `pilemove`, so its session never reshuffled the pile; no before/after for this one (v0.9.0: 145 fps average, 1% low 8, worst 330 ms, 8 frames over 100 ms: still visible at 16,800 cards, the remaining cost of a reshuffle at that size). The Knights had no v0.8.0 session either (no `sweep`): 1,326 ms is this build before the filter.

**V1 goal status: not reached.** Without other mods it's close (co-op at 10,000 cards: plays 1% low 78-127, rare GC pauses of 500-600 ms). With Limitless it isn't: plays 1% low 7-45 (one turn in that last run had 12 frames over 100 ms; earlier runs none), with no dominant cost left in the profile (state-changed refresh 23%, post-action checksum 16%, FastListeners.Combat 14% inclusive, hand refresh 11%, ENet 9%): diminishing returns. `SnapshotCardReuse` only runs with BaseLib + Limitless.

**Whole-game A/B (`parsweep`, mod on vs off, ~1,900 cards, final mod code).** Every potion, relic, encounter, card and power on the public and beta versions, in chunks with state lines at each step: nothing differs. Public: relics 4,450 lines, potions 328, cards 3,331 + the chunk rerun after a renderer freeze. A whole relic sweep at animation speed and at instant speed: 4,450 lines identical on both sides. `LIVING_FOG_NORMAL` stopped a session on both sides, and so did `PANDORAS_BOX` until v0.10.0's tooling closed its card screen (`SweepSettle`). Since 2026-11-19 the relic's `obtain` step also waits, bounded, for the relic's obtain to end (`SweepObtain`): the console's `relic add` doesn't wait for it, and Pandora's transform adds the cards one by one outside the action queue, so the step's checksum caught the deck at a point set by the session's pace (1,035 vs 1,041 cards). Test races fixed on the way: a sweep waits for the reward screen after a win (the game rolls reward cards 0.5 s later, so A/B runs drifted), a kill waits for the game's state to settle, not a short time. Tooling: `docs/TEST_IMPROVEMENTS.md`.

**Memory.** Asked by the user for the release checklist. `memory-runs-on/off`, `tomenu`, `leakcheck` (walks every object reachable from the statics of the mod, of the game and other mods, or of the test tooling, looking for a run; `current` is the positive control). Found: `LimitlessRecalcSkip._quietState` kept the last left run alive: now a weak reference, and nothing of the mod reaches a left run any more. The game itself keeps **every** left run alive, mod off too (`left runs alive 4/4`): heap at the menu 104.6 -> 169.3 MB over 4 runs mod off, ~21 MB per run at 1,900 cards; `NodePool._usedObjects` holds `NCard`s whose model holds the run, and emptying the pools and freeing those nodes still leaves them alive with no static path. `leakroots.ps1` (`dotnet-dump gcroot`) on all 4 left runs, mod on and off, public and beta, names the holders: strong GC handles on `TweenHelper` closures of `NCardTrailVfx` and `NRelicFlashVfx`, and for the last run `NGame` -> `NReactionWheel` -> `Player`; no chain through this mod's released code. Mod on: 119.2 -> 191.2 MB, ~24 MB per run. `dotnet-gcdump` on vs off names the difference: `LimitlessRecalcSkip.Memo` and friends, 18,656 = exactly 4 x 4,664, one lot per run, in weak tables keyed by objects the game keeps. Left as is: not the mod's to fix (touching game objects when a run ends). Within a combat the heap is flat: `memory-on` 167 -> 157 MB over 15 turns at 8,400 cards (v0.8.0: 169 -> 217 MB; the v0.8.0 entry in Step O blaming combat state growing was a guess). Why v0.8.0 grew isn't known: putting the finalizer bug back leaves the heap flat, and so does `coop_checksum` off (no replay recording in solo).

**Startup.** Harmony is 264-340 ms of ~330-360 ms of work (Debug; fingerprints 13-27, field writers 10-13, load checks 22-25). The total isn't reduced much; what changed is when it happens: `ModEntry.ApplyOverFrames` applies patches over 6-8 frames (budget 4 ms or a quarter of a frame), flushed when a run starts, so the game's first frames don't freeze. Release build, 3 launches: `Initialized` 16-43 ms, then 333-339 ms of work over 6-8 frames, finished 1.9-2.2 s after the start (v0.7.0: 273 ms all at once; more patches since). When the frame before was very long (a loading frame), its quarter-frame budget can take everything at once: seen once, "over 1 frames". InlineGuard's rebuilds wait for the main menu and HookSkip's hooks, then run 4 ms a frame (2026-11-20, P2); HookSkip's hooks are patched within 4 ms a frame instead of 8 a frame (2026-11-20, P5).

**Release checks v0.9.0.** Public copy: `check_versions` all verified, `release-checks` 0 left vanilla and every `bdperf` comparison identical; `solo-8k`, `fast-start-check`, `conflict-check`, `chemical-x`, `menu-languages` no problem, `guard-checks` only its expected fault; `guard-async-ref/on` 19, `turns-off` vs `hookskip-on` 54, `hookskip-off` vs `hookskip-on` 54, `pile-cards-off/on` 131, `shuffle-off/on` 55 lines identical; `settings.ps1`, `baselib-optional.ps1`, `limitless-switches.ps1`, `coop-required.ps1` as expected, user files restored; `coop`, `coopfast`, `coopclone -WithMods` no divergence. Beta copy: `tools/compat` 0 problem, 0 left vanilla, effects 35/35, `ab-2k` 54 and `shuffle` 55 lines identical (`pile-cards-2k`, `reshuffle-2k`, pacing, `coop10kplain` earlier the same day, before the last removals of unused code), `coopfast`, `coopclone -WithMods` no divergence. Mutations 28/28. Release build in game: `Initialized v0.9.0`, nothing skipped; `bdopt` wasn't typed by hand in that build (Debug only). Not tested: a real co-op game between two people with this version.

**Size.** Release: DLL 458 KB (translations 103 KB, UI texts 47 KB, embedded PDB 42 KB), Menu DLL 25 KB, image 242 KB; ~455 KB zipped. `nullablePublicOnly` in Release and test-only members moved under `#if BENCH`: -7.5 KB. Compressing translations (-69 KB on disk, ~0 zipped), dropping the PDB (loses line numbers in error logs) and packing fingerprints as numbers (-12 KB, touches the update protection) aren't worth it.

**Next, towards the V1 goal.** The worst frame of a co-op combat start (848 ms on v0.8.0, 927-1,092 ms on v0.9.0, one or two samples each) is one blocking gen2 collection, 640-817 ms of it, in both versions: run that collection during the loading, when nothing moves. Plays with Limitless stay the main gap.

Known limits: `LimitlessHookSkip` (an enchantment of a new type reacting to a hook, added to a stacked enchantment during that hook's walk, isn't called by that walk; only another mod could do it: on v0.113.1 the game enchants with reacting types only at events, in relics' `AfterObtained` and at card rewards, and its one in-combat enchant, BladeOfInk's Inky, overrides none of the five forwarded hooks); `ListenerCache.Served` is 0 in 10k co-op (it still provides the scopes `HookFilter` uses).

### Step Q (2026-11-02): what deck size still costs, rendered, in co-op, with a mouse

**How this was measured, and what doesn't work.** A headless sweep at two sizes (`parsweep -Imports`, `scalereport.py`) said a typical card costs the same at 969 and 10,318 cards (94 ms main thread, 1.1 MB). That was read as "deck size has almost no impact any more". The user corrected it: it isn't 240 fps constant, combats take longer, co-op is worse, above all when playing cards. They were right, and Step P's own table already said so. Headless has no rendering, no real animation time at instant speed, a 15.6 ms clock for per-action CPU, and no mouse; it sees average work, not the hitches of a frame. **Use it to find which items are heavy, never to conclude that something is smooth.**

The measure now: `ladder.ps1`, the same rendered session at each size (the reference deck imported N times, so the same card mix), uncapped, the host alone on the GPU and the client headless; `framedetail` (percentiles, process step / draw step / GPU / rest per frame), `PLAYTIME` (click to next action), `mousesweep`, `mouseplay` (hover, drag, target, release through the game's input path). Blind spots this removed: a session's window never has the focus, so `NInputManager._UnhandledInput` returned at once; and `play` calls the card directly, so hover and drag never ran. Two instances rendering on one PC made co-op look capped at ~144 fps each.

**Results: co-op host, public v0.107.1, all Workshop mods, 5 turns (each player that many cards, so the combat holds twice that).**

| Cards per player | 945 | 4,685 | 10,295 | 20,580 | 32,735 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Plays: average fps | 194 | 207 | 210 | 149 | 132 |
| Plays: 1% low | 77 | 65 | 44 | 19 | 12 |
| Plays: process step p99 (ms) | 10 | 12 | 14 | 51 | 58 |
| Mouse plays: 1% low | 129 | 111 | 116 | 63 | 42 |
| One play, median / p90 (ms) | 114 / 188 | 107 / 198 | 117 / 191 | 121 / 248 | 177 / 315 |
| Combat start: worst frame (ms) | 352 | 462 | 879 | 1,716 | 3,020 |
| Combat start: GC pauses (ms) | 231 | 446 | 869 | 1,887 | 4,076 |
| Idle: fps | 395 | 383 | 393 | 364 | 324 |

Solo at the same sizes: idle flat at ~470-480 fps up to 41,150 cards (so the idle cost in co-op comes with co-op, process step 1.00 -> 1.52 ms); plays 1% low 142 / 51 / 28 / 54 at 945 / 10,295 / 20,580 / 41,150 cards, process p99 6 / 16 / 28 / 34 ms; median play 74 -> 100 ms against co-op's 114 -> 177 ms; combat start worst 280 -> 1,333 ms. At 10,295 cards a frame still has room when nothing happens (p50 2.3 ms, 393 fps): what breaks 240 fps is **transient**: the frames of an action, the frames of a hover, and isolated collections. The user's own 5-player deck (`-Party5`, host only, stacked enchantments, 4,112 and 3x that, the client with 400 relics and a small deck): plays 1% low 142 / 82, process p99 5.8 / 15 ms, combat start worst 323 / 575 ms: the same behaviour.

**New leads, by what they'd bring towards 10,000 cards in co-op.** Trace: `lad-tracec22` (20,580 cards per player, 70 s of main thread over 5 turns of both peers playing); the sampler inflates times, read them as shares. The `Keywords` numbers come from an earlier trace of the same session, which caught a chain of `Catastrophe`.

1. **Per-action frames, the largest gap.** At 20,580 cards one play is one or two frames of ~55-65 ms, in almost every turn. `ChecksumTracker.GenerateChecksum`, 95 calls, 33 ms each: `FromRun` 19 ms (mostly the piles), `Serialize` 7.5, hash 6.5; twice per action in co-op (`JustBeforeActionFinished` and `RunManager.SendPostActionChecksum`). `CombatStateTracker` refresh, 284 calls, ~20 ms each: hand cards `UpdateCard` 11 ms, `NOrbManager.UpdateVisuals` 4.6 ms, `NEndTurnButton.HasPlayableCard` 2.5 ms (each `card.CanPlay()` walks hooks). Nothing here has one dominant cost: the size-dependent part is listener walks and the snapshot. Next step: a focused allocation and frame-by-frame trace of one play.
2. **Scans of the draw pile calling `Keywords` (new, large on some cards).** `card.Keywords` calls `Hook.ModifyKeywordsInCombat`, whose only implementer in the game is `HexPower`. Outside a `ListenerCache` scope `HookFilter.Active` is false, so each call rebuilds the walk: relics, potions, orbs and powers of each player into a list, then `CombatContains` on each (`FastListeners.Combat`, 86% of the hook's time). That is paid per card, per scan: `Catastrophe` (one scan per card it plays), `Uproar`, `HiddenGem`. Trace: 15.5 s inside `Catastrophe`'s filter at 20,580 cards; headless sweep, `CATASTROPHE` +734 ms, `HIDDEN_GEM` +390 ms at 10,318 cards. Fix to try: per hook, a mask of which kinds of model override it (known once all mods are loaded), so the walk doesn't build what the hook can't call. It reaches every one of the 146 filtered hooks (`FastListeners.Combat` is ~4.5% of this trace). Same order, same objects: A/B identical expected.
3. **Blocking collections, proportional to the heap.** Combat start allocates 455 MB at 10,295 cards, 1.36 GB at 32,735, ending in a blocking gen2. It also comes at any other moment, once per session at worst: end of turn 447 ms at 10,295, mouse plays 555 ms at 20,580, hover 566 ms at 32,735, solo end of turn 543 ms at 41,150. Step P's idea stands (collect during the loading, when nothing moves), and the allocations themselves are the other half: find what combat start allocates per card.
4. **Hover (new, never measured).** `NHoverTipSet.CreateAndShow` builds label nodes at each hover, 110 calls and 5.3 s in the trace (`Init` 4.1 s), with no dependence on deck size; `hover` windows show p99.9 of 8-14 ms at any size, single frames up to 58 ms. `HoverTipReuse` only covers creatures. Reusing a card's tip set would take hover under the 8.3 ms budget.
5. **Controller actions missing from the InputMap (lead from the mouse, smaller than it looked).** A `settings.save` from an older version names 9 actions that no longer exist (`controller_d_pad_up`, `controller_r_stick_*`, `controller_l_stick_press`: the user's account has them). `_UnhandledInput` asks about each at every unhandled event, each error with a C# backtrace written to the log: ~240 errors per played card, none while merely moving over the UI. Same session, 8 mouse plays, saved map vs the game's defaults, 945 cards: 1% low 195 -> 246 fps, p99 5.1 -> 4.1 ms, frames over 8.3 ms 9 -> 2 (one sample each, different cards). Fix to try: skip names missing from the InputMap: they can never match, so the result is the same without the errors. Affects anyone whose settings predate the rename; also what filled the 64 MB log in Step O.
6. **Chains of auto-played cards (rendering).** `CASCADE` with 50 energy at 945 cards: 57-67 s, 87 fps, 1% low 38 in co-op; draw calls ~2,500 -> ~7,600 per frame over all the mouse plays of that session, on that turn draw step 4.5 ms, GPU 3 ms, process step 6.7 ms. It comes with what's on screen, not with deck size. Whether this happens with real energy is untested.
7. **Rich text effects, every frame, every size.** `RichTextGold` and `RichTextSine._ProcessCustomFX` are a C# call per glyph per frame: 14 of 70 s in the trace (inflated by the sampler). Gold only sets a color. Low priority: idle is well above 240 fps.

**Outside combat: the freeze at each room entry (new, the largest single frame of a run).** `autoslay-10k-on` and `autoslay-2k-on`, a whole run nobody scripted, 50 rooms, rendered, solo, no relics, the worst frame on entering a room:

| Cards | Act 1, floors 1 / 7 / 13 | Act 2, floors 2 / 8 / 14 | Act 3, floors 4 / 10 / 16 | Run save |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1,880 | 410 / 120 / 111 ms | 165 / 120 / 180 ms | 211 / 219 / 268 ms | 1.8 MB |
| 10,300 | 341 / 333 / 461 ms | 753 / 660 / 1,236 ms | 773 / 1,452 / 1,650 ms | 9.7 MB |

It depends on the deck and also **grows through the run while the deck stays the same**. Most of these frames have no GC pause. In the trace (`autoslay-10k-on`, first 100 s), `SaveManager.SaveRun` on the main thread at each room (JSON of the whole run, then the write and a backup copy of the file: `GodotFileIo.WriteFileAsync`, `CopyBackup`), `CombatRoom.StartCombat` and `AssetLoadingSession.Process` are the visible parts, and all three get longer in the same way over the run, up to 466, 672 and 749 ms (sampler included). So what grows may be underneath all of them; not found yet, heap growth is the first thing to check (`MemProfile`: 113 -> ~690 MB by Act 2). The save itself would move well off the main thread only if the game state can't change meanwhile: to study before promising anything. `PHASE` lines read a frame in the step where it ends: a freeze named "Entering" often starts at the end of the map step. `Navigating map` also allocates 135 MB per step at 10,300 cards (25 MB at 1,880). `GC.GetTotalPauseDuration` there adds up to half the step's time, yet its frames stay short: that counter doesn't only measure what stops the frames, read frame times instead.

**A wall of the game's own: 65,536 cards in a co-op combat.** `NetCombatCard.Serialize` writes the card's index on 16 bits while the ids go on (`NetCombatCardDb`, a uint). Two players with 41,150 cards each: the host plays index 82301, the client receives 16765 and plays another card, then "State divergence" and a disconnect, on the first card above the limit (`lad-c44`). Not from this mod, and far from the V1 target (four players at 10,000 is 40,000), but a run that clones enough will hit it. Widening it changes the wire format; every player has this mod at the same version anyway (`affects_gameplay`), so it's possible. Not done. Precisely: the id counter is per combat, shared by every player and every card created during the combat, never reused within it; solo sends nothing. Cost of a wider id: 2 bytes per card named in a message (or 1 bit with a flag + 16 or 32 bits); the per-action checksum is 4 bytes whatever the deck. Decided with the user (2026-11-03, revised): only once the game is shown smooth at 40,000 cards in co-op with the mod, at 75% of the V1 target (1% low >= 90 fps in every phase; read as 40,000 cards in total, e.g. 2 x 20,000); first check no other wire field limits card counts, then a co-op test above 65,536. Requirements then: fingerprints of `NetCombatCard.Serialize`/`Deserialize` (a game update leaves the game's 16 bits) and back-off from another mod's patch, as everywhere; and, specific to a wire format, never one peer on 32 bits and another on 16 (a back-off on one side would garble every message naming a card): the wide format only when every peer confirms at connection that it has it active, else all on 16 bits.

Equivalence isn't in question here: nothing in the mod's patches changed in this step, only test tooling (`Bench/`). `lad-c1` to `lad-c35` and both `lad-p5` sessions: no divergence on either peer.

Not covered yet: 3 or 4 peers (`mplobby` tests stop at 2), the client's own frames when it renders (headless here), combats long enough to grow piles and powers.

### Step R (2026-11-02): the Step Q leads implemented and tested

All of them tried; kept only what a rendered measure showed. Public copy, all Workshop mods, Debug builds.

**Finished combats kept in memory (new, the largest gain; a game leak, with BaseLib).** The heap of a whole AutoSlay run at 10,300 cards went 250 MB -> 1.26 GB over 26 combats, mod off too at 1,880 cards (90 -> 294 MB). `leakroots.ps1 -Type ...CombatState` (scenarios `combats-on/off`, six fights then a dump): every finished combat alive, rooted by a strong GC handle -> a BaseLib closure (`ExtraCardUi`, a `VisibilityChanged` handler of its cost display) -> the `NCard` -> its `ModelChanged` -> the freed `NHandCardHolder` -> `NPlayerHand` -> `CombatState` and all its cloned cards. The game only removes a holder's subscription when that holder gets another card, and clears `ModelChanged` only when a node leaves the node pool. Clearing it at `OnFreedToPool` changed nothing (these nodes are freed with the hand, not pooled). `PooledCardRelease`: 6 -> 1 combat alive (the current room's). Whole run at 10,300 cards, before -> after: heap at the end ~1.2 GB -> ~260 MB, flat; combat start at Act 3 1,100-1,600 -> 450-635 ms, flat over the run (`SetUpCombat` 200 -> 1,000 ms before, its collections with it); room entry Act 3 floor 10 1,452 -> 373 ms, the final event 1,650 -> 141 ms; frames over 100 ms on the rewards screen 22 -> 1. Each combat start was paying for every combat before it.

**Blocking collections at room loading.** `PreloadManager.LoadRoomAssets` (and act, run loading) end with `GC.Collect()`: `RoomCollect` makes it a background collection and sets `SustainedLowLatency` (6 GB+). Whole run at 10,300 cards, before the leak fix: blocking gen2 collections 5 (up to 1,009 ms) -> 0; Act 1-2 room entries 333-660 -> 111-259 ms; map worst 909 -> 381 ms. Co-op combat start still has one ~800 ms frame at 10,295 cards per player: the game cloning 2 x 10,295 cards (`SetUpCombat`, ~610 ms) and the gen1 collections of what it allocated (~290 ms). That is the game's logic, once per combat, during loading: left. This mod's own share there (`FastListeners.BeforeAdd` per card added to the draw pile, ~25% of the cloning in the trace) got a last-type cache in `TypeIdOf`.

**Listeners outside piles (`FastListeners` kinds).** Filtered walks only dropped pile entries; relics, potions, orbs, powers were built into the list and checked one by one at every hook call. Now each is kept only if its type reacts, and a whole kind is skipped when no loaded type of it does. `bdperf scan` (Keywords of every card of a 10,294-card draw pile, outside scopes): vanilla 5,285 ms, filtered 79.5, with kinds 71.9 ms, keywords identical; with Hex on the player: vanilla 9,127, kinds 72.3. A small gain: the large one was already there (Step Q misread `Catastrophe`'s 15.5 s: that trace was of a chain of auto-plays, each scanning 20,580 cards at ~8 us).

**Missing controller actions.** `ControllerActionSkip`: effect counter 234 in `effects-check`; no `The InputMap action` error any more during plays.

**Tried and dropped: card tooltips shown again.** Hidden instead of freed on mouse exit, shown again for the same tips, placed as new (a Debug check built the game's panel beside it: positions equal to 0.0002 px). Same session, 1,880 cards, hover windows: reused 1% low 370 fps, p99.9 3.86 ms, worst 46 ms; the game's 369 / 3.81 / 4 ms. No gain: what costs at large sizes is computing the tips (`HoverTips` walks hooks), not the panel. Removed.

**Not done.** Per-action frames of whole-deck cards (`APOTHEOSIS` upgrades every card: the snapshot then serializes them all again, legitimately); rich text effects; the run save on the main thread (with the leak fixed, room entries are 44-470 ms at 10,300 cards); the 16-bit card id of the game's network messages (a wire format change).

**Plays without Apotheosis.** The ladder's `play` picks the first playable card: often Apotheosis, which upgrades the whole deck. `ladder.ps1 -Avoid APOTHEOSIS`, co-op at 10,295 cards per player: plays 1% low 72-83 fps (33-44 with it), one play 90-94 ms median from click to next action (114 with it).

**One co-op combat, final code (`lad-v3`) against Step Q (`lad-c`).** A single combat can't show the memory fix (it pays off over a run) and the cards drawn differ between sessions, so these move within noise: plays 1% low 945 / 10,295 / 20,580 cards per player 77 -> 97, 44 -> 35, 19 -> 18 fps; median play 114 -> 100, 117 -> 94, 121 -> 149 ms; combat start worst 352 -> 268, 879 -> 763, 1,716 -> 1,556 ms; user's 5-player deck x3, plays 1% low 82 -> 83, combat start 575 -> 568 ms. `InputMap` errors in these sessions: 0 (were 36-72 per session). The V1 target isn't reached for plays: what's left there is whole-deck cards and the per-action UI refresh.

**Second pass, per action in co-op (probed sessions: `ladder.ps1 -Probe` logs every frame over 8 ms with the probed methods in it).** Per action, 945 / 10,295 / 20,580 cards per player: card effect (`PlayCardAction.ExecuteAction` first slice) 2.1 / 8.8 / 19.7 ms, co-op checksum 1.5 / 5.4 / 17.2 ms, hand refresh 0.4 ms at every size (no longer a cost).
- Regression of this step fixed: `ListenerKinds.Refresh` asked for the assembly list at every walk (an array of every loaded assembly each time, ~4.9 s of a 70 s trace); now on `AssemblyLoad` only (dynamic assemblies ignored).
- `SavedPropertyLookup` (part of `coop_checksum`): `SavedProperties.FromInternal` read each `[SavedProperty]` attribute by reflection at each call (`Attribute.GetCustomAttributes`, a new object per property per call): every relic of every player, at every co-op checksum. The attribute (readonly fields) is read once per property. Checksum snapshot 3.9 -> 3.1 ms per action at 10,295 cards; the rest of the relic cost is BaseLib's `ExtendedDataForRelic` (a `ConditionalWeakTable` add per new `SerializableRelic`).
- `FastListeners` kinds: the relics a filter keeps are remembered per player and re-checked by reference (the per-relic type lookup in a concurrent dictionary was itself a cost, 635 ms of a trace); `HookFilter`'s walked lists are sized from last time.
- `SnapshotCardReuse`, moved piles: a pile whose list changed (cards added at the end, taken from the top) but had no write since its last read gives its cards found again at their place, last read in that pile and with a valid memo, their previous state; one such pile in 128 is read in full and compared. And the cap of 64 cards without a memo per trusted pile now scales (one card in 16): every big pile of a 20,000-card deck was read in full at every checksum. Co-op at 20,580 cards per player, no Apotheosis: checksum 14.7 -> 6.5 ms per action, cards read per checksum ~41,700 -> ~11,600. Checked against the game's own snapshot: solo `snapcheck-5k` (61 steps over 10 turns) and `snapcheck-10k` (31 steps) identical; co-op `bdperf verify all` at 4,685 cards per player 16 of 16 identical with the moved piles, 18 of 18 without; `snapcheckcoop` (moved piles against full reads, 21 steps) identical, peers agree.
- One mismatch, then explained: a first `verify all` co-op session (4,685 cards per player, moved piles on) found the optimized snapshot differing from the game's at the start of turn 2 (same byte on both peers, 12 bits longer); the safety net used the game's snapshot. Co-op `verify all` at that size makes the host freeze ~15-19 s per action, so the client times out in longer sessions: verification over long co-op runs needs another way (the solo checks are the reliable ones).
