### BaseLib 3.4.8 (2026-11-13)

Published 2026-11-12. `tools/fpdiff review` against the GitHub release DLL of 3.4.7: 289 fingerprints the same, two changed. The Harmony targets of both versions compared too (decompiled sources): new patches on `Hook.ModifyBlock`, `BlockVar`/`CalculatedBlockVar.UpdateCardPreview`, a second on `PotionModel.HoverTips`, the `NetHostGameService` constructor, and per-version tween patches for the beta's `CardPileCmd.GetTweenForCardsChangingPiles`; none on code this mod mirrors except the first.

- `ModifyHealAmountPatches.ModifyAdditive` / `ModifyMultiplicative` (HookSkip's BaseLib hooks) were left vanilla: the combat state parameter is now `ICombatState` instead of `object`, and the walk calls `runState.IterateHookListeners(combatState)` directly instead of through `BetaMainCompatibility`'s reflection. Still one RunState walk, the one the prefix filters: equivalents in `EquivalentFingerprints.cs`.
- `Hook.ModifyBlock` was left vanilla (`also patched by BaseLib`): BaseLib makes `CardModifier.ModifyBaseBlockAdditive/Multiplicative` work, through a prefix on `block` and a transpiler inserting `num = ModifyBaseBlockMultiplicative(num, props, cardSource)` before the first `ldarg combatState`, the start of the first walk. No walk added, the result independent of the listeners walked, compatible with HookSkip's redirect in either order: both added to HookSkip's allowed patches (now checked on a hook's own method too, not only its state machine), with the two callees fingerprinted (they loop over `cardSource.GetModifiers()`).
- The rest unchanged for this mod: `AfterCardPlayedPatch.AfterPlay` (the transpiler) is the same, only the method it injects copies the modifier list (`ToList()`); `HookUtils` now swallows a listener's exception (BaseLib's own hooks, not filtered here); `CardModifier` saves drop unknown modifiers; the menu's `NModConfigSubmenu.GetModTitle` handles a prefix without a trailing dash.

Proof (public copy, Debug, Limitless 1.2.1 installed meanwhile): `tools/compat` 0 problem; `release-checks` 0 part left vanilla (3 before the change, the `ModifyBlock` back-off gone in the same session); `ab-2k` 54, `pile-cards-2k` 131 lines identical without baselines; `effects-check` 53/56 (the usual three); `turns-32k-on` 31,800 cards, 0 left vanilla, verify mismatches 0; the back-offs (the check changed): `audit-conflict-ab` 54 lines identical to `ab-2k-off`, mutations 38/38. ### Unlimited 2.0.2 (2026-11-20)

"Unlimited: No Player Limit" v2.0.0 -> v2.0.2 (Workshop, published 2026-11-19). The old DLL kept by a copy's `mods_STEAMTEST` (the same as SteamCMD's download of 2026-11-16); decompiled in `decompiled/mods/Sts2Unlimited` (v2.0.0 in `Sts2Unlimited-2.0.0`). Only `DifficultyPatch` and `Sts2Unlimited.ApplyHarmonyPatches` changed (C# and normalized IL; every other class identical: `PacketSizePatch`, `ListHeaderWidthPatch`, `SaveDirPatch`, chest, campfire, sync, settings menu). `tools/fpdiff review` (game v0.113.1 both sides, `--old-mods/--new-mods`): 480 the same, none changed; the two constants then pinned (`Transpile_SerializeDeserialize` `908CDF014E4F04CF`, `IsVanillaListBitsConstant` `CB9DCCF74112728F`) are the same, and are no longer pinned: the same day `UnchangedTranspilers` replaced them ("Transpilers judged by the code they produce"), so an Unlimited update can no longer leave a part vanilla by fingerprint; its transpiler is judged by the code it produces.

- **What changed**: the difficulty transpiler inserts `DifficultyPatch.EffectivePlayerCount(int)` (a static `Func<int,int>` the initializer sets to `GetEffectivePlayerCount`, the same answer) after each `Players.Count` instead of calling `Sts2Unlimited.GetEffectivePlayerCount`; the HP prefixes call it too; each patch is recorded in `PatchRegistry` (its `loaded DEGRADED` line). `MultiplayerScalingModel` is looked up in `MegaCrit.Sts2.Core.Models` first, then `.Singleton`: on v0.113.x, where the type moved, 2.0.0 never patched `ModifyBlockMultiplicative` ("type not found"), 2.0.2 transpiles it (enemy block scaled by the effective count: with the test account's settings, 5 players in co-op).
- **Impact on this mod: none.** None of the targets (`Creature.ScaleHpForMultiplayer` / `ScaleMonsterHpForMultiplayer`, `MultiplayerScalingModel.ModifyBlockMultiplicative`, the six `GetScaledAmountForMultiplayer`) is fingerprinted, mirrored or watched, nor are their callers (`CombatState.CreateCreature`, `PowerCmd`, the monsters'); `MultiplayerScalingModel`'s override is a game type's real override, so HookSkip keeps it in `Hook.ModifyBlock` (only the `AbstractModel` defaults are checked) and `CodeSet` doesn't count it as foreign; HookFilter mirrors no block hook; a virtual override isn't inlinable (InlineGuard). On v0.111.1 the initializer still throws on `NBlitzScreen` before any patch (`parlib.ps1`'s known failure): no change there.
- **Test tooling**: baseline keys hash the test mods' files, so mod-off references made with 2.0.0 aren't reused. Co-op sessions with Unlimited on v0.113.1 now play enemy block at the effective count: a scenario's `expect` on block values written with 2.0.0 may drift (both sides alike).

Proof: pending (no game session yet): with `BDTEST_EXTRA_MODS=SpeedX,ModConfig,sts2unlimited` on v0.113.1 (and v0.111.1 for the known failure), `release-checks` then `check_versions.py --log` (no Unlimited `DEGRADED`, nothing left vanilla, no back-off), `ab-2k`, `outside-2k`, `effects-check`, and co-op `coopfast`, `audit-coopdeath`, `coopclone -WithMods` (the block hook now patched in a co-op combat).

### Audit of 2026-11-15: every line, eight auditors

Eight read-only audits, each reading every line of its part and the game and mod code it mirrors, skips or relies on in v0.107.1, v0.111.0 and v0.113.1 (listener walks; checksum snapshots; hook filtering and `classify.py`; the Limitless patches against a fresh 1.2.1 decompile; piles, card ids and relic state; combat flow and UI; infrastructure and settings; the test tooling's proofs). Each finding checked against the code before a change; the same agents then fixed their own files. No crash, desync or state difference with today's game and mods; what was found are holes a game update or another mod could open, a few real small bugs, and test tooling that could pass while wrong. `decompiled/mods/Limitless` was still 1.1.7 (the 1.2.1 review had decompiled it elsewhere): now 1.2.1, 1.1.7 kept in `decompiled/mods/Limitless-1.1.7`.

Real today (small):

- **InlineGuard** cached "doesn't reach a protected method" by method only: a method first searched as a depth-0 callee was never searched deeper as a patched method, and a cycle's recursion guard stayed as a final `false`. A patched method reaching a write-tracked setter through a small callee could keep the inlined setter. Now cached by depth searched, no recursion guard (the depth ends cycles).
- **HoverTipReuse** reused a panel when the tips were the same, though the game places it again from the hitbox at each rebuild: a creature moved or resized with the same tips (Osty growing, enemies laid out again for a summon) kept the panel at the old place. Now also compares the hitbox's global position, size and scale and the viewport size; backs off on patches to every `NHoverTipSet` method a reuse skips (v0.113's `CreateAndRegister`, `ReplaceExistingTip`, `ConnectTreeExiting`, `SetAlignment(Rect2)` among them), each pinned per version.
- **FastListeners' recorded moves** (up to 4,096, holding piles and cards) kept the left run alive until the next run's first shuffle: released at `RunManager.CleanUp`.
- **Off at start for good**: `SavedOff` read the cfg's `Enabled: False` whenever BaseLib was listed, but only the BaseLib menu can save a change; with the menu failing (BaseLib broken by an update, an older BaseLib), the mod stayed off at every start and `bdopt on` saved nothing. Now off at start only when the menu registered and loaded "off"; a menu failing after loading "off" turns the master switch back on.

Back-offs and fallbacks (a game update or another mod):

- **SnapshotCardReuse**: card and pile bits stand for the whole serializer chain, fingerprinted (`CardState`, `SerializableCard`, `SerializableEnchantment`, `SavedProperties` and `WritePropertyName` (two versions), `CombatPileState`, `WriteInt`, BaseLib's two `WriteExtended` and `ExtendedSaveHandlers.Write`); another mod's patch there or on the closed `WriteList<CardState>`/`WriteList<CombatPileState>` leaves bits to the game (only BaseLib's known postfixes allowed, and other mods' transpilers whose combined result is the game's code, `UnchangedTranspilers`; Unlimited's was allowed by fingerprint until 2026-11-20; before, only `CardState.Serialize`'s prefixes and postfixes were watched). Bits carry an epoch (id cache hash and widths, BaseLib saves, `CanModifyGameplay`); none kept for objects saving an `int[]` (changed in place). Trust also requires the private getters handing out `_keywords` and the MultiEnchantment list to have only their known callers (`RequireFieldWriters` can't see writes through a getter). A wrong reuse found while building a snapshot rebuilds it with that part off (`SnapshotVerifier.Rebuilt`); found while computing its checksum, the checksum is computed again (`Reserialized`); before, the rest of that snapshot kept its shortcuts. A pile's header is the empty pile's bits with the count written in (it was serialized again, card by card, at each count change). Combat end releases every entry, not only those still in piles.
- **FastListeners/ListenerCache**: a MultiEnchantment verdict could be stale for one walk when a new inner type was noted during that walk, or (subscriber tracking off) never resynced: synced before and after building the snapshot, and on every path judging entries one by one. ListenerCache backs off on the walks' whole code (iterators' MoveNext, `Contains`, ModHelper's subscriber walks), not just `IterateHookListeners`; pile caching backs off while another mod patches `CardPile.Cards`, `CardModel.Enchantment` or `Affliction`; ModHelper's subscriber list pinned (fingerprint `4F5EA1DCEC53C6F9`, field writers). Kinds rescanned only for assemblies that can define models.
- **PileIndex**: Harmony never calls a prefix taking the card once an earlier one returned false, so "an add or remove another mod's prefix skipped, judged by the pile's count" only held for a prefix registered after PileIndex's. A first prefix that always runs now takes the count; the finalizer judges a skipped or thrown add/remove by the card's actual membership; cards added to a pile during its own `Clear` handled; checks run while switched off (a stale index faults for the session). **CardDbScan**: walks are the game's while another mod patches `AddInternal` (until the next combat start), an unseen add walks its pile in full; field writers of `NetCombatCardDb._cardToId`/`_nextId` pinned. **DeckCountMemo**: a card type whose `Tags`, `CanonicalStarCost` or `HasStarCostX` isn't a constant (IL check, per type, mods loaded later included; BaseLib's `ConstructedCardModel` counts as changing) or a patched `CardPile.Cards` gives the game's count.
- **Limitless patches**: only Limitless 1.2.1's known code (15 fingerprinted methods, load scan of Limitless and mods referencing it) may change the inner list `EnchantmentsOnCards` hands out; back-offs on the getter, `set_SerializableEnchantmentsOnCards` and (HookSkip drops) the clone-build chain. LimitlessCloneMemo's chain covers the enchantment side, `OnUpgrade`/`AfterCloned`, the dynamic-variable and energy-cost copies; its overrides list named the non-virtual `ModifyCard` (matched nothing): overrides on the way (`AfterDeserialized`, `DeepCloneFields`, `AfterCloned`, `OnUpgrade`, `OnEnchant`, `RecalculateValues`) are judged per saved type, only the game's or known code, unpatched. LimitlessRecalcSkip lets the game's pass run and watches it (an exception there passes on unchanged, no rerun); bare enchantments with their own `RecalculateValues` move the quiet state; `CardEntered` is a finalizer; memos see later SavedSpireFields. LimitlessHookSkip arms only with no combat set up, starting or running; one forwarded hook call in 16 checks a dropped stacked enchantment (`Samples`, `SampleMismatches`).
- **Hooks**: HookSkip's transpiler at `Priority.Last`; HookSkip no longer requires `IterateCombatHookListeners`' fingerprint (only its redirects depend on it, checked per walk); it runs `HarmonyRebuild.CheckAll` itself once its hooks are patched. A listener's own exception in a HookFilter mirror is rethrown as vanilla would (once, from the hook) without faulting HookFilter or calling earlier listeners again; logged with its trace first. ExtraScopes' bookkeeping can't break the async first-turn setup (fault, query without a scope); the BetterSpire2 scope pinned (5 methods) with a back-off; both scopes read their faults and a switch in `combat_refresh`. `classify.py`: "changed value" comparisons must be the listener's own before/after, `out`/`ref` arguments only the try identity or new declarations, PushModel/PopModel paired, listener-call arguments side-effect free, `TaskHelper.WhenAny` pinned (table unchanged on all three versions).
- **Infrastructure**: TextPoolReuse a part like the others (a fault was switched back on by `ApplyAll`); a faulted class whose patches can't be removed keeps its flags off and is retried after 2, 10 and 60 s; `[PatchPart]` covers the part method's lambdas, local functions and state machines; `IsPatchedByOthers` counts inner prefixes/postfixes. Scope and shuffle finalizers restore depth from `__state` (Harmony runs every finalizer again when one throws).
- **Smaller**: RoomCollect's fault restores the GC latency mode; CardFlightLimit's flush keeps the other piles' counts when one throws, and after a fault adds the full count; PoolSignalScan watches the explicit `INodePool.Free`; RelicStateReuse lets the game's `JsonException` through without faulting; `RunVanilla` (the snapshot verifier's and the benches' reference) is fully vanilla for FastListeners' mirror, the BaseLib card-modifier skip, PileIndex, DeckCountMemo and SavedPropertyLookup.

Test tooling (could pass while wrong):

- A failed `cmd` line only logged `FAILED:` and no judge read it (a failed `bigdeck import` ran both sides on the starter deck: IDENTICAL); self-detected mismatches and disables, TIMEOUT, SWEEP FAILED and 0-vs-0 comparisons passed; test slots inherited the user's `BigDeckOptimization.cfg` (an "on" session could run vanilla); no script compared a co-op session mod on with mod off (see "Game beta v0.113.1": the pairs even played different runs); `bdperf` vanilla sides kept optimizations on, `bdperf refresh` compared after the vanilla side had already refreshed; baselines ignored extra mods, run files and profile progress; `coop.ps1 -WithMods` could write into the Steam install. Now: one judge (`tools/autotest/judge.py`) for every runner, failed commands fail the session, positive controls declare `expect <regex>`, slots run with default settings, `parcoop.ps1 -OnOff` compares on with off per peer, `bdperf` vanilla = the whole mod off, refresh from a dirtied state, baselines keyed by loaded mods, run files and profile, effect and leak checks that can't read clean when inconclusive. Applied to old logs, the judge found a co-op ladder snapshot difference of 2026-11-02 nobody had seen, and scenarios whose `cmd` failed unseen (fixed: `audit-deckcount-fault` never added its card, `snapcheck-freeturn` played into a full hand...).

Found while proving it (test tooling):

- **Every effect proved**: `effects-check` never ran three of them. `SnapshotCardReuse.Hooks` (piles kept with pure cost/keyword listeners): Curious applied, three checksums. `ControllerActionSkip`: `controllermap stale` binds game actions to controller actions the InputMap lacks (what a v0.107 `settings.save` keeps; v0.111+ drop such bindings themselves, `MergeSavedControllerBindings`), then a mouse sweep. `SyncedCardRelease` is co-op only: `effects-coop-host/-client` (parcoop). Solo: 55/56 on v0.111/v0.113, 54/56 on v0.107.1, where `ReplayStateSkip` can't run solo (v0.107 enables its `ChecksumTracker` in co-op only; v0.111+ checksum a solo combat too); co-op: both run (v0.113.1 1,903 releases and 78 skipped states per peer). HookFilter's effect counts the listeners its lists leave out (`ListenersDropped`), not lists built; HookSkip's filtered walks are counted as before, what they leave out is `FastListeners.Kinds/.Others`.
- **`play` in co-op**: the card goes through the networked action queue; the player looked idle before it ran and the next pick took the same card (the queue then dropped it). How fast the queue runs depends on the session: a mod-off client played 1 card of 3. `play` now waits until the combat history saw that card's play start (a set: the other player's plays start in between).
- **Co-op on/off needs turns**: both players playing at once interleave their actions in the shared queue at each peer's pace; random targets then land differently (same total damage) between a mod-on and a mod-off session (v0.107.1). `effects-coop` and `coopfast` take turns within a round (barriers) and wait with `waitfor round n` (both peers ended the turn), not `waitfor turn` (a client's end of turn reaches its queue only with the host's echo, so its next `waitfor turn` could still see the turn it had just ended, and it played a fourth card in round 1 when the host was slower; since the fourth pass the `endturn` line itself waits until the game took the end of turn). Concurrent play stays covered by the peers' checksums after every action; `audit-coopdeath` (a player dies, both play at once) is peers-only.
- **`autoroom`**: the game's event handler can call an event done before its rewards open (THE_LEGENDS_WERE_TRUE's potion, host, v0.113.1): a screen open with no option is now answered. `outside-coop`/`-off` run alone (`-Jobs 2`): mod off, the 1,900-card combat start took 237 s with other sessions running and the client got disconnected (the vanilla freeze).
- **Positive controls by chance**: `relics-scalarblind` relied on the part's 1-in-16 sample falling on a relic that really changed (none did on v0.107.1), and whichever of its two detectors saw the error first hid it from the other (the verifier saw nothing on v0.111.0). Split: `relics-scalarblind` (every answer checked, the part turns off) and `relics-scalarblind-verifier` (the part's sample out of reach, the verifier sees a snapshot differ). `snapcheck-apotheosis-wrong` now also expects the checksum computed again.
- **Benches**: a part not applied (the game's code changed: a mutation, another version) says "not applied: nothing to compare", without "OK" and without failing; applied but off still fails. `baselib-optional.ps1` accepts the two hooks HookSkip leaves vanilla on v0.113 by design; `started-off-nomenu.ps1`: a saved "off" with the menu DLL set aside starts on, patches applied.
- **mutations.ps1**: `SnapshotCardReuse.Hooks` goes with Trust/Moved/Dirty; a changed Limitless forwarder or `RecalculateValues` is a known inner-list writer, so LimitlessHookSkip and the pass skipping go vanilla whole; bdperf comparisons only (not ExtraScopes' first-turn line), parts left vanilla excused.
- A mod-off co-op client crashed once in the game's Spine animation call (native access violation in `MegaSpineBinding.Call`, under a Thorns damage chain, BaseLib's `SetAnimationTrigger` postfix on the way; no frame of this mod there): not reproduced on a rerun.

Proof (Debug, BaseLib 3.4.8, Limitless 1.2.1, the same DLL on v0.107.1, v0.111.0 and v0.113.1): `tools/compat` 0 problem on the four refs; `release-checks` 0 part left vanilla on v0.107.1/v0.111.0, the two known hooks on v0.113.1. On each version: `ab-2k` 54, `pile-cards-2k` 131, `combats3-2k` 63, `shuffle` 55, `clone` 4, `deckcount` 131 lines identical (`fool-2k` 153 on v0.113.1), `autoslay-2k` 613 / 387 / 747, `memory-runs` 32, `outside-2k` 48 (v0.107.1, v0.111.0) identical; every positive control saw its error, judge 0 problem; with every patched method rebuilt (`BDTEST_REBUILD_ALL=1`) `ab-2k` identical and `rebuild-check` ok; co-op `effects-coop` and `coopfast` on/off identical per peer (56 / 55 lines), `outside-coop` on/off 30 lines identical per peer, `coopclone` and `audit-coopdeath` peers agree; `settings`, `baselib-optional`, `limitless-switches`, `started-off`, `started-off-nomenu` ok. Mutations 38/38 (public). `turns-32k-on` 31,800 cards: 681 passes skipped, verify mismatches 0. `hover-tips-8k` rendered: no problem. Sweeps (v0.113.1 / v0.107.1): cards 4,269 / 3,452, relics 4,740 / 4,455, potions 348 / 328, encounters 3,321 / 3,146, powers 6,139 / 5,240 lines identical (powers stopping a session do so mod off too).

### Audit of 2026-11-15, second and third passes

Two more full passes, nine read-only auditors each (the test tooling split in C# and scripts), each reading its part, the game and mod code it touches on the three versions, and the uncommitted diff of the passes before; the third pass started without the earlier findings. No crash, desync or state difference with today's game and mods in either; what they found were holes a game update or another mod could open, and test tooling that could pass while wrong. `decompiled/mods/BaseLib` was BaseLib 3.4.7 and `BetterSpire2` a 1.0.0 build: now the installed 3.4.8 and Workshop 1.83.14 (old copies kept as `BaseLib-3.4.7`, `BetterSpire2-1.0.0`); `LobbyBanlist` (Workshop 3769761568, in the account's mod list since 2026-11-08: every test session loads it; its initializer throws on v0.113, it loads on v0.107.1) decompiled there too: its `NCardGrid.SortingAlgorithms` postfix and GridSortCache's give the game's order in either order, nothing else it patches is something this mod mirrors or relies on.

Real today (small): **IdleButtonInput** tracked only the mutes `NClickableControl.Enable` asked for, not those it made itself (at ready, after a release or a drag-cancel): turned off, most idle buttons never got their input back (nothing visible in the game; another mod's `_Input` patch would not have run). Every mute is tracked now.

Holes closed (a game update or another mod's patch):

- **Harmony skips a prefix taking a reference-type argument once an earlier prefix returned false** (checked in the game's 0Harmony 2.4.2): FastListeners' add/remove/reorder/affliction prefixes now leave a "never ran" state their finalizers turn into a full invalidation, and the caches back off while another mod patches `AddInternal`, `RemoveInternal`, `MoveToBottom/TopInternal` or `set_Affliction`; HookSkip's pending run filter is taken by an always-run prefix (`Priority.First`, no argument). PileIndex: a card mid-add or mid-remove gets the game's scan while another mod patches those; `Judge` faults instead of restoring over a nested change (an `Entry.Changes` counter), decides from membership, never counts; a `Clear` counts its own removes.
- **SnapshotCardReuse**: a replaced snapshot object invalidates the card's memo; a relic detection rebuilds the snapshot like a card one (`RelicStateReuse.Detections`); no kept bits for saved cards, for card arrays of SavedSpireField targets, or for an object whose BaseLib saves hand out a collection or an object; the getters a trusted pile skips (`Id`, `Affliction`, `Enchantment`, `CurrentUpgradeLevel`, `FloorAddedToDeck`, `EnergyCost`, the amounts, `Canonical`, `CostsX`, `Type`, `Owner`, `Pile`) back off on another mod's patch; `CardModel.Type` pinned (fingerprint, field writers) and card types overriding it read at every snapshot; `_localModifiers` and `LocalCostModifier.Amount` users pinned; the pile-bits check no longer delayed, its comments say what it covers (the per-card bits are the 1-in-2048 check's).
- **DeckCountMemo**: moves applied only from a count taken with no changing card in the piles; per-type patched-getter flags rechecked every 2 s; `AllCards` fingerprinted.
- **Limitless**: the `EnchantmentsOnCards` getter's fingerprint required before any drop; the game's clone and build chain pinned (25 methods, `GameChain`; v0.113's changed `CardModel.DeepCloneFields`, `AfterCloned`, `UpgradeInternal` reviewed as equivalent) and the six game overrides on it (`Adroit.RecalculateValues`, five `OnEnchant`; game card types with any override on the way are no longer trusted); assemblies loaded later that reference Limitless are scanned for inner-list writers; the pass skip backs off on `AllCards` patches; `CardEntered` checks `Holds`.
- **Infrastructure**: InlineGuard judges a patched method by what Harmony compiles (transpilers rerun, every mod's patch methods scanned, the JIT's inlining rules); `ApplyAll` sets each flag in its own try and schedules a faulted removal first; a removal failing at apply faults and retries; `tools/compat` shares the load check's accessibility and part rules; back-offs are recorded for every caller (the menu names them all); `check_versions.py` watches BetterSpire2, SpeedX and Unlimited.
- **Smaller**: ListenerCache invalidates through finalizers (a watched method that throws after changing its list), skips the BaseLib card-modifier subscriber only when nobody patches it, and a move-check mismatch reads the piles again; CardDbScan rescans a pile it starts tracking from a walk; CardFlightLimit leaves a null target pile to the game; ThemeColorSkip leaves a freed label to the game's call; ExtraScopes' first-turn part left vanilla now shows as not applied; `HookSkip.Patched` (awaited by nothing) removed.

**HookSkip now filters every v0.113 hook**: `classify.py` proves the four shapes it left to the game (AfterTakingExtraTurn's `if (model == source)`, ModifyUnblockedDamageTarget's and ModifyPreBlockDamageTarget's `combatState?.IterateHookListeners() ?? Array.Empty<AbstractModel>()` walk, TryReplaceDefaultRoomRewards' per-listener list), and requires what it had accepted unchecked (the `netId` guard, task lists only awaited by `WhenAll`, property chains reviewed one by one). v0.113.1: 154 hooks filtered, 0 left vanilla; the v0.107.1 and v0.111.0 rows unchanged.

Test tooling (could pass while wrong): a networked `cmd` in co-op couldn't fail (its result went to the console only; its task's fault too); `restsite`, `eventoption`, `playcard`, `autoroom` skipped a missing option or card silently (`optional` opts in); sweep refusals were unjudged; the judge ignored the game's `State divergence`, mod frames in game and Godot errors, a failed AutoSlay run, its own crash; `run.ps1` could judge a stale log and killed any SlayTheSpire2 process (yours included) on a stall; `try.ps1` deployed to the Steam install unasked; baselines cached partly-off scenarios; co-op on/off compared no checksum; benches said OK without their optimized path having run (refresh, grid sort/pool, tabs, listeners, pile, plaque, text, checksum rows: now `nothing to compare` / `not compared`, never OK); several effect counters counted when nothing was done; `checktips` compared text only (now the panel's input and placement against the game's own rebuild for the same hitbox); off sides of A/Bs now start vanilla (`bdopt off` before the run is created); `mods_STEAMTEST` shared per copy is reference-counted and each runner checks the mods loaded; restarted sessions, `-Chunks` reruns and sweep coverage are judged; `coop.ps1` runs in a test slot.

Found while proving it: the game's **"Common" preload** at the main menu is not awaited; a run started meanwhile (a script 0.4 s after the menu) unloads what it is still loading and the run's preload never completes, mod on or off (two sessions stopped there): sessions now wait for it (0.7 s). A co-op host's `bdopt off` at the menu is saved in the slot both peers share, so the off client may start with the mod off (judge `--mod-off` on off sides). On v0.111.0 `outside-coop`'s path meets Abyssal Baths: the game applies a remote player's event choice to that player's options at arrival without waiting for the previous choice's async work (`EventSynchronizer.ChooseOptionForEvent`); Immerse awaits max HP and damage before changing page, the auto player clicks Exit at once, and the client sometimes applied it as Abstain (a heal) to the host's player: 3 sessions of 3 mod on, 0 of 3 mod off, the client's view right again at the next state line (remote players are synced from the host). Measured frame by frame (`eventtrace on`, both peers, on and off): Immerse's damage awaits the game's own hit `CustomScaledWait` (88-106 ms, on both peers, mod on and off alike: not skipped outside combat); the host sends Exit right after its own wait, the client finishes the host's Immerse after its copy of that wait, and the second choice arrived only 21 ms (on) and 14 ms (off) after the page change: a game race won or lost by a frame, whatever the mod. With the trace's own logging both sides won. Nothing the mod does there differs; the auto player's instant clicks make it possible, a player's can't. On v0.113.1 `outside-2k`'s rest site clones the deck to 6,655 cards and mod off the next combat start takes over 7 minutes (`automap` gives up): compared up to there.

Proof (Debug, the same DLL on v0.107.1, v0.111.0 and v0.113.1): `tools/compat` 0 problem on the four refs; `release-checks` 0 part left vanilla on all three (HookSkip 147 / 148 / 154 hooks). On each version: `ab-2k` 54, `pile-cards-2k` 131, `combats3-2k` 63, `shuffle` 55, `clone` 4, `deckcount` 131 lines identical (`fool-2k` 153 on v0.113.1), `memory-runs` 32, `autoslay-2k` 613 / 387 / 747, `outside-2k` 48 / 48 / 42 (to the freeze above) identical; every positive control and audit scenario passed (new: `audit-idlebuttons`, `audit-deckcount-conflict`, `hover-tips-move`), judge 0 problem; with every patched method rebuilt `ab-2k` identical and `rebuild-check` ok; `effects-check` 55/56 (54 on v0.107.1), `effects-coop` gives the co-op ones (SyncedCardRelease 1,903 per peer, ReplayStateSkip 103 / 105); co-op `effects-coop` and `coopfast` on/off identical per peer (58 / 58 / 57 lines with the settled checksums), `outside-coop` on/off 30 lines (the v0.111.0 race above aside), `coopclone`, `audit-coopdeath`, `audit-cooptoggle` peers agree, `audit-waitspin` (plain) 2,001 waits then the frame given back; `settings`, `baselib-optional` (no excused hook left), `limitless-switches`, `started-off`, `started-off-nomenu` ok; `hover-tips-move` and `hover-tips-8k` rendered on all three: input and placement OK. Mutations 40/40 (public, new: `clone-from`, `limitless-getter`). `turns-32k-on` (public): 681 passes skipped, verify mismatches 0. Sweeps without baselines (v0.113.1 / v0.107.1): cards 4,269 / 3,452, relics 4,740 / 4,455, potions 348 / 328, encounters 3,321 / 3,146, powers 6,139 / 5,240 lines identical.

### Audit of 2026-11-15, fourth pass

Sixteen read-only auditors (listener walks; listener cache and hook filter; hook skipping and `classify.py`; snapshots; relics and net saves; the Limitless patches in two groups; piles, card ids and sorting; combat flow and GC; UI; infrastructure and settings; the test tooling in three groups: AutoTest, benches, scripts; tools and release; the docs), each reading every line of its part and the game and mod code it touches on v0.107.1, v0.111.0, v0.113.0 and v0.113.1, then an adversarial verifier per finding (confirmed, narrowed or rejected, often with a better fix) and a critic over the whole. The same groups fixed their own files, then a review of each group's diff. No crash, desync or state difference with today's game and mods; what was found: a few real bugs, most of them small or in the test tooling, holes a game update or another mod could open, and many stale docs. During the audit the Steam install switched to the `public-beta` branch (v0.113.1), Unlimited updated to 2.0.0, and every Workshop mod was disabled in the account's `settings.save` (the user's own doing; see the test mods below).

Real today:

- **Turning the mod off in a co-op run** (the user's request): `bdopt off` also turns off the fast combat start the other players keep, so this player starts each combat far later and the game drops the actions reaching a player still starting a combat. During a co-op run `bdopt off` now prints a warning and needs `bdopt off yes`; the menu's master switch can't be unticked there (greyed, its hover text says why and names `bdopt off yes`, 14 languages); `bdopt` warns while the mod is off in a co-op run; the menu's co-op note says so; a FastCombatStart fault in co-op is logged as such. Co-op players must now all have the mod on, or all off (`CoopModeTag`): with the whole mod off, this mod's entry in the game's gameplay mod list reads `BigDeckOptimization-<version>-off`, `-<version>+off` since 2026-11-16 ("Other mods: reflection interception and holes"), so the game's own join check refuses mixing on and off players ("mod mismatch", naming both); two off players can play, anyone can host. `bdperf opt off` asks the same in a co-op run (the run only, no lobby walk; both ways since the re-audit's second pass); `bdperf costwrites` (a cost change on one peer) is refused in co-op. After the re-audit (2026-11-16): the same lock both ways, since a player turning the mod on in an all-off game makes the same mixed pair: in a co-op game `bdopt on` warns and needs `bdopt on yes` too, and the menu's master switch is greyed during a co-op run whichever way it is (`why.coop_run_on` when off, 14 languages; not after a start with the mod off, where nothing of it runs this session); `bdopt` with the mod off in a co-op game says every player must have the same state (it said the others start fast and to turn it back on, false in an all-off game). "Co-op game" (`Settings.InCoopGame`) is the run, the run manager's net session connected as host or client (since the re-audit), or a connected co-op lobby found by a scene-tree walk when the whole mod is switched or `bdopt` shows the state while it is off: a field of type `StartRunLobby` (character select, custom, daily, v0.113's other modes) or `LoadRunLobby` (continuing a co-op save, where the run only exists once everyone is ready) whose net service is connected as host or client. The join lists v0.111+ keeps from when hosting or joining started are retagged in place at each switch (`CoopModeTag.Refresh`), so a later joiner is compared with the state at its handshake. The confirmations say a player who disconnects can only rejoin once every player has the same state again.
- **Stack traces**: 25 pass-through finalizers returned the exception. Harmony 2.4 rethrows unchanged only when every finalizer on the method is void, so every exception through those methods, the game's and other mods' included, lost its trace, even with `bdopt off` (the patches stay installed). Now void; only `HookFilter.RethrowFinalizer` returns one (it substitutes an exception, and logs the full trace first).
- **`bdopt` was a debug command**: on v0.113 using it (even to read the state) marked the run as having used a debug command in its save (`ExtraFields.MarkDebugToolUsed`). Now `DebugOnly => false`.
- **ListenerCache**: a hand selection ended by a state change resumes the card's play inline, inside the `CombatStateChanged` dispatch scope, with that scope's cached lists: `NPlayerHand.RevalidateSelectionAfterStateChange` now suspends the caches. Kept display lists held a finished combat until the next reuse: dropped at every invalidation.
- **HoverTipReuse**: with the mouse resting on a creature while a keyboard or controller opened a potion popup (or targeting), a co-op state change kept the creature's panel on screen where the game removes it (`NHoverTipSet.shouldBlockHoverTips`, `NGame.IsDebugHidingHoverTips`: the game's rebuild runs then).
- **ModImage** ignored the master switch: `bdopt off` kept the mod's image on the mods screen. An image Godot can't decode was read again and logged at every fill.
- **Menu**: the fast-start relic slider stayed active when `fast_combat_start` couldn't run (now greyed, without input, with that option's reason); the co-op grey-out compared a `StringName` with a string. `coop_checksum`'s description now says it also runs in solo on v0.111+.
- **Costs at run time**: `PatchSafety`'s 2-s recheck read each foreign patch's method through `AccessTools.GetMethodByModuleAndToken` (a `Parallel.ForEach` over every loaded assembly), on the main thread, mid-combat: now resolved once per (module, token). LimitlessCloneMemo's stamp read Limitless's two option getters (each a reflective `RunManager` call) for every inner card, ~60,000 at a 2 x 10,000-card combat start: now once per outermost `DeepCloneFields`. RelicStateReuse compiled `IsWax`/`IsMelted` getters again for every relic type (hundreds of compiles in one co-op checksum, estimated): now once per getter. GcPacing recounted a combat under 1,000 cards every frame, boxing enumerators. GridSortCache read a `ConditionalWeakTable` at every comparison (~260,000 per 10,000-card two-key sort).
- **Effect counters that counted nothing**: GcPacing's `Regions` counted refused regions; FastCombatStart's skipped waits counted waits at Instant speed or in the game's non-interactive mode; HookSkip's `FilteredWalks` counted NullRunState walks; ReplayStateSkip's `Skipped` counted calls where nothing was recording (it also skipped there, hiding the game's own error: now the game's method runs).

Holes closed (a game update or another mod):

- **Listener walks** (FastListeners): appends have their own switch, off with a changed `AddInternal` or a move-check mismatch (before, only shifted moves were); an exception in this mod's verdicts inside a filtered walk (kinds, relics, per-entry judgments, the version tails) turns FastListeners off and refills the walk unfiltered, as the pile entry cache did, and the kinds scan fails safe (keeps every kind); `set_Enchantment`'s tracking uses a byte state (a prefix that never ran bumps in the finalizer); shuffle depth stored + 1 (a skipped prefix can't reset an outer shuffle); the mirrored subscriber walk throws the game's "Collection was modified" when a subscription is added mid-walk.
- **ListenerCache / HookFilter / ExtraScopes**: creature lists and powers watched directly (six optional finalizers) instead of relying on a watched change running just before; cross-scope reuse refused while a concrete BaseLib `CardModifier` exists; scopes inert unless `Apply` finished; scope depth + 1 in `__state` (0 closes nothing: before, a skipped prefix could end the enclosing scopes). HookFilter's energy cost, ShouldPlay and hitting mirrors take the game's early exits before building a list; only the hooks walking `Hook.IterateCombatHookListeners` back off on patches to it and its getters (the energy cost color helper still does: shared lists, deferred).
- **HookSkip**: a hook's fingerprint compared exactly with its table row (an equivalence added for another patch could have let an unclassified body through); the async helpers' state machines checked for other mods' patches; a pending BaseLib run filter that NullRunState never took is dropped by a first-priority prefix; a game `Hook` method with no row that walks the listeners is counted and logged as left vanilla (v0.113.0's release checks said "two left vanilla" while four were unsafe: the two without a row went uncounted; 0 today on all four versions); BaseLib's allowed patches fingerprinted at the menu, not in a run's first hook. Accepted and documented: subscribers judged when the walk's list is built (UI only), and the transpiler's place among equal priorities registered later.
- **Snapshots** (SnapshotCardReuse, SnapshotVerifier): the reuse check compares bit lengths too (1 to 7 extra trailing zero bits shift the checksum stream); the verifier's vanilla side is serialized inside `RunVanilla`, through the game's own `WriteList`, so the mirrored list header is really checked; an overloaded hook name in a content mod no longer turns the part off (`AmbiguousMatchException`); a listener's own exception in the calls the game's `From` also makes lets the game's `From` run and throw it, without faulting the part (`GameExceptions`); `WriteModelEntry` and `WriteInt` in the card bits' back-off (the upgrade bits write into them); a card with a BaseLib custom keyword keeps its kept keyword list; pile bits require exactly one replaced `WriteList<CombatPileState>`; the verifier's size check inside its try.
- **Relics** (RelicStateReuse): Unlimited's transpiler resolved on its own, so a change elsewhere in RelicStateReuse no longer removes SnapshotCardReuse's allowance for Unlimited (both allowances were replaced on 2026-11-20 by `UnchangedTranspilers`, which judges the code produced, no mod named); patches on `SavedProperties.Serialize` / `RelicState.Serialize` back off; a type whose scalar plan can't be built is left to the game instead of turning the scalar part off and counting a detection; skipped parts logged with the shared "left vanilla" wording (`check_versions --log` sees them).
- **Limitless**: the pass skip checks the saved list's writers and getter callers; the list shortcut only for `AlwaysSave` saved properties; a late Limitless load applies only what isn't applied; off-main-thread bumps counted apart in the quiet word (a real race in the first fix: one atomic word now); duplicate BaseLib save ids counted once; **the kept-list self-check runs in every build** (it was Debug only and only logged): a difference turns LimitlessReserialize off and Limitless rebuilds the list. LimitlessCloneMemo: a mod type declaring an instance `[SavedProperty]` is never kept; the back-off chain goes one level down (keywords, `NCard.FindOnTable`, energy cost and dynamic variable upgrades, `MaxUpgradeLevel`; the known BaseLib and Limitless patches there allowed; on the card's own `AddKeyword`, `RemoveKeyword` and `MaxUpgradeLevel`, patches gated on the card's type stand for cards of other types, re-audit's second pass); lock-free per-type answers. LimitlessHookSkip: any use of the inner or saved list's members (matched by handle) outside the known code counts as a writer. EnchantmentTabReuse: three more methods its mirror relies on fingerprinted; an orphan duplicate freed on an error.
- **Piles and sorting**: PileCounterBatch's and PileIndex's `Clear` finalizers survive Harmony rerunning every finalizer when one throws (a depth at -1 froze the counters; a rerun faulted the index); `InNoPile` unsure for a card in flight; `ContainsCard` gets its own flag (`PileIndex.ContainsCardEnabled`, set by `pile_lookup` with the master switch, not by PileIndex's faults: the shortcut reads no index). CardSortKeys: where a cost listener reads `card.Keywords` (Veilpiercer), every keyword override in the combat must be a fingerprinted adder (Hex, Phantom Blades) no other mod patches, the keyword code pinned (the first version of this fix refused every sort in a Veilpiercer combat, found by its review); off-main-thread comparisons are the game's; a type re-implementing the comparison interface is refused.
- **Combat flow and GC**: FastCombatStart leaves a wait that is no longer async to the game alone (it lost the whole patch); RoomCollect counts its replaced `GC.Collect()` calls and skips itself with none, before setting the latency mode; CardFlightLimit re-queues a lost deferred flush; RichTextEnvRelease covers v0.111's `[scramble]` effect, and a failing tracker part comes off alone; TextPoolReuse pins the method that makes the shared pool's dirty buffers safe.
- **UI**: PoolSignalScan mirrors only the game's `NodePool<>` (BaseLib's `GeneratedNodePool<T>` or a mod's own pool could fault it) and backs off on a patch on any pool; IdleButtonInput pins the writers of `_isPressed`; GridSortCache pins `SetCards` for its index map.
- **Infrastructure**: `HarmonyRebuild` refuses methods carrying a transpiler known to keep state between runs (BaseLib's `ModInterop`); InlineGuard records protected methods even when its flag probe fails (MonoMod sets the flag anyway: the recompile is what matters), follows callees 4 levels deep; patches applied at once still get the recompile and `CheckAll` when frames can't be awaited.

Test tooling (could pass while wrong):

- **The test mods**: with every Workshop mod disabled in the account's settings, every copy session would have run with this mod alone and every A/B agreed. The supported mods (BaseLib, Limitless, BetterSpire2) now always load, plus the account's enabled mods and `BDTEST_EXTRA_MODS` (`BDTEST_MODS` replaces the list); a wanted mod missing stops the series; each test mod must log its initialization (an exception in it, or Unlimited's `loaded DEGRADED`, is a problem; LobbyBanlist's v0.113 failure a known note); slot profiles enable every mod of the test mods' folders.
- **Judges**: every `[WARN]` of this mod is a problem unless allow-listed; compare.py and sweepreport.py count game errors logged only, or far more often, with the mod on; frozen sessions keep their log (`.frozenN`) and a freeze in this mod's load is a problem; a cut-short session isn't held to `expect` lines it never reached; Test-SessionLog fails a negative check on a missing or cut-short log; exit codes for every runner; back-off scenarios assert each back-off (`expect`, `effectcheck ... zero`); the effects check lists CardSortKeys wherever needed (`EFFECT CardSortKeys 0` when it should run and didn't) and leaves the tooling's own `InlineGuard` protections out; benches restore the effect counters and refuse a part faulted earlier; `bdperf refresh`/`scan`/grid say `not compared` when nothing of this mod ran.
- **Script lines**: AutoSlay's 10-minute wait stretch no longer applies to the tooling's own waits (60/120 s); `endturn` waits until the game took it (a co-op client's next lines landed in the turn just ended); in co-op `enemyhp`/`immortal` fail after this peer's combat was set up (barrier missing: before, a divergence that looked like the mod's); only the game's Error lines set the turn-loop and divergence flags; the v0.113 fight seed logged; `selector local` on v0.113; long waits log their state at 180 s; a sweep abort stops the script; frame times kept only while measured; `leakcheck` weak tables on request (not judged: a value pointing back to its own key keeps nothing alive) and `leakcheck game` paths through this mod's objects marked `[through this mod: Type]`, a problem for judge.py; a `state` line outside a combat fails unless `optional` (`StateOutsideCombatFails`; clone-on/off, cloneopt-on/off and audit-coopdeath-client say it where they log outside a combat on purpose).
- **Runners**: run.ps1 refuses the Steam install without a Debug build there and closes a game whose runner logs nothing in 90 s; co-op ports per test slot; bounded robocopy retries; baselines hash every file of the test mods' folders and `BigDeckConsoleCmd.cs`; mutations.ps1 stops on a build failure, counts an undefined mutation, never excuses a runtime fault, runs one at a time; python reports never crash on a character the code page lacks.
- **Versions and release**: `check_versions.py` reads the installed branch (with Steam on `public-beta` every daily check said "game CHANGED", and `--accept` would have recorded the beta as the public game; verified betas now in `tools/versions.json` `betas`); `--accept` records only what is installed and refuses while a published update isn't downloaded (the v1.0.3 commit had recorded Unlimited v2.0.0's publish stamp against v1.0.3: back to 1.0.3's); `--log` knows every fallback phrase and lists every error and warning line of the mod; a crash exits 2 and `version_task.ps1` notifies it. `fpdiff` loads every pinned mod and says which it didn't find (its one-off `migrate` removed). `package.py` builds from committed sources (Source Link names HEAD; `--draft` otherwise), not incremental, against `refs/v<min_game_version>`, with the csproj version checked against the manifest and every menu key required in every translation. The no-Steam-deploy hook judges each command of a compound line and builds inside other commands. `dwarfsyms` warns on DWARF 5 units.

### Other mods: reflection interception and holes (2026-11-16)

**Mechanism.** CrossVersionCompat (CVC, v0.10.0) adds no member and leaves sts2.dll alone: it Harmony-patches Harmony's own reflection API for every caller. A failed `AccessTools.Method`/`DeclaredMethod`/`Field`/`TypeByName` on a game type is answered with another member: hard-coded name pairs (`Hook.BeforeTurnEnd` and `BeforeSideTurnEnd`, `AfterTurnEnd` and `AfterSideTurnEnd`), a "renamed" heuristic (name prefix, same parameters), a stand-in `FieldInfo` (`Mod.assembly` answered with the first of `Mod.assemblies`), stand-in types under game names in its payload assemblies. It also loads Cecil-rewritten copies of mods from its cache (prefixes on `AssemblyLoadContext.LoadFromAssemblyPath`). Raw `Type.GetMethod`/`GetField` are not intercepted. Effects seen on this mod: HookSkip's lookup of a hook of the other game version got this version's hook, failed that row's fingerprint and was logged "left vanilla" (2 hooks, no real loss: the real hook is filtered under its own row); multi-assembly mods lost their names in back-off lines (fixed in e7ffc78: `Mod.assemblies` read first, raw, validated). Latent: `SavedPropertiesTypeCache` looked up by name (right today only because CVC answers right), name lookups without fingerprints that patch what they get, and RequireResolvable passing on a rewritten copy of this mod.

**The second study** (64 Workshop mods, `mods-holes-study`, top-20 classes): the real holes are the Harmony state changing after the preflight, hidden IL manipulators inside Harmony's pipeline (Act 4 Heart's transpiler on `MethodCreator.CreateReplacement` applies IL hooks to methods whose `PatchInfo` lists nothing), runs or combats torn down outside the menu flow (Quick Restart, Quick SL, Rewind, KitLib, QuickLink), foreign finalizers that can swallow a fault of this mod (KitLib's at priority 0 on every KitLib target), the gameplay mod list (replacing postfixes, parsers splitting entries at their last dash), listener types the kind scan can't see, a v0.113 listener source outside the watched writers, UI shortcuts without a foreign-patch check, and (perf) ListenerCache invalidations by pileless clones (Random Foreseer). None but CVC's two hooks hit this mod today; all are fixed generically, with no list of mods.

Fixes:

- **`Reflect`** (`Patches/Reflect.cs`): every name lookup of every patch (Method, DeclaredMethod, Field, Property, getters and setters, TypeByName, Inner, Async/EnumeratorMoveNext, FieldRefAccess by name: ~430 call sites, Bench excluded) goes through the runtime's reflection with Harmony's own binding flags, base-type walk and ambiguity fallback, never through `AccessTools`. Each answer is checked: the name asked, the runtime's own `MemberInfo` (its object's type in the core library: a stand-in `FieldInfo` subclass fails), declared by the type asked or a base type, the parameters asked; a type under the game's root namespace only from the game's assembly (a stand-in under a game name is refused). A refused answer is treated as absent (logged once, the part relying on it stays vanilla). No type scan: the game's assembly, then each loaded assembly by full name (Harmony's last step, a scan of every type for a short name, is dropped: every caller gives full names, and it cost tens of ms for a missing mod's type). `AccessTools.FieldRef<,>` delegates, `MethodDelegate`, `FirstMethod`/`GetDeclaredMethods` (no name lookup) stay Harmony's.
- **HookSkip** tells "answered by another member" apart: for each classified hook, Harmony's own lookup is also asked (`Reflect.SubstitutedMethod`); a different or extra answer is logged "Harmony's lookup answered by another member (X: another mod intercepts reflection lookups), ignored", counted in `AnsweredByOthers` (status line), never "left vanilla". `MethodFingerprint.Of` gives `foreign-member!!` (matches nothing) for a method object that isn't the runtime's or whose module isn't its type's.
- **Version cross-check, not a lock**: the game's declared version (`ModManager._gameVersion`, raw) is logged at the first patch, with the mods patching Harmony's lookups (`Reflect.LookupInterceptors`, the patch info of `AccessTools`' lookup methods: a few reads). After HookSkip's last batch: the classified versions whose helpers this game's code matches (`HelperProfileVersions`, now emitted by `classify.py`); a classified declared version whose helpers don't match is logged (files changed, or reflection answered by another mod).
- **Own integrity** (`OwnIntegrity`): at the first patch, this mod's DLL must reference only what it is built against (runtime, game, Harmony, assemblies shipped in the game's or the runtime's folder) and, when loaded from another file than `<its Mod.path>/BigDeckOptimization.dll`, have the same bytes. Else `ModEntry.Rewritten`: nothing applied but CoopModeTag (`StartedOff`, the co-op tag says off), a warning, the menu and `bdopt` say why (`why.rewritten`, 14 languages). The menu DLL gets the same check after its load (BaseLib allowed): a rewritten one is refused, bdopt still works.
- **`HarmonyWatch`** (second in the queue): a finalizer (Priority.First, no PatchGuard) on Harmony's internal `PatchFunctions.UpdateWrapper(MethodBase original, PatchInfo)`, where every Patch and Unpatch of every mod ends (shape checked: another Harmony leaves it off, the 2 s rechecks remain). Per call: `Epoch++`; `PatchSafety.Invalidate(original)` drops the cached answers about that method (plain, allowed, tolerant; compared by module and token), so a late patch is seen at the next call instead of up to 2 s later (PileIndex's 1,024-call getter check and HookSkip's helper check also recheck when `Epoch` moved). This mod's own calls (a `[ThreadStatic]` scope in `TryApply`, `HarmonyRebuild.Run` and HookSkip's batches, else a stack walk to the first frame outside Harmony; Bench frames count as foreign) stop there. Other mods' calls: (1) on the next frame, a method carrying a patch of this mod that has no applied detour (Harmony undoes the detour before building the new one; a failed foreign rebuild leaves every mod's patches off) faults each part of this mod patching it, logged with the method (`Lost detours` in bdopt why); a failed foreign Patch/Unpatch is logged. (2) A patch on Harmony's replacement pipeline (its non-public types, MonoMod code) by another mod means hidden IL hooks: logged and noted; from then on every method another mod rebuilds is a suspect, and at load (if hooks were already there) every patched method listing no patch at all. `PatchSafety.WithHidden` adds the hooks' owners to the mods patching a suspect in every check (strict, allowed, tolerant), so what relies on it backs off. (3) A method of this mod's own assemblies patched by another mod: warned and noted. Public API patches (`AccessTools`, `Harmony`, `PatchProcessor`) aren't hidden hooks: they show in patch lists or are lookups this mod doesn't use. `bdopt why` appends `HarmonyWatch.Report()`.
- **Foreign finalizers**: PatchGuard's guard finalizer is registered at Priority.First: each finalizer gets the exception the ones before it left, and a foreign one returning null (KitLib's, registered before this mod's patches) would have hidden a fault. Void and read-only, the guard changes nothing for those after it. A listener exception rethrown by HookFilter's `RethrowFinalizer` (after the guard) is no longer seen by the guard, which already excused it (`ThrownByListener`).
- **Run or combat torn down outside the menu flow**: FastCombatStart's `CombatManager.Reset` hook is a finalizer (it runs after a throwing Reset or a foreign prefix skipping it); the window keeps a weak reference to its `CombatRoom` and closes itself (logged) when the run's current room is another or no run is in progress (`RunManager.EnterRoom` pushes the room before `Enter`, both versions; `EnterRoomDebug` goes through it). `Settings.InCoopGame` is also true while `RunManager.NetService` is connected as host or client, whatever the run and lobby objects (a mod's co-op reload or relobby): `bdopt off`/`on` then needs `yes` too.
- **Gameplay mod list**: CoopModeTag's postfix runs at Priority.Last (after a foreign postfix that replaces or filters the list, so the list tagged and tracked is the one the game gets); at the first list, a foreign postfix Harmony runs after it is logged. The off marker moved into the version part without a dash: `BigDeckOptimization-v1.0.3+off` (was `-v1.0.3-off`), so a last-dash parser (Multiplayer Mod Sync) reads this mod's id with a version `v1.0.3+off` instead of an unknown mod. Peers are version-locked, so the format change is safe; `coop-modetag.ps1` and the comments follow.
- **Listener types the kind scan can't see**: an assembly that may define models but whose `GetTypes` throws (other than a partial load) makes the kinds unknown (no kind left out) instead of being skipped; finalizers on `Player.AddRelicInternal`, `AddPotionInternal`, `OrbQueue.TryEnqueue` and `Insert` (`ListenerKinds.Meet`, one hash lookup per add) add a type no scan listed (emitted at run time, dynamic assemblies aren't scanned) and rescan before the next walk; without those finalizers (`_kindsWatched` false) no kind is left out.
- **Listener sources**: ListenerCache invalidates on `RunState`/`CombatState.set_RunModels` where present (v0.113: a private setter, the backing field's only writer, checked like `Dominion`).
- **UI shortcuts**: GridSortCache answers a comparer from its values only while no other mod patches that comparer lambda (asked once per comparer and `SetCards` call, watched for bdopt why), opens no scope when another mod transpiles `SetCards` (or hidden IL hooks may be there; asked again when `Epoch` moved; prefixes stand), and ends its scope in a Priority.First postfix so foreign postfixes re-sorting the grid run on the game's comparers. ThemeColorSkip and ControllerActionSkip make every call they would skip once another mod patches the Godot method skipped (`AddThemeColorOverride`, `IsActionPressed`/`IsActionReleased`; strict, watched). ExtraScopes' first-turn scope stays closed while another mod patches `SetupPlayerTurn`'s state machine or one of its lambdas (`MethodFingerprint.GeneratedCode`, watched). EnchantmentTabReuse unchanged: the game's `NCard.UpdateEnchantmentVisuals` is already bypassed by Limitless's own transpiler, and the Limitless method mirrored is checked.
- **Pileless clones** (perf, exact): ListenerCache's enchantment/affliction setter finalizer doesn't invalidate for a card the pile index knows is in no pile (`PileIndex.InNoPile`: false when unsure, off or mid-move): such a card is in no walk.

Tests: `foreign-reflection` (launch shape `reflection-intercept`, `Bench/ForeignLaunch.cs`: postfixes on `AccessTools.Method`/`DeclaredMethod` mapping the hook pairs both ways and on `AccessTools.Field` giving a stand-in `FieldInfo` for the missing `Mod` field, installed at the launch through `--bdforeign`, which `parlib.ps1` adds for a `launchforeign <shape>` line): expects "154 (v0.113.1) or 147 (v0.107.1) hooks filtered, 0 left vanilla" with the substituted lookups counted apart, the interception logged, the version cross-check, and BaseLib named from its game `Mod` entry (`foreignpatch modname`). `guard-checks`: `faultprobe` adds a third check, a foreign finalizer returning null registered before this mod's patch: the patch must still be faulted and removed.

Left, with the reason: writes to pinned game lists bypassing the watched methods (hit by no mod; the list identity and `_version` guard proposed by the study needs records in every tracked writer, the sampled checks remain: no Bench detection added); the generic subscriber re-run on a kept list (a stale subscriber tail is a display error only, between display scopes); a preflight note for foreign finalizers returning `Exception` on patched methods (the guard now runs first); logging the differing entry at a join refusal; ending an open ListenerCache scope when `Epoch` moves mid-scope (a patch landing inside a scope needs a mod patching from a listener); MonoMod.RuntimeDetour detours (no mod ships it); the needless back-offs (since then: "Needless back-offs made safe") and IL-diff judging of transpilers (a later task). The race of a check storing an answer computed just before a patch landed on another thread (stale up to 2 s, as before) is accepted.

Proof: pending (no game session yet). To run on v0.107.1 and v0.113.1: `foreign-reflection`; `guard-checks` (FAULTPROBE three OK); `release-checks` and `effects-check` (HookSkip 147/154, 0 left vanilla, no "answered by another member" without the shape; `HarmonyWatch` applied, no "lost its detour", no hidden-hook line with the usual test mods); `foreign-shapes-2k-on/off` and `foreign-controls` (the late `foreignpatch` lines now invalidate at once: identical checksums, the FOREIGN runtime back-offs still counted); `coop-modetag.ps1` (the `+off` entry in the refusal); `fast-start-check` (waits skipped as before: the room check must not close the window early); a grid sort and an orb/relic/potion add in `release-checks` (`grid`, kinds); startup time ("Patches applied" ms) against v1.0.3 (HarmonyWatch's finalizer on every patch, the stack walk only for calls outside the own-call scopes). Not provable here without a mod doing it: a real CVC-rewritten copy of this mod (OwnIntegrity), Act 4 Heart's hidden IL hooks, a failed foreign rebuild.

### Needless back-offs made safe (2026-11-16)

The second study's "needless back-off" classes (`mods-holes-study`, top-20): back-offs from other mods' patches that can't change what this mod relies on. Generic rules only (what Harmony says of each patch and, where needed, a strict IL shape of the patch method), no list of mods; each falls back to the back-off when unsure, and all of it is under `PatchSafety.ShapeRules` (false: every check strict again, Player.Piles a back-off again, HookSkip's helpers checked per version profile as before, CardFlightLimit's soul strict). Five classes, by priority (class 3 and HookSkip's helpers in class 4 revised the same day, "RitsuLib recovery" below: RitsuLib 0.6.14's constructor postfix had sent 170 of the 242 rows' hooks to the game at run time, its start-position postfix and trail override every reshuffle's souls):

1. **Postfixes augmenting the listener walks** (`IterateHookListeners`, RitsuLib's lazy per-owner expansion, EnchantLib's snapshot-filter-repeat; RitsuLib's dependents, Zone the Spire...): **done**. Harmony runs every postfix after a prefix that skipped the original, on the `__result` that prefix left. So where this mod's prefix serves a walk through the game's own call, a foreign postfix gets the stream the unpatched body yields and runs once per call as without this mod. The original only builds the iterator (its body runs at enumeration, outside the call): no exception path where the game would run no postfix. The walk method itself is checked with `SurroundingOnly` (postfixes by value with `ref __result`, no `__runOriginal`/`__args`/by-reference argument/object field; void prefixes sorted before every prefix of this mod); its state machine, `Contains` and ModHelper's subscriber walks keep the strict check.
   - **FastListeners** (`CombatServedPatched`, `RunServedPatched`): the prefixes serving the walk through the game's call let those patches stand. The filtered walks (`Filtered`, HookSkip's, called directly, not through the game's call) stay strict: filtering before a postfix would drop what it adds after an owner (RitsuLib's capabilities) or what it reads (EnchantLib's). They take the game's call then (`KeptOnly` over its result, the mirror or ListenerCache's list served inside it). A run walk with a HookSkip filter taken (BaseLib's hand size) is never served to a postfix: strict.
   - **ListenerCache** ("augmented" scopes, `_combatAugmented`/`_runAugmented`): with another mod's patch let stand on a walk, the scope's lists are built from FastListeners' unpatched mirrors called directly (`UnpatchedCombat`, `UnpatchedRun`), never through the game's call (the list would hold the postfix's output and the postfix run on it again at each replay). Only the unpatched part is kept, what the scopes already assume stable; the postfixes compute their part live at each call. The list reaches them behind a plain enumerable (`Replay`: no `IList` to test, cast or change). A run walk with a child combat isn't served while the combat walk carries another mod's patch (the child's postfix output would be kept inside the run's list): the game's call, the child combat walk served inside it. Without FastListeners' mirrors (option off, not applied) no scope caches then (a note, `walk_mirrors`). HookFilter, which uses the lists directly (`ListenerCache.IsCaching`), stays off in such scopes, all of it (a whole back-off registered under HookFilter on both walks: red in the menu, where before it was shown green while idle and ListenerCache red). The snapshot verifier still compares these scopes (`IsInSnapshot`).
   - **HookSkip** unchanged: over a patched walk it filters the game's call's output (`KeptOnly`), after the postfixes, which is the only exact order (the study's critic: RitsuLib's expansion also drops items, EnchantLib's isn't per-item). It gains the served walk inside that call.
2. **A foreign prefix sorted before a replacing prefix** (RitsuLib's extra hand on `CardModel.Pile`, its mod piles on `Player.Piles`): **done**. New tolerance `PatchTolerance.BeforeReplace` for PileIndex's getter: `SurroundingOnly`'s patches plus prefixes returning bool or taking `ref __result`, when Harmony sorts them before every prefix of this mod (each copy). One returning false makes Harmony skip this mod's prefix as it skips the original (the caller gets its answer, as without this mod); one returning true leaves the call to PilePrefix, whose answer is the original's. Postfixes as SurroundingOnly's (`__runOriginal` refused: false where this mod answered). The sampled check (1 in 1,024) then compares with the original body's scan (`OriginalScan`, through the same getters) instead of calling the patched getter again, so another mod's prefix doesn't run twice. `Player.Piles` is no longer a back-off: the answer is only "the indexed pile, if the owner's piles hold it", so with any other mod's patch there `OwnsPile` reads the piles through the patched getter, as the original does (not `_runPiles`); the original's first-match over those piles is the same pile under the index's own premise (pile lists change only through the tracked methods). `CardPile.Cards` stays strict.
3. **A foreign patch on a skipped visual callee** (Rewind's replay-gated bool prefix and RitsuLib's trail override on `NCardTrailVfx.Create`, RitsuLib's start-position postfix on `NCardFlyShuffleVfx.Create`; CardFlightLimit): **done on a stated premise** (owner's decision, 2026-11-16, with safeguards). No analysis of another mod's code can prove its effect is only visual (its context checks are calls into its own state), so the rule is a premise, written down and shown: **code that runs only inside a card-flight soul's lifetime is that soul's own display**; worded precisely since 2026-11-17: **the calls a skip removes are all made from inside a soul's lifetime (its creation path, its `_Ready`, its tween), and are display**. Scope, nothing wider: the soul's own visual classes' methods the skip leaves out, `NCardFlyShuffleVfx._Ready`, its flight `PlayAnim` (and its state machine) and the trail's factory `NCardTrailVfx.Create` (CardFlightLimit's `_ownDisplay` and `_readyMethod`): any other mod's patch there stands. Shared game code a soul also calls stayed strict until the owner's decision of 2026-11-17 (below): the sound command `SfxCmd.PlayCardSwooshSfx`; RelicFlashMerge's `SfxCmd.Play` was unchanged (rule D since 2026-11-17: "Hardening of 2026-11-17"). `NCardFlyShuffleVfx.Create` itself is called on a skip (this mod's bool prefix answers null), so other mods' patches there are judged by shape: a new tolerance `NullSkipped` lets only postfixes stand that provably do nothing for a null `__result` (`PatchSafety.NullResultGated`: static, void, from the first instruction only `ldarg __result; [ldind.ref]; brfalse|brtrue` to `ret` on the null path, nothing else; not itself patched, no `HarmonyArgument`; no initializer judged since the re-audit round 2 of 2026-11-17, B1: the postfix runs at every call, a skipped one too, and the first is never a skip), so on a skipped call they run as no-ops, as in the game's test mode where `Create` returns null itself; what they would do with a real soul falls under the premise. Prefixes, transpilers and finalizers there still back off. **The decompiled game, checked before (safeguard 2)**: no player setting skips souls (FastMode only changes tween lengths: v0.107.1 `CardPileCmd.cs:552-576`, v0.113.1 `:708-724`); the game itself makes none in its test mode (`NCardFlyShuffleVfx.cs:143` and `NCardTrailVfx.cs:96` on v0.107.1, `:143`/`:103` on v0.113.1 return null; v0.113.1 `CardPileCmd.GetTweenForCardsChangingPiles` returns at `:560`), none for another player's cards (v0.107.1 `CardPileCmd.cs:378`/`:425-437`, souls only with `owningPlayerIsLocal` or a card leaving the play pile, which never flies as a soul; v0.113.1 `:581-584`), and none for silent moves (`skipVisuals`: v0.107.1 `:437`, v0.113.1 `:521`; on v0.113.1 the shuffle's silent add then its own per-card tween, `:1100-1108`, and the turn-end discards, `CombatManager.cs:1774`). Souls exist only on the local screen, so co-op peers never run each other's: a soul's code can't feed synchronized state without desyncing the game itself. What a soul reaches besides its nodes: the pile's `CardAddFinished` event (`NCardFlyShuffleVfx.cs:193`; listeners `NCombatCardPile.AddCard` and `NTopBarDeckButton`, counts on screen; CardFlightLimit already sends it itself, `Flush`), and `Rng.Chaotic` in `_Ready` (`:158-162`; `Rng.cs:22`, seeded from the clock on v0.107.1, from the OS's random source on v0.113.1, `:26-36`): one static stream per process, also drawn for an unseeded run's seed (`SeedHelper.GetRandomSeed`: that seed is saved and sent to the peers) and cosmetic values (a monster's skin, Trial's entrant number). So "nothing checksummed reads it", written here before, was false. The argument that holds: `Rng.Chaotic` is seeded from the clock (v0.107.1 `Rng.cs:22`) or the OS's random source (v0.113.1 `:26-36`), unpredictable in the game itself and never shared between peers (an unseeded run's seed is drawn by one peer and sent to the others), so a shift in it changes no outcome anyone can reproduce or compare; skipping souls has shifted it since CardFlightLimit exists (v0.9.0), and the premise adds no draw. Nothing surprising: fix 3(b) went ahead. **Shown**: a kept note (`PatchSafety.Kept`, not a back-off: no colour change) "other mods' code on a flying card's own visuals (...) doesn't run for the cards that don't fly: taken as display only <- mods" in `bdopt why` (its own section after the back-offs) and the log, and on the option's hover in the menu ("Still active next to {mods}: changes made to a flying card's visuals are left out for the cards that don't fly", key `kept_display`, every language), listing the mods patching the soul's own code and the null-gated postfixes let stand on `Create`. With `ShapeRules` off the methods checked before the rules are strict again (`Create`, `PlayCardSwooshSfx`, `_Ready` with SpeedX's fingerprinted postfix allowed as before, `PlayAnim` and its state machine, `NCardTrailVfx.Create`; SpeedX's allowance is kept for that case: with the rules on it is covered by the premise), and since the audit of 2026-11-17 the whole computed set too, other mods' overrides included (before, the rest stood silently with the rules off). Not every method a skip removes was ever checked, before the rules or with them: the souls' other code (below) and the game code a soul calls besides the sound command (`SfxCmd.Play` under `PlayCardSwooshSfx`, scene instantiation, `AddChildSafely`/`MoveChildSafely`, `TaskHelper.RunSafely`, `Rng.Chaotic`, frame awaits) are covered by the premise's wording only. **2026-11-17**: the souls' visual classes' code a skip removes is listed and named in the kept note (`_premiseOnly`: `NCardFlyShuffleVfx`'s constructor and `_ExitTree`; `NCardTrailVfx`'s constructor, `_Ready`, `_Process`, `FadeOut` and its state machine, `StopParticles`, `_ExitTree`), never checked (as before the rules, also with them off), and pinned with `NCardTrailVfx.Create` (`DisplayFingerprints`, per version: v0.107.1 and v0.111.0 alike, v0.113.1's `_Ready`, `FadeOut` through `OwnerFreeingTween`, constructor and `_ExitTree` differ, reviewed: node, tween and particle calls only); a change leaves CardFlightLimit off until reviewed. **Owner's decision of 2026-11-17, option (a)**: everything a skip removes is under the premise, the swoosh `SfxCmd.PlayCardSwooshSfx` included (the skip only acts above 80 souls a second, burst 12). What a skip removes is computed from the game's code (`CodeSet`, as RelicFlashMerge's sets: `NCardFlyShuffleVfx`'s and `NCardTrailVfx`'s whole classes, a skipped soul never existing, and every game method they reach; 251 methods on v0.107.1, 253 on v0.111.0, 276 on v0.113.1, 264, 266 and 289 with `NCardTrail` since the re-audit round 2, pinned per version, `FlightSets`; tools/fingerprint `closure` with roots `NCardFlyShuffleVfx::*` and `NCardTrailVfx::*` prints them all). Among them: `PlayCardSwooshSfx`, `SfxCmd.Play` and both `NAudioManager.PlayOneShot` overloads, `PileTypeExtensions.GetTargetPosition` (BaseLib's transpiler, RitsuLib's prefix), `NonInteractiveMode.IsActive`, `CombatManager.IsEnding` (and the listener walk it makes when no primary enemy is alive), `PreloadManager.Cache.GetScene`/`Instantiate`, `AddChildSafely`, `MoveChildSafely`, `TaskHelper.RunSafely`, `MathHelper.BezierCurve`, the frame and tween awaits (`AwaitProcessFrame`, `AwaitFinished`), `QueueFreeSafely`, `Rng.Chaotic`'s `NextFloat`, v0.113.1's `OwnerFreeingTween` (constructor, `Play`) and `OwnedTween.Play`, `FadeOut`'s lambda, the souls' Godot-generated members (`InvokeGodotClassMethod`, `HasGodotClassMethod`, `Set`/`GetGodotClassPropertyValue`, `Save`/`RestoreGodotObjectData`); its delegates: `CardAddFinished` (v0.107.1; the event CardFlightLimit sends itself, `Flush`), the log's listeners and the task helper's exception callback (error paths). Left out of the checks: `_Ready` (SpeedX's allowance with the rules off), `CardPile.InvokeCardAddFinished` and `NCombatCardPile.AddCard` (Flush still calls them, strict in `OnlyPileButtons`). Other mods' patches anywhere in it stand and are named in the kept note, other mods' classes overriding a game virtual method it calls too ("overridden by ..."). `OwnerFreeingTween`'s constructor and `Play` and `OwnedTween.Play` are also pinned on their own (`TweenFingerprints`, by name: absent before v0.113). A set not reviewed for this game version skips no soul. The kept note is cleared while `Create` or a strict method backs off (no soul is skipped then), names a mod with only a hidden IL hook there (HarmonyWatch) instead of an empty list, and says it is a stated premise; with the mod off `bdopt why`'s kept section reads "Once on, these parts would also run next to ..." as its back-off section does; the menu's hover says "taken as visual only: a stated premise, see bdopt why" (key `kept_display`, every language) and only for this note.
4. **Shapes HookFilter/HookSkip refused**:
   - *Void prefix rewriting a declared argument by reference* (ARAM's `ModifyMerchantPrice`, RitsuLib's `AfterOrbEvoked`): **done**, every tolerance but TypeGated. A prefix's by-reference parameter whose real name is one of the original's parameters (no `__` name, no field; `HarmonyArgument` already refused) stands when Harmony sorts the prefix before every prefix of this mod: it writes the argument slot before any code of this mod reads it, so this mod's prefixes, the body or this mod's mirror, and its finalizers read the value it leaves, as the original would with the caller passing it (`ArgumentRefusal` checks the name per original). `[OrderFreePrefix]` prefixes, which may run before it, read no argument (stated on the attribute).
   - *By-reference postfix parameter mirroring an out parameter, only read* (Who Carried?'s `ModifyHpLost`): **done**. A postfix's by-reference parameter named after one of the original's `out`/`ref` parameters stands when its IL only reads through it (`OnlyReadThrough`: every instruction naming it is `ldarg` immediately followed by `ldind.*`/`ldobj`: no `starg`, `ldarga`, `dup`, store or call taking the address). Writing variants (Downfall, EnchantLib) and a by-value argument's slot by reference (this mod's finalizers read it) stay refused.
   - *Patches on HookSkip's choice-context helpers* (RitsuLib's postfix on the 4-argument `HookPlayerChoiceContext` constructor, at its load: every hook using any helper went vanilla, 170 of 242 rows): **done**, two parts. (a) **Per-hook helper sets**: `classify.py` records the helpers each hook's loop bodies call (the constructor, `AssignTaskAndWaitForPauseOrCompletion`, `WaitForCompletion` on the per-listener context; `PushModel`/`PopModel`; `InvokeExecutionFinished`), closed over the helpers' own calls (`CLOSURE`: the constructor pushes its source and, on v0.113, calls `GetOwner`; `AssignTask...` calls `ExecuteTaskThenInvokeExecutionFinished` and `TaskHelper.WhenAny`; the former invokes the source's `InvokeExecutionFinished`), each version's helper bodies checked against that list (any other call to a helper's name stops the script). Six distinct sets (`HelperSets`): only AfterDeath, AfterDiedToDoom, BeforeSideTurnStart, BeforeFlush and the side-turn-end pair (v0.107.1: BeforeTurnEnd/AfterTurnEnd) construct contexts; AfterCardPlayed, AfterCardDrawn and the like push models; AfterCardChangedPiles, BeforeCardPlayed and most others only signal ExecutionFinished. `FilterFor` asks per hook about its own set only (`HelpersPatched(group)`: one answer per set, rechecked each second and at each Harmony epoch, as the profile's was), and the preflight registers each set once (`bdopt why` names only those hooks' helpers). The per-profile fingerprint check stays (`HelperProfileHolds`: "code changed" is another question). (b) **A gate on the listener argument**: in every call a dropped listener's iteration makes to a helper, the listener sits in one known parameter (`HelperListeners`, emitted by `classify.py`: the constructor's and `GetOwner`'s `source`, `PushModel`/`PopModel`'s `model`, `InvokeExecutionFinished`'s instance; none for the others, which stay strict, as do async helpers' state machines). `PatchSafety.SetListenerParameter` registers it before any check, and `TypeGated` then accepts TypeGates' shape (`isinst` tests from the first instruction to the no-op exit) only on that parameter, by its declared name or Harmony's `__N`; a gate on `__instance` doesn't count where the listener is another parameter (a helper's instance is the choice context). The gate types join the hook's filter (`PileFilter.WithGates`, combined with the defaults' and judged transpilers' gates; `IsAssignableFrom`, so RitsuLib's `IModelCapability` interface works): a dropped listener is one whose calls would have done nothing, every kept one makes the same calls. **beforefieldinit**: RitsuLib's class has a static field initializer, so a type initializer; `AnalyseGates` refused any.
