Now one marked `beforefieldinit` stands when its code is the same whenever it runs (`PatchSafety.InitializerFree`, narrowed 2026-11-17: "Hardening of 2026-11-17"). The first wording here ("only a gate-type call can require it; any earlier run is the runtime's own, with or without this mod alike") was wrong: CoreCLR may run a beforefieldinit initializer when the JIT first compiles a method reading the class's statics (the patch method itself, compiled at its first call), and this mod removes calls, so that first call, and the initializer with it, can come later or never. So only an initializer storing constants, arrays of them, new BCL collections and reflection lookups (`typeof`, `Type.GetField/GetMethod/GetProperty/...`, HarmonyLib's `AccessTools.Field/Method/Property/TypeByName` and their variants) in the class's own static fields is accepted: RitsuLib's `OwnerField = AccessTools.Field(...)` passes, one reading the clock or calling anything else is refused. An explicit static constructor (no `beforefieldinit`) is still refused. This applies to every `TypeGated` user (the defaults' gates, LimitlessCloneMemo's card methods), and applied to `NullSkipped` until the re-audit round 2 of 2026-11-17 (B1, below); `TranspilerJudge.GatesOn` keeps refusing any static constructor (no mod needs it there).
   - *Result-only postfix on a hook HookFilter mirrors* (EnchantLib on `ModifyEnergyCostInCombat`): **kept**. Behind the mirror Harmony runs postfixes where the game runs none: after a listener throw the mirror returns false and `RethrowFinalizer` throws afterwards. The fix (throwing from the mirror prefix itself, the guard told apart) changes the exception path and PatchGuard's fault attribution of every mirrored hook, a change to make with game sessions behind it, and only then does a postfix stand there. HookSkip keeps letting it stand on the same hooks (the invariant with HookFilter's PrefixesOnly is unchanged).
5. **CardSortKeys' cost listeners it can't prove pure** (ARAM's run modifier): **kept**, documented. No sound purity analysis of other mods' hooks without escape analysis and every overrider resolved (the study: ARAM's allocates, writes, dispatches virtually and reads `card.Pile`). The note now names up to three of the listener types besides their mods, so the cause (a run modifier present in every run) shows in `bdopt why` and the menu.

Display: `PatchSafety.Watch` registrations follow the runtime checks (ListenerCache's walks and FastListeners' served walks SurroundingOnly, FastListeners' filtered walks strict, PileIndex's getter BeforeReplace, Player.Piles unregistered, HookFilter's walks strict and whole), so `Settings.PartState` and `bdopt why` show the change: with RitsuLib, ListenerCache and PileIndex no longer listed, FastListeners partly (its filtered walks), HookFilter inactive. `PatchSafety.Describe` gives a fourth verdict (`BeforeReplace`).

Tests (`Bench/ForeignPatches.cs`): shapes `walk-expand-postfix` (lazy, a second copy of each Squeeze after it, Squeeze reacting to no hook; the same Squeeze twice in a row in its input counts "applied twice"), `walk-filter-repeat` (snapshot, Squeezes moved to the end), `pile-prefix-narrow` (bool prefix answering Hand for one card while `foreignpatch narrow` sets it), `piles-postfix` (a pile appended to `Player.Piles`; `foreignpatch pilecard` moves a draw card there and back through the game's pile methods, silently), `byref-arg-prefix` (`ModifyHpLost`: a monster loses 1 HP more; `AddInternal`/`RemoveInternal`: the card passed through), `byref-out-postfix` (the out `modifiers` only read, summed), and the control `byref-write-postfix`. Lines `foreignpatch narrow|pilecard <label>` (STATE: the answers; FOREIGN: whether the index answered), `foreignpatch require nodouble`; `foreignpatch report` adds `FOREIGN listener scopes with walk patches N` (`ListenerCache.AugmentedScopes`, Debug). `bdperf listeners` also compares the walks inside a refresh scope, each twice (built, then replayed), with the reference. Scenarios: `foreign-needless-2k-on`/`-off` (A/B, CHECKSUM and STATE line for line; the on side expects the "kept" lines and `FOREIGN runtime ... kept` for ListenerCache, FastListeners, PileIndex's getter and AddInternal, HookSkip's `ModifyHpLost`, the narrowed and appended piles answered by the index, `bdperf listeners` and `bdperf pile` identical, effects of ListenerCache, FastListeners.CombatWalk, HookSkip and PileIndex growing, `require nodouble`); `foreign-needless-controls` (the near misses back off: a walk postfix reading `__runOriginal`, the narrowing prefix sorted after PilePrefix, a by-reference argument prefix after the tracking prefixes, a postfix writing through the out parameter).

**RitsuLib recovery (2026-11-16)**: the helper sets and listener gates (class 4) and the flying cards' premise (class 3). Tests (`Bench/ForeignPatches.cs`): a helper target by its fingerprint key (`Namespace.Type::Method(ArgTypes)`, the constructor included); `count-postfix` on the constructor (ungated: only the constructing hooks go vanilla); `ctor-source-gated squeeze` (RitsuLib's shape in a `beforefieldinit` class with a static field: tolerated, Squeeze kept, hits as STATE lines) and its controls `ctor-combatstate-gated`, `ctor-instance-gated`, `ctor-cctor-gated` (explicit static constructor), `ctor-read-then-gated`; on `NCardFlyShuffleVfx.Create` `create-null-gated-postfix` (counts the souls it sees; `foreignpatch require souls` compares them with the souls the scene tree got, a `NodeAdded` signal, not a patch) and its control `create-nearmiss-postfix` (a static read before the null test); on `NCardTrailVfx.Create` `trail-replacing-prefix` (a bool prefix building every trail itself, priority 800). Per hook: `foreignpatch walks mark`, `foreignpatch require filtered|vanilla Hook1,Hook2` (HookSkip's per-hook walk and filtered counts since the mark; names of the other version skipped and listed), `FOREIGN filtered walks` and `FOREIGN kept` lines in `foreignpatch report`. Scenarios: `foreign-helpers-2k-on`/`-off` (A/B: the gated constructor postfix, constructing hooks filtered with gates; then an ungated one, constructing hooks vanilla and the rest filtered; CHECKSUM and STATE line for line), `foreign-helpers-controls` (the four near misses refused and the constructing hooks vanilla; an ungated `PushModel` postfix sending AfterCardPlayed, AfterCardDrawn and the constructing hooks to the game while AfterCardChangedPiles and BeforeCardPlayed stay filtered), `foreign-flights-2k-on`/`-off` (A/B over a ~1,900-card reshuffle with both of RitsuLib's soul shapes: CardFlightLimit grows, the kept note logged, the souls seen equal the souls made), `foreign-flights-controls` (the near miss on `Create` and a postfix on `SfxCmd.PlayCardSwooshSfx`: every soul flies, CardFlightLimit's count unchanged). `HookSkipTable.cs` regenerated from v0.107.1 and v0.113.1 (147 and 154 hooks safe, rows unchanged but for their helper set). Proof: pending (no game session on this code): those five scenarios on both versions, then `BDTEST_EXTRA_MODS=RitsuLib` ab-2k on both versions with `bdopt why` (HookSkip's constructor and `PushModel` lines gone, the kept note listing RitsuLib) and `effects-check`.

**Hardening of 2026-11-17 (audit of the RitsuLib recovery, branch generalize-2)**: the owner asked "vraiment safe": where unsure, back off. Safety fixes, then three generic rules (no list of mods; all under `ShapeRules` but fix 3, which tightens both states).

- **Fix 1, PushModel's prefixes.** `PlayerChoiceContext.PushModel` creates the context's model stack on its first call (v0.107.1 and v0.113.1 `PlayerChoiceContext.cs`): a passed-in context whose earlier listeners were all dropped keeps `_modelStack` null at the first kept listener's call, where the game has an empty stack. A prefix gated on PushModel's `model` (TypeGates admitted it) could read `ModelStack` or `____modelStack` there and see the difference. HookSkip registers PushModel with no gated prefix (`PrefixesNeverGated`; `ListenerSlot.PrefixesGated`): every prefix there backs off; postfixes (the stack exists by then on both sides, same contents) and PopModel's prefixes still stand. Test: `model-gated-prefix` on PushModel (foreign-helpers-controls step 3, must back off), `model-gated-postfix` on PushModel and `model-gated-prefix` on PopModel (foreign-helpers-2k-on/off step 3, stand, counts equal). **A4 (verification audit of 2026-11-17): made exact (owner-approved, 2026-11-17).** The lasting difference was a filtered walk that kept no listener where the game's walk yields some: the passed-in context then kept `_modelStack` null where the game leaves an empty stack (`LastInvolvedModel` answers null for both and no game code reads `ModelStack` or the field, but another mod could: a postfix on the hook or a later reader of that context, through `ModelStack` (v0.111+), the field by reflection, or CrossVersionCompat's bridge, which returns the field as is). Why that is the only lasting case: in Hook.cs every `PushModel` (24 on v0.107.1 and on v0.113.1) is the first statement of its `foreach` over a walk (`IterateCombatHookListeners`, `combatState.IterateHookListeners()` or `runState.IterateHookListeners(combatState)`), so any kept listener creates the stack, and it stays (popped empty, never set back to null). How: (a) HookSkip's filters keep every badge (`PileFilter.KeepingBadges`: the mirrors' `AddReacting` over `BadgeModels` takes no filter; the kind skip dropped them before, the two badge types overriding only `AfterCardPlayed`, `AfterSideTurnStart` and `AfterPowerAmountChanged`), and the redirects (`CombatWalk`, `RunWalk`, `CombatHookWalk`) walk vanilla when the state the walk reads has none (`HasBadge`: the combat's `BadgeModels`; the run walk's are the run's without a child combat, the child's with one, as `RunState.IterateHookListeners` adds them). The game's walks yield every badge of their state (combat: after the piles and modifiers, `Contains` true for a BadgeModel; run: with the relics, potions and modifiers when `childCombatState == null`), so a filtered walk of a state holding one keeps one at least. Every run has them: `RunState.CreateShared` sets `BadgeModels` to clones of `ModelDb.BadgeModels` (two types, `CccComboModel` and `DebufferModel`, on v0.107.1 and v0.113.1), and every CombatState of a run gets the run's (`CombatRoom`, and `EventModel` on v0.107.1 / `EventCombatSynchronizer` on v0.113.1); `NullRunState` and a CombatState built without a run have none, and walk vanilla. The guard reads the getters as the walks do; both are auto-getters pinned by fingerprint (`CombatState.get_BadgeModels` 9B31700B809E179C, `RunState.get_BadgeModels` 9179602CD0ECA943, the same on v0.107.1, v0.111.0 and v0.113.1; changed or not found: HookSkip is not applied, shown as such in the menu and bdopt) and watched: another mod's patch there (the guard would run it once more) sends the walk to the game's. A kept badge runs its default hook body through the game's own loop, as vanilla does: two more iterations per filtered walk. (b) The walks over the game's own walk (`FastListeners.KeptOnly`, taken when another mod patches a walk: RitsuLib, Zone) keep its first listener: the stack is created at the game's own moment there, not only by the walk's end (argued from the loops' shape, not measured: no test reads the context during a walk; KeptOnly drops only pile entries, so on that path a walk with a power, relic or monster never ended empty anyway). Its callers: HookSkip's filtered walks, `Run`'s child combat walk (one more listener kept in the middle of the run walk: its iteration as vanilla) and HookFilter's lists (its override mask drops the extra listener again: the list is unchanged). In the mirrored walks the stack may still be created later than the game's (at the first kept listener, the first listener having been dropped): that moment is what Fix 1's back-off covers, and it stays. Proof (test builds): `HookSkip.Emptied` counts filtered walks that ended with nothing kept while the game's walk of the same state (run as the vanilla reference after the empty walk) yields something, expected 0; `HookSkip.NoBadge` the guard's fallbacks; `FastListeners.FirstKept` the first listeners KeptOnly kept for HookSkip's walks that the filter would have dropped (HookFilter's lists not counted). `modelstack-badges` (both game versions): 0 emptied and no fallback in a real run, then `bdperf badges clear` (a state the game never makes) sends every filtered walk to the game's walk, the guard off on it (`bdperf badges guard off`) gives emptied walks (the counter's positive control), and with another mod on both walks (KeptOnly) none ends empty and the first listeners are kept; `hookskip-on` and `audit-walk-conflict` check 0 emptied. No longer a premise.
- **Fix 2, beforefieldinit narrowed** (class 4 above): `InitializerFree` accepts a type initializer only when its IL stores constants, arrays, BCL collections and reflection lookups in the class's own statics (`PureInitializer`). Refusals caused by a static constructor say so in the verdict. Test: `ctor-impure-cctor-gated` (a beforefieldinit class whose initializer reads the clock: must back off); the Bench soul shapes' class keeps a plain initializer (the trail's `FieldRefAccess`, not an accepted lookup, moved to its own class). **Narrowed again by the verification audit of 2026-11-17 (P1, P2, D).** P1: an initializer that can *throw* is refused too (run at another time, a throw would be a behaviour difference): `PureInitializer` now accepts only constants, `ldtoken`/`typeof`, arrays of runtime element types, `new object()`, empty runtime collections of runtime element types (no argument, a constant non-negative capacity, or a comparer from `StringComparer.*`, `EqualityComparer<T>.Default`, `Comparer<T>.Default`), runtime static readonly fields (`Type.EmptyTypes`), `Array.Empty<T>()`, HarmonyLib's `AccessTools.Field/DeclaredField(Type, string)` (Harmony 2.4.2: null on a null type or a missing field; corrected by the re-audit round 2: it can still throw, an AmbiguousMatchException, for two fields of one name declared in one class, which only emitted IL can make, not C#; `FileLog.Debug` only with Harmony's debug flag) and `Type.GetField` on a `typeof` (the most derived field wins, no ambiguity), locals and its own static fields. Refused now: `AccessTools.TypeByName` (assembly-resolve handlers would run at another time), method, constructor and property lookups (`AccessTools.Method`, `Type.GetMethod`, `GetProperty`: an ambiguous name throws; Harmony's `Property` calls `GetProperty`, which throws on an indexer or a `new` property of another type), any call on a value that may be null, collection methods with arguments (`Add` throws on a duplicate key, `Sort` runs comparers), collections of other element types (P4: another mod's `Equals`/`GetHashCode`/`CompareTo` would run). P2: compiling the patch method may run the beforefieldinit initializer of any class whose statics it reads or calls, not only its own: `InitializersFree(patch)` requires `InitializerFree` for the patch's class and for every class outside the runtime's assemblies whose static field, static method or constructor the patch method (its generated code included) uses, when that class is beforefieldinit (a class without it runs its initializer at its first access, on the gated path, which runs as without this mod). Applies to `TypeGated` (and to `NullSkipped` until B1 of the re-audit round 2, below). The Bench's `ForeignPatches` keeps a pure initializer (its two tables of game objects moved to a nested `Tables` class): its gated shapes call `Count`. D: a refusal now tests the IL shape first (a patch not gated is told "not provably gated", whatever its class), then the initializers ("postfix gated, but its class X has a static initializer that could run at another time with this mod (when the runtime compiles or first calls the patch, which this mod can delay or remove)" or "... but it reaches X, whose static initializer ..."); the tolerated line's detail says "the same values whenever the runtime runs it" (it said "when it runs changes nothing"). Tests: `ctor-throwing-cctor-gated` (an initializer calling `AccessTools.Method`) and `ctor-reach-gated` (no initializer, but reading another class's clock-reading static) in `foreign-helpers-controls`, both backing off; `ctor-source-gated` (RitsuLib's shape) still tolerated (`foreign-helpers-2k-on`, its expected detail updated).
- **Fix 3, the helpers' game callees.** The v0.107.1 constructor inlines the owner lookup (`CardModel.Owner` with `AssertMutable`, `RelicModel/PotionModel.Owner`, `AfflictionModel/EnchantmentModel.Card`, `PowerModel.Owner`, `Creature.IsPlayer/Player`, `ICombatState.Players` and its implementations with `CombatState.PlayerCreatures`), v0.113.1's `GetOwner` calls the same getters, the helpers read `Source`, `Task`, `GameAction`: a dropped listener skips those calls too, and a patch on one ran fewer times. `tools/fpdiff hookfps` now prints the helpers' call graph inside the game's assembly (`Calls` lines: generated code included, a virtual or interface call to every implementation, up to the other helpers; each key checked to round-trip through `MethodFingerprint.Resolve`), minus the edges reviewed as never taken (`NeverTaken` in fpdiff, `NEVER_TAKEN` in classify.py with a regex proof on the decompiled caller: PopModel's error branch with `Log.Error`, never run for a pair), and the reviewed chains' getters (`Getter` lines: `Creature.CombatState`, `Player.Creature`, which v0.107.1's BeforeFlush and AfterDeath pass per listener; `REVIEWED_CHAINS` treated them as "no code runs", but a Harmony patch there does run). classify.py adds both to the hook's helper set (strict: no listener argument) and pins them in the version's profile (a change leaves those hooks vanilla), except compiler-generated ones (lambdas, the `<>z__` collection types): their names change with any renumbering, a lambda's code is already in its caller's fingerprint, so they are only in the sets, and one whose key no longer resolves sends its group to the game. Only the constructing hooks' sets grow (6 on v0.113.1, 8 on v0.107.1); AfterCardPlayed and the other PushModel/ExecutionFinished hooks are unchanged (no game callee). HookSkipTable.cs regenerated: rows unchanged but for their set index; a second run is identical; v0.111.0's helpers and callees equal v0.113.1's (its rows keep matching). Test: foreign-helpers-controls step 4 (ungated postfixes on `CardModel.get_Owner` and `Player.get_Creature`: the constructing hooks vanilla, the others filtered). **Verification audit of 2026-11-17 (auditor 2, A1 and C).** fpdiff's call graph now comes from `MethodFingerprint.GameCallees`, shared with the patches' `CodeSet`: an operand that doesn't resolve stops hookfps (it was skipped), a call through a closed generic interface reaches the implementations of that instantiation (variance included; every instantiation for generic code or a generic implementing class, as the `<>z__` collections), default interface methods are implementations too, the type initializers a call may run are listed (`Calls ... -> X::.cctor()`, pinned and checked, not followed: they run once per process; PopModel's never-taken branch also skips `Log`'s, a `NeverTaken`/`NEVER_TAKEN` pair with the same proof), and the delegates the graph invokes are printed (`Invokes` lines): classify.py stops on one not in `INVOKED` (today only `InvokeExecutionFinished`'s `ExecutionFinished` event: HookSkip keeps every listener with a subscriber in the hooks that signal it, so a dropped listener's event has none). A1: classify.py stops on a call to a helper from a non-helper (a game method calling `PushModel` or constructing a context would get a listener argument it never matched) or from a helper as `CLOSURE` doesn't list it, and checks every helper-to-helper edge fpdiff prints against `CLOSURE`. The chain getters are pinned only in the profiles of the versions that have a hook passing them (v0.113.1's profile no longer pins `Creature.CombatState` and `Player.Creature`, which no v0.113.1 hook passes: a change there left its hooks vanilla for nothing). Generic members are named by their open definitions while Harmony patches a closed instantiation's own method: theoretical today (no generic member in a set), noted. HookSkipTable.cs regenerated (v0.107.1, v0.113.1): rows identical; the constructing sets gain `CombatState+<>c::.cctor()`; a second run identical; v0.111.0 still holds profile 1, as before. **A2, A3, B1.** The implementers of each virtual or interface call in the pinned helpers and callees are pinned per version (fpdiff's `Dispatch` lines, profile entries `dispatch:<caller>|<callee>` = the sorted implementer keys: `ICombatState.Players` -> `CombatState`'s and `NullCombatState`'s, v0.107.1's `IReadOnlyList<Player>[i]` -> the three `<>z__ReadOnly*` indexers), and so is each pinned method's virtual/final flag (`Flags` lines, `virtual:<key>`): `HookSkip.ProfileValue` recomputes both at load (`MethodFingerprint.DispatchTargets`, the same code as the tool), so a new implementer or a method turning virtual leaves the profile's hooks vanilla. At run time, a loaded class outside the game overriding or implementing one of a group's dispatched methods (a mod's `ICombatState`) sends that group to the game (`ForeignImplementers`, rechecked when an assembly loads), logged and noted in `bdopt why` (`overriders_N`). A3: with `ShapeRules` off a group is a profile again, and now also holds the compiler-generated callees of the sets its hooks use (no profile pins them; since a30358c they were neither checked nor able to back off there). B1: a generated key that no longer resolves sends its group to the game as before, now logged and noted (`unresolved_N`: "a compiler-generated game method the choice-context helpers call (...) isn't found in this game"). Regenerated table: rows identical, a second run identical; each version holds its own profile and v0.111.0 holds v0.113.1's (profile 1), flags and dispatch entries included (checked offline from the three versions' hookfps).
- **Fix 4, the listener table.** Keyed by module and token (another `MethodBase` object for the same method finds it), registration drops the cached answers about the method, and a TypeGated original whose instance isn't a model (a helper: its instance is the choice context) gets no gate unless registered: the fallback to `__instance` (and the comment saying a gate there would find nothing) is gone.
- **Fix 5**: CardFlightLimit's merged pile-button count also backs off from a patch on `CardPile.InvokeCardAddFinished` itself (Hermit's HandChangedPatches postfix is one). foreign-flights-controls step 3. Since the verification audit of 2026-11-17 (C), also from a patch on anything `NCombatCardPile.AddCard` reaches (it runs once for the merged cards: `CardPile.Cards`, `MegaLabel.SetTextAutoSize` and its font sizing, 10 methods computed from the game's code, `CodeSet`, the same set on v0.107.1, v0.111.0 and v0.113.1, pinned): one event per card then. foreign-flights-controls step 4 (a postfix on `SetTextAutoSize`).
- **Fix 6**: RelicFlashMerge checks `Creature.GetVfxContainer` and `GodotTreeExtensions.AddChildSafely` strictly too (both called per flash by `Player.OnRelicFlashed`, v0.107.1 to v0.113.1).
- **Fix 7**: CardFlightLimit's premise worded precisely, its code pinned and named, the kept note's conditions, the Chaotic argument corrected (class 3 above).
- **Fix 8**: the Tolerated log line names the gated argument ("gated on its argument source") and an accepted beforefieldinit initializer; HookSkip's helper gates let stand show in `bdopt why`'s kept section (`helper_gates`: "other mods' patches on the hooks' choice-context helpers (...) do nothing unless the listener is a ...: those listeners keep their calls (proven from the patches' code)"), not in the menu hover (that sentence is the flying cards'). Since the verification audit of 2026-11-17 (B2, D): only helpers of a group that runs filtered are listed (a group another patch sends to the game keeps every listener anyway), each with its own gate types ("... do nothing unless the listener is of these types, judged from the patches' code (HookPlayerChoiceContext..ctor (source): Squeeze; ...): those listeners keep their calls"), no "proven" wording.
- **Rule A, `PatchTolerance.TranspiledOnly`** (HookSkip's `Hook` methods: sync hooks and async kickoffs). This mod's only code there is the walk-redirecting transpiler (plus, on some, HookFilter's mirror and the read-only guard). `SurroundingOnly`'s refusals (bool prefixes, by-ref writes, `__args`, `__runOriginal`) protect a skipping prefix or a finalizer of this mod reading the argument slots, which don't exist there. So every prefix and postfix stands, any shape (factories and renamed parameters included): a prefix decides whether and with which arguments the transpiled body runs, as without this mod; a postfix sees the same `__result`, out values and `__runOriginal`, the dropped iterations being no-ops (the rows' classification). Finalizers, transpilers (the IL judge aside) and inner patches still back off; the state machine (`MoveNext`) stays strict. `HookSkip.MethodTolerance` checks, at each Harmony epoch and each second (the epoch moves before Harmony stores the new patch list: the timed recheck bounds a patch made off the main thread), that this mod's own patches on the method are only HookSkip's transpiler, HookFilter's mirror prefix and `RethrowFinalizer`, PatchGuard's guard and order-free prefixes (LimitlessHookSkip's `Sample0`-`Sample3` on AfterCardPlayed, AfterCardDrawn, BeforeFlush and AfterAutoPrePlayPhaseEntered, Release builds included: `[OrderFreePrefix]` since 2026-11-17, no argument, void, only reading; the test builds' CombatStartTimer and PerfProbe prefixes); anything else of this mod there gives `SurroundingOnly`, as before: the test builds' postfixes, CombatStartTimer's on BeforeCombatStart and AfterRoomEntered and PerfProbe's on AfterCombatEnd and AfterCombatVictory (so the Release path, TranspiledOnly, is never run on those four in tests: acceptable, the other async kickoffs run it, and the shapes are the same). The preflight (bdopt why, the menu) asks the same function (`PatchSafety.Watch` with a tolerance function), so the display never says "kept" where the runtime backs off (before 2026-11-17 it registered TranspiledOnly unconditionally: with Limitless, the four sampled hooks were shown kept while the runtime used SurroundingOnly); a flip of the answer forgets the back-off recorded under the other tolerance (`PatchSafety.ForgetOtherTolerances`). HarmonyWatch's finalizer is checked again at each preflight (`HarmonyWatch.Revalidate`: another mod's `UnpatchAll()` with no owner removes it; the caches then use their timed rechecks). Invariant with HookFilter: its mirror checks the same method with `PrefixesOnly`, once per ListenerCache scope (`_safe`, reset when the scope ends), and runs the original (transpiled) whenever more than void prefixes sorted before it are there; both call sites name it (rule A doesn't depend on when that check runs: a mirror that skips the hook skips the transpiled body with it). Holds on every version (it relies on no hook's code, only on the transpiler keeping each hook's effects). Data: EnchantLib's ModifyBlock bool prefix and ShouldDie postfix (ref preventer), KitLib's ShouldClearBlock and ShouldAllowFreeTravel bool prefixes, Zone's ModifyHpLost modifiers and ShouldAllowTargeting preventer, Downfall's ModifyCardPlayResultLocation modifiers, PengoTarot's replacing prefix on ModifyEnergyCostInCombat (there HookFilter's mirror backs off, HookSkip stays filtered). BaseLib's ModifyBlock prefix allowance becomes redundant with the rules on (kept: with them off it is still needed; its transpiler's isn't). Tests: shapes `hook-replacing-prefix`, `hook-out-write-postfix`, `hook-args-prefix`, `runoriginal-postfix`, `bool-prefix`; scenario `foreign-bodyonly-2k-on/off` (945 cards); controls in `foreign-controls` (`noop-finalizer` on ModifyHpLost, a synthetic inner postfix on ModifyBlock); `foreign-controls`, `foreign-shapes`, `foreign-needless` and `foreign-needless-controls` expect the new verdicts (their bool, ref, `__runOriginal`, factory and by-ref-write shapes on Hook methods are HookSkip's positive cases now, still SurroundingOnly's and HookFilter's controls).
- **Rule D, RelicFlashMerge's sound replayed.** When another mod patches `SfxCmd.Play(string, float)` or the `FlashSfx` getter a relic runs (RelicModel's or its override), a merged flash plays its sound through the game's own call, `SfxCmd.Play(relic.FlashSfx, 1f)` (the default volume, pinned by `PlayerFlashFingerprint`), then returns false: only its creature effect is merged. Invariant: those patches see the same calls with the same arguments in the same order (one per flash, before the next flash's), on every version (the handler's fingerprinted code is the same three calls). The call goes through a delegate to the method's entry (where Harmony's detour is) and outside the prefix's catch (a throw from another mod's patch leaves the handler where the game's would, before the effect). Not a back-off: nothing recorded. Still strict: `NRelicFlashVfx.Create`, `GetVfxContainer`, `AddChildSafely`; ARAM's DoFlash prefix (the relic bar part) stays a back-off. `ShapeRules` off: `SfxCmd.Play` strict as before. **Audit of 2026-11-17 (R1, R2).** What a merged flash leaves out is now computed from the game's code, not listed by hand (`CodeSet`, `MethodFingerprint.GameClosure`: every game method the roots reach through calls, the game's own overrides of the game's virtual methods they call included; type initializers and calls into other assemblies left out, a bound of 1,500 methods), and pinned per version (`MethodFingerprint.SetFingerprint`, the names and code fingerprints of the set and the delegates it invokes; tools/fingerprint `closure` prints them; a set not reviewed for this version leaves its part vanilla, the sound replayed). Three sets: (1) the **sound**, `SfxCmd.Play(string, float)` and `RelicModel.FlashSfx` with every game override of it (CentennialPuzzle, GamePiece, IronClub, JossPaper, Pendulum, UnceasingTop...: 102 methods on v0.107.1, 105 on v0.111.0, 106 on v0.113.1): both `NAudioManager.PlayOneShot` overloads, `NonInteractiveMode.IsActive`, `TestMode.IsOn`, `NGame`'s getters, and `CombatManager.IsEnding`, which walks the combat's listeners through `Hook.ShouldStopCombatFromEnding` when no primary enemy is alive (the listeners' models, the mods' combat hook subscriptions). A merged flash replays its sound (rule D) when another mod patches any of it (not only `SfxCmd.Play` and the getter: BaseLib's `PlayResourcePatch`, RitsuLib and Merchant2CuteII patch `PlayOneShot(string, Dictionary, float)`), when a class outside the game overrides a game virtual method of the set (a modded relic's `FlashSfx`, a modded listener's `ShouldStopCombatFromEnding`: every override in every relic's class chain, not only the most derived as before), when a mod subscribed to the combat's hooks (`ModHelper`'s subscriptions: their delegates run in that walk), or when `NonInteractiveMode.AutoSlayerCheck` isn't the game's own function. (2) the **creature effect**, `NRelicFlashVfx`'s whole class (its instances exist only through flashes: constructor, `_Ready`, `StartVfx` and its state machine, `_ExitTree`, the Godot-generated members) with `Creature.GetVfxContainer` and `AddChildSafely` (466 / 463 / 523 methods: `Create(relic)`, `NCombatRoom.GetCreatureNode`, `NCreature.GetTopOfHitbox`, `RelicModel.Icon` and the icon path getters, `TaskHelper.RunSafely` and its error paths), and (3) the **relic bar effect**, the holder's `OnRelicFlashed` and `DoFlash` (360 / 356 / 407 methods: the scene cache, `NRun`'s UI, `AddChildSafely`, the relic's icon). (2) and (3) are strict for now (the owner's decision: the relic-flash display premise, DoFlash's as approved on 2026-11-17 and the creature effect's in the same spirit, comes with the other premises): another mod's patch on any of their methods keeps every flash of that effect (RitsuLib's `RelicTexturePatch` and Orchis-NecrobinderSkin on `RelicModel.Icon`), and so does a class outside the game overriding a game virtual method they call, except those rooted in `RelicModel` (the icon path getters), which keep only the flashes of the relic whose own class overrides them (`CodeSet.InstanceOverrides`); such classes are named in `bdopt why` (a note, no patch shows them). Their delegates are the log's listeners and the task helper's exception callback, reached on error paths only (an exception inside the effect's own code): listed with the sets and reviewed, not checked (owner: part of the premise). The replay itself (R2) runs in `RelicFlashMerge.PlaySound`, marked `[GameCallReplay]`: PatchGuard's walk of an exception's stack stops at such a frame, so a throw from another mod's code inside the replayed call (where the game's own call would throw it, passed on to the game's caller as such) no longer faults or blames RelicFlashMerge; this mod's patches inside the call are still judged. The sets are computed once on the main thread, at the preflight's note step (the menu's frames; about 0.1-0.2 s each), or at the first flash if none ran (nothing merged if they can't be computed). Tests: `relicflash-deep-on/off` (a counting prefix on `NonInteractiveMode.IsActive`, deep in the sound set: replays grow, counts equal), `relicflash-ctl-on/off` with a counting prefix on `RelicModel.get_Icon` too (both effects back off). Data: ARAM's GoldrendRune, RegentFX's CardAnimPatch, Merchant2CuteII's MerchantVoiceSfxPatch (prefixes on `SfxCmd.Play`). Tests: shape `sfx-observer-prefix` (a bool prefix counting relic sounds and suppressing the general one), opt-in effect `RelicFlashMerge.SoundsReplayed`; `relicflash-sfx-on/off` (a 945-card campfire clone: merges and replays grow, the sound counts equal), control `relicflash-ctl-on/off` (counting prefixes on `NRelicFlashVfx.Create` and `GetVfxContainer`: every player flash kept, counts equal, no replay).
- **Rule E, interface-keyed SavedSpireFields.** `BaseLibTypes.MayBeOnCards` counted any interface key, so CardAscension's ten `SavedSpireField<IRunState, ...>` gave three false notes (LimitlessReserialize, LimitlessRecalcSkip, SnapshotCardReuse). Now only when a loaded card or enchantment type (or a generic definition of one) implements it, per interface, again when an assembly loads; a type load error counts. Display only (the runtime's per-type test is unchanged). Test: `spirefield-interfaces` (`foreignpatch spiretarget irunstate` false, `icomparable` true).
- **Not done**: rule G (type gates on a named parameter or `___field` for the memo optimizations): optional, always blocked by RitsuLib in the data, and the memo would need its gate set in its key.

**Re-audit round 2 of 2026-11-17 (of dec4fe7..0bc4113, two auditors)**: no path changing game state, checksums or co-op, no back-off wrongly lifted with today's game and mods. Fixed (low-risk only: where unsure, back off):

- **B1, a needless back-off (RitsuLib).** P2 applied to `NullSkipped` stopped CardFlightLimit whenever RitsuLib was loaded: its start-position postfix calls `ModCardPileRegistry.TryGetByPileType`, whose beforefieldinit initializer creates a `Lock` and dictionaries of mod types. Needless: on a skipped call this mod's prefix leaves a null `__result` and Harmony still runs the postfix, so it runs as many times as in the game and its first call is never a skip (burst 12): it is compiled and first called when it is without this mod. `NullSkipped` judges no initializer now (`NullResultGated` is the IL shape only; the tolerated line has no initializer detail there); what the postfix's non-null path reaches stays under the premise (what it does with a real soul). `TypeGated` keeps P1/P2 (this mod removes calls there). Test: `create-null-gated-reach-postfix` (the null-gated shape in a class whose initializer reads the clock, reaching another such class on its non-null path), foreign-flights-controls step 5: tolerated, souls still skipped, real souls seen.
- **CodeSet's overriders (second auditor A1, A4, A5, A6; first auditor C2, C4).** (a) *Interface and abstract callees* (A1): an implicit interface implementation is `virtual final` and an interface or abstract method has no body, so neither reached `Virtuals`: a mod's `ICombatState` reached by `Hook.ShouldStopCombatFromEnding` was never seen. `GameClosure` now reports every virtual or interface call it follows (`dispatches`), and `CodeSet.Compute(..., interfaces: true)` adds those game callees to `Virtuals` (no change to any set fingerprint). Used by the sets whose code is game logic or decides a replay: CardFlightLimit's new logic set and RelicFlashMerge's sound set. **Not** by the visual sets (RelicFlashMerge's two, CardFlightLimit's flight set): their added interfaces would be `ILogPrinter.Print` (error paths), `INodePool.Free`/`IPoolable.OnFreedToPool`, `IPlatformUtilStrategy.GetLocalPlayerId`, `IMigration`'s getters (tools/fingerprint closure now prints them as `dispatch` lines), reached through the game's own instances there; BaseLib's `GeneratedNodePool` (`INodePool`, `IPoolable`) and RitsuLib's migrations (`IMigration`) implement some, so checking them would stop RelicFlashMerge whenever BaseLib or RitsuLib is loaded, for display code. Owner decision (listed). (b) *Dynamic assemblies and indirect references* (A4, C4): the candidates for an override are now every type of an assembly depending on the game directly or through another loaded assembly (a class deriving from a game type through another mod's base class needs no reference to the game), and the created types of dynamic assemblies (`LoadedCode.Types`); an assembly that can't list its types makes the answer "can't tell" (true), as before. (c) *Cache key* (A4): `LoadedCode.Key`, the AssemblyLoad count plus, sampled at most every 2 s, the created types of dynamic assemblies (a type emitted into an assembly already loaded raises no event), for CodeSet and HookSkip's helper groups (`ForeignImplementers`, keyed on the assembly count before). (d) *Default interface methods* (A5): a mod class getting a game interface's member from a mod interface's default method counts as overriding it (`Overrides` required the class itself to declare the target). (e) *Torn reads* (A6): CodeSet's overrides answers and HookSkip's per-group overriders are immutable records replaced whole (were nullable tuple structs). (f) *Allocations per flash* (C2): `ForeignOverrides` keeps its filtered virtual methods per instance class and asks the cheap key (no `GetAssemblies`, no `ToArray` while the code stays the same); `InstanceOverrides` looks the type up before creating its lambda; the delegates check (`GameHookDelegates`, shared by RelicFlashMerge and CardFlightLimit) caches its answer per subscription count and test-hook delegate (no reflection invoke, no invocation list while they stay).
- **A4 (first auditor), the card-flight premise covers display code only (owner's decision; narrowed on in-game evidence).** What a skipped soul leaves out also held game logic: `Create` plays the swoosh, `SfxCmd.Play` asks `NonInteractiveMode.IsActive` then `CombatManager.IsEnding`, which, when no primary enemy is alive (and the combat is in progress, no loss pending), walks the combat's listeners (`Hook.ShouldStopCombatFromEnding`, `IterateHookListeners`): every listener's `ShouldStopCombatFromEnding` (other mods' overrides) and the mods' combat hook subscriptions (BaseLib's "BaseLibCardModifiers" walks every card). The game's own code there only reads (its overrides return a constant), so with the game alone leaving it out was never a difference. A plain back-off from any other mod's code there would stop CardFlightLimit for every BaseLib user (its CardModifier subscribes at BaseLib's init; the test copies load BaseLib), so the check is split in two computed, pinned sets (`CodeSet`, interface callees included; `stopAt`, new in `GameClosure`, `CodeSet.Compute` and tools/fingerprint closure as `!Key`): **the head** (`_endHead`: IsActive and its test hook, IsEnding up to the walk, `CombatManager.Instance`, `DebugOnlyGetState`; 22, 25, 25 methods on v0.107.1, v0.111.0, v0.113.1, `EndHeadSets`) runs at every swoosh: another mod's code there (a patch: a back-off, watched and whole; an override, such as ZoneTheSpire's power overriding `OwnerIsSecondaryEnemy`; a test hook not the game's: the note `combat_end`, whole) keeps every soul. **The walk** (`_endWalk`: `Hook.ShouldStopCombatFromEnding` and what it reaches; 69, 69, 70 methods, `EndWalkSets`) runs only when no primary enemy is alive: with another mod's code there (a patch, an override, any combat hook subscription) a soul is skipped only while a primary enemy is alive, read by the game's own test (`e != null && e.IsAlive && e.IsPrimaryEnemy` over `DebugOnlyGetState().Enemies`, the state IsEnding reads on every version), which runs only the head's code, then free of other mods' code: no side effect, and the walk can't run for that soul. Otherwise the soul flies (`KeptAtCombatEnd`, Bench). The log says it once per change ("... every card flies while no primary enemy is alive (mods)"). Checked against the decompiled Workshop mods: none patches a method of the head (CrossVersionCompat and Watcher only call `DebugOnlyGetState`); ZoneTheSpire overrides `OwnerIsSecondaryEnemy` (every soul kept with it: owner decision). With the shape rules off the whole flight set's delegates count too (A3). Test: `foreign-flights-end-2k-on/off` (a counting postfix on the walk: mid-combat souls skipped, none kept; with STOCK_POWER on the player and every enemy killed, no soul skipped, the postfix sees the same calls as mod off).
- **RelicFlashMerge (first auditor A1, A3, A5, C1).** A1: `DoFlash` instantiates `res://scenes/vfx/relic_inventory_flash_vfx.tscn`, whose root "RelicFlash" carries `NVfxParticleSystem.cs` (read from the game's pck: `_Ready`, v0.113.1's `TryPlayParticles`, `AfterExpired`, the Godot-generated members): the engine runs it for each flash shown, so a merge leaves it out, and no set had it. Its declared code (11 methods on v0.107.1, 12 on v0.111.0 and v0.113.1) is checked strictly with the relic bar effect and pinned (`HolderScriptSets`, tools/fingerprint closure with the new `FP_ROOTSONLY=1`), **not followed**: what it calls is shared node code (`QueueFreeSafely`, `NodePool.Free`, `NCard.OnFreedToPool`, tween getters), which BaseLib and Limitless patch for their own nodes (the closure would have added 29 such methods). The scene's scripts are read once, before the first relic bar merge, from the game's asset cache (`SceneState` of `NRelicInventoryHolder._flashPath`, sub-scenes included): anything but that one script keeps every relic bar flash (logged). A3: with the shape rules off (or the sound delegate unresolved), the sound set's overrides, delegates and review keep the sound too, as its patches did. A5: the merge state is recorded by a postfix on each handler (`Shown`, through `__state`), so a flash whose handler threw (another mod's code in `SfxCmd.Play`, a caller going on) doesn't make later flashes of the frame merge with nothing shown. C1: `PlaySound` is `NoInlining | NoOptimization` and a block body: no tail call can drop its frame, which PatchGuard's replay attribute needs. A6: the visual sets' delegates (the log's listeners, `TaskHelper`'s exception callback) are reached on error paths only (an exception inside the effect's own code): not checked, written here. **A1 superseded by the merge with the premises** ("Owner-approved premises", DoFlash and the relic-flash visuals): the script is now a root of the relic bar effect's set and followed (pinned with it, `HolderVisualSets`; `HolderScriptSets`, `ScriptPatched` and the `PreloadManager`-based scene read are gone): with the shape rules on, under the display premise like the rest of that set; with them off, strict as the whole set, what it reaches included (so BaseLib's and Limitless's patches on that shared node code keep every relic bar flash with the rules off). The scene check stays, as the premise's `SceneScriptsKnown` (both effects' scenes, from Godot's resource cache, where the game's `AssetCache` loads them with `CacheMode.Reuse`; replaced by the re-audit of the premises with a check of the node the frame's first flash created, `ShownNodeKnown`: "Owner-approved premises"). A3, A5, C1 and the CodeSet rework stand as written; the premise's strict-overrides answer is keyed on `LoadedCode.Key` (no `GetAssemblies` per flash) and the scene check takes one script type (no array per flash).
- **The strict visual sets and shared code (coordinator, from the proof run on v0.107.1): reported, not changed.** RelicFlashMerge's two visual sets (closures of the handlers and of `NRelicFlashVfx`) reach generic game code other mods patch for their own purposes: `TweenHelper.AwaitFinished(Internal)`, `ModelDb.GetEntry`/`get_AllCharacters`, `ConsoleLogPrinter.Print`, `NCard.OnFreedToPool`, `RelicModel.get_PackedIconPath`: with BaseLib or Limitless loaded RelicFlashMerge backs off. Restricting the strict check to the code specific to the merged path needs each method's callers across the whole game (a method is specific when all its game callers are in the set or it has none: engine-called members), too slow at run time (every game method's IL), so it would be an offline table per version (tools/fingerprint, pinned like HookSkipTable). Owner decision (listed): that table, or a display premise for shared code as CardFlightLimit's, or strict as now. The sets this round touched: the holder's scene script is added without its callees (above); CardFlightLimit's combat-end head (22 to 25 methods: IsActive, IsEnding's own tests, the creature and power getters they read) holds no utility code and no Workshop mod studied patches it; its walk set only narrows (souls kept while no primary enemy is alive); the flight set is under the premise with the rules on (named, not a back-off).
- **C3, C5 and nits (first auditor).** The large pinned closures fail safe: a game hotfix touching any of their methods changes the set's fingerprint and stops what relies on it (no flight skipped, no flash merged) until re-pinned. C5: HookSkip's comment on the per-second recheck gave a wrong race (Harmony 2.4.2's `PatchProcessor` holds its lock across `UpdateWrapper` and `UpdatePatchInfo`, and `GetPatchInfo` takes it: a check reading the new epoch reads the new list); the recheck stays for what moves no epoch. A flip of tolerance (`ForgetOtherTolerances`) now also forgets the tolerated records and log lines under the other tolerance (logged again if it comes back). Not changed (only stricter): static members a skip never removes in the flight set; the handler's target-list enumerator in no set (the list is the game's `IReadOnlyList`, checked by `SingleTarget`).
- **A2 (first auditor), the trail's line script.** The trail scenes (`res://scenes/vfx/card_trail_*.tscn`, every character's, read from the game's pck) carry `NCardTrail.cs` on their OuterTrail and InnerTrail lines (`_Ready`, `OnToggleVisibility`, `_Process`, `CreatePoint`: points of a `Line2D`, display only, the same code on v0.107.1, v0.111.0 and v0.113.1): a skipped soul's trail never runs it. It joins the flight set's roots (264, 266, 289 methods now, `FlightSets` re-pinned) and the pinned premise code (`DisplayFingerprints`), so it is named in the kept note like the rest of the souls' visual classes. A modded character's own trail scene (its path is the character's) carries that mod's scripts: under the premise as before.
- **Initializer rules for `TypeGated` (second auditor A2, A3, B2, B3, B4, C6).** *What is compiled with the patch* (A2, B4): P2 now scans the patch method and the methods of its own assembly it calls directly, transitively to depth 3 (an own-class helper the JIT may inline, and its static accesses with it), plus their state machines; lambdas only referenced by a delegate are compiled on their own when invoked and are no longer scanned (over-inclusion). *Throwing initializers* (A3): array sizes and collection capacities must be constants in [0, 1 << 20], a concurrency level -1 or at least 1, a bounded capacity at least 1, no other integer argument; array stores only in the compiler's pattern (`dup; ldc index; value; stelem` after the `newarr` or the previous store, the index inside the array, the value a constant, `ldnull`, a string or a `typeof` the element type accepts); `Type.GetField` without `IgnoreCase`; the one-argument `GetMethodFromHandle` only for a method of a non-generic type. *Accepted* (B2, B3, B4): Roslyn's `<>c` class for non-capturing lambdas (`newobj <>c::.ctor(); stsfld <>9`, the constructor only calling object's); HarmonyLib's `AccessTools` reached by a patch (Harmony runs it while patching, this mod's own patches at its load included: initialized before any other mod's patch is judged; `Traverse` not taken, not established); empty collections of any element types and `ConditionalWeakTable<,>` when constructed with no comparer (no code of those types runs; a comparer argument still needs runtime element types, and no `Add`). *Identity, not names* (C6): `AccessTools` must come from the 0Harmony this mod uses, and a "System.*" assembly counts as the runtime's only when loaded from the core library's folder. Tests: `ctor-lambda-gated` and `ctor-accesstools-gated` (tolerated), `ctor-capacity-cctor-gated` and `ctor-helper-reach-gated` (back off), foreign-helpers-controls step 3. Not done: the offline check of Zone's, Synergies' and RitsuLib's gated patches against the new rules (B4's last part: it needs those mods loaded with the game's assemblies; the next in-game run's `FOREIGN classify` and back-off lines show it), and the report of the game or Godot classes that commonly cause refusals (B3: from in-game logs).
- **HookSkip's helper notes (second auditor C1, C2, C3; C4, C5/C7, C8).** C1/C2: before the first walk of a group (no runtime answer yet) the kept note asks the group itself (`PreflightPatched`: a renumbered callee, other mods' overriders of its dispatched virtual methods, worked out at the note step so their note shows in the menu and `bdopt why` before any combat, an ungated patch on a helper or a refused gated one; recorded under the patch's name, not counted as the runtime's), so it no longer lists helpers of groups that will run vanilla. The scan's cost is the candidate types (`LoadedCode.Types`, built once per loaded-code key) times each group's few virtual methods: not measured in game. C3: one note `overriders` for every group (the union of classes) and one `unresolved` naming every key not found (were one per group, the same text repeated). Not done: C4 (pin the virtual/final flags of the chain getters `Creature.get_CombatState` and `Player.get_Creature`, which have no `virtual:` entry: needs tools/fpdiff to emit flags for Getter lines and the three profiles regenerated; today both are non-virtual on every version). C5/C7, what the computed sets don't see (written here; the code comments say the same): `GameCallees` follows `call`, `callvirt`, `newobj`, `ldftn`, `ldvirtftn` only (no `calli`, no `jmp`: none in the sets reviewed), not the game's methods called back through the runtime, Godot or SmartFormat (a comparer, a signal handler, a format extension: engine-called node methods are roots only when their class is), and it records type initializers only where a set asks for them (`initializers: true` for HookSkip's helper closure, false for CodeSet's sets, whose initializers are pinned through their fingerprints, not followed); a listener's "INVOKED" walk text and `Entry.Subscribed` share the accepted gap written in "Hardening of 2026-11-17". C8: the model-stack premise (3345ded) is no longer one: made exact (A4 under "Fix 1, PushModel's prefixes").
- **B (first auditor), tests.** `relicflash-ctl-on/off`: one counting prefix per phase (a new run and a campfire clone each: NRelicFlashVfx.Create, Creature.GetVfxContainer, RelicModel.get_Icon), each phase proving its detection alone: no flash merged in it (`effectcheck same`), the runtime check's own back-off (`FOREIGN runtime RelicFlashMerge X (None) kept 0 backed off N`), STATE as mod off; SoundsReplayed no longer asserted (no merge, nothing to replay). Since the merge with the premises those three members are under the display premise (shape rules on): their positive cases are in `relicflash-display-on/off`, and `relicflash-ctl` keeps this form (a phase per prefix, runtime lines) for what stays strict: the two handlers and `Rng.get_Chaotic` ("Owner-approved premises"). `relicflash-flashsfx-on/off` (0bc4113): a counting prefix on CentennialPuzzle's FlashSfx override; merged flashes replay their sound. `foreignpatch report` says whether combat hook subscriptions exist (`FOREIGN combat hook subscriptions present|none`): with BaseLib loaded every merged flash replays its sound, so the replay scenarios (`relicflash-sfx`, `-deep`, `-flashsfx`) discriminate only with `BDTEST_MODS=none`. Not done: the exception-path test of `[GameCallReplay]` (a throwing foreign patch in the sound set during merged flashes: the game's caller of `RelicModel.Flash` would have to survive the throw in both runs, unverifiable without a session), foreign-controls' BeforeCombatStart display-versus-runtime split, the expect that LimitlessHookSkip's sampling prefix is installed, ForgetOtherTolerances and HarmonyWatch.Revalidate tests, and foreign-flights-controls steps 3-4 with an off twin.

**Battery fixes on 0177e77 (same day)**: `guard-checks`' third FaultProbe check found no patch: PatchGuard faults every patch name registered from one outermost class, so the throwing-prefix check's fault also took the swallow check's patch away; that check is now its own class (`FaultProbeSwallow`). `handselect-on/off`: turn 2 drew a full hand, `emptyhand` first. `foreign-shapes-2k`: `probe-prefix` read the added card's cost before the add, where the card is between piles (no combat state: no read); it reads the target pile's first card. `limitless-gated-2k`: the gated prefix installed at run time at the default priority sorted after SnapshotCardReuse's write mark on `CardModel.AddKeyword` (registered earlier), and TypeGated's order rule rightly refused it (a gated prefix after the mark could serialize the card before the write, leaving the memo stale); the scenario installs it at priority 800, as a mod patching at its load sorts. LimitlessCloneMemo's clone chain (~10 ms in one preflight step) is built and registered entry by entry (`ChainPart`, each type's methods read once).

**Preflight timing (owner's decision)**: rather a little more loading time than a settings page showing less than it should. (1) During the game's loading (the patches finished before the main menu): every entry resolved **and checked** in one go; only the note checks wait for the menu (they need every mod's setup done). (2) Once the main menu shows, what is left within 3 ms per frame. (3) The settings page or `bdopt` opened before that ended: the rest at once (`FinishStartup`, a one-off pause of tens of ms at most), then the usual synchronous pass; the page and `bdopt why` never show a half-checked state. The `Preflight:` line logs each part (steps, ms, longest step with its patch; "finished on demand (by)").

**Battery on 4b586ef (same day, both versions)**: what failed, and why none of it was the walk tolerance.

- `foreign-needless-2k-on`, `require nodouble` ("walk-expand-postfix ran on its own output 19,559,802 times", v0.107.1 14,826,966): **the detector, not the mod**. The mod-off side (`bdopt off`: no scope, no served walk, "walks served 0") counted as many (19,724,970; 14,882,978), and its STATE line differed between the sides only by how often each enumerates the walks. The game's `RunState.IterateHookListeners(child)` ends with `childCombatState.IterateHookListeners()`, the combat walk's patched call (both versions): the combat walk's postfixes have run on that tail, so the run walk's postfix gets each combat Squeeze as a pair, with or without this mod, and the detector counted any pair. The code paths were reviewed again and keep the rule (a walk postfix sees the unpatched stream once per game call, nothing of this mod keeps its output): the served walks (FastListeners' prefixes, ListenerCache's `Replay`) go through the game's call; ListenerCache's augmented lists come from the mirrors called directly (`UnpatchedCombat`/`UnpatchedRun`), the run mirror's child combat walk through the interface (its postfixes run there, live); a run walk with a child is not served while the combat walk is augmented; HookSkip's and the filtered walks' `KeptOnly` read the game's call's output without keeping it. `bdperf listeners` in a refresh scope already gave the reference's sequences (combat 5,314/5,314, run 13,172/13,172, 4 of 4 served): a list holding a postfix's output would have shown more Squeezes. The detector is now exact (`ForeignPatches.ExpandWalk`): on the combat walk any model twice in a row is a second application; on the run walk (`WalkExpandRunPostfix`) a pair is the game's only for a Squeeze the combat walk's expansion yielded (recorded in a weak table; the deck's cards are other objects than the combat's), any other pair or three in a row counts. Both sides should now log no `applied twice` line.
- The same scenario's HookFilter line ("CombatState.IterateHookListeners is also patched by ...walk-expand-postfix...: normal game for that part") was never seen because the script aborted before its last `cmd bdopt why`: HookFilter has no runtime check of its own on the walks (`Active` = `ListenerCache.IsCaching`), so its whole back-off is the preflight's line. The design is unchanged; the scenario runs `bdopt why` right after installing the patches too.
- `preflight-check`, `audit-conflict-ab`, `audit-conflict-saves`: their controls were no-op postfixes (`fakeconflict`), which the new rules rightly let stand on `CardModel.Pile` (BeforeReplace) and on the walks (SurroundingOnly), and `Player.Piles` is no back-off any more. The controls keep proving the back-off machinery with shapes that must still back off: `fakeconflict ... boolprefix` (a bool prefix installed after this mod's patches at the default priority, so sorted after a prefix of this mod: refused by BeforeReplace, and by SurroundingOnly as a bool prefix) on `CardModel.get_Pile` (preflight-check, its expected texts now with "(postfixes and prefixes running before this mod's not enough)" and bdopt's "(beyond ...)") and on both walks and `get_Pile` in audit-conflict-ab (ListenerCache's runtime back-off on the combat walk is counted again). audit-conflict-saves keeps the `Player.Piles` postfix (the read-through answers, checked by the checksums against ab-2k-off) and asserts PileIndex's getter back-off on `CardPile.get_Cards` instead (strict: the game's scan reads each pile through it). conflict-check passed as it was.
- `effects-check` (v0.113.1): `cmd card DUAL_WIELD hand` found the hand full after the plays: `emptyhand` first, as handselect-on/off. handselect-on/off passed on both versions.

To run on v0.107.1 and v0.113.1: `foreign-needless-2k-on/off` (compare.py line for line) and `foreign-needless-controls`; `foreign-shapes-2k-on/off`, `foreign-controls` (the byref and Describe lines changed wording only where stated), `guard-checks` (FAULTPROBE three OK), `handselect-on/off`, `limitless-gated-2k-on/off`; `release-checks`, `effects-check`, `preflight-check` (the `Preflight:` line's parts; open the settings page right after the menu shows once: "finished on demand"); then `BDTEST_EXTRA_MODS=RitsuLib` ab-2k on both versions, solo and `parcoop -OnOff` (bdopt why: ListenerCache and PileIndex absent, HookFilter inactive, FastListeners partly; checksums identical; `bdperf listeners` identical in a refresh scope), and a rendered ladder against the same run without RitsuLib (the snapshot and refresh gains back).

**Another mod's later patch inlined away from this mod's replacements (2026-11-17, `InlineGuard.AfterForeignFirstPatch`)**. Found by `foreign-helpers-2k-on/off` (STATE different on both versions): a postfix gated on Squeeze put on `PlayerChoiceContext.PushModel` mid-run counted 3 calls mod on, 83,399 mod off (v0.107.1; v0.113.1 6 and 24,680), and on v0.113.1 the 4-argument `HookPlayerChoiceContext` constructor's postfixes 2,950 and 7,705 against 45,015 and 67,387; the prefix on `PopModel` counted the same on both sides (119,397), and so did the v0.107.1 constructor's. Cause: Harmony compiles a replacement when the method is patched (a standalone check on .NET 9 with the game's 0Harmony: a transpiled caller's replacement, never called before, inlined a 15-byte callee, and a later postfix on that callee saw 0 of 1,000 calls; a 274-byte one saw all; Harmony building the caller again: 1,000), so HookSkip's hooks, compiled at this mod's load, carry the bodies of the small methods they call; MonoMod's no-inlining flag, set when a method is first detoured, only stops code compiled after it. The sizes match the JIT's limit for code without profile data (128 bytes of IL): `PushModel` 32, `InvokeExecutionFinished` 18, the v0.113.1 constructor 87 (it calls `PushModel` too), against `PopModel` 191 and the v0.107.1 constructor 281. In the game without this mod a hook is compiled at its first call, in a run, after the other mods' load-time patches. Every method this mod patches had the same exposure (its replacement holds the original body and the patch methods, compiled at this mod's load), not only the transpiled hooks; other mods' load-time patches were already safe (this mod's patches apply over the frames after every mod's load), the later ones (lazy patching, a run start, an assembly loaded later, a switch) were not.

Fix: HarmonyWatch's finalizer of `UpdateWrapper` sees another mod's first Harmony patch ever on a method (no patch list before this call, so no flag before either); if the method can be inlined (`InlineGuard.Inlinable`: a body of at most 128 bytes or AggressiveInlining, not NoInlining, not a virtual call that can't be devirtualized), every method carrying a patch of this mod whose replacement may have inlined it (`ReplacementReaches`, as RecompileInliners judges: the code after every transpiler and every mod's patch methods, through inlinable callees up to 4 levels, the method matched by module and metadata token so a generic's instantiations count) is built again by Harmony through `HarmonyRebuild.Run`, the same patches in the same order, at once, inside that Patch call (Harmony's lock held, the new detour in place: nothing called after the Patch call returns misses the patch; a call already running finishes in its old code, which Harmony keeps). Logged (`InlineGuard: <owners> patched <method> for the first time, after this mod's patches were compiled: N methods carrying them that may have inlined it compiled again (...)`), counted (`InlineGuard.Refreshed`, an opt-in effect). Chosen over setting the no-inlining flag on every callee before this mod patches: that flag is global, so every small game getter a patched method calls (thousands, `CardModel.Owner` among them) would stop being inlined in the game's own code too, a cost on every frame for a case that is rare; the rebuild costs nothing until another mod patches a small method after this mod's load, then one rebuild per reaching method (~1.5 ms each; `PushModel` is called by most hooks: expect some tens of rebuilds, a few hundred ms, once). Not covered: methods patched by other mods only (their replacements were compiled by them, as without this mod); another mod's detours outside Harmony (MonoMod hooks: not seen); this mod's own patches applied after `RecompileInliners` (unchanged, see the `InlineGuard` row); without HarmonyWatch (Harmony internals not as expected) or `HarmonyRebuild`, nothing is rebuilt (logged). The reads are cached: a method's own callees once, the code after transpilers once per list of transpilers (a transpiler whose output changes with state would leave that cache stale: none of this mod's does).

Proof offline (a console harness, scratch only, loading the Debug DLL with v0.107.1's reference assemblies, the game's logging sent to the console): HarmonyWatch applied, another Harmony id patching after this mod's: a 15-byte helper called by a method this mod transpiles: rebuilt, its postfix sees 1,000 of 1,000 calls; the same helper behind a small wrapper (depth 1): rebuilt, 1,000; called from this mod's prefix: rebuilt, 1,000; a 274-byte helper: nothing rebuilt, 1,000; called only by a method another mod transpiles: nothing rebuilt, 0 (as without this mod). In game: pending (no game session on this code). `foreign-helpers-2k-on/off` on v0.107.1 and v0.113.1: the STATE lines identical (expected: `model-gated-postfix squeeze PlayerChoiceContext.PushModel` 83,399 and 24,680 both sides, v0.113.1's `ctor-source-gated squeeze` 21,401 at t2 and 45,015 at the end, `count-postfix` 67,387), the new expect (the `InlineGuard:` line for `PushModel`) seen and `effectcheck InlineGuard.Refreshed grew start`; then `foreign-helpers-controls`, `foreign-flights-2k-on/off`, `rebuild-check`, `ab-2k-off/on` and `release-checks` (nothing to rebuild there: no `InlineGuard: ... for the first time` line expected without a foreign patch after the load).

### Lazily built instances (2026-11-18)

A back-off scan (one game session per group of the downloaded Workshop mods, `bdopt why` against a control) found
RelicFlashMerge backing off on v0.113.1 next to Unlimited 2.0.0 (a postfix on `SaveManager.ConstructDefault` redirecting the save
folder; Unlimited no longer applies it on v0.111.1) and next to RitsuLib (`ProgressState.FromSerializable`): both are save code,
strict under the relic-flash display premise (`StrictMember`, re-audit A1), and both only run while `SaveManager.Instance` builds
the save manager, at the game's start, long before any flash. Generic rule (`Patches/LazyGuard.cs`, owner-approved 2026-11-18):
a method of a skipped call's set behind a lazily built singleton can't run once the instance exists. Found from the game's code
once per set: a static parameterless getter whose IL is exactly `ldsfld F; brtrue L; call Build; stsfld F; L: ldsfld F; ret` (nops
aside), after any number of early returns `ldsfld X; brfalse M; ldsfld X; ret; M:` on static fields of its type (SaveManager's
`_mockInstance`, which never reach the builder); F a static reference field of the getter's type, `Build` a static parameterless
game method; within the set, the getter alone calls `Build` (call, callvirt, ldftn, generated code) and alone stores or takes the
address of F; the methods behind it are those the set no longer reaches when the closure stops at `Build`
(`MethodFingerprint.GameClosure`, the set's own options). Any other shape, or a method whose code can't be read: no guard. Asked
per flash: F read then (non-null: the guard is shut, its methods are not asked about, not watched for the menu, named in a kept
note `flash_lazy` with their mods). With `PatchSafety.ShapeRules` off: strict as before. v0.111.1: `SaveManager._instance`, 191
methods behind it; v0.113.1: 231. Only RelicFlashMerge uses it (the only set whose strict members reach a lazy singleton today).

Proof: `relicflash-lazy-on` on both versions (a counting postfix on `ConstructDefault`, Unlimited's shape: 3,946 flashes merged,
no back-off, the kept note; control `foreignpatch lazyguard open`, every guard read as not built: the back-off line and no merge),
`relicflash-ctl-on/off` and `relicflash-display-on/off` identical, `effects-check`; with the real mods on v0.113.1 (scan groups
with Unlimited, and with RitsuLib): 104 merges each where there were none, the note naming `ConstructDefault` /
`ProgressState.FromSerializable`.

### Candidate generic rules (2026-11-18, to decide after the top-500 study)

Leads only: nothing implemented. The owner decides which to do once the top 500 Workshop mods (by unique subscribers) are
studied. Source: a back-off scan on v0.113.1 (one session per group of the 59 downloaded mods with code, `bdopt why` against a
control) and two read-only studies of the patches behind each back-off (copies/saves/card data; hook filters). Mods that don't
start on v0.113.1 (AutoAnthony, Act4Heart) or whose run start the script can't drive (ChooseTheAncient, ARAM) were not scanned.

| Lead | Accept | Recovers (scan) | Risk |
|---|---|---|---|
| R1 pure constructor in a type initializer | In `PureInitializer` (`PatchSafetyTolerance.cs`), a `newobj` of a non-generic class with no finalizer, no or a pure initializer, an unpatched constructor (rechecked), base `object`, whose constructor only does `ldarg.0; <pure value>; stfld <own field>` and `object::.ctor()` (pure value: what `PureCall` allows; a runtime comparer such as `StringComparer.Ordinal` matching the key type). SynergyMod's `SynergyRuntime` (`new SynergyHandlerRegistry()`, `new SynergyRunState()`: empty dictionaries and sets) | SynergyMod: 9 HookSkip hooks and CardSortKeys | low |
| R2 deterministic reflection lookups in an initializer | `AccessTools.Method(Type, string, null, null)` / `AccessTools.Property(Type, string)` on an `ldtoken` type and a constant name, BDO doing the same lookup once at judge time (must not throw: an ambiguous name refused), both lookups unpatched. Still refused: `TypeByName`, computed names, `IgnoreCase` | with R1, 11 more SynergyMod hooks (37 backing off today -> 17) | low |
| P pile-type gate on CardPile events | CardFlightLimit, `CardPile.InvokeCardAddFinished`: a void postfix whose IL starts `ldarg __instance; call CardPile::get_Type; ldc.i4 K; bne.un -> ret` with nothing before the branch, judged per merged pile (stands when its type isn't K). `Type` get-only, written in the constructor only (`RequireFieldWriters`), the getter fingerprinted and unpatched | Downfall (Hermit's HandChangedPatches) | low |
| K KitLib's transpiled hooks | `Hook.ShouldClearBlock`, `Hook.ShouldAllowFreeTravel` back off "beyond prefixes and postfixes around the transpiled body" although KitLib's patches are plain bool prefixes rule A accepts: first log `Intolerable`'s reason (KitLib.Core loads in its own AssemblyLoadContext: patch-method resolution suspected) | KitLib: 2 HookSkip hooks | low once the cause is known |
| R3 HookFilter takes type-gated patches | Its four defaults SynergyMod patches (`ModifyDamageAdditive`/`Multiplicative`, `TryModifyEnergyCostInCombatLate`, `TryModifyKeywordsInCombat`): HookSkip's gates as overriders in the override mask, the gates in the mirror's cache key; needs R1 + R2 | SynergyMod: 4 mirrors | low to medium (the mirror's cache) |
| L per-content reached set for Limitless copies | LimitlessCloneMemo/LimitlessHookSkip: a memo hit or a drop skips only what that inner card reaches (the spine FromSerializable -> enchant -> ModifyCard -> clone; the upgrade group only when `CurrentUpgradeLevel > 0`; `CardCmd.Enchant` only for a non-storage card; the closure of its own enchantment type's overrides), on fingerprinted code; HookSkip checks each instance's saved inner cards | DanmakuSpire (`CardCmd.Enchant`), Downfall (`get_MaxUpgradeLevel`, `NCard.FindOnTable`) for most contents | low to medium, the most code |
| G3 type gates on the keyword getter | SnapshotCardReuse, `CardModel.get_Keywords`: `AnalyseGates` on `__instance` (cards of a gate type read the game's way, their piles never trusted) | SynergyMod (KinglyPunch only); Zone blocks the same part next to it, few players gain | medium |
| C copy prologue before a type gate | `ldarg.k; stloc.0` before `ldarg.0; isinst` (SynergyMod's 6 patches); kept strict on the owner's earlier choice | 7 SynergyMod hooks with the 5 `ModifyCardPlayCount` postfixes still refused | to judge |
| M initializer already run | an initializer the mod's own init method provably runs on every path (SynergyMod's `MainFile`): path analysis of another mod's code | 1 hook | medium, not recommended |

Back-offs judged justified (keep): RitsuLib's clone, save and card data patches (it copies its registry state into clones and
exports it), Free Loadout (`UpgradeInternal` replaced, `MutableClone` rewrites the source card, `ToSerializable` may write a file;
its `SavedProperties.Serialize` strip-and-restore is needless in fact but needs effect analysis), PengoTarot's
`Hook.ModifyEnergyCostInCombat` (a replacing prefix with its own walk), Downfall's damage bridge transpiler, Master of Mankind's
saved fields on every card, SynergyMod's `get_Tags` and PengoTarot's `HasStarCostX` (state-dependent). RitsuLib's ExtraScopes
"hidden IL hooks" (its importer postfix only restores return-type modifiers) is needless in fact but not judgeable as a rule.

#### Top-500 study (2026-11-18)

Coverage: the 500 Workshop items with the most unique subscribers (#500 about 7,400). 34 have no code, 24 code without Harmony
(they patch nothing), 443 patch with Harmony. Each of those ran in a scan session (groups of about 8, then alone with its
dependencies on the other version when its group failed), `bdopt why` read against a control: 413 to the session's end, 13 only up to
the run start (their combat doesn't start on either version), 16 never (they freeze or stall on both versions, mostly mods that
change the run start: ARAM Mayhem, More Players), 1 with a dependency not published (Flagellant, DD2ModConfig). Each back-off
studied read-only from the mod's IL. About 150 of those mods ship Debug builds. Nothing implemented: the owner picks.

Refused: any call, ldsfld (but G), ldlen/ldelem/ldind/castclass/unbox, stores outside locals, a branch on an unknown value. Lead K resolved: KitLib attaches `SwallowIncompatibleApiException`, a finalizer returning null for MissingMethod, MissingField and
TypeLoad exceptions, to every method it patches: it can swallow, so the back-off is justified (KitLib.cs:154-190).

Tooling (no Release effect): CombatStartTimer's postfixes (Bench/CombatStartTimer.cs:46-47) make HookSkip's OwnPatchesOnly false in
test builds (HookSkip.cs:874), so BeforeCombatStart and AfterRoomEntered take SurroundingOnly there and scans report back-offs a
Release build doesn't have (NinjaSlayer, Sinclair, Chiikawa). Fixed the same day: `ReadOnlyProbePostfixAttribute`
(PatchSafetyTolerance.cs) on CombatStartTimer's and PerfProbe's postfixes (they only read the clock, `__result` by value), which
`HookSkip.OwnPatchesOnly` doesn't count, so a test build takes TranspiledOnly there as a Release build does. Its fallback control
moved to a synthetic no-op postfix under this mod's own Harmony id (`foreignpatch own-postfix`, foreign-controls). Proven on both
versions: NinjaSlayer and Sinclair's `Hook.BeforeCombatStart` kept (backed off before), CombatStartTimer still records the
combat start, foreign-controls' expects all seen, foreign-shapes-2k off/on IDENTICAL (84 lines), 0 problems.

Justified (keep): save round trips writing mod state into saved data (Succubus, MomoLib, YUICore, YUISpireExpansion, Koishi,
Multiplayer Expanded), state-dependent getters (Succubus, YUISpireExpansion, Koishi), MomoLib's strip-and-restore around Serialize,
YUICore's listener-walk postfix replaying relics, LibraryOfRuinaLib's subscriber lists BDO doesn't watch, Koishi's replacing damage
prefix and relic seal filter, Flaw's per-call preview queue on `NCombatCardPile.AddCard`, SHADOWVERSE's `AddKeyword` (Exhaust),
GensokyoSpire's `CardPile.get_Cards` (a fused card's child swapped in), STS2YxpPak's `NodePool.Free` finalizer (state, swallows),
Deck Tracker's `RemoveKeyword` event, JanusSpire2's `get_AllCards` filter, Card Upgrader's initializer building a game model,
async `MoveNext` insertions (Typhon, Koishi EcoLib, HakureiReimu), Mods depending on RitsuLib gain nothing from the data rules
(RitsuLib's own patches on those methods stand).

#### Re-verification of the leads (2026-11-18)

Four independent read-only verifications (adversarial: re-derive the gain from the logs and IL, look for a shape the rule would
accept that can see a difference). The owner's criterion (same day): every low-risk generic rule that helps is worth doing,
whatever the audience of the mods it helps today. Their corrections to the study are folded in; "with changes" means the rule as
first written was unsound or incomplete there.

| Lead | Verdict | Gain verified | Changes needed / implementation |
|---|---|---|---|
| F1 | recommend with changes | 84 HookSkip hooks per mod (not 88-90) for YUICore and EnderLilies, 2 Chiikawa, 1 STS2YxpPak; the walk itself stays the game's next to RitsuLib/YUICore (what returns is the skipped no-op calls) | "every ret follows ldarg __exception" is unsound (`return c ? null : __exception` merges into one ret): the ret and the store feeding it are no branch target, every store to the returned local is `ldarg __exception` right before it, no ldloca, no starg/ldarga of `__exception`, no exception clauses; GetPatchInfo on the finalizer method at each recheck; factories and HarmonyArgument refused. In `Intolerable` before the kind switch (TranspiledOnly + finalizer), cached in `_shapes`, ~60 lines. foreign-controls' `noop-finalizer` flips to kept: replace that control with a swallowing finalizer. On async hooks the finalizer only sees the stub's synchronous exceptions (MoveNext catches the rest into the Task) |
| E | recommend with changes | BetterExperience 9 of 22 hooks alone, 18 with the initializer extensions; BetterCharacters 9 / 14 of 15; YutaMod 1 (with `or`); the base of C, BL, FW, TG on IdleButtonInput | Model `or` with a nonzero constant as nonzero, not 1; a passthrough exit only for the parameter named `__result`; never-written locals unknown; value-type and open-generic gates refused; the name test as one composite instruction. Not reusable as is in F1 (F1 holds on every path); they share the decoder. Replaces AnalyseGates' loop (`EvaluatePath`), ~200 lines; Bench accept cases emitted with Reflection.Emit (Debug IL) |
| I | recommend with a change | Acheron, Kafka: 6 HookSkip parts | Counter-example: a call into another assembly's static whose initializer is impure (CompiledWith follows same-assembly callees only), or a depth-3 callee never scanned: stop counting a call or newobj only when the callee's own body is in CompiledWith |
| R1, R2, T, DEC, PropertySetter, Type.GetMethod, R1g | recommend (after E) | SynergyMod (R1+R2: 20 hooks), BetterExperience / BetterCharacters (with E), FGO Core 3+3, Rixian's MSPain 2, StS2Aris 1+1 | R2: AccessTools throws on an ambiguous name, so repeating the lookup at judge time is exact; both lookups unpatched. T only after a lookup R2 found. DEC: scale <= 28. R1g: SpireField / NotNullSpireField constructors only, fingerprinted per BaseLib version, CopyOnClone refused. FieldRefAccess medium-low (it emits a DynamicMethod) |
