| G | only as changed | ZZZLib 2 | Unsound as proposed: ZZZLib's flag is set from BaseLib's config at run time, so its initializer's constant isn't its value; sound only as a fork evaluating both branches |
| H | only as changed | Koishi 1 | Unsound as proposed: on a NullRunState the pending filter is still set when foreign postfixes run; needs a Priority.First postfix of ours clearing it with foreign postfixes sorted after, or call-free postfixes |
| M | no | | |
| X | recommend with changes | Card Upgrader (cost, keywords, ShouldPlay, cost colour), Night Must Stay, erowatcher, Chimera, NueMod; with Priority.Last also Manosaba, MEP Sakuraba Ema, Hades Ancients. Not Pokemon, YiSang, Yuki Makoto, BetterExperience, YutaMod, RelicCombo (HookFilter off as a whole next to RitsuLib, LibraryOfRuinaLib or a walk patcher) | The proposed fallback (return true when a listener throws) is unsafe: the game's hook reruns every listener before the thrower (a throw-once listener returns a value where vanilla threw: a reproducible desync). Instead rethrow from the mirror prefix (`throw;` when ThrownByListener), PatchGuard skipping that exception (ReferenceEquals), `_passOn` as the marker; BeforeReplace tolerance; ~50 lines; bench flips `cost-postfix-squeeze` and `noop-postfix` on ModifyKeywordsInCombat to kept, a new "listener throws once" test |

HookFilter mirrors, ReplayStateSkip, JoinMismatchNote |

Two caps on the data parts: all but RelicStateReuse need Limitless; RitsuLib alone turns them all off.

#### Rules implemented from the top-500 study (2026-11-19)

Every rule the re-verification recommended, as changed there, on branch `rules-merge` (four implementers in clones, merged,
then the review below). "Mods" names the ones whose IL the study showed in that shape (static, not yet a real-mod scan). Most
proofs are synthetic shapes emitted in the bench (`foreignpatch gates selftest`, Bench/ForeignShapes*.cs, many with
Reflection.Emit for Release or Debug IL) plus `foreign-*` scenarios with their on/off A/B; answers that depend on other patches
are rechecked per HarmonyWatch epoch and at most every 2 s, and each accepted patch's own method must be unpatched.

| Rule (commits) | Accepts | Why it is exact | Still backs off | Controls; mods |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F1 (c174d98) | On TranspiledOnly hooks (HookSkip's `Hook` methods), another mod's finalizer handing Harmony the exception it got: void, or every `ret` returning `__exception` directly or through a local whose only stores are `ldarg __exception; stloc`, stored on every path, no store or ret a branch target | Harmony rethrows the same object, our guard finalizer having run first: the exception passes on as without that mod | returning null or another value (KitLib), the merged ret `c ? null : __exception`, an unwritten local, `ldloca`/`starg`/`ldarga`, exception clauses, factories, HarmonyArgument, by-ref `__exception`, a patched finalizer, finalizers under any other tolerance. On async hooks it only sees the stub's synchronous exceptions | selftest 3 accept / 9 controls; foreign-controls (noop-finalizer kept; a swallowing and a merged-ret finalizer back off on BeforeSideTurnStart / AfterPlayerTurnStart); foreign-gates-1k on/off. YUICore, EnderLilies (84 hooks each), Chiikawa 2, STS2YxpPak 1 |
| E (0e865a0) | AnalyseGates' fixed shape replaced by `EvaluatePath`: the path a listener of none of the gate types takes, each value a constant, null, the listener or unknown through stack and locals. Debug IL (tests and returns through locals, `ceq` negations, bool locals set as constants, copy prologues `ldarg; stloc`, non-short-circuit `or`), passthrough postfixes returning their `__result`; NullSkipped postfixes too | on that path only nop, `ldarg` by value, `isinst`, locals, constants, compares, `or`/`and`, `dup`/`pop`, branches on known values and the name test (one composite) run, then the no-op exit: nothing is read or written | any call (but G, FW), field, store outside locals, address, loop, branch on an unknown; `or` with a nonzero constant is nonzero, not 1; an unwritten local is unknown; passthrough only for a parameter named `__result`; value-type and open generic gates. TranspilerJudge's GatesOn keeps its strict matcher (other exits) | selftest 7 / 11, NullSkipped 2 / 1; foreign-gates-1k five Debug-IL shapes + a count-first control. BetterExperience 9 of 22 hooks (18 with the initializer extensions), BetterCharacters 9 (14 of 15), YutaMod 1, Into The Spireverse |
| G (f0b68e6, cf6d0e1) | A call to a trivial static getter (`ldsfld F; ret` or its Debug form; no parameter, not generic) gives a forkable unknown; a branch on it explores both sides (at most 16 paths, no loop); every path must reach a no-op exit | whatever the flag's value at run time (ZZZLib sets it from BaseLib's config), the patch does nothing for other listeners. The getter is a path callee and its class's and the field's class's initializer reliances are asked at each answer | a getter reading two fields, an impure initializer, one side acting for every listener | selftest 1 / 3 (+ the reliance case of the review); foreign-gates-1k (AfterSideTurnStart kept, the ungated twin on BeforeSideTurnStart backs off). ZZZLib 2 |
| I (883ba1f) | InitializerRefusal no longer counts a static call or `newobj` whose callee's body is itself in the scanned code (same assembly as the patch, depth 3) | CoreCLR runs a beforefieldinit initializer at the first access to a static field, not at a call or a construction; the callee's own static accesses are scanned | static field accesses, callees of another assembly or past depth 3, `ldftn` targets; the patch's own class keeps its rule | selftest 2 / 2 (the callee reads the impure class's field; a call into another assembly's impure class). Acheron, Kafka (6 HookSkip parts) |
| Initializer extensions (450b46f, 5d8756f) | `PureInitializer` reads the straight-line initializer on a symbolic stack. R2: `AccessTools.Method(typeof(X), "n", null, null)`, `AccessTools.Property`/`PropertySetter`, `typeof(X).GetMethod("n"[, constant flags][, new[] { typeof(..) }])`, repeated at judge time. #### Review of 2026-11-19

Two adversarial reviewers read `rules-merge` (one the analysers in PatchSafety, LazyGuard, BaseLibTypes, OwnNormalizedIl; one
the parts consuming them). Found sound as written: F1, E, G, FW, I, the initializer extensions, X's rethrow and prefix rules,
Priority.Last, R3, AS, reorders, H, both TGs, C0, NG, null-fill (its non-null half), P, RO, the latch consumer, rule B's
consumers, ReplayStateSkip, JoinMismatchNote, L's worker threading and stamp clearing. Findings and fixes (all on
`rules-merge`, built Debug and Release, `check_scenarios` clean):

- Latch: a `stsfld F` that is a branch target (`F = a || b`) passed the writer check, so F could be set false again: such a
  store is no latch store (e534729; control `latch-or-postfix`).
- PostfixesBefore: Harmony runs void postfixes before passthrough ones whatever their sort, so a passthrough postfix "sorted
  before" ours ran after it: the order is computed Harmony's way (61e58bb; control `passthrough-postfix`).
- `_pathCallees` was keyed by patch method only, each analysis overwriting the others': keyed per analysis like `_gates`; rule
  G's getter initializers judged pure only, their reliances asked at each answer (cf6d0e1).
- `_tolerant` answers had no epoch check; NG's guard shapes and B's twin shapes cached `InitializerFree` (whose reliance part
  changes with patches) for good: keyed on the epoch, caches hold the pure part, reliances asked live (57122ed).
- Rule B: the twin lists, read by the closures worker and the main thread, were a torn-readable nullable tuple: one volatile
  reference (5b11016); a member of another class with the owner's simple name matched BaseLib's fingerprint: refused (ef14c67,
  control b3a).
- R1 refused a self-constructing singleton (`static readonly S Instance = new S()`): accepted (5d8756f). Left as they are (rated
  lesser): the hidden-hook check done by some analysers only, LatchedShut's `GetValue` able to run an initializer (harmless at
  RelicFlashMerge), the repeated lookup called before its own unpatched check.
- C1: L's patched-method snapshot and per-closure answers were kept a whole stamp, pinning a PatchSafety answer cached just
  before a patch: 2 s at most (a9e3291). C5: the chain answer kept 1 s whatever the patch set: keyed on the stamp too (50f2a76).
- C2: closures name generic definitions while Harmony patches closed instantiations, so a patch on `CardCmd.Enchant<T>` was
  invisible: each patched instantiation is asked (6e124b4; controls g1, g2).
- C3: an allowed BaseLib/Limitless patch's callees counted only in its entry's group and not through its own helpers: a closure
  reaching a chain method now adds that method's allowed patches' callees, read through the patch's assembly (25d89c8).
- C4: null-fill ran the foreign getter for every card at scope open where the game reads it only on cost ties, and only the
  non-null path was proven a no-op: the fill must pass RO (9fac495; control `nullfill-impure-postfix`). No premise added.
- C6: rule A's gates were tested against the content's card type inside closures, where game code calls those methods on other
  cards: gates only on the chain's own entries (3775b65; control g3, 3eef86f comment).
- C7: `ContentPatched` called SaveUtil.CardOrDeprecated (a spine method another mod may patch) before any check, possibly off
  the main thread: the spine is asked first; the worker's walk stops no longer record back-offs (cf9b27e; control c7).
- C8: LimitlessHookSkip's type-level answer left the second-enchantment group out (fa0dea8; control c8). C9: CardFlightLimit read
  `pile.Type` through the patchable getter: the backing field while `get_Type` is the pinned one and unpatched (cec7b6c).

Gain lost by these fixes, all on L (contents next to Limitless): C6, a type-gated patch on `CardModel.AddKeyword` (CardAscension's
shape) keeps Goopy, RoyallyApproved, Steady and TezcatarasEmber contents from the memo and the drops. C3, top-500 mods patching a
newly counted callee: shadowverse, shadowversebydmod, GuidasExpand (`CardModel.get_Type`: every content); SaibaMomoi,
MultiplayerExpanded (`DynamicVarSet.InitializeWithOwner`: enchanted or upgraded contents); CardFusion (`AbstractModel.get_Id`:
enchanted contents); ManosabaLin (`get_Enchantment`: enchanted contents, hence no Manosaba claim for L); cardupgrader
(`EnchantmentModel.get_Amount`). C8: next to a `CardCmd.Enchant` patch (DanmakuSpire) the type-level drops become per instance.
C2 and C9 lost nothing known.

Owner's decisions (2026-11-19):
- L keeps no per-game-version data: closures are computed live at each launch; correctness stays guarded by the per-override
  fingerprints that predate L.
- Adroit, TezcatarasEmber and SoulsPower contents stay vanilla next to BaseLib + Limitless rather than allowing those two mods'
  own patches by fingerprint (per-mod-version upkeep refused).
- RitsuLib's hidden IL hooks: no premise; a measurement and an effect check later.
- The cost-colour item waits for a trace.

#### Review of 2026-11-19, later rounds (2026-11-20)

**Second review (four reviewers: the analysers, the parts consuming them, the delta of the fixes above, v1.1.0..main; fixes on branch `fix2-bdo-vanillaref`, e9a5d74..f85dbf8).** No desync, crash or unsound acceptance in main's production code; 16 of the 22 first fixes complete, the stack-overflow claim of the first round confirmed. Found and fixed:

| Item | Found | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| D1 (e9a5d74) | Rule B matched a twin's members by simple name: a postfix of class `MyCopy` calling another class named `SavedSpireFieldPatch` passed while rule B judged `MyCopy`'s own (empty) list and the code run read the other one: a field there would be dropped from the reserialized save (desync-reproducible, if ever) | `JudgeTwin` requires the postfix to use that very `GetFieldsForModel` and that method to read that very list (module, token, declaring type). Control: `foreignpatch lim-twin ... mycopy` (a Debug override stands in for the IL no C# compiler emits): backs off, LimitlessCloneMemo idle. No gain lost |
| D2 | A patch stamp could pin an answer cached just before Harmony stored a new patch list (about 4 s stale) | the 2-s bounds of C1, and B-2 below for LimitlessHookSkip |
| D3 (22d4e98) | Game closures name generic definitions, Harmony patches closed instantiations: a patch on `Foo<Bar>` was invisible to a set holding `Foo<T>` (CodeSet, CardFlightLimit's and RelicFlashMerge's sets) | `PatchSafety.PatchedInstantiations` (same module and token from `GetAllPatchedMethods`, per epoch, at most 2 s; Harmony unable to tell: patched). No set holds a patched generic member today: no gain lost, no in-game control |
| D4, B-5 (f6c8c0f) | C4 makes null-fill refuse CardFusion's real postfix (its fill calls `FusedCard.ChildToParentMap.TryGetValue` and another card's getter): the rule's motivating gain is gone, CardSortKeys backs off next to CardFusion; comments and DESIGN still said accepted | docs and comments (null-fill row above). No premise added |
| D5 (2e7556c) | C9 moved `get_Type`'s fingerprint out of rule P's try: a changed getter would have turned CardFlightLimit off entirely | the fingerprint is required inside the try again: only rule P and the field read are lost, the pile's type then comes from the getter (while unpatched) |
| D6 (22d4e98) | `GateRefusal` named "not provably gated" for a verdict from TypeGates' `__result` fallback key | follows that key and names the patched callee (log text only) |
| M1, M2 (22d4e98) | `LazyGuard` trusted the lazy getter's IL from metadata whatever patched it (a future lazy getter outside the strict namespaces with a foreign `__result = Build(); return false;` prefix would have been accepted; `SaveManager.get_Instance` is itself strict today); the strict loop read each guard per member | `Shut` is false while another mod patches the getter (hidden owners included); one guard read per flash. No control distinguishes the first in game today |
| M3, M4 (f85dbf8) | DESIGN still said "each mod with its count"; the test tooling's async probe postfix threw in `ContinueWith` on a null task | text; Debug only: returns on a null task |

The lesser items stay documented, not fixed: the hidden-hook check done by some analysers only, `LatchedShut`'s `GetValue` able to run an initializer (harmless at RelicFlashMerge), the repeated lookup called before its own unpatched check (fixed 2026-11-20, with D2's window: "Safe leads of 2026-11-20").

**Full audit (analysers, consumers, tooling; clone `bdo-vanillaref`, branch `fix3-bdo-vanillaref`, bea2fb6..3da03d6, then 6e25dc3).** Nothing result-changing, no crash or hang found in the analysers; one pre-existing gap in the consumers (B-1). Fixes:

| Item | Found | Fix; gain lost |
|---|---|---|
| A-L1 (b1d78b8) | `PatchedInstantiations` built its map in one try: one unreadable entry made it null (every generic member counted as patched, CardFlightLimit's and RelicFlashMerge's sets idle) with nothing recorded | `PatchedByTokenNow` reads each entry on its own; an entry that can't be a generic instantiation is left out; a null map records a note ("Harmony couldn't list the methods other mods patch..."). None lost |
| A-L2 (b1d78b8) | the preflight's strict checks left out patched instantiations, as the display lists did | they ask `IsPatchedByOthersWithInstances` like the runtime. Control `foreignpatch limitless cantlist on|off` (Debug `CantListForTest`): the two notes logged, a storage Sharp content not kept, kept again once off |
| A-L3 (f6c8c0f) | `PatchSafetyValueGate` said the merged pile event "still runs at the same first moment": it runs at CardFlightLimit's deferred flush | comment |
| B-2 (55814d0) | A MultiEnchantment verdict made just after a Harmony change stayed in FastListeners' per-filter cache until the next stamp | `LimitlessHookSkip`'s stamp also changes every 2 s for 8 s after each patch stamp change (stale at most ~5 s). No in-game control (a timing window) |
| B-3 (b1d78b8) | LimitlessCloneMemo's own patched-methods map: one unreadable entry made it null silently | stays null (no entry can be left out) but records a note under LimitlessCloneMemo and LimitlessHookSkip, both idle then |
| B-4 (3da03d6) | `ClosureWalker`'s comment said delegate invokes were listed; `CalleeGraph` dropped them | kept in `Delegates`; judging them in the overrides' closures isn't cheap (measured: card events with no subscriber on a card being built, the logger, Sentry, `CalculatedVar` functions, and on combat paths an inner card never takes ModHelper's subscriptions): a lead, the comment says so; the chain's own callees' delegates are logged at the worker's start |
| B-1 (3da03d6, final 6e25dc3) | Rule L judged the chain methods' patches and the closures, never what the chain's own code calls (`AfterCloned`'s setters, `ModelDb.GetById`, `DeepCloneFields`' `GetKeywordsWithSources`, `get_DynamicVars`, `get_EnergyCost`, `Enchantment.get_Amount`...): a stateful foreign postfix there would run fewer times on a memo hit or a drop (desync-random, implausible) | see below |

**B-1, what was done and the owner's decision.** A whole strict closure of the chain was measured (scratchpad `chainclosure/`, refs v0.111.1 and v0.113.1): about 1,800 to 2,200 methods a group with the game's dispatch (a card's `CanonicalVars` dispatches to every game card's), still 37 to 151 without any dispatch on models, the spine's one reaching `Hook.ModifyKeywordsInCombat` and `CombatState.IterateHookListeners` (RitsuLib patches the latter) through a combat branch an inner card never takes: each a back-off, too costly for a risk rated implausible. Done instead: the direct game callees of every chain entry that has a group (spine, enchantment and upgrade groups; the chain's code is pinned in `GameChain`, `CardCmd.Enchant`'s read live) are judged strictly with that entry's group, about 20 to 60 methods a group, one level into the game as the allowed patches' callees; a virtual call adds the game's implementations (dispatch filter on `GameCallees` and `CalleeGraph`) except the overrides on the way, judged per content type. The owner's decision: the second-enchant way (an enchanted inner card other than exactly `EnchantmentStorageCard`, the only one Limitless's pinned `set_Enchantment` prefix sends to `CardCmd.Enchant`, as `Enchant<MultiEnchantment>`) is not judged: `KnownTypes` never keeps such a content and `ContentPatched` counts it patched, both before any check, no back-off recorded (`LimitlessHookSkip`'s type-level answer reacts once such an inner card is met: none in Limitless 1.1.7 to 1.2.1, whose inner list writers are guarded). The `EnchantmentModel.CanEnchant` chain entry and Limitless's `Patch_CanEnchant:Transpiler` fingerprint `1B24CFB2B8A10CE5` were removed (no fingerprint of another mod's code needing per-version upkeep). The overrides' closures were checked offline on both refs: none reaches `CardCmd.Enchant` directly, so that entry stays as `Reach.None` for the closures, with Limitless's `Patch_Enchant` fingerprint `46E29C1A39F295FA`. Controls (foreign-limitless-controls): s1 (a count postfix on `CardModel.set_HasBeenRemovedFromState`, the spine: no content kept, LimitlessCloneMemo and LimitlessHookSkip idle through a combat start), s2 (on `CardEnergyCost.SetCustomBaseCost`, the upgrade group only: level 1 not kept, level 0 kept), h1, k1 (a count postfix on `CanEnchant`: storage Sharp kept, no new back-off), k2 (a Clumsy+Sharp content: not kept, counted patched, no new back-off), c8 (the type-level answer unchanged by a `CardCmd.Enchant` patch); g1 removed, C2 stays covered by g2.

Gain lost by the final B-1 (a regex scan of `decompiled/mods` for patches on the newly judged callees, 24 mods found): 15 were already idle without B-1 and the 6 second-enchant-only ones lose nothing now. Aemeath, ZoneTheSpire (`CardModel.GetKeywordsWithSources`) and MultiplayerExpanded (`DynamicVarSet.InitializeWithOwner`) now idle LimitlessCloneMemo and LimitlessHookSkip's drops next to Limitless: harmless in fact (their patches don't change what an inner card's build saves), but no existing rule proves it. Lead recorded: an RO-L extension (read-only shapes on the chain's callees) would recover Aemeath only, and isn't low-risk. Known limit: the judged frontier is at depth 1 (what the chain's direct callees call isn't judged, as for the allowed patches' callees). The spine mods that already cost contents next to Limitless and now idle the memo: CardFusion, cardupgrader, HonkaiFlameChasers, YUISpireExpansion, scbsmod, SaibaMomoi, sts2decktracker, AutoAnthony.

**Test tooling audit.** (1) `mutations.ps1` ran no mutation from 654b6c9 to a61d496: the build loop reused `$out` (the game copy's version folder) for each mutated DLL's path, so later `Join-Path $out` pointed inside the last mutation's copy, no log was found, `parlib.ps1`'s `Select-String` on the missing log threw and the script exited 0; the batteries of 2026-11-18 and 2026-11-19 had counted it as passed. The loop variable is renamed. (2) A session whose game exits before creating its log aborted `Invoke-Sessions` with the jobs never started keeping their old logs, `par.ps1` then compared stale logs as IDENTICAL, and unpaired scenarios were never judged: `Test-Path` guard, results count equals jobs count, errors stop the script with exit 1. (3) `battery.ps1` passed a step on "exit 0 and no problems" without the runner's closing line, and a step's timeout killed every game under its copy, other parallel steps' included: the closing line is required, the kill scoped. (4) `coop4` and `parcoop` compare player-side turn starts only when synced (an unsynced block isn't a state difference); the relic sweep's obtain waits for the obtain; triage's pace-checkpoint and limitless-rng labels narrowed (a state divergence, a found-wrong or a CardSortKeys shuffle desync no longer gets the false-alarm label). After the fix `mutations` ran 45/45 on both versions once the reference's one expected vanilla line was recalibrated (the reference's own back-offs pinned and not held against each mutation, 066d69d).

In-game proof and real-mod scan: the real-mod rescan of 40 groups (every group of the top-500 study with the downloaded mods loaded together, a build before the review's fixes and the fixed build) against the old scan (the leads' back-off scan of 2026-11-18) recovered 136 (part, method) kinds that backed off before, over 517 session-parts. Real losses of the fixes, as recorded above: first the Limitless memo per content in 2 groups, since B-1 three mods (Aemeath, ZoneTheSpire, MultiplayerExpanded) idle it. Every on/off pair is IDENTICAL on both versions (v0.111.1 and v0.113.1); co-op peers agree. Batteries (41 steps): every failure known and not caused by the code: `outside-2k-off` on v0.113 (the game's own combat start outlasts the session), the co-op mod-off twins too slow (the host's combat start outlasts the network timeout), Pandora's pace checkpoint (fixed in the tooling); mutations 45/45. The final battery of the fixed tree: see Release checks (still running when this was written).

#### Transpilers judged by the code they produce: Unlimited without a fingerprint (2026-11-20)

Clone `bdo-premises`, branch `unlimited-rule` (from `rules-merge` 25bc6dc): ae11033 (rule, Bench, scenarios), 5c72383 (scenario with the real Unlimited). Not pushed.

**Why.** RelicStateReuse allowed "Unlimited: No Player Limit"'s `Sts2Unlimited.PacketSizePatch:Transpile_SerializeDeserialize` by fingerprint (`908CDF014E4F04CF`, its helper `IsVanillaListBitsConstant` `CB9DCCF74112728F`; `UnlimitedCompat`, `_allowedUnlimitedOnly`, `UnlimitedTranspilerMethod`) on `SerializableRelic.Serialize`, `SavedProperties.Serialize` and `NetFullCombatState.RelicState.Serialize`; SnapshotCardReuse allowed it through `RelicStateReuse.UnlimitedTranspilerMethod` on its card serializers (`CardState`, `SerializableCard`, `SerializableEnchantment`, `SavedProperties`, `WritePropertyName`, `WriteModelEntry`, `WriteInt`) and pile serializers (`CombatPileState.Serialize`, `WriteList<CardState>`, `WriteList<CombatPileState>`). The owner's decision: no rule specific to one mod, so no fingerprint of its code to keep up per version. All of that is removed (the fingerprints, the class, the Apply throw "Unlimited's transpiler not understood").

**What Unlimited's transpiler does.** `PacketSizePatch.Apply` patches `Serialize(PacketWriter)` and `Deserialize(PacketReader)` (public, instance) of every type of every loaded assembly. For each `call`/`callvirt` of a method named `WriteList`/`ReadList` declared by `PacketWriter`/`PacketReader`, the instruction just before it, if it is the constant 3 (`ldc.i4.3`, `ldc.i4.s 3`, `ldc.i4 3`), becomes `call PacketSizePatch.GetRequiredBits()` (`RequiredBits(16)` = 5: a pure function of a constant); the generic argument of such a call is recorded (`RewrittenListElementTypes`) and `ListHeaderWidthPatch` prefixes those `WriteList<T>`/`ReadList<T>` instantiations (lobby value types). Elsewhere it returns the same instructions. In the game only `ClientLobbyJoinResponseMessage` and `LobbyBeginRunMessage` write a list on 3 bits; every other `WriteList`/`ReadList` uses the default 32 (`ldc.i4.s 32`). So on the relic, card and pile serializers the transpiler changes nothing: the compiled code is the game's.

**What the parts rely on there.** RelicStateReuse hands a previous `SerializableRelic` again instead of a new `ToSerializable`, which the game's `Serialize` then writes: a patch on `SerializableRelic.Serialize`, `SavedProperties.Serialize` or `RelicState.Serialize` keyed on the object would write the reused object's old data, and a transpiler changing what is written is unknown code there. SnapshotCardReuse keeps the bits the game's `CardState.Serialize` wrote and appends them again; `Compose` builds a pile's bits itself (its type on 32 bits, the list's count, each card's kept bits), mirroring `CombatPileState.Serialize` and `WriteList`; `PatchUpgradeBits` writes the upgrade level as `WriteInt(level, 8)` would: a transpiler changing any of those methods makes kept or composed bits differ from the game's.

**The rejected idea: a constant replaced by a pure session-constant getter.** Exact for RelicStateReuse (nothing mirrored) and for kept card bits (both written by the same patched writer), not for composed pile bits nor upgrade bits (they mirror the 32-bit type, the list count width, the 8-bit level) unless each part knew its constants and the getter's value equalled the constant (the identity anyway), plus a purity proof of the getter (RO interpreter, statics never written, value fixed for the session). No mod needs it (Unlimited changes none of these methods): not adopted.

**The rule (generic, exact): `UnchangedTranspilers`.** On a method a part registers (`UnchangedTranspilers.Register`: RelicStateReuse's three serializers, SnapshotCardReuse's card and pile serializers; generic methods and members of generic types left out, always strict), every other mod's transpiler is let stand, together, when their combined result as Harmony compiled it is the game's code instruction for instruction: `TranspilerJudge.Unchanged` = as many instructions, same opcode and operand each (`Same`: locals by index, members by token, module, declaring type and generic arguments, numbers by value), every branch, `leave` and switch target at the same instruction, the same exception blocks, no label on two instructions (`CheckStructure` with the identity match). Labels no branch reaches may differ. The proof obligation: the replacement Harmony compiles from those transpilers alone has the original's body, so the method behaves as the game's, and whatever a part assumes of it holds. The other kinds of patch on the method (prefixes, postfixes, finalizers, inner patches) are not concerned: still another mod's patch, judged as before (BaseLib's known postfixes by fingerprint).

What Harmony compiled: a capture transpiler of this mod (`UnchangedTranspilers.Capture`, Harmony id `<mod>.UnchangedTranspilers`, `Priority.Last`, hands its input on unchanged) records what it is handed at each rebuild (`TranspilerJudge.Capture`), confirmed when the rebuild ends well (`TranspilerJudge.RebuildEnded`, HarmonyWatch's finalizer on `UpdateWrapper`). It is installed only where another mod's transpiler is: at the part's Apply when it is already there, else the frame after another mod's Patch/Unpatch of a registered method (`HarmonyWatch.CheckPending` calls `AfterForeignRebuild`), or queued by the check itself (`Callable.CallDeferred`), through `HarmonyRebuild.Run` (refused where a rebuild isn't safe: stateful transpilers, patch methods that can't be found again). It never runs another mod's transpiler itself and stays installed (identity) when the other transpilers go. Answers are cached per `HarmonyWatch.Epoch` and `TranspilerJudge.CaptureVersion`; nothing is allowed while HarmonyWatch isn't applied.

What it refuses: a rerun instead of the compiled code (a transpiler with state gives another body when run again, as `PatchProcessor.GetCurrentInstructions` would: only the capture of the last confirmed rebuild is judged); a transpiler sorted after the capture (priority 0 registered later, `[HarmonyAfter]`); no confirmed capture (rebuild failed or under way, HarmonyWatch not applied, capture refused by HarmonyRebuild; not logged while waiting); another transpiler of this mod on the method, or a factory transpiler (never run another mod's code to sort); generic methods (captures are keyed by module and token, shared by every instantiation: not registered); any difference: a constant replaced by a call (pure or not, same value or not), a mutable static, an inserted `nop` or branch, a moved branch target or label, an exception block, a dropped instruction; the same opcode and operand with a branch retargeted (what `SameInstructions` alone lets through), by the structure check.

Implementation: `Patches/UnchangedTranspilers.cs` (`Register`, `Allowed(method)`, `With(others, method)`, `AfterForeignRebuild`, the capture transpiler; logs `UnchangedTranspilers: <Type.Method>: other mods' transpilers (<owners>) leave the game's code unchanged (<n> instructions, the same): let stand` or `... refused (<why>): what relies on it backs off`, once per change; test builds count `Kept` and `Refused`); `TranspilerJudge.Unchanged` and `TryCaptured`; `HarmonyWatch.CheckPending`; RelicStateReuse's `GuardsPass` and preflight watches use `With(_allowedOnSerialize, _serialize)` and `Allowed(...)` on the other two serializers; SnapshotCardReuse's `Blocked` per method with `With(_baseLibWrites, method)`, the two `WriteList<T>` instantiations staying strict as generic methods. `tools/check_versions.py`: Unlimited is no longer pinned. Cost with Unlimited: none expected (the parts keep running); without it nothing is installed; the capture adds no code to the compiled method.

**Offline proof** (scratch tool `unlimproof`: loads `refs/v<version>` (sts2, 0Harmony 2.4.2), Unlimited 2.0.2's `sts2unlimited.dll` and this mod's Debug DLL; runs `Transpile_SerializeDeserialize` on `PatchProcessor.GetOriginalInstructions` of every public instance `Serialize(PacketWriter)` and `Deserialize(PacketReader)` of sts2, which is what Unlimited patches, and judges each result with the mod's own `TranspilerJudge.Unchanged`): v0.113.1, 257 methods, 253 unchanged, 4 changed (`ClientLobbyJoinResponseMessage` and `LobbyBeginRunMessage`, `Serialize` and `Deserialize`: "instruction 9|2 changed (ldc.i4.3 -> call Int32 GetRequiredBits())": the judge sees the real rewrites); v0.111.1, 251 methods, 247 unchanged, the same 4 changed. The seven registered serializers Unlimited patches (`SerializableRelic`, `SavedProperties`, `RelicState`, `CardState`, `SerializableCard`, `SerializableEnchantment`, `CombatPileState`.Serialize) are unchanged, so relied on, on both. The Bench selftest ran offline on both (read again kept; branch retargeted, label moved, exception block, constant changed, getter for the constant, instruction dropped: refused). On v0.111.1 Unlimited's initializer throws before any patch in game (`parlib.ps1`'s known failure): the rule has nothing to judge there.

**Controls** (`Bench/ForeignShapesUnchanged.cs`): `foreignpatch <shape> <Type:Method> [priority N]` with `unchanged-transpiler` (Unlimited's code line for line, kept), `getter-transpiler` (the first 8 or 32 replaced by a pure static getter: refused), `field-transpiler` (a mutable static: refused), `nop-transpiler`, `branch-transpiler` (a `br` to the next instruction: refused), and ForeignPatches' `identity-transpiler` (kept; at priority 0 once the capture is there: refused, sorted after); all keep the game's behaviour (A/B identical); `foreignpatch unchanged selftest` and `report`. Scenarios, not run in game at the time of writing: `foreign-unchanged-controls`, `foreign-unchanged-1k-on/off` (A/B, both copies), `unlimited-unchanged` (`BDTEST_EXTRA_MODS=sts2unlimited` on v0.113.1: the real Unlimited kept on the seven serializers, RelicStateReuse, CardBits and PileBits grow; v0.111.1 ends SKIPPED by `requiregame v0.113`), the `extra-ab` block of the release battery, and the regression `foreign-controls`, `foreign-il-2k-on/off`, `rebuild-check`. Every Bench change invalidates the mod-off baseline keys.

**The owner's decisions (2026-11-20).** No rule specific to one mod (this generic one, whose gain is any mod transpiling every serializer without touching these seven: Unlimited today); Unlimited is removed from the version check (`tools/versions.json`, `check_versions.py`) and from its notifications: no part relies on its code by name any more (it stays a test mod through `BDTEST_EXTRA_MODS`; `tools/fpdiff` still lists `sts2unlimited` among the mods whose constants it loads, harmless since no constant pins it).

#### Safe leads of 2026-11-20 (owner-approved; one measured and dropped)

Clone `bdo-vanillaref`, branch `safe-leads` (from 735ba02). From the leads evaluation of 2026-11-20 and the RitsuLib verdict.

**1. Cost colours next to a walk-wrapping patch: implemented, measured slower, not kept** (branch `costcolors-walk`, 9133308).
With another mod's postfix on `CombatState.IterateHookListeners` (RitsuLib's), ListenerCache's scopes serve with it let stand
but HookFilter uses no list there, so `CardCostHelper.TryModifyEnergyCostWithHooks` / `TryModifyStarCostWithHooks` ran the
game's helper. The lead: in that case call the game's own walk (same call points and count as the helper), filter its output
with `FastListeners.KeptOnly` (memoized over a kept output) and the override mask, and pass a throw from the walk's code on as
the game's loop does (X's rethrow machinery, extended by `PassOnWalk`: the first frame outside the runtime libraries is not this
mod's). It worked and was exact: `foreign-costcolors` (a postfix yielding Spiked Gauntlets twice: 1,040-1,164 answers over the
game's walk, 32-36 x 64 Debug comparisons with the game's helper, 0 mismatches; a walk throwing in the helper's first and second
walk: same exception after the same steps, no postfix, HookFilter not faulted; a postfix on the cost default and a bool prefix
sorted after the mirror backed off), `foreign-needless-2k` identical with it serving, every proof scenario clean on both
versions. But `bdperf costcolors` (v0.113.1, RitsuLib alone, solo Necrobinder, 10 cards in hand, ms per hand refresh, two
sessions each at ~1.9k):

| | display scope (the game's handlers) | dispatch scope, handler not the game's | outside |
|---|---|---|---|
| ~1.9k (3,839 listeners), game's helper | 4.9-5.2 | 16.6-18.8 | 21.8-23.3 |
| ~1.9k, mirror over the game's walk | 5.8-6.0 | 19.4-21.1 | 21.7-23.1 |
| ~10.3k (19,247), game's helper | 33.3-33.5 | 109-110 | 133-135 |
| ~10.3k, mirror over the game's walk | 40-65 | 128-173 | 150-202 |

The evaluation's 17 ms and 109 ms were measured in the dispatch scope with the bench's own handler, which ListenerCache treats as
another mod's (no walk output reused there): the game's hand refresh runs in the game's own handlers and display scopes, where
the walk's output is already reused (4.9 ms, 33 ms). What the cost colors spend is the walk itself (another mod's postfix and
the replay of its output), which the lead must keep (the game's call at the same point and count); the listener calls it skips
are a small share, less than what filtering and memoizing the output costs. Not kept: the runtime branch, its display (the walk
back-off stays whole for HookFilter) and its controls are on `costcolors-walk` only. Kept: the bench `bdperf costcolors
[iterations]` (Debug), which now times the display scope too. A gain there would need the walk calls themselves to go, which is
a premise on another mod's postfix (Walks Tier 2 already reuses its output once per refresh).

**2. PatchSafety's `Repeat`**: the initializer extensions' repeated lookup (`AccessTools.Method`/`Property`/`PropertySetter`,
`Type.GetMethod`) is no longer called when anyone patches it or it carries a hidden IL hook: asked before the call, so another
mod's prefix on AccessTools' lookups (CVC) never runs at this mod's judgement time. The verdict was already refused afterwards
(`PatchedInitializerReliance`).

**3. D2's stale window**: `HarmonyWatch.CheckPending` (the next frame after another mod's Patch or Unpatch, when Harmony has
stored the new list) drops PatchSafety's cached answers for those methods again and moves the epoch once more: an answer another
thread cached between the finalizer and Harmony storing the list (up to ~4 s trusted before) lasts one frame at most.

**4. P5: HookSkip's batches by time**: hooks are patched until 4 ms of a frame are spent (one at least), instead of 8 a frame.
The batches run during the loading frames, before InlineGuard's rebuilds (which wait for `HookSkip.HooksPending`, unchanged) and
the preflight: none reaches the menu, no new pause. Measured rendered, one game at a time, BaseLib+Limitless, Debug with
`--bdstartprof`, 735ba02 vs this branch, two runs each (and a warm-up run of the new build) per version: 19-20 batches -> 47-53,
the same ~255-280 ms of work over ~0.28 s -> ~0.29-0.32 s, still 9.0-9.4 s before the menu; the longest batch 20-29 ms -> 11-20
ms (one hook alone can take 10-16 ms: a batch takes one at least); loading frames over 16.7 ms 6-9 -> 2-3 (the two over 33 ms are
the game's, unchanged).

**Proofs** (both versions, Debug and Release builds 0 warnings 0 errors, `check_scenarios` 0 problem): with item 1 in,
`foreign-costcolors` (above), `foreign-hookfilter`, `foreign-hookfilter-2k-on/off` (54 lines), `foreign-shapes-2k-on/off` (84),
`foreign-needless-2k-on/off` (49, the mirror serving there), `foreign-controls`, `preflight-check`, `effects-check`,
`release-checks`, `walkcode-check`, `conflict-check` no problem, pairs identical; after item 1's revert the same list again (no
problem, pairs identical, `effects-check` all ran but its allowed zeros), and `mutations.ps1` 45/45 on both copies.

**Declined by the owner (2026-11-20): an effect check on hidden IL hooks.** HarmonyWatch treats every method another mod rebuilds after a patch on Harmony's own pipeline (RitsuLib's ImportReference postfixes; CVC, Act 4 Heart elsewhere) as patched by it. A check reading each rebuilt DynamicMethod's emitted IL and member references and comparing them with Harmony's instruction list proved feasible in a spike (RitsuLib's shape unchanged; a renamed call target and a retyped class caught), and would recover ExtraScopes' first-turn filter next to RitsuLib (first turn -4.0 s at 10,300 cards; the filter is lost in 125 scan sessions; ~105 of 454 decompiled mods use RitsuLib). It reads private CoreLib fields (medium-low risk: fails closed if they change, but a bug in the check itself would let a redirected call through), so the owner declined it ("si le risque est moyen non"). The suspicion stays as it is; the premise alone was declined earlier.

#### Card sorts and stacked enchantments next to walk-wrapping mods (2026-11-20)

Branch `trace-proposals` (clone `bdo-coverage`, from 735ba02; 4688e29 code, 4d60df8 scenario expects). Evidence: a CPU-sampling trace of a 10,295-card combat (10,309 in combat, ~19.2k listeners) with RitsuLib 0.6.14 on v0.113.1, sampler attached from outside, plus timers and PerfProbe.

**Finding.** v0.113's `CardModel.CompareTo` reads `EnergyCost.GetResolved()` (v0.111's stops at the upgrade level, so no cost is read there). `StoneCracker.AfterRoomEntered` sorts the draw pile at combat start (`StableShuffle`: `List.Sort` then shuffle; 8 sorts in the session). CardSortKeys already answers the ~650k comparisons from keys and reads each card's cost once, but each read runs `Hook.ModifyEnergyCostInCombat`, i.e. HookSkip's filtered walk: `KeptOnly` over the game's `CombatState.IterateHookListeners`, with RitsuLib's by-value postfix expanding every owner's capabilities (~19,200 entries). HookFilter is off there (the postfix on both walks) and a sort was no ListenerCache scope: O(cards x listeners) per sort. AfterRoomEntered took 167.6 s of a 203 s combat start (v0.111.1 with the same mods: 0.8 s; v0.113.1 without RitsuLib 5.1 s of 9.3 s, the same shape with this mod's own cheaper walks). Time split of that frame: the game's walk this mod mirrors ~53%, RitsuLib's expansion ~27%, KeptOnly/TypeIdOf ~16%, Limitless verdicts ~3.5%; RitsuLib's other code (`get_Type`, `get_Tags`, `GetWithModifiers`, hook dispatcher) is under 1%. A play on both versions: 7-10 s, of which the post-action checksum 5.9-6.6 s: the walk is already reused (SnapshotWalks), but the memo holds every stacked enchantment as a live entry (LimitlessHookSkip backs off next to RitsuLib's clone-chain patches), re-judged per card per hook: 37% of the frame in `LimitlessHookSkip.get_Active` (reached through `PileFilter.Keeps`: NoteMulti, SyncMulti's `Stamp`, MultiDoesNothing), 36% the memo replay itself, 21% the game's hook loop. The idle weight of BetterSpire2's `IsHittable` in the trace is sampler bias (4.9 ms x 4/s measured, 143 fps idle): no action.

**Change 1: sort walk reuse** (`ListenerCache.SortWalks`, part of "Card pile lookup"; kept note `sort_walks`, menu sentence `kept_sortwalks`). `CardSortKeys.StableShuffleInScope` (and the bench's `SortInScope`), once its own scope opened (an answered sort), opens `ListenerCache.EnterSort()`; `ExitSort` in its `finally`. A refresh scope of the game-value kind (`BlockReuse`: nothing kept from an earlier display scope is used in it nor handed on), opened only when nothing of ListenerCache is open on the thread (no display refresh, snapshot, suspension, `RunVanilla`, foreign handler, set-aside context, output being pulled); otherwise the sort runs as before. Two tiers:
- *Exact, no premise*: the unpatched lists served by state version, HookFilter's per-hook lists with them while no other mod patches the walks (`IsCaching`): a sort's cost reads are filtered lists, no walk per read.
- *With another mod's postfix on a walk* (`_combatAugmented`/`_runAugmented`): Walks Tier 2's machinery (`Augmented`, `LazyOutput`, `ReusedOutput`, `CheckedOutput`, KeptOnly's memo) under the owner-approved premise, on a narrower scope: **within one synchronous `List.Sort` CardSortKeys answers, at one state version, the walk's output after other mods' postfixes is the same at every call.** It sits inside CardSortKeys' premise Q (nothing in the sort changes a card's cost) and the game's own demand for a consistent comparer. Shape rules off: the sort scope is exact only.

Nets, every existing one kept: (a) the sort's own check schedule (a sort is a synchronized action, peers check the same walks): the first reuse of each key is a checked one (`CheckedOutput`, until one covered what its caller read), then, since the owner's decision of 2026-11-20 (below), one fresh walk of each reused key at the sort's end (`EndSortWalks`; first built with one in 16 of that key's reuses checked); (b) a state version change ends the reuse for the rest of the sort; (c) CardSortKeys' own check (each sort's first answer, one in 1,024; 16 in test builds) runs the game's `CompareTo` with the sort's walk reuse paused (`ListenerCache.PauseSort`), so the live comparison stays independent of the reuse; (d) a nested game-value refresh ends the reuse. A difference found, or an error of this code in a sort (`SortWalksFailed`): no more reuse in that sort, the part off for the session (`PatchSafety.Fault(ListenerCache.SortWalks)`, logged "in a card sort, ..."), later sorts not scoped; the display part untouched. Residue: a walk output that changed between two checks gave the reads in between the first walk's listeners (the sort is not redone). No co-op join tag (as CardSortKeys). Test counters: `SortScopes`, `SortWalksKept`, `SortWalkReuses`, `SortWalkChecks`, `SortWalkMismatches`; `foreignpatch sortwalks status`, `require sortwalks on|exact|off`.

**Change 2: held-inactive Limitless verdicts** (`LimitlessHookSkip.HeldInactive = !_armed && CombatUnderWay()`; exact, no premise). `Active` answers false at once when applied and held; `PileFilter.Keeps` for a stacked enchantment on the main thread while held is `SyncMultiTo(-1)` then the type's cached verdict `Reacts(_multiTypeId)`: no `NoteMulti`, no `Stamp`, no `MultiDoesNothing`. Exact because a failing condition only clears `_armed` (already clear), arming needs no combat under way, and the evaluation's only effects are lazy caches (`ScanLate`, the 2-s patch recheck) that the next evaluation outside a combat runs before it can arm; with `Active` false, `NoteMulti` notes nothing, `Stamp` is -1 and `MultiDoesNothing` is false. Tested per entry, not once per replay (a replay's caller runs game code between entries): one field read and `CombatManager`'s state. Test builds compare one held answer in 256 with the conditions evaluated (`HeldAnswers`, `HeldMismatches`, 0 expected). Counter `FastListeners.MultiHeldVerdicts`; `foreignpatch require multiheld`.

**Proofs** (Debug, copies StS2-beta-b-0.113.1 and StS2-public-b-0.111.1, `par.ps1`). Test mods only, v0.113.1: `foreign-sortwalks-1k` (sort scopes 9, kept 8, reused 11,079, checked 741, mismatches 0; `bdperf sortkeys` identical orders, 957 cards: the game's sort with the expanding test postfix 3,829 ms, the part 527 ms at 1-in-16 checks), `multiheld-2k`, `foreign-walkreuse-sub-1k` (self-test, 79 cases as expected, 6 new for sorts: the schedule in two sorts; a pause and a version change give the call's own output; a nested refresh and a difference end the reuse; no sort scope inside another), `sortkeys-premise-1k`, `foreign-parts-sort-1k`, `shuffle`, `ab-2k`, `foreign-snapwalks-1k`, `snapcheck-5k-a`/`-b` on/off pairs identical; `clonememo-check`, `sortkeys-wrong`, `sortkeys-conflict-1k-on`, `foreign-keptmemo-ctl` pass; the positive control `foreign-sortwalks-ctl` (an unstable postfix) is caught: the sort's check finds it, the part off and faulted, logged; `sortkeys-violate-1k-on` logs the same 23 errors as before (the violation also reaches HookFilter's and SnapshotVerifier's checks: pre-existing). With the real RitsuLib (`BDTEST_EXTRA_MODS=STS2-RitsuLib`): `sortwalks-real-1k` (kept 8, reused 11,079, checked 741, mismatches 0, held verdicts 128 M; `bdperf sortkeys` identical orders, game 4,441 ms, part 211 ms), `ab-2k`, `shuffle`, `multiheld-2k`, `snapcheck-5k-a` identical on v0.113.1; `ab-2k`, `shuffle`, `multiheld-2k` identical on v0.111.1. Held verdicts: 13,986 (v0.111.1, no RitsuLib) and 420.8 M (RitsuLib), held answers compared with the conditions evaluated, 0 contradicted; `bdperf checksum 2` identical with the whole mod off. Problems listed there are as without the change: RitsuLib's two HarmonyWatch warnings, `clonememo-check` "nothing compared" next to RitsuLib, `bdperf checksum` rows not compared.

**Before / after** at 10,295 deck cards (10,309 in combat), v0.113.1, Debug, `bdperf checks off`, the trace's scenario minus its waits; before = 735ba02 (two runs), after = the branch (two runs):

| | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| RitsuLib: combat start | 194.7 / 196.0 s | 49.4 / 48.5 s |
| RitsuLib: AfterRoomEntered | 161.1 / 162.6 s (six ~9,950-card sorts, ~27 s each) | 22.9 / 22.6 s (a sort 3.5-4.2 s; 2.4-2.9 s without the 1-in-16 checks, diagnostic build only) |
| RitsuLib: first turn | 32.3 / 32.2 s (longest frame 13.7-14.0 s) | 25.4 / 24.8 s (12.6-13.1 s) |
| RitsuLib: a play | 7.1-9.6 s | 3.5-5.4 s |
| RitsuLib: post-action checksum | 6.1-6.4 s | 2.6-2.8 s |
| no RitsuLib: combat start | 10.2 s | 5.0 s |
| no RitsuLib: AfterRoomEntered | 5.9 s | 0.3 s |
| no RitsuLib: a play | 175-209 ms | 181-206 ms |
| v0.111.1, RitsuLib: combat start | 31.7 s | 24.4 s |
| v0.111.1, RitsuLib: a play | 6.8-9.4 s | 3.3-5.3 s |
| v0.111.1, RitsuLib: checksum | 6.3 s | 2.85 s |

(A first "after" run with RitsuLib read 77.8 s while another agent's sessions ran on the main copies; its rerun and the diagnostic run agree on 48-49 s. The v0.111.1 rows come from the held verdicts alone: that version has no sort cost.)

**Net schedule of 2026-11-20 (owner's decision; commit dc1d591, branch `sort-nets`).** The sort's 1-in-16 checked reuses are replaced by the snapshot's schedule: each key's first reuse checked (`CheckedOutput`, until one covered what its caller read), none after it, then one fresh walk of each reused key at the sort's end (`ListenerCache.EndSortWalks`, called by `ExitSort` before the scope closes, so after the game's `StableShuffle` returned: its shuffle only reorders the list it was given, a copy in every game caller, which no walk reads). Each kept output the sort handed to an unchecked reuse (`KeptOutput.InNet`, `_sortReused`) is compared with one call of the game's walk under `RunVanilla` (every other mod's postfix run as the game runs it), as far as it was pulled, element by element and by reference (`CompareWithFresh`, the snapshot net's comparison). A difference, a reused output that threw, or the comparison failing (another mod's code throwing in the fresh walk, an error here): the part off for the session, logged ("in a card sort, ... at the sort's end", the checked reuse's line now says "at its checked reuse"); the sort is not redone (the premise's residue, as before: a walk output that changed during a sort and came back by its end is not seen; the 1-in-16 checks narrowed that window, the end walk instead sees any output still different at the end). An output kept at a state version the sort no longer has (a change ended its reuse) is not compared at the end: its walks read a state that is gone (counted `SortEndSkipped`; none seen in game). Kept unchanged: the schedule per sort (the same walks checked on every peer), a version change ending the reuse, CardSortKeys' live comparison with the reuse paused, a nested game-value refresh ending the reuse, the part's own switch and fault; the KeptOnly memo's own checks (its part, exact) keep their schedule. The end walk is one extra call of the game's walk per reused key and sort (other mods' postfixes run once more), where the reuse already skips calls; the same residue as the reuse's own (no seal: a sort feeds no checksum directly, CardSortKeys' premise Q). Test counters: `SortEndWalks`, `SortEndSkipped`, `SortEndMismatches`; the status line `FOREIGN sortwalks ... checked C, end walks E, end skipped S, mismatches M (at the end F)`, `require on` needs end walks. Self-test (86 cases on both versions): the schedule `kc` + 38 `r` in two sorts with one end walk each, nothing reused nothing walked, a version change's outputs skipped, a nested refresh's reuses compared, the end finding another listener, a shorter walk, a longer one than a complete output, none past a partly pulled one, a throwing fresh walk found, nothing escaping. Controls, deterministic: `walk-sortcheck-postfix` (the sort's second walk without its last listener; `foreign-sortwalks-ctl`: found at the checked reuse, mismatches 1, at the end 0) and `walk-sortend-postfix` (only the end's fresh walks without it; new `foreign-sortwalks-endctl`: found at the sort's end, 1 of 1); the former control's `walk-unstable-postfix` (every third call anywhere) is still caught (a scratch run: at the first checked reuse), but only by chance of the call count now, so the controls moved to the sort-relative shapes.

Proofs (Debug, copies StS2-beta-b-0.113.1 and StS2-public-b-0.111.1). v0.113.1, test mods only: `foreign-walkreuse-sub-1k`, `foreign-sortwalks-1k` (scopes 9, kept 8, reused 11,812, checked 8, end walks 8, mismatches 0; `bdperf sortkeys` identical orders, the game's sort 3,845 ms, the part's 503 ms), `sortkeys-premise-1k`, `foreign-parts-sort-1k`, `shuffle`, `snapcheck-5k-a` on/off identical; both controls caught. With RitsuLib (`BDTEST_EXTRA_MODS=STS2-RitsuLib`): `sortwalks-real-1k` (kept 8, reused 11,812, checked 8, end walks 8, mismatches 0, held verdicts 128.8 M; `bdperf sortkeys` identical orders, game 3,603 ms, part 169 ms, 211 ms before), `sortkeys-premise-1k`, `shuffle`, `snapcheck-5k-a` identical; `foreign-sortwalks-1k-on` and `foreign-parts-sort-1k-on` stop at their positive controls next to RitsuLib (the synthetic postfix's reuse never starts: kept 0, served 0; CardSortKeys never grows), their CHECKSUM lines up to there identical: the same two lines fail with main's build (2f4dbcc) next to RitsuLib, so not this change. v0.111.1 (no answered sort there): `foreign-walkreuse-sub-1k` (self-test 86 cases), `shuffle`, `ab-2k`, `snapcheck-5k-a`, `foreign-snapwalks-1k` identical; with RitsuLib `ab-2k`, `shuffle`, `snapcheck-5k-a` identical, `foreign-walkreuse-sub-1k-on` stops at its `GameHandlerReuses` effect check next to RitsuLib, as main's build does.

Numbers at 10,295 deck cards with RitsuLib, v0.113.1, Debug, `bdperf checks off`, the same scenario and per-sort timer as above, before = 2f4dbcc and after = dc1d591 alternated three times while another agent's battery ran 6 to 14 game sessions on the main copies (absolute times above the quiet runs of the table; compare within a pair): a ~9,950-card sort 4.5-5.3 / 7.2-7.6 / 4.1-4.4 s before, 2.9-3.4 / 2.9-3.5 / 3.0-3.5 s after; AfterRoomEntered 30.3 / 45.5 / 26.0 s before, 19.1 / 19.5 / 19.7 s after; combat start 67.9 / 87.4 / 70.7 s before, 54.5 / 55.0 / 54.0 s after. In the quietest pair (the third), a sort -27%, AfterRoomEntered -24%, the combat start -24%; the quiet runs' diagnostic build without the 1-in-16 checks (2.4-2.9 s a sort, against 3.5-4.2 s with them) gives the same share. Plays and the post-action checksum don't run a sort (unchanged by this, their spread is the load's).

**Out of reach (generic and low-risk).** The checksum floor, ~2.6 s per action at this deck: the replay and the game's hook loop over ~8k stacked enchantments kept in every hook (LimitlessHookSkip backs off on RitsuLib's clone-chain patches, justified in the RitsuLib study) and per-card serialization (SnapshotCardReuse backs off on postfixes reading per-card capability state no tracked write sees). What is left of a sort: the memo replays and the same hook loop (the 1-in-16 fresh walks, ~30%, gone with the net schedule of 2026-11-20). RitsuLib's expansion wherever a fresh walk is required (the first walk of each scope and every net check: ~21% of a hand refresh): reusing outputs across scopes at one state version would need a wider premise (its capability attach/detach is no tracked state change), medium risk, not proposed. HookFilter's per-hook lists on an augmented walk (the expansion is dynamic per-owner state). The first turn's 12-15 s frame (ExtraScopes' first-turn scope not standing next to RitsuLib's hidden IL hooks): a separate lead, not part of this change.

### Re-audit round 3 (2026-11-17, from cf119ae)

- **R3-1, RelicFlashMerge: merges refused out of combat (perf regression of B1).** Out of combat `Creature.GetVfxContainer()` is null (`NCombatRoom.Instance` is null at a rest site) and the game's `target.GetVfxContainer()?.AddChildSafely(NRelicFlashVfx.Create(relic, target))` skips `Create`: the shown flash makes no node, B1's capture saw none and refused every repeat (`SceneRefusals` 1,973 at Bing Bong's campfire clone in the second proof run: thousands of one-shot sounds a frame again). Fix: a flash whose handler provably left no node is a merge that leaves no scene code out (`Capture.LeftNoNode`, read from Godot's own counters, no game or mod code): no node entered the tree while the handler ran, and Godot's orphan node count (`Performance.Monitor.ObjectOrphanNodeCount`: every node alive outside the tree, one waiting for a deferred `AddChildSafely` among them) is the one it began with. A node made and kept for a later add raises the orphan count: not seen, refused as before; a node made and freed in the handler shows nothing. The only miss: a node made while another orphan node is freed in the same handler (a visual difference at most). Counted `RelicFlashMerge.NoNode` (Bench). Tests: `relicflash-display-on/off` (the campfire's creature merges are "no node" ones; then a combat and `bdperf relicflash 50`, a burst of the first relic's flashes in one frame: the node is made, checked and merged with, `NRelicFlashVfx.Create` counted), `relicflash-scene-ctl` (the control moved to that combat burst: out of combat the postfix on `Create` never runs).
- **R3-2, CardSortKeys: post-combat deck sorts refused (perf regression of A3).** A3 judged every card's owner's combat (`_owner.Creature.CombatState` from the fields), a superset of `card.CombatState`. After a combat ends the owner's `Creature.CombatState` still points to it (only `CombatState.RemoveCreature` clears it) while the combat piles are emptied: WarHammer's `AfterCombatVictory` stable-shuffles the deck's upgradable cards and any impure cost listener of the ended combat (Brilliant Scarf, a modded cost hook) refused a sort the game's code answers without the cost hook (`card.CombatState` is null for deck cards). Fix: the owner's combat is judged only for a card that can be in a combat pile or a combat upgrade preview (`InCombat`): `_upgradePreviewType == Combat`, or the card in one of the owner's combat piles read from the fields (`Player.<PlayerCombatState>k__BackingField`, its `_piles` array or the five pile auto-properties as `AllPiles` builds it, each pile's `_cards`; one reference set per owner and sort, built only when a card's combat isn't judged yet). Why it is a superset: `CombatState` is non-null only when `Pile` is a combat pile or the preview is Combat, and `Pile` is the first of `Player.Piles` (`AllPiles`, then the deck) holding the card. Fingerprinted (v0.113.0 and v0.113.1 alike): `Player.Piles`, `Player.PlayerCombatState`, `PlayerCombatState.AllPiles` and its five pile getters, `CardPile.Cards`, `CardModel.UpgradePreviewType`; a change, or another mod's patch on one of them or on `CardModel.Pile` (under the sort-cost premise otherwise), leaves A3's superset (every card's owner's combat). Test: `sortkeys-postcombat` (`bdperf sortkeys deck` after the combat with Brilliant Scarf held: answered, the game's order; in combat the combat piles' sort is left to the game with that listener, the deck's answered).
- **R3-3, CardSortKeys: the A2 schedule's scope (documented, inherent to the premise).** A2's check schedule is "the same on every peer *that answers*": a peer with CardSortKeys off, faulted, refusing (its shape rules or loaded mods differ, `ListenerCache` suspended, another mod's patch on a strict method) runs the game's comparison for that sort. When the sort-cost premise holds (inside one sort no card's cost changes), the answering peers' orders equal the game's, so the two kinds of peers agree. When it is violated (other mods' cost code changing a cost inside a sort), an answering peer's order can differ from a non-answering peer's before the first check sees it: the check catches it only at its scheduled comparisons (the sort's first answer and every `VerifyEvery`-th), and a difference between those is not seen. That is the premise's stated risk (a co-op desync only when it is false), not something the schedule can close; co-op also requires every player on the same mod version and switches (CoopModeTag), which makes a peer refusing while another answers rare (different loaded mods, a fault).
- **R3-4 (nits and tests).** (a) `LoadedCode.Key`: a dynamic assembly that can't list its types made every 2 s sample a new key, so `PatchSafety.TypesNamed` (the name gates) and the override answers were scanned again every ~2 s on the main thread; it now counts as one unreadable assembly (a stable key; the answers given meanwhile are the conservative ones: the candidates table is incomplete, "can't tell"), the key changing when it becomes readable or an assembly loads. (b) RelicFlashMerge's capture: the handlers' `__state` is the capture itself (a token), so a nested handler of the same key whose prefix didn't push one can't end the outer one, and a second finalizer run ends nothing (committed with R3-1). (c) Its `node_added` signal is never connected twice: the connected tree is kept, a failed disconnect leaves it connected and reused (the next `+=` logged "already connected" before). (d) `foreignpatch iljudge selftest` gains accept cases on the cap store (`identity-bridge-cap`) and on the additive store found as the others are (`identity-bridge-additive-store`); `foreign-il-2k-on` expects both. (e) `bdperf sortkeys counters`: every answered sort's first answer was compared with the game's (checks >= sorts), in `sortkeys-premise-1k-on` and `sortkeys-postcombat`.
- **Proof run 2 (cf119ae) triage.** Test bugs: `foreignpatch report` printed the kept notes as the last preflight left them, before the scenario's patches (it runs a preflight first now, as `bdopt` does: `relicflash-display-on`, `foreign-walkreuse-1k-on`, `hover-tips-display-on` only showed theirs in `bdopt why`); `NRelicFlashVfx:Create` is ambiguous (two overloads: `::Create(RelicModel,Creature)` now); `sortkeys-premise-1k-on`, `sortkeys-violate-1k-on` ran their `expect` lines before `requiregame` (required on v0.107.1, where the session is SKIPPED: `requiregame` first now, as `sortkeys-conflict-1k-on`); `wrong-pilebits-1k-on` may also see SnapshotVerifier's checksum recomputation (the fallback's own path when the detection falls inside a checksum's serialization). A mod fix: `PatchSafety.TypesNamed` refused every name gate while one loaded assembly had a type that can't load (`ReflectionTypeLoadException`, `foreign-namegate-1k-on`'s self-test): it now takes that assembly's loadable types, as `LoadedCode.Types` does (a type that couldn't load has no instance, so no listener of that name); any other exception still refuses, and the self-test names the failure.
- **Proof run 2: `foreign-dmgbridge-1k` off/on different in the bench's own counters only** (game CHECKSUM and STATE identical): the bridge's Squeeze calls (5,798,716 off, 5,745,652 on on v0.107.1) and, on v0.113.1, the ungated control's every-call count too. Those counts include the game's damage previews (`DamageVar`, `CalculatedDamageVar`, `OstyDamageVar.UpdateCardPreview`, `PoisonPower.CalculateTotalDamageNextTurn`, `AttackIntent.GetSingleDamage`: every caller of `Hook.ModifyDamage` but `CreatureCmd.Damage` and Thrash's play, both versions), recomputed at the display's pace. The bench now scopes those previews (a counting prefix and finalizer of its own, both sides alike) and compares the bridges' calls outside them only (STATE: `squeeze calls outside previews`, `callfirst calls outside previews`: the actions' damage, where a dropped Squeeze iteration shows as a count and in the damage), the totals as FOREIGN display lines (not compared). If the outside counts match while the totals differ, the explanation holds. The control phase plays one turn (the deck's Doom ends the combat at that turn's end on both sides: the next `waitfor idle` waited 300 s on an ended combat).
- **Proof run 2: `foreign-helpers-2k` off/on different on PushModel's gated postfix (83,399 off, 119,397 on).** The inline fix (b64e896) makes this mod's replacements see every PushModel call (PopModel's count); with the mod off the game's own JIT had compiled some callers with PushModel (32 bytes of IL) inlined before the scenario patched it mid-session, so vanilla misses those calls: vanilla's behaviour for a late patch, not this mod's. The fix stays. The scenario: the late PushModel postfix's count is a FOREIGN display line (not compared), the on side requires it to equal PopModel's (`foreignpatch require pushpop`: every call seen), PopModel's count (not inlined) and the rest stay STATE lines. New pair `foreign-helpers-load-2k-on/off`: the same gated patches installed at the launch (`launchforeign model-gated-pushpop`, before any caller is compiled, the real case of a mod patching at its load): every count a STATE line, on and off must match exactly.
- **Proof run 2: `mutations.ps1` `unrelated-body` lost RelicFlashMerge** (0 merges in that session, some in the reference): how a session's flashes fall into frames is pace. Chosen: a deterministic burst in `effects-check` (`bdperf relicflash 50` in the first fight: 50 flashes of the first relic in one frame, the game's own `Flash()` and handlers; the one effect a bench line drives there), not an allowance at zero, so a mutation that really loses RelicFlashMerge still shows. The dedicated `relicflash-*` scenarios are unchanged in what they require.
- **Open (proof run 2, v0.113.1): `blitz-1k-off` vs `blitz-1k-on` DIFFERENT from the combat start** (CHECKSUM start 8C6467C7 vs 17E1E38C, `cardids` 3D466370 vs 7B80DB14, 945 ids and next 945 on both: the same cards, another order in the piles) though the run state before the fight is identical (relics, deck hashes, run models). Not explained offline: the draw pile's order at the combat start comes from `StableShuffle` (CardSortKeys answers its sort on v0.113) and BlitzRunModel has no cost hook. To rerun with `bdperf checks on` and `cmd bdperf sortkeys` right after the fight on both versions, and, if it repeats, with CardSortKeys off (`bdopt CardSortKeys off`) to bisect.
- **Proof run 2: `audit-coopdeath` on/off different on v0.107.1, a test artifact.** Limitless 1.2.1's per-player combat RNG (`Patch_PerPlayerCombatRng`: `RunRngSet.GetRng`'s prefix picks the slot from the running action's `OwnerId`, an AsyncLocal) and v0.107.1's end of turn (phase two, the enemy turn, the next turn's start) running inside whichever player's `ReadyToBeginEnemyTurnAction` executed last (`CombatManager.SetReadyToBeginEnemyTurn` -> `TaskHelper.RunSafely(AfterAllPlayersReadyToBeginEnemyTurn())`): a network race, so Parrying Shield's random target draws from slot 1 on one side and slot 0 on the other when the last ready player differs (v0.113.1 resumes the turn loop in its own context). `parcoop.ps1`'s on/off comparison now finds, on v0.107.x with Limitless's `per-player combat rng prefix: ACTIVE` line, the owner of the last `ReadyToBeginEnemyTurnAction` before each round's first `STATE floor F round N Enemy` line in both host logs; from the first round where it differs, both roles compare only the lines before that round's enemy turn, and an otherwise identical pair is `PACE (Limitless per-player RNG: last ready player differs from floor F round N)`, not counted as a problem (an earlier difference stays `DIFFERENT`). Checked offline on the proof run's logs: `PACE` from floor 2 round 2, 12 identical lines before it, host and client.
- **Re-audit of b64e896 (InlineGuard.AfterForeignFirstPatch), A1, A2, A3, A5, C1.** (A1, A5) No rebuild inside the other mod's Patch call any more (a whole PatchAll batch, maybe on another thread, Harmony's lock held): the patched method is queued and, at the end of the frame on the main thread (`Flush`, a deferred call), every queued method of the batch is handled at once, each stale candidate rebuilt once. A call made between the other mod's Patch call and that frame's end still runs the inlined body (as before b64e896). A candidate carrying another mod's transpiler is rebuilt only when `HarmonyRebuild.Blocker` lets it (its stateful-transpiler list: such a candidate's reach is read from its original's calls, its rebuild refused). (A2) A stale candidate not rebuilt (refused, failed, no detour table) is surfaced: a warning, a `bdopt why` note under "Other" naming it, the helpers and the other mods (`PatchSafety.Note("InlineGuard", ...)`), and its helpers recorded (`InlineGuard.InlinedUnseen`): HookSkip then keeps no tolerance for any patch on such a helper (`AnyHelperPatched`: the hook's helper group is strict, vanilla), since its inlined body stays in the replacement. (A3) Value types' methods and shared generic code are never queued (a rebuild changes nothing there). (C1) `Inlinable` is memoized; per candidate, the methods its replacement may have inlined are worked out once per `HarmonyWatch.Epoch` (`ReachedBy`); the scan time is always logged, also when nothing is stale ("none of the N methods ... may have inlined it"). Test: `foreign-helpers-2k-on/off` install the PushModel postfix from another thread (`foreignpatch model-gated-postfix ... squeeze background`): the InlineGuard line says "queued from another thread". Not tested in game: a refused rebuild's note (needs a stateful transpiler on a candidate).
- **Re-audit of c2f613b (walk reuse), D1-D6.** (D1) A checked reuse marks its key checked only when its comparison covered everything its caller read (each element within what the kept output had pulled, or the kept output complete and the lengths compared at the end); otherwise the next reuse is checked again. The kept output is never pulled further to compare. (D6) The first walk's own output is wrapped in the same scope guard as the reuses (`ReusedOutput`: outside its scope, or after the scope's reuse ended, the call's own output). (D3) Unfinished kept outputs are closed at the scope's end (`ExitAny`), when exact mode starts (`ExactFromHere`), on a version change of their key and when the part turns off: the source's enumerator is disposed (other mods' iterators' finally blocks run: EnchantLib returns ArrayPool arrays there), never while a caller is in it or its source runs (closed when the last caller leaves). (D2) The 1-in-16 per-key sample's position moves with the scope (`(_scopeSerial * 7) % 16` added; 0 in the self-test): display only. (Replaced on 2026-11-17 by a fixed offset per key, spread between keys: the moving offset could skip or repeat samples; see the memo below.) (D5) The last CombatStateChanged verdict holds its delegate weakly. (D4, owner decision) Under the walk premise, other mods' postfixes on display-scope methods (`NCard.UpdateVisuals`, `NHandCardHolder.UpdateCard`: RitsuLib, SkinChanger and others) and other mods' patches on the game's CombatStateChanged handlers run inside reuse scopes and may read the reused walk outputs: taken as display like the rest of the premise, behaviour unchanged; the owner to confirm. Tests: `foreignpatch walkreuse selftest` gains three cases (D1, D3, D6): 25 cases as expected offline on v0.107.1 and v0.113.1.

### Owner-approved premises (2026-11-17)

The owner approved six premises ("Pour chacun oui, mais vérifie l'impact réel et réaudite"; only low-risk forms): each is an assumption, not a proof, about other mods' code next to a part of this mod that would otherwise back off. Each is scoped as narrowly as its gain allows, stated here, shown as a kept note (`PatchSafety.Kept`: `bdopt why`'s kept section and the log; the menu's hover where it has a sentence, `Settings.MenuKept`), and off with `PatchSafety.ShapeRules` false (every check strict again, as before the premise). Everything outside a premise's scope stays exact: game state, checksums and the co-op rule are unchanged. Proof per premise: Bench shapes, an on/off A/B (CHECKSUM and STATE identical), effect counters showing the optimization runs, controls that must still back off; then the real in-game impact with the mods concerned (docs/RELEASE.md, the premises item). Pending: no game session on this code yet. **Re-audited the same day** (the premises audit's two auditors, applied on integration-2 in the low-risk form, the narrowest each time): each entry's "Re-audit of the premises" part gives the scope as it stands; the co-op point (the sort check's schedule, Q's A2) first.

- **DoFlash and the relic-flash visuals (RelicFlashMerge, `flash_display`).** Premise: the calls a merged flash removes from the two visual paths are display. The owner approved it for the relic bar's flash (`NRelicInventoryHolder.DoFlash`, ARAM's prefix); **extended in the same spirit to the creature-side flash effect** (to be confirmed by the owner). Scope: the two computed visual sets (`CodeSet`, reviewed per version, "Hardening of 2026-11-17" R1): the holder's `DoFlash` and every game method it reaches (the scene cache, the run's UI, the relic's icon getters and what they read), and the creature effect (`NRelicFlashVfx`'s whole class, `Creature.GetVfxContainer`, `AddChildSafely`, and what they reach: `NCombatRoom.GetCreatureNode`, `GetTopOfHitbox`, `TaskHelper.RunSafely`, the icon reads in `StartVfx`); other mods' patches there stand, and other mods' overrides of the game virtual methods they call (a content mod's `PackedIconPath`) too, named in the kept note ("overridden by ..."; the "overrides" note is the shape-rules-off one). Data: ARAM's `DoFlash` prefix, RitsuLib's `RelicTexturePatch` and `RelicIconPathPatch`, Orchis-NecrobinderSkin's and Watcher's icon getter patches, BaseLib's `PackedIconPath` prefix and its `ModelDb.GetEntry`/`AllCharacters` postfixes (reached through the game's own icon getters: `LoomingFruit`, `YummyCookie`), RitsuLib's and others' `AssetCache` prefixes; with BaseLib the strict sets had left every flash to the game. Strict still: the two handlers (`NRelicInventoryHolder.OnRelicFlashed`, `Player.OnRelicFlashed`: a patch there sees the whole call skipped, the sound's included), the random number generators the sets reach (namespace `MegaCrit.Sts2.Core.Random`: `Rng`, `MegaRandom`; an icon getter may draw from `Rng.Chaotic`: a patch on a draw is never taken as display, `StrictMember`), other mods' overrides of a strict member (none: no strict member of either set is virtual, so `StrictVirtuals` is empty on every reviewed version; corrected by the re-audit of the premises, which found this list claiming overrides were strict), the sets' review per version, and the sound (rule D: replayed, exact). The menu says "changes made to a relic flash's visuals are left out for the flashes merged" (key `kept_flash`, 13 languages, my translations). Tests: `relicflash-display-on`/`-off` (display-count prefixes on `DoFlash`, `NVfxParticleSystem._Ready`, `NRelicFlashVfx.Create`, `GetVfxContainer`, `get_Icon`: merged, kept note, CHECKSUM and STATE identical; their counts are FOREIGN lines, fewer with the mod on by design), `relicflash-ctl-on`/`-off` (controls, one prefix per phase with the runtime check's own back-off line, re-audit round 2's form: a counting prefix on `Player.OnRelicFlashed` keeps every player flash, one on `NRelicInventoryHolder.OnRelicFlashed` every relic bar flash, same counts on/off; a no-op prefix on `Rng.get_Chaotic` merges nothing). Impact to measure: the Bing Bong burst at a campfire clone next to BaseLib and RitsuLib (no black screen, Merged > 0). **Revised the same day (coordinator's in-game data at 0bc4113)**: the strict sets left nearly every flash to the game with the default mods, since they reach generic code BaseLib and Limitless patch (`ModelDb.GetEntry`, `ModelDb.AllCharacters`, `RelicModel.PackedIconPath`; `TweenHelper.AwaitFinished`/`AwaitFinishedInternal`, `ConsoleLogPrinter.Print`, `NCard.OnFreedToPool`): all inside the sets, so under the premise (named in the kept note). The relic bar scene's own script is now in the holder set too: relic_inventory_flash_vfx's root carries `NVfxParticleSystem` (`_Ready`, `TryPlayParticles`, `AfterExpired`, and what they reach: `QueueFreeSafely`, the node pool's free path, `NCard.OnFreedToPool` among it), the set recomputed and pinned (roots `NRelicInventoryHolder::OnRelicFlashed`, `::DoFlash`, `NVfxParticleSystem::*`: 388, 385 and 436 methods on v0.107.1, v0.111.0, v0.113.1). A scene's scripts are data, not code: at the first merge of each effect the scene is read from Godot's own resource cache (no game code runs; the frame's first flash just instantiated it) and every script its nodes carry (instanced scenes included) must be the reviewed class's (`ScriptPath` of `NVfxParticleSystem`, resp. `NRelicFlashVfx`), else that effect's flashes are kept for the session (`SceneScriptsKnown`, logged with what was found). Test: `relicflash-mods-on`/`-off` (no synthetic patch, the default test mods: merged, the kept note naming BaseLib's and Limitless's members, CHECKSUM and STATE identical). **Merged with re-audit round 2** (integration-2, 2026-11-17): round 2's strict, unfollowed check of `NVfxParticleSystem`'s own code (A1) gives way to this premise with the shape rules on and to the whole set's strict check with them off; its A3 (the sound set's overrides, delegates and review with the rules off), A5 (the merge recorded by the handlers' postfix `Shown`), C1 (`PlaySound`) and the CodeSet rework (`LoadedCode.Key`, no allocation per flash, `GameHookDelegates`) are kept. The fingerprints of both visual sets were recomputed with the merged `MethodFingerprint` (tools/fingerprint closure, same roots): unchanged. **Re-audit of the premises (2026-11-17, A1, B1, C1, F2, F3, G): the scope narrowed.** (1) The premise stated in full: **the calls a merge removes from the two visual sets have no effect at all on the game's state** (models, run, combat, save): a mod with a game-state side effect there could desync co-op (merges are per frame, `Engine.GetProcessFrames()`, and remote players' flashes and async sequences make what is merged differ between peers; the game's own code there only builds effects). (2) Strict now besides the handlers and the random number generators (`StrictMember`): every member of the sets under `MegaCrit.Sts2.Core.Saves` (the save managers, `ProgressState`, `SerializableProgress`, the stats and migration classes, `CloudSaveStore`, `GodotFileIo`: reached on lazy initialization and error paths, not on a normal flash, their writes included) and the members of `RunState`, `CombatState` and `Creature` the sets reach (getters on a flash's path, `Creature.GetVfxContainer` among them); nested classes count as their outer class. No Workshop mod checked patches any of them (decompiled/mods, attribute and `AccessTools.Method` patches); BaseLib's and Limitless's members of the proof run (`ModelDb.GetEntry`, `AllCharacters`, `PackedIconPath`, `TweenHelper.AwaitFinished`/`AwaitFinishedInternal`, `ConsoleLogPrinter.Print`, `NCard.OnFreedToPool`) stay under the premise.
