(3) The scene check (B1) reads the node the frame's first, shown flash actually created, not Godot's resource cache by path (the game instantiates `PreloadManager.Cache.GetScene(path)`, its `AssetCache`'s own entry, which `SetAsset` or a `GetScene` prefix can replace, and a node's script can be set after it is made): while the handler of a flash later ones may merge with runs, Godot's `node_added` (no game code) records the first node entering the tree whose scene file is the effect's (a finalizer on both handlers ends the capture, on a throw too); at each later flash of that frame its tree (8 levels, 64 nodes at most) must carry only the reviewed class's script or none and only Godot's classes or the reviewed one (`ShownNodeKnown`, checked once per key and frame with merges); a node not seen (an add deferred, another scene), freed, a built-in or other script: that frame's flashes are kept (logged once per finding, counted `SceneRefusals`). `SceneScriptsKnown` and its per-session verdict are gone. (4) The kept note (C1) says the members are game code a flash's visuals *may* reach (most only on a first use or an error path) and states the no-state-effect premise; the menu sentence (`kept_flash`) is unchanged. (5) G: the note isn't shown once the part faulted. Tests: `relicflash-display-on`/`-off` no longer patch `GetVfxContainer` (strict now) and expect both effects merged (F2: `RelicFlashMerge.Holder` and `.Creature` grew, `SceneRefusals` zero); `relicflash-ctl-on`/`-off` gain a phase with a counting prefix on `Creature.GetVfxContainer` (no creature merge, its count as mod off); `relicflash-scene-ctl` (F3: a postfix on `NRelicFlashVfx.Create(RelicModel, Creature)` giving the node a child with an in-memory GDScript: no creature merge at all, the refusal logged, relic bar merges go on). F1 (the controls masking each other) was already answered by round 2's one prefix per phase with runtime lines. Owner to confirm: the creature-side extension, and the menu sentence's wording.
- **HoverTipReuse: what other mods add to hover tips is display (`tips_display`).** HoverTipReuse keeps a creature's tip panel when its tips are unchanged; it backed off when the panel's methods carried another mod's patch (Free Loadout, Minty, RitsuLib, RandomForeseer, Downfall, PengoTarot, Zone). Premise: those patches only change what the tips show. Scope: only `NHoverTipSet`'s methods a reuse doesn't run (`SkippedNames`: `CreateAndShow`, `Init`, `SetAlignment`, `Remove`, `CreateAndRegister`, `ReplaceExistingTip`, `ConnectTreeExiting`, `CorrectVerticalOverflow`, `CorrectHorizontalOverflow`, both versions' overloads). Accepted as display: a decoration a patch adds from state outside the tips (a setting, an overlay) may stay as it was on a reused panel until the tips change. A patch replacing the build without registering the panel in `_activeHoverTips` is never reused (the reuse requires the registered panel to be the one it recorded). Strict still: the handler `NCreature.ShowCreatureHoverTips`, `NCreature.ShowHoverTips`/`HideHoverTips`, every fingerprint, the field-by-field comparison and the placement check; with `ShapeRules` off the panel's methods are strict again. Menu: key `kept_tips` (13 languages, my translations). Tests: `hover-tips-display-on`/`-off` (display-count prefixes on `CreateAndShow`, `Init`, `Remove`: reused, kept note, CHECKSUM and STATE identical), `hover-tips-ctl` (a prefix on `NCreature.ShowHoverTips`: no reuse). Impact to measure: the post-action UI refresh while hovering a creature at 8,400 cards (`hover-tips-8k`) with Minty and RitsuLib (`BDTEST_EXTRA_MODS`), before/after: about half of that refresh was the rebuilds. **Re-audit of the premises (2026-11-17, D1, G).** The scope named in full: a reuse also leaves out what building the panel runs besides `NHoverTipSet`'s methods, its constructor and `_Ready`, the scripts of the tip scenes it instantiates (`hover_tip.tscn`), `AddChildSafely` and the labels' `SetTextAutoSize`: under the same premise, named in the kept note. The build's "seen" marks (`SaveManager`, `ProgressSaveManager` and `ProgressState`'s `MarkCardAsSeen`/`MarkRelicAsSeen`/`MarkPotionAsSeen`, made by `Init` for a tip whose canonical model is a card, relic or potion) are strict: the game's own only add an id once (a second call with the same tips does nothing, so a reuse leaves out no-ops), but another mod's patch there is a save write a reuse would leave out (RandomForeseer's bool prefix on `SaveManager.MarkCardAsSeen`): a panel with such a tip is rebuilt while any mark carries another mod's patch, or when they can't be found. Not tested in game (a creature's tips rarely carry a card, relic or potion). G: the note isn't shown once the part faulted.
- **Q, CardSortKeys next to patches on a card's cost (`sort_cost`).** Premise: inside one synchronous `List.Sort`, foreign code reached from a card's cost doesn't change what any card's cost reads (the game's own sort already needs a consistent comparer: an inconsistent one throws or scrambles the order in the game too). Scope: the cost chain a sort reads once per card (`_costChain`: `CardModel.EnergyCost`, `CardEnergyCost.GetResolved` and `GetWithModifiers`, `LocalCostModifier.Modify`, `Hook.ModifyEnergyCostInCombat`, `CardModel.Pile`); with a foreign patch there the sort is still answered, the cost read live through the patched methods, once per card instead of once per comparison (the patches run fewer times: the premise). Data: RitsuLib (a transpiler and a postfix on `GetWithModifiers`, a prefix on `get_Pile`), CardAscension, EnchantLib, PengoTarot (a replacing prefix on `ModifyEnergyCostInCombat`). Strict still: `CardModel.CompareTo`, `AbstractModel.CompareTo`, `ModelId.Compare`, `StableShuffle` (open and closed), `CardModel.DynamicVars`, `DynamicVarSet.Keys`; the cost hook defaults (`TypeGated` as before: a patch there runs per listener, not named by the premise); the listeners' own cost and keyword hooks (fingerprinted pure ones only); the combat hook walk; the keyword reads; the check (each sort's first answer and one in 1,024 after it, 16 in Debug), which compares an answer with the game's live comparison and turns the part off at a difference; with `ShapeRules` off the cost chain is strict again. Menu: key `kept_sort` (13 languages, my translations). Tests (v0.113.1 only; v0.107.1 has the cheap comparison: not applied): `sortkeys-premise-1k-on`/`-off` (counting prefixes on `GetWithModifiers` and `get_Pile`, a bool prefix on `ModifyEnergyCostInCombat`: sorts answered, mismatches 0, kept note, CHECKSUM and STATE identical), `sortkeys-conflict-1k-on` (control: a patch on `CompareTo` still refuses), `sortkeys-wrong`. Impact to measure: RitsuLib on v0.113.1 at 10,300 cards, the combat start and a reshuffle (frozen more than 5 minutes before). **Re-audit of the premises (2026-11-17, A2, A3, F4, F5, G).** (A2, co-op) The check's schedule was a process-wide counter: peers checked different comparisons, and a difference found by one peer only (the live `CompareTo` running other mods' cost code at peer-dependent points) would have turned that peer's order to the game's alone, a desync. It is now the sort's own: each sort's first answer and every `VerifyEvery`-th after it (`scope.Answered`). A sort is a synchronized game action: the same list and, with the same answers, the same comparisons on every peer, so every peer answering it checks the same comparisons, runs the same live code at the same points, and reaches the same verdict at the same one. What stays outside: a peer whose sort is the game's (another setting, another mod set) checks nothing; under the premise its order is the same, and where the premise fails the cached answers differ from the game's order anyway (the check doesn't make that worse). (A3) `Open` read `card.CombatState` (so `CardModel.Pile`, under the premise) for every card, also in sorts that never reach the cost step: the card's possible combat is now read from the fields (`CardModel._owner`, `Player.Creature`, `Creature.CombatState`: a superset of `card.CombatState`, which is the same object when the card is in a combat pile or a combat upgrade preview), no getter run; `CardModel.CombatState`, `Player.Creature` and `Creature.CombatState` are fingerprinted (v0.113.0 and v0.113.1: `19AC49F43ACE99F6`, `0D20F8EB8EC69AA5`, `18BE5475C459C82D`) and strict (no Workshop mod checked patches them). Left as it was: the combat's listener walk `ImpureCostListener` makes in `Open` (`CombatState.IterateHookListeners`, which RitsuLib's walk postfix sees once per sort with a combat, also when the sort never reaches the cost): owner decision (making that walk strict would refuse every combat sort next to RitsuLib). Tests: `sortkeys-premise-1k-on`/`-off` now carry RitsuLib's real shapes (F4: a transpiler and a by-ref postfix on `GetWithModifiers`, +1 for Squeeze at every read, shape `energy-cost-postfix`, and the prefix on `get_Pile`); `sortkeys-violate-1k-on` (F5: every other cost read one more, shape `energy-cost-unstable-postfix`: the check must find a difference and turn the part off). G: the note isn't shown before the part applied or once it faulted.
- **P, reflection identity reads before a gate: partial (`name_gates`).** Done: the name test as a type gate in `TypeGated` patches (`PatchSafety.NameTest`, Skin Changer's `__instance.GetType().Name != "MultiplayerScalingModel"`): `ldarg __instance; callvirt object.GetType(); callvirt get_Name|get_FullName (MemberInfo or Type); ldstr S; call string.op_Equality|op_Inequality; [stloc; ldloc;] brtrue|brfalse`, the instance only (never null in a hook default; none of these calls can throw or write on a non-null receiver). The gate is every loaded type whose `Name` (or `FullName`) is S (`WithNamedTypes`: types assignable to them are kept too, more listeners kept, never fewer), worked out again when an assembly loads (PatchSafety's answers expire within 2 s); a name no loaded type has gates on nothing (`NoTypeOfThatName`); a generic type of that name, or an assembly whose types can't be read, refuses the gate. The premise: within those 2 s no assembly adds a listener type of that name (mods load at startup). Shown: the Tolerated line says "a name test on the instance's runtime type taken as a gate on every loaded type of that name (a stated premise)", a kept note `name_gates` (menu key `kept_names`, 13 languages, my translations). Strict: anything else before the gate (a call, a field read, another argument's type), and with `ShapeRules` off no name test is recognized. **Not done, and why**: (1) Skin Changer itself still backs off: its patch class reaches `StatefulCardArtRuntime`, a beforefieldinit class whose initializer builds `Dictionary<string, Runtime?>` and `ConditionalWeakTable<CardModel, ...>` (collections of non-runtime types: refused by P2/P4 of the verification audit), so its gate never stands; (2) Minty's prologue (`isinst MethodInfo; Harmony.GetOriginalMethod; starg` before `isinst PowerModel` in `AfterHook`, and `MethodBase.GetCurrentMethod` in its inserted run) was not added to `TranspilerJudge.GatesOn`: its class `SpecificFixes` has an initializer calling `AccessTools.Method`, BaseLib's `PatchAsync` and `Dictionary`/`HashSet.Add` (refused by P1), so no gain could follow, only risk. Both need an owner decision on the initializer rules (for instance: an empty runtime collection's constructor whatever its type arguments; or "the initializer already ran when Harmony applied the patch", read by the class's own `[HarmonyTargetMethods]`, Minty's case). Tests: `foreign-namegate-1k-on`/`-off` (Name and FullName gates on `AfterCardPlayed` and `AfterPlayerTurnStartEarly`: HookSkip keeps filtering, STATE counts identical; controls: a call before the test on `AfterSideTurnEnd`, a name test on the drawn card on `AfterCardDrawn`: back off). Impact: none measurable yet (see "not done"). **Re-audit of the premises (2026-11-17, P-1).** `TypesNamed` cached its answer by `AppDomain.GetAssemblies().Length`: a type emitted later into a dynamic assembly already loaded (RitsuLib's `PlaceholderModelTypeEmitter`, BaseLib's generated badges) was missed for good. It is keyed on `LoadedCode.Key` now (the assembly loads and the dynamic assemblies' created types, sampled every 2 s): the premise reads "within 2 s of a load or an emitted type, no listener type of that name appears". Refused too: a name with `[` or `` ` `` (a closed generic's runtime `Name`/`FullName`: no loaded type is a closed generic, so none would match while its instances' names do), and a type of that name still being built (`TypeBuilder`; on .NET 9 its assembly's `GetTypes` throws meanwhile, refused as unreadable as before). Each answer still scans every loaded assembly once per name and key (a nit left). Test: `foreignpatch namegate selftest` (closed generic names, a generic arity, an unknown name, a type being built, a type emitted later found once the key moves; in `foreign-namegate-1k-on`; offline only the first four, Steamworks.NET's types being unreadable there).
- **DMG, identity bridges on `ModifyDamageInternal` (TranspilerJudge, `identity_bridges`).** Downfall and Watcher (byte-identical copies) insert, right after the game's `stloc.s num2` (a listener's `ModifyDamageAdditive`/`Multiplicative` result), `ldloc item; ldloc.s num2; ldarg.2; ldloc.0; ldarg.s 5; ldarg.3; ldarg.s 6; ldarg.s 7|ldnull; call Bridge; stloc.s num2`; the bridge returns `vanillaNum` unless `listener is IModifyDamage*`. Invariant: for a listener of none of the gate types the bridge stores back the very bits the game stored (the iteration stays the no-op HookSkip's premise already takes it for); a kept listener runs the same call on equal values. Objection (b) above, the total read at a kept iteration: the run may read only locals the game's own code read just before in the same straight code (`ldloc.0`, `num`, is the argument of the game's own `item.ModifyDamageAdditive(target, num, ...)`, which HookSkip's premise already makes equal at kept iterations, or the game's own call would be wrong). Accepted shape (`CheckRun`, shape rules on): the run follows the game's own `stloc X` (no jump target there nor at the next instruction, X's address never taken; the alignment's other reading, `stloc X; A` before the game's `stloc X`, is read as `A; stloc X` after it: `AfterStore`), ends with one `stloc X` taking the result of one call that takes the current listener and `ldloc X`; its other values are constants, unwritten arguments, or locals read in the straight code before the gap (`ReadBefore`: back from the gap an `ldloc` of it before any write, jump target, exception mark, branch; at most 32 instructions; address never taken). The callee passes `IdentityGatesOn`: `GatesOn`'s `isinst` tests on the listener, whose only no-op exit is `ldarg <the parameter fed by ldloc X>; ret`, returning X's type, parameter of that type, its class without a static constructor, every class it reaches initializer-free (the TypeGated rule, P2), not patched itself (re-checked at each answer). Several mods' bridges after the same store (Downfall's and Watcher's together) are split at each `stloc X` and judged one by one. Refused: another argument given back, a store to another local, a run not right after the store, a local the game writes between or doesn't read just before, any call or field read before the `isinst`, a static constructor, the listener twice, two loops. Gates join the filter as other judged gates (`PileFilter.WithGates`). HookFilter's damage mirror stays off with any transpiler there (PrefixesOnly). Shown: the judge's log line ("N storing their result back into the iteration's own: identity bridges, a stated premise"), the kept note `identity_bridges` (menu key `kept_bridges`, 13 languages, my translations). With `ShapeRules` off no identity form is recognized. Tests: offline harness (Debug DLL, v0.107.1 and v0.113.1 reference assemblies): `foreignpatch iljudge selftest` 59 cases as expected on both, the exact insertion accepted once and twice, the eight near misses refused, the sweep unchanged (144 of 147, 151 of 154); and the real transpilers from the Workshop DLLs (Downfall 3747508091, Watcher 3747492505) run on the game's `ModifyDamageInternal` and judged: accepted alone and together on both versions (2 runs, 2 or 4 bridges, gates `IModifyDamageAdditive`/`IModifyDamageMultiplicative` of each). In game: `foreign-il-2k-on`/`-off` (the self-test there), `foreign-dmgbridge-1k-on`/`-off` (the bridge gated on Squeeze adding 1: accepted, judged walks, Squeeze's calls the same on both sides, CHECKSUM and STATE identical; then the control with a static write before the gate: refused). Impact to measure: Downfall and Watcher (`BDTEST_EXTRA_MODS`), `ab-2k-on`/`-off` and the rendered ladder: damage previews and attacks at 8,400 cards (every damage hook walk was vanilla with them). **Re-audit of the premises (2026-11-17, DMG-1): the scope narrowed to the owner's wording.** `IdentityStore` accepted a bridge after any `stloc X` in any hook HookSkip judges. Now only in `Hook.ModifyDamageInternal`, and only right after the game's `callvirt` of `AbstractModel.ModifyDamageAdditive`, `ModifyDamageMultiplicative`, `ModifyDamageMultiplicativeLate` (v0.113) or `ModifyDamageCap` on the current listener and its `stloc X` (X of that call's return type, neither instruction a jump target): the listener's result local, in any of the hook's listener loops. Self-test cases added (66 on v0.107.1, 67 on v0.113.1, offline, all as expected): the bridge in the multiplicative and v0.113 late loops accepted; refused a side effect on the no-op path after the gate (a static counter), a pair whose second bridge isn't an identity, an identity of another type (`int` into a `decimal` local), the stored local passed by reference (its address taken), a label on the game's instruction after the store, and the same shape on `Hook.ModifyBlock`'s additive result (accepted before this change: the scope control). The sweep is unchanged and Downfall's and Watcher's real transpilers are still accepted alone and together on both versions (offline harness, 2 runs, gates `IModifyDamageAdditive`/`IModifyDamageMultiplicative`).
- **Walks Tier 2, the listener walks' outputs after other mods' postfixes reused within a display refresh (ListenerCache, part `ListenerCache.Walks` of "Combat refresh", `walk_reuse`).** Before: with a by-value postfix on `CombatState`/`RunState.IterateHookListeners` (RitsuLib's lazy per-owner expansion, EnchantLib's snapshot-filter-repeat, Zone's lazy `Where`), a scope serves the unpatched list and the postfixes run on it at every walk, HookFilter is off and HookSkip's filtered walks take `KeptOnly` over the game's call: every walk pays the postfixes' enumeration, O(cards) per hook call. Premise: **within one display refresh, at one state version, the walk's output after other mods' postfixes is the same at every call**; it is then materialized at the first walk (the game's call, every postfix run, enumerated at once) and handed to the scope's later walks of that key ((combat state) or (run state, child)) behind the same `Replay`, so the postfixes' lazy enumeration (and what it does: RitsuLib's `MarkOwnerWithoutHookCapabilities`) runs once per refresh instead of once per walk (what the premise accepts); the postfixes themselves still run at every call (Harmony always runs them), but not what an output does only while enumerated: `RunState.IterateHookListeners(child)` is an iterator that calls the child combat's walk only while it is enumerated, so a replayed run output never makes that call and the combat walk's postfixes don't run for it (corrected by the re-audit of the premises, W-3: this sentence said "an eager one does its work every time", which holds only for work done at the call). HookSkip then filters the kept output (`KeptOnly`, after the postfixes: the same order). How: a postfix of this mod on both walks at `Priority.Last`, acting only when Harmony sorts it after every other postfix (asked again per Harmony epoch; a factory-made postfix: never), the shape rules on, the part on, inside a scope this class serves, the walk carrying other mods' patches. Scope, nothing wider: only the current outermost scope (dropped at its end, at a state version change, by the same invalidations as the unpatched lists), and **never in a scope where a snapshot (the co-op state check) or a game-value refresh (`RecalculateCardValues`, ExtraScopes' first-turn filters) is or was open**: only the CombatStateChanged dispatch (the post-action UI refresh; game logic resuming in it runs vanilla, `SuspendPrefix`) and the display refreshes (`NCard.UpdateVisuals`, `NHandCardHolder.UpdateCard`, ExtraScopes' display scope). So checksums and game values never read a kept output. Verification: one reuse in 16 materializes the call's own output and compares it with the kept one (same models, same order); a difference turns the part off for the session (logged, faulted `ListenerCache.Walks`, that call gets its own output). A throw in another mod's code while materializing reaches the caller after the entries read before it (`FailingReplay`), as the game's enumeration would meet it; nothing kept. (Superseded by the re-audit below: no eager materialization, `FailingReplay` gone.) HookFilter stays off in these scopes (`IsCaching`), unchanged. Not done: reuse across refreshes (`_kept`) and outside scopes (the hooks a play runs): the narrow form. Shown: kept note `walk_reuse` (menu key `kept_walks`, 13 languages, my translations). With `ShapeRules` off the postfix isn't even installed. Tests: `foreign-walkreuse-1k-on`/`-off` (RitsuLib's and EnchantLib's shapes: reused, verified, no difference, CHECKSUM and STATE identical, `require nodouble`), `foreign-walkreuse-ctl` (a postfix whose output changes every third call: the check must turn the part off), `foreign-needless-2k-on`/`-off` (the same shapes with the rest, now reused too). Impact to measure: RitsuLib (`BDTEST_EXTRA_MODS=STS2-RitsuLib`), EnchantLib, Zone on v0.107.1 at 10,300 cards: a play's time (4 s with RitsuLib before), the rendered ladder's post-action refresh. **Re-audit of the premises (2026-11-17, W-1 to W-7, nits): the scope narrowed.** (W-2) The kept output is no longer enumerated to its end at the first walk (that ran other mods' code, RitsuLib's marks, Zone's warnings, for listeners a caller stopping early never reaches, and before the caller's loop bodies): `LazyOutput` pulls the first walk's own output one element per step of its caller, interleaved with its loop as the game's would be, and replays what was pulled for the scope's later walks of that key, pulling further when one goes further; the source is disposed at its end or on its throw, as a foreach does; one no caller enumerated to its end is left to the collector (not disposed when the callers stop: a later walk may go further; the game's iterators' finally blocks only dispose list enumerators: part of the premise). A throw from the output's code reaches every caller at the element the game's enumeration would meet it, and the key is no longer reused. Other mods' code a pull runs gets its own output for any walk it makes (never the kept one). (W-1a) A CombatStateChanged dispatch with any handler outside the game's assembly (RandomForeseer's prediction refresh walks the real state; RitsuLib's extra hand and secondary resource nodes) reused nothing (`GameHandlersOnly`, cached per delegate, logged once; replaced on 2026-11-17 by the per-handler rule below): the reuse was left to dispatches whose handlers are all the game's UI nodes (`NCreature`, `NCreatureStateDisplay`, `NEndTurnButton`, `NEnergyCounter`, `NIntent`, `NPlayerHand`, `NPower`, `NOrbManager`, `NPotionPopup`, `NCeremonialBeastBgVfx`, v0.113's `NFoolCombatUi`). No display-scope mod is whitelisted. (W-1b) A reuse is handed out as `ReusedOutput`: the kept output only while enumerated inside the scope it was kept for with reuse still allowed there; enumerated later (a caller keeping the enumerable) or after the scope's reuse ended, it is the call's own output, the game's. So a kept output never reaches code outside its display scope (the scopes' methods return nothing). (W-4) The first reuse of each key in each scope is checked, then one in 16 per key, counted across scopes (weak on the state): a checked reuse hands the caller the call's own output and compares it as the caller enumerates it, element by element with what the kept one pulled, its end when the kept one's length is known (`CheckedOutput`), only within the scope and state version the kept one is for. (W-5) A suspension (`SuspendPrefix`: game logic resuming inline) ends the reuse for the rest of the outermost scope. (W-6) So does a declined unpatched mirror (`_mirrorDeclined`). Nits: `PostfixLast`'s verdicts are records (no torn read); the error path returns an output not yet enumerated (nothing is enumerated there now). Tests: `foreignpatch walkreuse selftest` (22 cases, offline on v0.107.1 and v0.113.1: lazy pulls and early stops, replays, one enumeration disposed at its end, a throw met at the same element by every caller and the key forgotten, every difference found and none where the kept length isn't known, the scope's end, a nested game-value refresh, a suspension and another mod's handler each ending the reuse, the game's own handlers keeping it); `foreign-walkreuse-sub-1k-on`/`-off` (the walk shapes and a handler of another mod walking the combat's listeners in each dispatch, `foreignpatch statechanged subscribe`: no reuse in those dispatches, `ListenerCache.Walks.ForeignDispatches` grew, CHECKSUM and STATE identical, the self-test in game; changed by the rule below). The kept note says the output is enumerated only as far as the game goes and which refreshes never reuse it. **Other mods' CombatStateChanged handlers: the per-handler rule (owner decision 2026-11-17, replacing the morning's "strict for now").** Evidence: a trace with RitsuLib at 10,295 cards (v0.107.1) put 64% of the rapid-play time in RitsuLib's `ModelCapabilityHookListeners.ExpandOwnerHookListeners`, reached through `FastListeners.KeptOnly` from `NPlayerHand.OnCombatStateChanged` (`NHandCardHolder.UpdateCard`, `NCard.UpdateVisuals`: playability, energy cost, keywords, damage previews), and the reuse never applied: RitsuLib's `RitsuDebugToolsPanel.OnCombatStateChanged` (it only queues a deferred refresh while its panel is visible) made every dispatch "foreign" under W-1a. The rule, generic (no per-mod list): where an augmented walk may be reused (`_combatAugmented`/`_runAugmented`, the shape rules and the part on) and some handler isn't the game's, `InvokeStateChangedInScope` runs the delegate's invocation list one handler at a time (`InvokeHandlers`), in its order, with the same argument, each once, a throw stopping the rest and reaching the game's dispatch unchanged, as `Action<CombatState>.Invoke` would; otherwise `Invoke` itself, as before. Invocation semantics checked against the game: `CombatStateTracker.CallCombatStateChangedDeferred` is `this.CombatStateChanged?.Invoke(_state)` in both supported versions (decompiled v0.107.1 line 225, v0.113.1 line 232; IL of the state machine identical, `IL_00cf`-`IL_00e1`: `ldfld CombatStateChanged; dup; brtrue; pop; br; ldfld _state; callvirt Invoke`): the field is read once and tested for null by the game's own IL, which stays; the transpiler swaps only the `callvirt`, so the method gets the very delegate the game read, and a delegate's invocation list never changes (a handler subscribing or unsubscribing during the dispatch makes a new delegate the dispatch never sees, as with `Invoke`). A handler is the game's when its method is declared in the game's assembly (closures and lambdas the game compiles included), its target null or of a game type, no generic argument from outside the game or the runtime (`IsGameHandler`); otherwise, or when that can't be told, it is another mod's: its lambdas, a game method bound to another mod's subclass of a game node, a dynamic method. Other mods' Harmony patches on a game handler don't change its class (D4, accepted under the walk premise). So another mod's prefix or postfix on a game handler runs inside that handler with the reuse on, may read reused walk outputs, and nothing kept is dropped after it: PengoTarot's `NEndTurnButton_OnCombatStateChanged_MoonPatch` on `NEndTurnButton.OnCombatStateChanged` runs `Hook.ShouldFlush` and `Hook.ModifyHandDraw` there for its moon prediction icon (display, under the walk premise as D4 decided). The game's handlers run with the reuse as before. Another mod's runs with it suspended for its whole run (`_foreignDepth`, restored not decremented; nested walks, nested scopes and anything it runs or waits on inline included): no output kept, reused or compared there (`ReuseOpen`, `ReusedOutput`, `CheckedOutput` test it), and the scope serial moves on at its start and end, so an output handed out before it is the call's own when enumerated inside it or later. Its walks are then what they are without the walk premise: the scope's unpatched lists (the exact tier, by state version, as every refresh's walk) with every other mod's postfix run on them at each call. Kept outputs after it: dropped (closed, `ClearAugmented`) when it returns, the conservative choice: the version bump covers the game state the unpatched lists depend on, not what other mods' postfixes read (RitsuLib's own capability marks, any mod's state), which another mod's handler may change; the next game handler's first walk keeps a new output. Faults: an error of this code before any handler ran (the classification, the typed list) falls back to `Invoke` and turns the part off; the code between handlers never throws (an error while dropping turns the part off, the rest run with no reuse), so no handler is ever skipped or run twice. Shown: an exact note (`PatchSafety.Exact`, new: neither a back-off nor a premise, no colour, not in the menu; `bdopt why` lists it under "Running next to other mods' code with no premise for it (only their exact optimizations)"), naming up to four handlers and their mods, recorded at the first dispatch of each new handler set, cleared when no walk can be reused; it replaces W-1a's log line. Counters (test builds, opt-in effects, EffectCounters): `ListenerCache.Walks.GameHandlerReuses` (reuses inside the game's handlers of such dispatches), `.ForeignHandlers` (other mods' handlers run suspended), `.DroppedAfterForeign` (kept outputs dropped after them), `.ForeignDispatches` (dispatches run per handler). Tests: `foreignpatch walkreuse selftest` gains 9 cases (classification index for index; order, argument, a subscription during the dispatch and a throw against `Invoke` on the same chain; the dispatch itself with every handler foreign; the fallback after an injected error before any handler: each handler once; a game handler reusing, another mod's seeing its own outputs and a held reuse as its own, nothing kept surviving it, a game handler after it keeping and reusing again): 33 cases as expected offline on v0.107.1 and v0.113.1, and two mutations (no suspension, no drop) caught. `foreignpatch statechanged subscribe [noop] [afterhand]`: a handler counting its calls only, and placed right after the hand's handler (else mid-list); the walking one now walks the combat and the run and compares each walk with the game's own call (`RunVanilla`), `foreignpatch require freshhandler` failing on a mismatch or a kept output made or reused while it ran. `foreign-walkreuse-sub-1k-on`/`-off` (walking, after the hand: GameHandlerReuses, ForeignHandlers and ForeignDispatches grew, require freshhandler, the exact note, CHECKSUM and STATE identical) and the new `foreign-walkreuse-noop-1k-on`/`-off` (noop, after the hand: Walks, GameHandlerReuses, ForeignHandlers and DroppedAfterForeign grew, CHECKSUM and STATE identical). Review of 2026-11-17 (the drop was only counted inside `EndForeignHandler`, and a noop handler changes nothing a missing drop would show): `foreignpatch statechanged subscribe mutating` flips what walk-expand-postfix returns at each call (state no version bump sees); the keys kept when another mod's handler returns are recorded apart from the drop, and each one's next walk counts as kept anew (`ListenerCache.Walks.KeptAfterForeign`) or reused/checked (`ReusedAfterForeign`, must stay 0); `foreignpatch require dropped` fails unless kept anew > 0, reused 0, no walk mismatch and the part on. `foreign-walkreuse-mut-1k-on`/`-off` (mutating, after the hand: DroppedAfterForeign and KeptAfterForeign grew, require dropped, CHECKSUM and STATE identical). Self-test: one more case (34 as expected offline on both versions); the drop removed, three cases fail. In game: pending. Impact to measure: RitsuLib at 10,295 cards (v0.107.1), rapid plays. **KeptOnly's memo over the kept outputs (owner-approved change, 2026-11-17; part `ListenerCache.WalkMemo` of "Combat refresh").** Evidence: a rendered trace at 10,295 cards (v0.107.1, RitsuLib, after the per-handler rule) put 77% of the rapid-play time inclusive in `FastListeners.KeptOnly` (through `HookSkip.EndCheck`/`CombatHookFiltered`, from `NPlayerHand.OnCombatStateChanged`: `CanPlay` 40%, energy cost 23%, damage previews 17%, keywords 15%), `TypeIdOf` alone 13%, `CheckedOutput` 8%, RitsuLib's expansion 19.5%: each hook call of a refresh filtered the same reused ~10,000-element output with the same filter again, O(cards) per card per hook. Now, when KeptOnly's walk is a `ReusedOutput` that gives the kept output if enumerated now (`KeptNow`: its own `GetEnumerator` test, at KeptOnly's first step, where its loop would call it), each filter's result over that output is memoized on the output (`FilteredMemo`: the kept listeners in order, each one's index, how far it judged, whether it reached the end) and replayed by the later filtered walks of that key and filter, judging further elements only when a caller goes further (each element judged once for every caller, two callers interleaved share it); a memo caller enters and leaves the output as its enumerator would (`LazyOutput.Enter`/`Leave`: a close asked meanwhile waits for it), so the output is pulled exactly when and as far as KeptOnly's own loop would pull it, and a throw from it meets the caller at the same element. The type id of each pulled element is worked out once per output for every filter (`LazyOutput.TypeIdAt`; an object's type never changes, a type's id is never given again). **No new premise, exact:** the output is one append-only sequence replayed identically to every caller (the walk premise's own object), and KeptOnly's result over a sequence reads, per element, only its position (the first one kept: A4), its runtime type (an element outside piles kept) and, for a pile entry, `Keeps`; for a filter not keeping ExecutionFinished subscribers and a type other than Limitless's MultiEnchantment, on the main thread, `Keeps` is `Reacts(TypeIdOf(type))`, a per-filter verdict cached for the filter's life (`_reactsById`/`_byType`, reset only for the MultiEnchantment type by `SyncMulti`; `_lastFilter`/`_lastType` serve other threads only). What it reads besides is never memoized: a MultiEnchantment (its verdict reads its inner enchantments, and judging it notes them: `NoteMulti`, `LimitlessHookSkip.Dropped`) is a live entry, its place kept and judged with `Keeps` at every replay, at the same point of the sequence and as many times as KeptOnly's loop judges it (first version: it turned the memo off where met; in game, with the reference deck's stacked enchantments and Limitless, every memo went off at its first pile entry: built 2,741, replayed 0, on both versions); filters keeping subscribers (`_executionFinished(model)`, per-object state) are never memoized; a filter verdict that throws turns the memo off with KeptOnly's handling for that caller (`Disable`, the rest kept); another thread (the type ids and verdict arrays are main-thread state) takes KeptOnly's loop for that caller, the memo not extended; FastListeners off or faulted: no memo. A memo turned off or dropped under a caller, or the part turned off (the replay loops on both), lets it go on with KeptOnly's own loop from its position in the same output (the elements after its last kept one judged again: the same verdicts). An error in the memo's own code (its lists, a type id, the live test, a checked use's comparison) turns only `ListenerCache.WalkMemo` off (logged once) and never FastListeners or the caller. Test builds: `FastListeners.FirstKept` counts each replay of a first listener the filter would have dropped, as KeptOnly's loop does. Drops: the memos live on their output (`DropMemos` when its close is asked, at once even while a caller holds the close: the scope's end, a version change of its key, exact mode, after another mod's CombatStateChanged handler, the walk reuse's faults); a reuse out of scope (another serial, version, exact mode, `_foreignDepth`, closed, pulling) reads no memo; a checked walk reuse (`CheckedOutput`) is the call's own output and runs KeptOnly's loop. Verification: the first use of a filter's memo over each walk key in the session, then one in 16 per filter and key (counted from a fixed offset per counter, spread between counters: exactly one use in 16; the walk reuse's per-key sample counts the same way since, D2's offset moving with the scope could skip or repeat samples), runs KeptOnly's own loop for the caller and compares each kept listener with the memo's entries by its index in the output (a live entry the filter dropped is no difference) as far as the memo judged, its end when the memo's is known; builds are not checked (the build is the memo's only judgement; checking it would filter the whole output twice per refresh); a check counts as done only when it covered what its caller read (D1's rule). A difference, or an error in the memo's own code, turns `ListenerCache.WalkMemo` off for the session (faulted, logged, every memo dropped, callers in one going on with KeptOnly's loop); the walk reuse itself stays on. Counters (test builds, opt-in effects, EffectCounters): `ListenerCache.WalkMemo` (uses replayed), `.Built`, `.Verified`; `foreignpatch keptmemo status`. Tests: `foreignpatch walkreuse selftest` gains 21 memo cases (the same result as KeptOnly's loop, the first kept, the pulls of an early stop, a partial memo extended, a second filter's own memo, live entries judged at every replay, two callers interleaved, a drop under a caller, the part turned off under a caller, a close asked during a caller, the checked use finding a memo made different with the caller unaffected, an error falling back, a type id error during an extension, a throwing verdict during an extension, a caller resumed on another thread, the type ids, exact mode and a reuse after its scope reading no memo): 55 cases as expected offline on v0.107.1 and v0.113.1; mutations caught (first not kept, scope ignored, no resume position, an element skipped, no Enter/Leave, no drop at close, a memo keyed by the output only, live entries turning the memo off). `foreignpatch keptmemo status` also gives the live verdicts. `foreign-walkreuse-1k-on` (WalkMemo, .Built, .Verified grew; `foreignpatch keptmemo require on`: hits, no difference, on) and `-noop-1k-on` (WalkMemo grew in the game's handlers), CHECKSUM and STATE identical with their `-off` sides as before; controls `foreign-keptmemo-ctl` (`foreignpatch keptmemo wrong`: the next memo use loses its first kept listener and is checked: the difference logged, `require off`: off and faulted, walk reuse on; WalkMemo the same afterwards, Walks still growing) and `foreign-keptmemo-fault-ctl` (`foreignpatch keptmemo fault`: the same fallback after an error). In game, first run (both versions): CHECKSUM and STATE identical, the sub and mut pairs passed, but no memo was ever replayed (the stacked enchantments above, fixed since; the plain and noop scenarios now run `require nodouble` before the memo checks, whose ABORT had hidden its line); to run again. Impact to measure: RitsuLib at 10,295 cards (v0.107.1), rapid plays and the rendered ladder.
- **Snapshot walk reuse: the augmented walks' outputs reused inside the checksum's outermost snapshot, behind a net (owner-approved change, 2026-11-17, approved on the condition that the risk is really low with the net mandatory; part `ListenerCache.SnapshotWalks` of "Co-op state check", kept note `snapshot_walks`, menu key `kept_snapwalks`).** Evidence: v0.111+ checksums the combat after every action, solo included (`ChecksumTracker.ObtainAndTrackChecksum` → `NetFullCombatState.FromRun` → `CombatPileState`/`CardState.From` per card: its cost through `Hook.ModifyEnergyCostInCombat`, its keywords, each walking every listener); with another mod's by-value postfix on `CombatState`/`RunState.IterateHookListeners` (RitsuLib's `ExpandOwnerHookListeners`, EnchantLib, Zone) every walk re-runs it over ~10,000 elements: O(n²), one card play 35-108 s at 10,295 cards with RitsuLib on v0.111.1 (`lad-rl11Ts11`), the walk reuse being barred from snapshots. **Premise (as Walks Tier 2's, narrower scope): within one checksum snapshot (synchronous, one state version), the walk's output after other mods' postfixes is the same at every call.** Scope: only the outermost snapshot scope (`EnterSnapshot` with no other scope open: `BeginSnapshotReuse`), the part and the state check on, other mods' postfixes on a walk, this mod's walk postfix last (`PostfixLast`, as in display refreshes), and `SnapshotVerifier.WalkNetFor`: its FromRun postfix in place and no timing-dependent live re-check possible for that snapshot (Sample mode: above 1,000 combat cards only; Off or All: any size). A nested snapshot, a game-value refresh, a suspension or a state version change ends the reuse for the rest of the snapshot; every other exactness rule (versions, other mods' handlers, `ExactFromHere` everywhere else, faults) is unchanged. Inside it the reuse is Walks Tier 2's (kept `LazyOutput`, `ReusedOutput`, the KeptOnly memo), with a schedule of the snapshot's own: the first reuse of each key is a checked one (`CheckedOutput`, until one covered what its caller read), the first replay of each memo too (`FilteredMemo.CheckedHere`), none after; no count kept across scopes (`CountReuse`, `MemoStats` untouched), no display switch or session fault read (`PartOn`, `MemoOn`). **The net** (`ListenerCache.EndSnapshotWalks`, called first thing in `SnapshotVerifier.FromRunPostfix`): for each kept output the snapshot handed to more than its first walk, one fresh call of the game's walk (`RunVanilla`: the game's iterator, every other mod's postfix run as the game runs it), enumerated as far as the kept output was pulled and compared element by element, by reference (`CompareWithFresh`): every element any caller of the snapshot read is one of those (a reuse replays the kept elements, a memo judges them); a kept output that reached its end must end there in the fresh walk too, one no caller finished must have at least that many. Also a detection: a kept output that threw, the state version moved during the snapshot, anything the snapshot's own checks found (a checked reuse or memo check differing, an error of this code: `SnapshotTaint`), the fresh walk throwing or the comparison failing (`SnapshotNetErrors`). A postfix whose output changed mid-snapshot is found when its output at the end differs from the first call's (the end walk) or the second call's differs from the first's (the checked reuse); what it can't see: an output that changes after the second call and comes back to the first call's by the end (the premise's residue, below). **Order of operations, checksum sent = the game's:** `ChecksumTracker.ObtainAndTrackChecksum` (decompiled v0.111.1/v0.113.1 line 158-161, v0.107.1 164-167) calls `FromRun`, then `GenerateChecksum` on what `FromRun` returned, then sends it (client) and tracks it. Inside `FromRun`'s Harmony replacement: ListenerCache's prefix opens the scope (`EnterSnapshot`), the game's body runs the walks (reusing), then `SnapshotVerifier.FromRunPostfix` runs `EndSnapshotWalks` (nothing reused after it: `_exactScope`, outputs closed); on a detection (`ListenerCache.SnapshotDetections`, part of `SnapshotVerifier.Detections`) `RebuildIfDetected(..., walks: true)` replaces `__result` with a nested `FromRun` (`BuildAgain`, `[GameCallReplay]`), never the outermost scope, so exact: the game's call at each walk, every postfix run, as the snapshot was before this part; then the ListenerCache finalizer closes the scope; only then does `FromRun` return to `ObtainAndTrackChecksum`. If that rebuild throws, the exception (the game's or another mod's, met where the game's own `FromRun` would meet it) goes on to the game, as without the part: a reused snapshot found wrong is never kept. A snapshot that reused and returned without its net having run (never seen: the postfix is gated on `NetApplied`) turns the part off for the session in the scope's finalizer (`EndSnapshotScope`), logged. Version coverage: the checksum code is the same on all three (`ObtainAndTrackChecksum` identical in v0.111.1 and v0.113.1; v0.107.1 differs only in the divergence popup); v0.107.1 checksums only in co-op (`RunManager.InitializeShared`: `IsEnabled` for the net game types 2-4), v0.111+ in solo too (`IsEnabled = !TestMode.IsOn`). **Peer determinism and the owner's rule of 2026-11-17 (a desync this mod could cause must never be reproducible).** (1) Every decision of the part is a function of the state, the mods loaded and the settings: the gate (`BeginSnapshotReuse`: switches, the walks' patches, combat cards), the in-snapshot schedule (its own counts only), the net (a fresh walk at a fixed point, compared with what this state's calls pulled) and its outcome (a detection rebuilds that snapshot and nothing else: no session switch-off, so the next snapshot is decided the same way on every peer, a peer that re-hosts or reloads included). The Sample mode's live re-check, whose schedule depends on timing, never sees a reused snapshot (`WalkNetFor`): a re-check finding a difference the net missed would have made that peer alone use the game's snapshot and turn its snapshot optimizations off (a desync at that point, then later ones at the same points). (2) What remains: a premise violation the net misses (above), and a player whose snapshots can't reuse (the state check's option off, a fault of this class or of SnapshotVerifier, a net that couldn't run) next to one whose snapshots do. Players who all reuse compute the same snapshot from the same state (deterministic code and calls), so they agree with each other even then; players who all run exact agree too. Only the mixed case would differ, and at the same point every time (a violation tied to a state recurs on a reload): closed by **CoopModeTag's `+exactsnap`** (`ListenerCache.SnapshotWalksTag`: the mod on, other mods' patches on a walk, and this player's snapshots unable to reuse it; per walk since the second review: `+exactsnap-c`/`-r` when only one walk is reused): such a player and one whose snapshots reuse can't join each other (the game's mod mismatch), retagged at every settings change (`Settings.ApplyAll` → `CoopModeTag.Refresh`, also after `ListenerCache.Fail`); with no other mod's patch on the walks nothing is tagged (nothing to reuse anywhere). A switch-off during a session (a fault on one peer, the option changed) can still make that session's later checksums differ when a violation also escapes the net, but the next handshake refuses the mixed peers instead of replaying it; a player restarting the game starts with the part on again. The KeptOnly memo is exact (KeptOnly's result over the same output), so whether a peer uses it (its own switch, a display-side fault) never changes a snapshot. Faults of exact parts elsewhere (FastListeners, HookSkip switches) are as before this part: exact either way. (3) Test tooling only: `bdperf verify all|off` lets ≤1,000-card snapshots reuse (the Sample gate is the players'). Counters (test builds, opt-in effects, EffectCounters): `ListenerCache.SnapshotWalks` (reuses), `.Kept`, `.Checked` (in-snapshot checked reuses), `.Net` (fresh walks compared), `.Detections`, `SnapshotVerifier.Rebuilt`; `bdperf checksum` prints the snapshot walks reused, compared and rebuilt. Tests: `foreignpatch walkreuse selftest` gains snapshot cases (no game state: the net's fresh walk is the test's; the snapshot's own schedule whatever was counted before, the same fresh walk found equal, every kind of difference found and counted, none past a partly pulled output, a checked reuse differing, a state change, the fresh walk throwing, a reused output that threw, no reuse no comparison, nothing reused after the net, the part off): with `EndSnapshotWalks` returning false, the detection cases fail. `foreign-snapwalks-1k-on`/`-off` (v0.111+; RitsuLib's lazy and EnchantLib's eager shapes; `bdperf verify all`: every snapshot also compared with a vanilla one built apart, `verify check`; `foreignpatch require snapwalks on`; CHECKSUM and STATE identical); `foreign-snapflip-1k-on`/`-off` (`walk-snapflip-postfix`: from each snapshot's third combat walk on, Squeezes doubled, no game value changed; the checked second walk sees nothing, only the net can: `require snapwalks detected`, every snapshot rebuilt, verify check equal, the part still on; CHECKSUM and STATE identical); `coop-snapwalks-host`/`-client` (parcoop `-OnOff`, thousands of cards, Sample mode: both peers reuse and compare, no detection, no divergence, each peer equal to its mod-off twin); `coopmodetag exactsnap` checks the tag. In game: pending. Impact to measure: RitsuLib at 10,295 cards on v0.111.1 (`BDTEST_EXTRA_MODS=STS2-RitsuLib`), a play's time and the rendered ladder (35-108 s per play before). **Adversarial review of 2026-11-17 (f93e63d..snapwalks), fixed.** (1) *Reuse depended on the call stack.* `NPlayerHand.RevalidateSelectionAfterStateChange` ends a hand selection inside the CombatStateChanged dispatch and the card's play goes on inline (`SuspendPrefix`), on the selecting host or solo player with the action queue: `ActionExecutor.AfterActionExecuted` → `RunManager.SendPostActionChecksum` (decompiled v0.111.1 line 568-574) → `ChecksumTracker.GenerateChecksum` → `FromRun` runs there, nested in the dispatch scope and suspended, so that peer's snapshot was exact while the others' reused. Now the first snapshot (no snapshot open, not inside `RunVanilla`, told apart from a suspension by `_vanillaRef`) sets whatever encloses it aside (`SetAside`: refresh depth, suspension, another mod's handler, the kept outputs and lists) and opens as an outermost scope; `ExitSnapshot` restores it with what a nested snapshot always did to it (`BlockReuse`, `ExactFromHere`, a new serial). The decision is then a function of the state, the mods and the settings only. (2) Every output a snapshot keeps is compared by the net, a key walked once too. (3) *Side effects of skipped enumerations* (the premise's residue the comparison of outputs can't see): `FromRun` only reads in vanilla, but another mod's lazy walk postfix may draw an RNG or bump a counter while its output is enumerated, and a reused output is enumerated fewer times. `SnapshotSeal` records at the snapshot's start the run's and every player's RNG counters (FromRun writes the run's at its start and each player's before that player's piles), the next choice and reward ids, and the creature and player numbers FromRun reads (HP, max HP, block, powers and amounts; gold, potion slots, energy, stars, turn, phase); the net compares them after its fresh walks. A change is a detection; the RNGs drawn are first put back in place (`Rng.LoadFromSerializable`, v0.111+), so the rebuild draws them as the game's own `FromRun` and the state ends as vanilla's. No seal, no reuse. **Not switched off per run or session** (the review suggested it): a switch-off kept in this mod's memory differs on a peer that rejoined (its run state is loaded fresh) or restarted, while the per-snapshot decision is the same on every peer for the same state; restoring the RNGs makes the detection leave nothing behind. What can't be put back: an RNG on v0.107.1 (its RNGs only go forward; co-op only there), the choice and reward ids and the numbers (no way back): the snapshot is still rebuilt, and every peer whose snapshots reuse does the same thing at the same point (the same state), so they agree with each other; the state then differs from vanilla's by what the reused part skipped, identically on those peers (peers whose snapshots can't reuse are kept apart by `+exactsnap`). That residue needs a foreign walk postfix with a side effect on game state while enumerated; the walk postfixes studied don't change game state as far as recorded here (RitsuLib's marks are its own cache, EnchantLib returns pooled arrays, Zone's lazy filter logs warnings): not re-read for this change, to confirm for each mod adding one. (4) A snapshot that threw with reused outputs before its net ran: SnapshotVerifier's last finalizer on `FromRun` (after ListenerCache's scope finalizer, which reports it: `TakeThrewAfterReuse`, the RNGs put back) builds it again without reuse (`WithoutSnapshotReuse`) and returns it, or that build's exception; other exceptions pass on unchanged. A finalizer returning an exception makes Harmony throw it again from `FromRun`, the stack trace restarting there (as HookFilter's `RethrowFinalizer`): it logs each one with its full trace first (warning listed in judge.py). (5) SnapshotVerifier's `FromRun` postfix is `Priority.First`: another mod's postfix sees the checked (or rebuilt) snapshot, and its walks run exact (a foreign postfix also at `Priority.First` and registered earlier still runs before it). (6) The KeptOnly memo follows its own part's switch in snapshots too (a memo faulted in a display refresh stays off there; exact either way), and the net compares every memo used in the snapshot with KeptOnly's own loop over the fresh walk (`FastListeners.MemoAgainstLoop`: first kept, pile entries by the filter's verdict, live entries left out, the end where the memo reached it). (7) `+exactsnap` is computed from `BeginSnapshotReuse`'s inputs that don't depend on the snapshot: the switches and faults, the net in place, the scope check (`ScopesSafe`, shared with `EnterAny`), and this mod's walk postfix sorting last after another mod's on a walk it patches (`PostfixLast`, HarmonyWatch's cached verdict). Left out: the state (the Sample mode's 1,000-card gate is the same on every peer; `Mode` is test tooling), a mid-scope declined mirror, `RunVanilla`. The "Co-op state check" description says the join rule (13 languages); the owner hasn't validated the tag yet. (8) Tests: `foreign-snapcost-1k-on`/`-off` (`walk-snapflip-cost-postfix`: Spiked Gauntlets yielded twice from the second pile each `FromRun` call reads on, a power card's cost +2 there in the game's own snapshot: the reused one would differ, `bdperf verify all` + `verify check` prove the rebuilt one is used, CHECKSUM and STATE identical); `foreign-snaprng-1k-on`/`-off` (`walk-snaprng-postfix`: each enumeration inside a `FromRun` draws the run's Niche RNG: `require snapwalks sealed`, CHECKSUM and STATE identical, the RNG's later draws included; verify off: the vanilla re-check would draw too); the snapflip shapes count per `FromRun` call (a test prefix and finalizer on it: a rebuild or a re-check starts again, as the game's call); `bdperf snapnet` in `foreign-snapwalks-1k-on` (the whole path through `FromRun`: plain, the net made to differ, both inside a suspended display refresh with the same reuses, each checksum the game's own); self-test 18 snapshot cases (a key walked once compared, the context set aside and restored once, kept inside `RunVanilla`). Counters: `ListenerCache.SnapshotWalks.Memos`, `.Sealed`; `SnapshotVerifier.RebuiltAfterThrow`. In game: pending. **Owner decisions (2026-11-17, evening).** (a) The RNG put-back is kept, **an owner-approved exception to "never change game state"**: it writes only to restore the RNGs to what they were at the snapshot's start, after another mod's walk postfix drew them during a reused snapshot (or its net's fresh walks), so that the exact rebuild leaves them as the game's own `FromRun` would; nothing else is ever written, and only when the seal saw a draw. (b) The `+exactsnap` join tag is approved; the Workshop description's co-op paragraph says it in one sentence. **Second adversarial review (8f78219..snapwalks), fixed.** (1) The Sample re-check (`RunVanilla(FromRun)`) ran other mods' walk postfixes again, unsealed: one drawing an RNG would start it from RNGs the snapshot had advanced (a difference on that peer alone, its snapshot optimizations then off, recurring at every rejoin: index 0 is always re-checked). `FromRunPrefix` now seals the state where a re-check may come; the re-check puts the RNGs back to it, runs, then puts them back to what the snapshot left; a change the RNGs don't explain discards the re-check (`ReChecksDiscarded`, nothing turned off). (2) The seal and the put-back rely on fingerprinted code (`RunRngSet`/`PlayerRngSet.GetRng`: changed, no seal and no snapshot reuse; `Rng.ToSerializable`/`LoadFromSerializable`, `MegaRandom.FillSerializableState`/`Reinitialise(SerializableRng)`, `_s0` to `_s3` its only state: changed, no put-back, a drawn RNG then only makes a rebuild). (3) `+exactsnap` per walk (`+exactsnap-c`/`-r` when only the other walk is reused: two players reusing different walks are kept apart), from setup and settings inputs only (`FastListeners.MirrorsApplied`, not the thread's suspension). (4) `coop-snapwalks` lightened (one import per peer, the lazy synthetic postfix on both walks): its first version's mod-on logs showed the reuse in use in every checksum (v0.111.1 host: 29 outputs kept, 651,734 walks reused, 29 compared by the net, no detection), the time spent in walks outside the state check (combat start, the play's own hooks) where the synthetic postfix runs at every walk; the combat start on v0.113.1 held the main thread long enough for the client to time out. (5) The finalizer returns an exception it doesn't handle as it is (returning null would swallow it), its trace so far kept in front of the rethrow's (the runtime's remote stack trace, as ExceptionDispatchInfo). **What the seal detects (the owner's condition for the put-back: "as long as it really detects mods that touch it").** Detected: any change, between the snapshot's start and its net, of what it seals (the run's and every player's RNG counters, the next choice and reward ids, each creature's HP, max HP, block, powers and amounts, each player's gold, potion slots, energy, stars, turn and phase), made by any code that runs in the snapshot: the kept output's enumeration (the first call's own output, pulled by the snapshot's callers), the checked reuse, the net's fresh walk of every kept key, and the sampled re-check (sealed apart, above). Since the first walk's output and the net's fresh walk are both enumerated in full as far as the snapshot read, a walk postfix that does something at every call or every enumeration is always seen. Logged once per walk set and session, naming the walk, the Harmony ids of the other mods' patches on it and their mods (`ListenerCache: other mods' code on CombatState.IterateHookListeners (Harmony ids: ...; mods: ...) changed what a co-op state check reads while it was enumerated (...)`), named in bdopt why (the kept note), counted (`ListenerCache.SnapshotWalks.Sealed`, every one). Not detected: (a) what isn't sealed: another mod's own state (its caches, its counters), which no checksum reads (no desync; a mod reading it back into game state later would be (b)); (b) something a postfix would do only on calls the reuse skips and on none of the calls the snapshot still makes (one call in N, by call count): such a postfix's effect depends on how many times the walks run, which already differs between peers in vanilla co-op (each peer's display refreshes walk the listeners its own number of times: hovers, previews, its hand), so that mod desyncs vanilla co-op by itself; one counting only the state check's calls would not, and is the residue: every peer whose snapshots reuse skips the same calls (the same state), so they agree with each other, the state then differing from vanilla's identically on them (peers that can't reuse are kept apart by the tag); (c) on v0.107.1 (co-op only) a drawn RNG is detected but can't be put back: the snapshot is rebuilt, the RNG left advanced by what the reused part drew, identically on every reusing peer. Tests: `foreign-snaprng-1k-on` asserts the line (walk and Harmony id) and the counter, with the re-check on every snapshot (`bdperf verify all`, `verify check`).

### Foreign transpilers judged by IL (2026-11-16)

The study's classes "foreign transpilers on Hook state machines, judged by IL diff" (Minty Spire 2's per-play hooks, library helpers) and "interface-gated transpiler bridge on a hook method" (Downfall's and Watcher's `Hook.ModifyDamageInternal`). Until now any other mod's transpiler on a hook HookSkip filters (the hook, or an async hook's `MoveNext`) kept that hook vanilla, BaseLib's three aside (accepted by fingerprint). Now `Patches/TranspilerJudge.cs` judges what they produce, generically, under `PatchSafety.ShapeRules`; anything it can't prove keeps the back-off.

Second consumer of the capture store (2026-11-20): `UnchangedTranspilers` judges the code other mods' transpilers produce on methods that are not hooks (RelicStateReuse's and SnapshotCardReuse's serializers) with the strict identity verdict, `TranspilerJudge.Unchanged` ("Transpilers judged by the code they produce").

**Which code is judged, without running anyone's transpiler again.** HookSkip's transpiler is Harmony's last (`Priority.Last`): what Harmony hands it is the game's code with every other mod's transpiler applied, at every rebuild of that method. It keeps it (`Capture`); HarmonyWatch's finalizer on `UpdateWrapper` confirms it when that rebuild ended without an exception, before the epoch moves (`RebuildEnded`), and drops it otherwise. No foreign transpiler is replayed (they may be stateful; `GetCurrentInstructions` would run them). The game's code comes from Harmony's reader (`PatchProcessor.GetOriginalInstructions`: no transpiler runs; a transpiler made by a factory is refused first, Harmony's sort would call it). Refused before any diff: a transpiler Harmony sorts after this mod's (it would see the redirected walk, and the capture misses it), this mod's transpiler missing or twice, no confirmed capture yet (asked again, not cached), HarmonyWatch not applied (never confirmed). All other mods' transpilers on the method are judged together (the capture holds their combined result) and accepted or refused together; those the caller already allows (BaseLib's) don't trigger a judgement on their own.

**The rule.** (1) The game's code is found whole and in order in the result (an alignment minimizing the inserted runs, bounded at 1.5 M cells): same opcodes and operands (members, locals by index, constants), branch and switch targets mapped to the same instructions, exception blocks identical; inserted instructions carry no label and no block. (2) Each inserted run is straight code: no store of any kind, no branch, no address (`ldloca`, `ldflda`...), no field read but the state machine's own field holding the current listener, no `callvirt`, `newobj`, `calli`; only constants, the hook's arguments it never writes (static hooks), the current listener (`dup` of it, its local, its state machine field), `pop` of its own values, and `call`s of static, non-generic methods without by-reference parameters, outside the game's assembly and this mod's code; it consumes nothing of the stack it found and leaves it as it was. (3) Where it runs, from an abstract interpretation of the game's code (`TranspilerJudge.Analysis`): the walk calls HookSkip redirects, followed through `GetEnumerator`, locals and the state machine's fields to `MoveNext`/`get_Current`/`Dispose`; a `MoveNext` result's branch starts (true) or ends (false) that loop's iteration, and `MoveNext` forgets the previous current listener everywhere; an async state machine is analysed once per entry state (the first call with state -1, then each state a suspension leaves, with the fields and iterations it leaves, the dispatch branches decided by the state's constant), to a fixpoint; catch and finally handlers get the join of their region's states. It refuses (the hook stays vanilla) whatever it doesn't follow exactly: the walk or its enumerator used other than that (or a null test), a mixed walk value used, a finally or fault handler that writes, an exit whose state isn't a constant, stacks that differ where paths meet, a call passing the state machine to anything but the async method builders. (4) A run outside every listener iteration (every loop's phase "out" on every path and entry state) is accepted: HookSkip's premise (classify.py's patterns) is that a dropped listener's iteration changes nothing the rest of the hook reads, so the rest runs the same path; such a run runs as often, with the same values, as without this mod. (5) A run inside the iteration of exactly one walk loop (that loop "in", every other "out") is accepted only as a single call taking that loop's current listener, other arguments constants or unwritten arguments, whose method provably does nothing unless the listener is of some types (`TranspilerJudge.GatesOn`: TypeGates' shape on that parameter, `isinst` tests from the first instruction, then `ret`, or one constant or argument load and `ret` for a method returning a value, whose result the run pops; no static constructor in its class; not itself patched, asked at each recheck). Those types are the hook's gates: `FilterFor` keeps them like TypeGated defaults' gates (`PileFilter.WithGates`, combined with the defaults' gates), so a dropped listener is one whose call would have done nothing, and every kept one gets the same call with the same values. Nothing else: a plain call inside an iteration (its calls would follow the walk's length), the listener of another loop, a stale listener after its loop, two listeners.

**Where.** `HookSkip.BodyAllowed` (per hook, recomputed when HarmonyWatch's epoch or the judge's capture moved, each second without HarmonyWatch; the hot path compares two ints): BaseLib's allowed patches plus, when the judge accepts, every other mod's transpiler on the transpiled code, and the judged gates. `FilterFor` checks the hook (SurroundingOnly) and its state machine (strict) with that list: other patch kinds keep their rules. The preflight's load part asks the same (`Watch`'s lazy allowed list per hook), so `bdopt why` and the menu show only refused transpilers. Verdicts are cached per method while its transpiler list and capture stay the same; each is logged once: `HookSkip: Hook.X: other mods' transpilers judged by their IL (<owners>): N insertion(s) that change nothing this mod relies on[, gated on T (kept)]: kept`, or `refused (<why>)` followed by the usual back-off line. HookFilter is unchanged: its mirrors replace the hook's body (inserted code could never run there), so it backs off from every transpiler (PrefixesOnly), and the original body, transpiled, runs with HookSkip's filtered walk.

**What stays refused, and why.**
- *Minty Spire 2* (`dup; MethodBase.GetCurrentMethod(); call AfterHook` before `InvokeExecutionFinished` in four hooks' state machines): three reasons, each enough. A second call (`GetCurrentMethod`) runs inside the iteration, once per listener; `AfterHook`'s first instructions call `Harmony.GetOriginalMethod` before its `isinst PowerModel` test (no call is accepted before the gate: proving a library call has no effect needs a list of trusted members, which the rules exclude); its class has a static constructor (the `AfterHookPowers` dictionary). On `AfterCardPlayed` BaseLib's own transpiler (an awaited call: stores and branches) is judged with it, so that hook would stay vanilla with BaseLib anyway. The self-test's `minty-shape` and `cctor-gated-observer` cases are these near misses.
- *Downfall* (all its sub-mods) and *Watcher* on `ModifyDamageInternal`: the bridge's result is stored back into the loop's own local (`stloc`), and its arguments include the running total. Accepting it would need (a) the store proven an identity for non-implementers (the bridge returning its argument: a shape `GatesOn` could check) and (b) the total read at a kept iteration proven equal with and without this mod, which only holds through HookSkip's premise extended to every value live at that point (a liveness analysis plus the classification's no-op defaults): not provable from the IL alone here. Refused. HookFilter's damage mirror can never take such a bridge (it replaces the body), so damage previews keep the game's walk with these mods either way. **Reversed on 2026-11-17 by an owner-approved premise** ("Owner-approved premises", DMG): (a) is checked (`IdentityGatesOn`), (b) is answered by reading only locals the game's own code read just before in the same straight code.
- BaseLib's three hook transpilers stay accepted by fingerprint (their insertions store and branch: the judge refuses them).

**Proof.** Offline (a console harness loading the Debug DLL with each version's reference assemblies, scratch only): `foreignpatch iljudge selftest` gives 42 expected verdicts on v0.107.1 and v0.113.1 (AfterCardPlayed and BeforeCardPlayed state machines, ModifyDamageInternal: unchanged, prewalk observer, gated observer accepted; entry observer of a state machine, ungated observer, plain observer in the iteration, gated method with a static constructor, Minty's shape, a popped listener, the listener field or local read after its loop, a store, a call into the game, a jump, a reordered pair, a branch turned, a handler mark removed refused; an entry observer of a plain hook accepted); the sweep accepts both shapes on 151 of 154 hooks (v0.113.1) and 144 of 147 (v0.107.1): the three `AfterPreventing*` hooks pass the walk to `Contains` (not followed: refused). In game, pending (no session on this code): `foreign-il-2k-on`/`-off` on v0.107.1 and v0.113.1 (compare.py line for line: CHECKSUM and STATE, the gated calls and prewalk counts among them; the on side expects the accepted verdicts and `FOREIGN judged walks` > 0 for BeforeCardPlayed, AfterEnergySpent, ModifyHpLost, the refused verdicts and back-off lines for the ungated observer, store, game call, jump and the identity transpiler at priority 0 on ModifyDamageInternal, the self-test and sweep lines), then `foreign-controls`, `foreign-shapes-2k-on/off`, `effects-check`, `preflight-check` (the judge runs in the preflight's load part only for hooks with other mods' transpilers: none in the usual test set; BaseLib's alone don't trigger it) and `release-checks` (HookSkip 147/154 hooks, 0 left vanilla).

Left: the race of a hook walked on another thread between another mod's rebuild of that hook and HarmonyWatch's finalizer (the old verdict for microseconds), as for every cached check; a bridge store (Downfall) and pure library calls before a gate (Minty), both above; transpilers on an async hook's kickoff method (no walk there, SurroundingOnly refuses them as before).

### Game beta v0.113: sorting cards before a shuffle (2026-11-15, `CardSortKeys`)

v0.113 extended `CardModel.CompareTo`: after the id (`AbstractModel.CompareTo`) and the upgrade level, the enchantment and affliction ids (`ModelId.Compare`), `EnergyCost.GetResolved()` (in a combat pile: `Hook.ModifyEnergyCostInCombat`, two walks of every listener), `BaseReplayCount`, then each dynamic variable in `DynamicVars.Keys.Order(StringComparer.Ordinal)` order (allocated and sorted at each call). `ListExtensions.StableShuffle` sorts a copy of its list with it before shuffling: every draw pile reshuffle (`CardPileCmd.Shuffle`), Stone Cracker's `AfterRoomEntered` and 13 more card, relic and event methods. dotnet-trace of a combat start at ~1,900 cards on v0.113.1, mod on: `AfterRoomEntered` 959 ms, Stone Cracker's sort 856 ms of it; 6.5 s with 4 players (a co-op client then timed out). On v0.107.1 and v0.111.0 the comparison is the id and upgrade level only.

- **What**: inside a `StableShuffle` of a `List<CardModel>`, a prefix on `CardModel.CompareTo` answers with the same logic, each value read live but two computed once per card for that sort: the resolved cost and the ordered key list. The list's `Sort` is the game's: the same answers give the same order, bit for bit.
- **The scope**: a Harmony patch on `StableShuffle<CardModel>` itself is not possible: a generic method's code is shared by every reference type, Harmony compiles the replacement for the one instantiation it is given and detours the shared code, and the next `StableShuffle` of another type ran the `CardModel` replacement (checked outside the game with its 0Harmony 2.4.2 on .NET 9, the same method shape: `EntryPointNotFoundException` in `List<T>(IEnumerable<T>)` for the other type; in the game, the map's, events' and relics' shuffles of other types). Instead the 15 methods of v0.113.1 that call `StableShuffle<CardModel>` (found at load by scanning 15 listed types, state machines included; the whole assembly has no other caller) get a transpiler (`Priority.Last`) replacing that call with `CardSortKeys.StableShuffleInScope`, which opens the scope and calls the game's `StableShuffle`. PunchOfJoy and EcstasyPower shuffle a `List<Sadness>`: left alone. A caller a future version adds stays the game's.
- **Exact because nothing changes during the sort**: a scope opens only when every card's runtime type keeps `CardModel.CompareTo` (no override, no comparison interface of its own: the game would run other code) and, for each combat the cards are in (`CardModel.CombatState`, read once per card; a card in no combat pile runs no cost hook, `GetWithModifiers`' own test), every listener of that combat overriding `TryModifyEnergyCostInCombat(Late)` is a known pure one (`SnapshotCardReuse.PureHooks`, same fingerprints on v0.113, now internal) not patched by another mod, and the combat hook walk is the known one (`HookFilter.CombatHookWalkPatched`). Then a comparison only reads: the costs and keys can't change between two comparisons, and the cards' lazily created `EnergyCost` and `DynamicVars` are created at the same comparison as in the game (same reads, same order). Otherwise the whole sort is the game's (`Refused`, `LastRefusal`). Not kept: the ordered keys alone while costs stay live, which a side-effecting cost listener could make stale, and which saves little next to the cost walks.
- **What else could make two `GetResolved` of a card differ within a sort**: only a cost listener with side effects, or one reading something that changes during the sort; nothing else runs (the sort is synchronous; scopes open on the main thread only). The cost's local modifiers (`LocalCostModifier.Modify`) and `CostsX`/captured X value are fields. Excluded through the listener rule (Void Form, Brilliant Scarf, Ensemble, Free Card, and v0.113's Cho Limbs and Coda count the turn's plays: not in `PureHooks`, so their combats keep the game's sort; they only read, so a list of read-only listeners could extend this later).
- **Safety**: fingerprints required (v0.113.0 = v0.113.1): `CardModel.CompareTo` `F6388632B42CF646` (the v0.107.1/v0.111.0 one, `2CC3990F98A5EF89`, makes it "not needed": skipped, logged, not shown as missing: `OptionPart.NotNeeded`), `AbstractModel.CompareTo` `BB460DAC433857B6`, `ModelId.Compare` `9044DFF12964F53B` (called through a delegate: v0.113 only), `ListExtensions.StableShuffle` `68E6CDB3F3082D3C`, `CardEnergyCost.GetResolved` `5FAA75114FDAFC70` and `GetWithModifiers` `60FE75B459B24CFC`, `LocalCostModifier.Modify` `E11E84E9412802B6`, `Hook.ModifyEnergyCostInCombat` `081395225799F6BD`, the two `AbstractModel` cost hook defaults `DB289946DCE7F74D`, `CardModel.EnergyCost` `D6AF1AAE1A2FEE0D` and `DynamicVars` `19C079D25E68C079`, `DynamicVarSet.Keys` `E7FAD3B477754438`. Another mod's patch on any of them (and on `StableShuffle<CardModel>`) leaves the sort to the game. An exception faults the part (the game's comparison runs, its own exception again). One answer in 1,024 (Debug 16, `bdperf checks off` 1,024) is compared with the game's `CompareTo`: a difference faults it. Inside `ListenerCache.RunVanilla` no scope opens.
- **Setting**: a part of `pile_lookup` (card pile lookup: it already speaks of reshuffles; the option is unavailable only when neither PileIndex nor this applied). Its description and its 13 translations unchanged.
- **Proof hooks**: effects `CardSortKeys` (sorts answered) and `CardSortKeys.Answers` (comparisons), listed only where it applies; `counters` prints sorts, answers, refusals with the last reason, checks, mismatches; `bdperf sortkeys`: the local draw, discard and hand cards sorted with the whole mod off and with the part (the piles' order and two shuffled ones), then `StableShuffle` both ways from one seed: same order element by element, times both ways; `nothing to compare` when not applied or refused; restores the effect counters (only the game's sorts count). `bdperf sortkeys wrong`: answers reversed (positive control `sortkeys-wrong`, v0.113+ only).
- **Proof** (v0.113.1, Debug): `bdperf sortkeys` 959 cards, 87 kinds: the game's sort 1,245 ms, with the part 6.7 ms, identical orders (30,387 answers, 4,549 checked against the game's); `effects-check` 17 sorts, 153,177 answers, 11,480 checked, 0 mismatch, 0 refusal; `ab-2k` 54, `shuffle` 55, `fool-2k` 153, `combats3-2k` 63, `pile-cards-2k` 131, `clone` 4, `deckcount` 131 lines identical; every patched method rebuilt: `ab-2k` identical; co-op `effects-coop` and `coopfast` on/off identical per peer (58 lines), `coopclone`, `audit-coopdeath`, `coop4` agree; `coop4leak` (4 players, 20 run states) agrees with no disconnect (before: a client timed out at a combat start, its `AfterRoomEntered` frame 6.4 to 6.7 s, now 0.85 to 1.56 s); sweeps relics 4,740, cards 4,269, powers 6,139, encounters 3,321, potions 348 lines identical; `sortkeys-wrong` caught (`bdperf sortkeys` MISMATCH, the part off after its own check); mutations 40/40; on v0.107.1 and v0.111.0 "not needed", `ab-2k` identical, effects unchanged. A combat start at ~1,900 cards, nothing else running: `AfterRoomEntered` 0.42 s (0.92 s before; v0.107.1 0.10 s), longest frame 0.65 s (v0.107.1 0.52 s).

### Audit of 2026-11-13: BaseLib 3.4.8, Limitless 1.2.1, beta v0.113, every patch

Five read-only audits (listener caches; snapshot and relic reuse; hook filtering; Limitless and BaseLib patches with the settings; infrastructure and UI), then three reviews of the resulting diff. Each finding checked against the decompiled code before a change. Fixed:

- **Dead back-offs** (since they were written): five "another mod patches what this reproduces" checks started from `long.MinValue`, so `now - at >= 2000` overflowed and never ran: LimitlessReserialize, LimitlessHookSkip, three in SnapshotCardReuse (pile and card serialization, upgrade bits). Started at `long.MinValue / 2`; they don't fire in the reference setup (only BaseLib's allowed postfixes there).
