- **BaseLib SavedSpireFields on cards or enchantments** (a content mod's per-card field): their value is exported into the saved properties by BaseLib's `FromInternal` postfix, which the property plans, the per-card snapshot memo and `SavesNoProperty` didn't see (stale saved state, a checksum difference). `BaseLibTypes.SpireFieldTargets` (BaseLib's registry, read again when it grows) leaves such types without a shortcut; the stamps include the registry's size; `FromInternal` patches other than BaseLib's (fingerprinted) count as a conflict.
- **What a reuse skips calling** must back off when another mod patches it (the rule): SnapshotCardReuse's memo, trusted and moved piles (ToSerializable, SavedProperties, keyword getters); CardDbScan (`IdCardIfNecessary`, Limitless's ledger allowed); HookSkip and HookFilter (`Hook.IterateCombatHookListeners`, now fingerprinted for HookFilter too, with v0.113's `ShouldSkipCombatEffect` it calls, and the getters its ending check reads; HookSkip's choice-context helpers, checked once a second); DeckCountMemo (the counted getters, the count lambda); LimitlessCloneMemo (the whole clone chain: FromSerializable, saved properties, enchantment, upgrades, MutableClone, dynamic variables, CardCmd.Enchant, the card and enchantment types' overrides on the way; BaseLib's and Limitless's patches there allowed by fingerprint, Limitless's uncapped options in the stamp); PileIndex (`Player.Piles`; every add, remove and clear checked by its effect when another mod patches one; an add or remove another mod's prefix skipped, or did itself, judged by the pile's count: only for a prefix registered after PileIndex's, see "Audit of 2026-11-15"); HoverTipReuse, RelicFlashMerge, CardFlightLimit (the effects, sounds and souls they skip; SpeedX's soul postfix allowed); IdleButtonInput (`_Input`, the drag check).
- **LimitlessHookSkip turned on during a combat** dropped stacked enchantments its walks had never noted (one that reacts, e.g. SlumberingEssence on BeforeFlush: a desync): it now arms outside a combat only (the next combat's piles are noted as they fill), and needs LimitlessReserialize applied.
- **PileIndex**: a move whose event handler throws after the list changed was undone in the index (the card answered no pile); now kept when the pile changed by that card, `Fail` when it changed otherwise. Not applied when a run already exists (its cards wouldn't be indexed). Reorders always checked by their count.
- **CardFlightLimit**: a flush stands for several cards only when the pile's only listeners are pile buttons' own `AddCard` (not overridden, not patched); otherwise one event per card. Re-entrant flushes queue for the next one.
- **IdleButtonInput**: turned off (`bdopt`, a fault, another mod's patch), buttons it had muted got their input back only when pressed (`bdopt off` wasn't the unmodified game for idle buttons); now within 30 frames (about half a second at 60 fps).
- **Smaller**: PileCounterBatch's clear depth paired through `__state`; GridSortCache judges each comparer method, not each sort order; LimitlessRecalcSkip's quiet passes see BaseLib registrations; the snapshot verifier also runs while the listener cache backs off; FastListeners and BaseLibTypes find model and BaseLib-derived types through other mods' assemblies (transitive references), not by assembly name; the left run's listener lists released at `RunManager.CleanUp` (or at the end of the scope it ran in); ListenerCache invalidates on v0.113's dominion setter; `PatchSafety`'s allowed-patch cache keyed by the allowed set; the load check accepts a protected member only from a derived class (an update making a used member protected is now a clean skip).

Not changed, on purpose: the fingerprint algorithm doesn't hash a method's own signature, callees' return types or field types (a change there nearly always changes the IL that uses them; changing the algorithm would change every constant of three versions for no case seen); a virtual a patch prefixes could get an override in a subclass (CardFlightLimit, the only one relying on it, now checks the actual handler).

Proof (Debug, BaseLib 3.4.8, Limitless 1.2.1, the final DLL, no baselines; three full batteries, the last one after the last review's fixes):

- **Public v0.107.1**: `tools/compat` 0 problem; `release-checks` 0 part left vanilla, the `bdperf` comparisons OK; A/B `ab-2k` 54, `pile-cards-2k` 131, `combats3-2k` 63, `shuffle` 55, `clone` 4, `outside-2k` 48, `autoslay-2k` 613 lines identical; `audit-toggles-2k`, `audit-conflict-ab` and the new `audit-conflict-saves` (fake patches on the eight methods whose back-offs were revived or added: each back-off named in the log) identical to `ab-2k-off`; `effects-check` 53/56 (the usual three), CardDbScan, LimitlessCloneMemo, HoverTipReuse, DeckCountMemo, IdleButtonInput firing as before, no back-off in the reference setup; co-op `coopfast`, `audit-coopdeath`, `coopclone`, `outside-coop`, `outside-coopoff` peers agree; `turns-32k-on` 31,800 cards, 0 left vanilla, verify mismatches 0; mutations 38/38 (`limitless-body` now also turns LimitlessHookSkip off, on purpose); `baselib-optional.ps1`, `limitless-switches.ps1`, `settings.ps1` ok; with SpeedX, ModConfig and Unlimited `ab-2k` and `shuffle` identical, no back-off (SpeedX's soul postfix allowed); `pile-cards-off/on` at 8,400 cards 131 lines identical (second battery; the last round touched no path that depends on the deck's size).

### Co-op with a player without the mod (2026-10-24, verified)

Local co-op, ~3,750 cards per player, Workshop mods, the client's mod off (`bdopt off`) as the stand-in for a player without it:
- `coopvanilla` (both play at once): divergence at the first action, with v0.4.0 and with v0.3.0 alike. The state dumps (the game logs both sides' states on a divergence) show the actions ran in a different order.
- `coopsolo1` (only the modded host plays): divergence. The unmodded client took 33 s to start the combat; the host's first plays reached it meanwhile and were dropped (`ActionQueueSet`: "currently cancelling all combat actions"; Limitless logs these too: "combat action(s) were rejected since the last combat start (healthy sessions show 0 or 1)").
- `coopsolo2` (only the modded client plays): no divergence but a frozen run: the unmodded host dropped the client's plays and its end of turn, so the enemy turn never came.
- `coop` (both modded) and `coopoff` (both off): no divergence.

Cause: the game's own handling of actions received during a combat start, made near certain by a speed gap of tens of seconds (combat start ~4 s with the mod, 30+ s without; the faster combat start option alone skips ~70 s of waits). Hence `affects_gameplay: true` and `FastCombatStart` always on in co-op (a player with that option off would start ~70 s late). Verified: `coop-required.ps1` (client with this mod disabled) gets the game's "Mods mismatch: the host has mods we don't have: BigDeckOptimization-v0.4.0" and can't join; `coopfast` (both modded, the client's fast start off) skips the waits on both sides, identical states, no rejected action. Two modded players on very different PCs can still meet it, as in vanilla, with a much smaller window.

### Co-op desyncs (earlier hypothesis)

The group often desynced. `RecalculateCardValues` only runs for the local player, and Limitless' `MultiEnchantment.RecalculateValues` rewrites its serialized nested cards (`SetSerializableCards`), which are part of the checksum. SpeedX's own log also warns its multiplayer timer override risks desyncs. Closed: no desynced session's logs ever came, and the desyncs reproduced since were the speed gap of players with and without the mod ("Co-op with a player without the mod" above, Step J), hence `affects_gameplay: true`; Limitless's re-serialization stays the game's own work, only skipped when it would give the same checksummed content (`LimitlessReserialize`).

## Implemented patches (`src/BigDeckOptimization/Patches`)

Safety nets around every patch (v0.6.1, after v0.6.0 broke combat on game beta v0.111.0, where `AbstractModel.ModifyDamageCap` gained a parameter: HookFilter's prefix couldn't be JIT-compiled, so it threw on its first call before its `Enabled` check and try/catch, and every damage calculation threw even with the mod switched off):

- **Load check** (`PatchSafety.RequireResolvable`, ~25 ms for all patches): before applying a patch, `ModEntry.TryApply` resolves every member and type its class uses (nested types, lambdas and state machines included) and checks they are accessible. A member removed, changed or made private skips that patch (vanilla). `RuntimeHelpers.PrepareMethod` would also catch other JIT failures, but methods compiled that way skip tiered compilation: turns at 32k cards were ~2% slower.
- **Rollback** (`PatchGuard`): an Apply that fails halfway has the patches it already installed removed.
- **Runtime guard** (`PatchGuard`): every patched method gets a void finalizer (inlined null check, no measurable cost). Harmony 2.4 rethrows an exception unchanged only when every finalizer on the method, from any mod, is void; one returning an `Exception` makes it throw again, its trace then starting at the replacement method (the game's or another mod's frames lost, even with `bdopt off`: patches stay installed). All pass-through finalizers of this mod are void since the fourth 2026-11-15 audit pass (25 returned the exception); only `HookFilter.RethrowFinalizer` (7 mirrored hooks) returns one, because it substitutes an exception, and it logs the full trace first. An exception leaving a patched method through a frame of a patch class faults that patch (off for the session, menu says so) and removes all its Harmony patches on the next frame. Test: `faultprobe` (scenario `guard-checks`). It can't see a patch method the JIT refuses to compile (the exception comes from the replacement method, with no frame of the patch class): that case is the load check's (`PatchSafety.RequireResolvable` resolves every member a patch class uses before it applies; `[PatchPart]` methods, another game version's code, are checked before their part is used) and each prefix's own try/catch (an access failure is thrown at the call, inside it).
- **Fingerprints of what each patch relies on** (v0.7.0, `MethodFingerprint`): IL with tokens resolved to names, the compiler's numbering of generated members ignored (`<Hook>d__42`, display classes, lambdas: it shifts when the game adds a member above, with the same code), and the code of the generated methods it uses included (lambdas, local functions, state machines: a changed lambda changes its user's fingerprint). Besides the mirrored methods themselves, patches pin what their reasoning assumes: HookSkip and HookFilter the `AbstractModel` defaults they skip (a default that stops being a no-op leaves its hooks vanilla), HookSkip the choice-context helpers (`HookPlayerChoiceContext` constructor/`GetOwner`/`AssignTaskAndWaitForPauseOrCompletion`/`WaitForCompletion`, `PushModel`/`PopModel`, `InvokeExecutionFinished`) for the hooks using them. Patches whose caches are kept up to date by watching some methods check that no other code may change what those methods guard (`PatchSafety.RequireFieldWriters`: assignments, mutating calls, the collection passed to a method taking a mutable one; new readers don't count; a new writer would change no fingerprint): `CardPile._cards` (ListenerCache, PileIndex, FastListeners), `Player._relics` and `_potionSlots` (ListenerCache), `AbstractModel.ExecutionFinished` (FastListeners' subscriber tracking). `tools/fpdiff fieldusers <dir> <type> <field> [--writers]` lists them. HookSkip also backs off a hook whose state machine another mod patches (a transpiler there could add a walk that this patch's redirect would filter), except BaseLib's AfterEnergyReset and AfterCardPlayed transpilers (3.4.7 and 3.4.8, the same) (they only make the hook first await a method of BaseLib's own; transpilers and their injector `AsyncMethodCall.Create` fingerprinted) and BaseLib 3.4.8's prefix and transpiler on `Hook.ModifyBlock` (they apply the card's own modifiers to the block before the walks; patches and the two methods the inserted code calls fingerprinted). Until v0.6.4 this wasn't checked: those two BaseLib transpilers ran with the redirect (harmless, see above), any other would have gone unnoticed. A fingerprint from another game version is accepted when that code was checked to behave the same, so one DLL keeps the patch on both: per version where a generator re-validates it (HookSkip's table: `tools/hookskip/classify.py` classifies every supported version on its own code), by hand otherwise (`EquivalentFingerprints.cs`, each entry saying what changed: beta v0.111 `CardPile.RemoveInternal`, `SavedProperties.FromInternal`, `SetupPlayerTurn`; beta v0.113 `CardPile.AddInternal`, `Hook.IterateCombatHookListeners`, `NCardFlyShuffleVfx.PlayAnim`, `NHoverTipSet.Init`, `CheckMouseDragThreshold`, `CardModel.DeepCloneFields`, `AfterCloned`, `UpgradeInternal`; BaseLib 3.4.8's heal hooks; HookSkip's own table compares exactly, never through these equivalents), or by a mirror per version chosen from the fingerprint (FastListeners' walks, HookFilter's damage hook). `tools/fpdiff review <old> <new>` lists every fingerprinted method that changed between two versions with its IL and C# diff, the members it newly calls (with their code, and whether they changed) and renames; `tools/autotest/mutations.ps1` checks the reactions in game. Beta v0.111 with v0.7.0: nothing left vanilla (147 hooks), A/B `ab-2k-off/on` identical on both versions.
- **Errors inside async hooks** (v0.7.0): most hooks run in async state machines, where an exception only faults the hook's task and never leaves the patched method, so `PatchGuard` can't see it. The code of this mod running there falls back by itself: FastListeners' own pile-entry cache, on an exception, turns FastListeners off (HookSkip with it: its option lists both faults) and the walk adds every pile entry like vanilla (a superset whose extra entries keep the no-op defaults), so the hook completes normally; HookSkip's per-call checks turn HookSkip off and walk vanilla. Test: `bdperf fault piles`, scenarios `guard-async-on` vs `guard-async-ref` (same checksums). A method, field or type missing at load is logged as a skipped patch (update), not an error.
- **Harmony rebuilds** (`HarmonyRebuild`, v1.0.1): every Patch and Unpatch of this mod (`PatchGuard`) and every `InlineGuard` recompile builds the method's replacement again from all its patches, every mod's. Harmony refinds a stored patch method by module and token, which for one declared in a generic type gives the open generic method: that rebuild can't compile. And Harmony 2.4 undoes the method's detour before making the new one, so a failed rebuild left the method with no mod's patches at all (v1.0.0, see "Harmony rebuilds that dropped another mod's patches"). Now a rebuild is refused before anything is touched when a patch on the method can't be refound (`Blocker`) or carries a transpiler known to keep state between its runs (`StatefulTranspilers`: BaseLib 3.4.8's `ModInterop+QuickTranspiler` inserts a static list it reassigns before each `[ModInterop]` stub is patched, so a later rerun would give a stub the last stub's call; no installed mod uses `[ModInterop]`; InlineGuard's reach check doesn't rerun such a transpiler either), and a rebuild that fails anyway gets the previous replacement back (`PatchTools.detours` and `DetourMethod`, read by reflection and checked at load; without them `InlineGuard` rebuilds nothing). After the patches are applied, `HarmonyRebuild: all N patched methods detoured` is logged, or an error naming each method left without its patches. Tests: `rebuild-check` (also with `BDTEST_REBUILD_ALL=1`: every patched method rebuilt), `rebuild-control` (v1.0.0's way: must fail).
- **Off at start** (`ModEntry.StartedOff`): when BaseLib's saved settings have the whole mod off when the game starts, no patch is applied but `CoopModeTag`, `JoinMismatchNote` (display only, the explanation of the join refusal: not when this mod's code was rewritten, `ModEntry.Rewritten`) and the test tooling: switching it off and restarting always gives the unmodified game, whatever the bug. Turning it back on then takes effect at the next start (bdopt and the menu say so). Scenario `started-off` (run with `"Enabled": "False"` in the cfg).

| Patch | What | Scope / safety |
|---|---|---|
| `ListenerCache` | Materializes `CombatState.IterateHookListeners()` once and replays it | Only inside scopes: **snapshot** (`NetFullCombatState.FromRun`) and **refresh** (`PlayerCombatState.RecalculateCardValues`; the whole `CombatStateChanged` dispatch via a transpiler on `CombatStateTracker.CallCombatStateChangedDeferred` → one scope for hand, end turn button, intents, creature state, energy, orbs; `NHandCardHolder.UpdateCard`, `NCard.UpdateVisuals` when called outside it). Also caches `RunState.IterateHookListeners` (deck + combat). Invalidated (finalizers, so a watched method that throws after its change still invalidates; `ListenerCache.Apply` has the list) on `CombatStateTracker.NotifyCombatStateChanged`, every `CardPile` list method (`AddInternal`, `RemoveInternal`, `MoveToBottom/TopInternal`, `RandomizeOrderInternal`, `Clear`), `Player.Add/RemoveRelicInternal`, `RelicModel.IsMelted`, `Player.IsActiveForHooks`, the potion methods, `OrbQueue.TryEnqueue/Remove/Insert/Clear`, the `CardModel.Enchantment`/`Affliction` setters (not for an owned card PileIndex knows to be in no pile, `PileIndex.InNoPile`: a mod's previews and predictions no longer drop the kept lists; any doubt invalidates), v0.113's dominion and `RunModels` setters (`RunState`, `CombatState`), `RunManager.CleanUp`, and (fourth audit pass, optional, each a watch of its own) `CombatState.AddCreature/RemoveCreature/SortEnemiesBySlotName/SetEnemyIndex` and `Creature.ApplyPowerInternal/RemovePowerInternal`; field writers of `CardPile._cards`, `Player._relics`/`_potionSlots`, `OrbQueue._orbs` and the setters' fields pinned. A hand selection ended by a state change resumes the card's play inline in the dispatch scope: `NPlayerHand.RevalidateSelectionAfterStateChange` suspends the caches. Thread-static state; scope depth stored as depth + 1 in `__state` (0: our prefix never ran, nothing closed). `_safe` also needs `Apply` to have finished. Backs off while another mod patches the walks' code (`FastListeners.WalkCodePatchedByOthers`: iterators, `Contains`, ModHelper's subscriber walks). Other mods' postfixes on the walks themselves are let stand (`SurroundingOnly`, "Needless back-offs made safe"): such an "augmented" scope builds its lists from FastListeners' unpatched mirrors called directly (`UnpatchedCombat`/`UnpatchedRun`, never the game's call) and hands them to the game's call behind a plain enumerable (`Replay`), the postfixes running live; without the mirrors no scope caches (note `walk_mirrors`), and a mirror that declines mid-scope (another mod patched the walk code since the scope opened) gives the game's call, not a fault. `IsCaching` (HookFilter's lists) is false in augmented scopes, `Serving` true. See "Card sorts and stacked enchantments next to walk-wrapping mods (2026-11-20)". |
| `HookFilter` | Inside any `ListenerCache` scope (snapshot or refresh), `Hook.ModifyEnergyCostInCombat`, `Hook.ModifyKeywordsInCombat`, `Hook.ShouldPlay`, `Hook.ShouldAllowHitting`, `Hook.ModifyDamageInternal` (additive/multiplicative/cap, and v0.113's `ModifyDamageMultiplicativeLate`) and the cost colors' own copies of the cost loops (`CardCostHelper.TryModifyEnergyCostWithHooks` / `TryModifyStarCostWithHooks`, fingerprinted, base methods return false with the cost unchanged) only call listeners whose type overrides the `AbstractModel` hook (base = no-op: +0, ×1m, cap MaxValue, true), same order. Lists built from FastListeners' filtered walk per hook (cached per scope and version), or from the materialized list when FastListeners is off. | Mirrors the vanilla hook bodies; each hook fingerprinted (a changed hook stays vanilla), each skipped `AbstractModel` default too (a default that changed leaves its hook vanilla). Damage: one mirror per signature (v0.107 / v0.111's extra `CardPlay`), listener methods called through delegates made at load, so the same class loads on both. Each mirror lives in its own class with its own load check (`HookFilterEnergyCost`, `...Keywords`, `...ShouldPlay`, `...Hitting`, `...CostColors`, `...Damage`): a game member one of them uses that changes leaves only that hook vanilla. Backs off per hook if another mod patches the hook or its base method (BaseLib patches `Hook.ModifyDamage`, not `ModifyDamageInternal`); only the hooks whose game code walks `Hook.IterateCombatHookListeners` (energy cost, ShouldPlay, hitting) also back off when that walk or the combat getters it reads are patched (keywords, damage and star cost walk the listeners directly). The energy cost, ShouldPlay and hitting mirrors take the game's early exits (negative cost, combat ending) before building a list. A listener's own exception is rethrown as vanilla would (`RethrowFinalizer`: its trace reset, logged in full first). |
| `SnapshotVerifier` | Live safety net for the checksum: first snapshot of the session (plus the next two while cheap), then 1 in 25 within a 1% play-time budget, rebuilt vanilla and compared byte for byte. Skipped above 1,000 combat cards or after a re-check over 1 s (a vanilla rebuild freezes the game for seconds at that size) | On mismatch: vanilla snapshot is used, snapshot optimizations off for the session, error logged. Also runs the snapshot walk reuse's net first (`ListenerCache.EndSnapshotWalks`) and, on a detection, builds that snapshot again the exact way before the checksum reads it (nothing turned off; a rebuild that throws passes the game's exception on). |
| `IdleButtonInput` | Input processing off for idle `NButton`s whose `_Input` is NButton's own (drag-cancel), on while pressed: a prefix on Godot's `Node.SetProcessInput` (runs for every node; only eligible idle buttons are muted, every mute tracked), input back from the postfixes on `OnPressHandler`, `OnReleaseHandler`, `CheckMouseDragThreshold` and `ConnectSignals`; turned off, muted buttons get it back within 30 frames | UI only. `_Input` and the drag check pinned (v0.113's drag check equivalent); the writers of `NClickableControl._isPressed` pinned (`RequireFieldWriters`, same on the four versions): a new way of pressing leaves the patch off. |

Each comparer is replaced only if its fingerprint matches the vanilla lambda; the cost and title values live in their own classes (`GridSortCost`, `GridSortTitle`, own load check): a changed cost or title API only leaves those orders vanilla. The index map is exact only while `SetCards` sorts `_cardsCache` and refills `_cards` after the sort: `SetCards` fingerprinted (`224B19A92FC91776`, four versions), a change leaves only the obtained orders vanilla. The `SortingAlgorithms` getter's postfix returns at once once the current `SetCards` call checked that dictionary; whether another mod patches a comparer is asked once per comparer delegate and call (a reference compare per comparison). The scope ends in a postfix at the highest priority (`int.MaxValue`): only another postfix at that priority registered earlier re-sorts inside it. |
| `EnchantmentTabReuse` | Limitless only (applied when its assembly loads, skipped if absent): mirrors `CardExtensions.UpdateEnchantmentVisuals` but reuses the card's existing tabs instead of freeing and duplicating them | UI only. Off unless the mirrored method's fingerprint matches (Limitless v1.1.7 to v1.2.1, same code), with what the mirror relies on (`CardExtensions.SetEnchantmentStatus`, `TabStride`, `EnchantmentTabHost.EnsureAttached`, fingerprinted on 1.2.1); backs off if another mod patches it. A new tab is added to the host after it is configured, in Limitless's order; an exception before that frees it, then falls back. |

Fingerprints of the 4 mirrored methods required (static `Free`, `Free(T)`, the explicit `INodePool.Free`, the walk); only the game's constructed `NodePool<>` classes are mirrored (BaseLib's `GeneratedNodePool<T>` or a mod's own `INodePool` stays vanilla, judged per class before any member is read); another mod's patch on any pool's mirrored methods keeps every free vanilla; names re-checked vs vanilla for the first 3 nodes per type then 1 in 64, any difference turns it off. |

Not while a mod defines a concrete BaseLib `CardModifier` (its subscriber's output changes with nothing watched; looked up only in the loaded "BaseLib" assembly). 1 reuse in 16 rebuilt and compared; a difference turns reuse off for the session (fault `ListenerCache.Reuse`). |

The query lambdas are checked strictly; on the state machine other mods' prefixes, postfixes and finalizers stand (they run outside the `ToList` calls), and so do their transpilers when Harmony runs them before this mod's (`Priority.Last`): the transpiler redirects the calls only when the code feeding them is the game's (`TranspilerJudge.FeedsUnchanged`; RitsuLib's hand size rewrite there passes), else a note (`first_turn_code`) and the game's calls. | `FastCombatStart` | Visual concession: while the combat loads, from entering a combat room (not a pre-finished one) until its first turn starts or the combat is reset (prefix on `CombatManager.StartTurn`: room entry and "start of combat" effects only; the first turn's start-of-turn effects, draw and everything after keep the game's pace), `Cmd.Wait`/`CustomScaledWait` return at once (like the game's Instant speed). Until v0.6.1 the window only closed on a wait during the local player's play phase, which in solo often never came (the game sets every phase to `None` outside the player's turn): whole combats ran without waits; scenarios `pace-on/off/vanilla/instant` time the turns (identical to vanilla). Option `instant_combats` (v0.6.2, off by default): every wait of every combat instant, this player only. Option `fast_combat_start` and its threshold, solo only: always on in co-op whatever the relics (master switch and faults still apply), so no modded player starts ~70 s after the others; the BaseLib menu shows a note about it at the top (which also says that the whole mod can't be turned off during a co-op run without confirming, see `bdopt`). A wait that is no longer async is left to the game alone. `SkippedWaits`/`SkippedSeconds` count only waits the game would really have made (not its non-interactive mode, not at Instant speed, a positive time). |
| `Settings` / `SettingsMenu` / `BaseLibSettings` | Master switch + one switch per optimization (effective = master && option && not faulted, `PatchSafety.Fault`). With BaseLib (optional), shown in its mod configuration (`SimpleModConfig`, one static bool property per option, saved in `mod_configs/BigDeckOptimization.cfg`, bdopt changes saved too; menu texts merged into the `settings_ui` table at runtime, no pck, English and the game's 13 other languages, the same keys in each: `package.py` checks it). `BaseLibSettings` lives in `src/BigDeckOptimization.Menu` (own DLL, built by the main project's `BuildMenu` target): the game calls `GetTypes()` on a mod's DLL, so a type deriving from a BaseLib class made the whole mod fail when BaseLib wasn't loaded yet or at all. `SettingsMenu` loads that DLL in the mod's load context once BaseLib is `Loaded` (now or via `ModManager.OnModDetected`: mods load in the player's order). A switch whose patch can't run (`Settings.Why`: Limitless missing or changed, game code changed or the patch failed to apply (`ModEntry.NotApplied`), faulted) is greyed out and not clickable, its hover text starting with the reason (`BaseLibSettings.SetupConfigUI`, texts rebuilt each time the page opens). Limitless-only switches (`limitless_tabs`, `limitless_saves`): the box shows ticked only while the patch is applied and wanted; the wanted state is saved in hidden properties (`LimitlessTabsWanted`, `LimitlessSavesWanted`), so a box shown unticked without Limitless comes back ticked once it is installed (the shown values are ignored when loading and excluded from Restore defaults). During a co-op run (`Settings.InCoopRun`: a run in progress, not singleplayer) the master switch can't be switched either way (greyed, its hover text giving `why.coop_run`, or `why.coop_run_on` when the mod is off; not after a start with the mod off): a guard only, BaseLib 3.4.8 opens its mod configuration from the main menu alone (`InjectSettingsModConfigPatch` pushes it on `NMainMenuSubmenuStack` only; in a run its button logs "Unable to open"), so in a run only the console switches the whole mod, after `bdopt off yes` / `bdopt on yes`. Each optimization's title is coloured by its state (`Settings.StateOf`, the same one `bdopt` and `bdopt why` print): orange when part of it runs the normal game (a back-off or note recorded for one of its parts, a part faulted or not applied while others run), red when it is switched on but nothing of it runs (not applied, faulted entirely, every part off or kept vanilla as a whole: `PatchSafety.WholePatch`/`WholeMethods`, a whole note), the normal colour otherwise, also when the player switched it or the whole mod off, when the mod started off, or when Limitless isn't installed for a Limitless option (nothing to speed up). The game's `StsColors.orange`/`red` (`FFA518`/`FF5555`) as the title label's `default_color` theme override (BaseLib's row label, a `RichTextLabel` named `Label`; a row without one keeps its colour). The open page follows `Settings.StatesChanged` (a back-off or note recorded or dropped, the startup preflight done, a switch): one deferred refresh per frame of its hover texts, greys, colours and summary line, nothing checked again; the fast-start relic slider is greyed, without input, whenever `fast_combat_start` itself is (started off, faulted, unavailable), with that option's reason | Without BaseLib: all patches, `bdopt` session-only. A later `RecalculateValues` on it returns at once while all of it holds and every inner enchantment type keeps the game's empty `RecalculateValues` and saves no property (then the inner calls are no-ops and `SetSerializableCards` would keep the list); the memo's values are re-read at each call. On top, whole passes: a `RecalculateCardValues` pass is skipped while it would be quiet, tracked through setters: prefixes on `EnchantmentModel.Amount`, `CardModel.CurrentUpgradeLevel`/`FloorAddedToDeck`/`Enchantment`, `MultiEnchantment.SerializableEnchantmentsOnCards`/`AddEnchantment`/`DeepCloneFields` and a finalizer on `CardPile.AddInternal` bump a quiet counter (main-thread bumps in its low 32 bits, other threads' in the high bits: a pass seeing only an off-main bump with nothing changed is not a mismatch). This relies on InlineGuard (a setter inlined into a caller would be missed) and on `RequireFieldWriters` at load (the saved list's backing field written only by `.ctor` and its setter, its private getter called only by `get_EnchantmentsOnCards` and `DeepCloneFields`: else only pass skipping stays off). One pass in 64 runs for real and is checked: a memo not holding then means a change went unseen, and pass skipping stops for the session | Checksummed state: `party5-on/off` CHECKSUM/STATE identical (190 lines). One skip in 1,024 re-runs the full comparison (release builds too): a difference or an exception turns it off for the session (the rest of `limitless_saves` keeps working). Fingerprints: `MultiEnchantment.RecalculateValues`, the `EnchantmentsOnCards` getter and `DeepCloneFields` (Limitless 1.1.7 to 1.2.1), `EnchantmentModel.RecalculateValues` (empty), `PlayerCombatState.RecalculateCardValues`, `AllCards` (v0.107.1 to v0.113.1). Needs `LimitlessReserialize` applied (its prefix fills the memo); backs off with it or when another mod patches these (rechecked every 1,024 calls, no clock read per card; `AllPiles` gets no back-off: BaseLib transpiles it, and quiet passes read the same getter). |
| `SnapshotCardReuse` | With `LimitlessReserialize` (BaseLib + Limitless): the co-op checksum after every action (`NetFullCombatState.FromRun`, also in solo from v0.111 on) builds `card.ToSerializable()` for every combat card, with BaseLib's extended save data (a ConditionalWeakTable entry per new SerializableCard / SerializableEnchantment). A transpiler replaces only that call in `NetFullCombatState.CardState.From` (exactly one call, else skipped; the rest of `From` stays the game's): the card's previous result (kept per card) is used again when `LimitlessReserialize`'s field-by-field comparison says `ToSerializable` would hold the same; otherwise the game's call, and its result becomes the kept one. The snapshot only serializes and keeps it (printed on a divergence), never changes it. Step S: a pile with written cards reads only those (each write marks its card in the pile it was last read in: write-marking prefixes on `CardEnergyCost` and the other written members), and piles stay trusted while only known pure cost or keyword listeners stand with the same amounts (a prefix on `CombatPileState.From`). Card bits (a prefix on `CardState.Serialize`) and pile bits (a transpiler replacing `PlayerState.Serialize`'s one `WriteList<CombatPileState>` call, checked to be exactly one before and after patching) reuse what was written; their back-off list includes `WriteModelEntry` and `WriteInt`, which the upgrade bits write into. `Player.AfterCombatEnd` releases the combat's entries. A cost or keyword listener throwing inside the calls the game's `From` also makes lets the game's `From` run (that pile untrusted, `GameExceptions`), so the game's own error doesn't fault the part; an error in `card.ToSerializable()` still does. | Checksummed: `party5-on/off` identical (the `checksum` command uses `FromRun`); co-op `party5coop`: no divergence. One reuse in 2,048 is serialized next to a fresh `ToSerializable` (release builds too): a bit-length or byte difference or an exception turns it off for the session (fresh objects from then on; the rest of `coop_checksum` keeps working). Off if `LimitlessReserialize` faulted or another mod patches what its comparison mirrors. |
