All patches catch their own exceptions and fall back to vanilla.

Changes to existing patches (2026-10-30, Step O):

- `FastListeners`: affliction changes of a card in a pile bump `AfflictionVersion` instead of `PileVersion` when the old and new affliction types are both known (types are recorded the first time they are seen, by the setter or a snapshot rebuild; a change involving a new type bumps `PileVersion` as before). Combat snapshots depend on both versions (an affliction change rebuilds them, no catch-up across one); a filter's segment survives affliction changes when the filter keeps no subscriber and reacts to none of the known affliction types (`AfflictionFree`: the known set only grows, so free now means free of every change since). Hex no longer rebuilds `ShouldAfflict`'s entries for each card. `HookFilter`'s walked lists are keyed on `EntriesVersion` (both versions). `bdperf segments verify all` (`party5-verify`): 0 mismatch over ~107,000 cached walks.
- `PileIndex`: the owner's piles are read without `Player.Piles`' `Concat` iterator (two allocations per `CardModel.Pile` call) while that getter has its v0.107.1/v0.111 code (`_runPiles` field, combat `AllPiles`); otherwise through the getter as before.
- `HookFilter`: `ForCombat`/`ForRun` look the walked list up before creating the walk lambdas (two closures per call, even on cache hits: ~70 MB per 13 s of co-op plays at 4,100 cards).
- `ListenerCache`: materialized listener lists are sized from the previous count (thousands of entries grown by doubling allocated twice as much, the last arrays on the large object heap).
- `Settings`: parts of an option (`LimitlessRecalcSkip`, `SnapshotCardReuse`, `TextPoolReuse`) turn themselves off alone after an error (`Part`), and don't make their option unavailable when not applied.

### Back-offs visible before they happen (2026-11-15)

A player with a big mod pack (RitsuLib, Zone the Spire, Spire Synergies, Ascended Expansion, Master of Mankind, CVC...) reported the mod "isn't working": nothing broke, but the back-offs turned most combat optimizations off, unseen. They were recorded only when each optimization first checked its methods (the first combat), some weren't recorded at all (another mod's types or data), and the menu still showed every box ticked.

- **Registry** (`PatchSafety.Watch`, `WatchAll`, `WatchPairs`, `Patches/PatchSafetyWatch.cs`): each patch registers, where its Apply already resolved them, the methods its runtime `IsPatchedByOthers` checks pass, with the same allowed lists (a lazy list where the patch builds it once mods are loaded, e.g. HookSkip's BaseLib patches, CardDbScan's Limitless ledger, CardFlightLimit's SpeedX postfix), under the name the runtime check records (its file name). Registering only stores; it never throws (it runs inside Apply, where a throw would take the patch away). Methods known only later are sources asked at each preflight: the walk code (ListenerCache), HookSkip's helper profiles and the combat hook walk (once a hook's call to it is redirected), BaseLib's card modifier subscribers, the clone chain (LimitlessCloneMemo), pools (PoolSignalScan), and what depends on who is in a combat: the listener types met so far (CardSortKeys' cost hooks, SnapshotCardReuse's pure overrides, DeckCountMemo's per-type getters, LimitlessCloneMemo's overrides on the way). Not registered: CardSortKeys' keyword reads (only checked in a combat where a cost hook reads keywords).
- **Preflight** (`PatchSafety.Preflight`): checks every registered entry with the runtime checks' own code (`CheckPatched`: every `IsPatchedByOthers` overload ends in it, the tolerant ones after their own shape check, `AggressiveInlining`: same hot-path cost), so a back-off is recorded under the right patch; display only: the runtime checks keep deciding, sharing the same 2 s cache (same computation, same answer). At startup `PreflightOverFrames` starts after `Patches applied` is logged (outside its timing), in up to three parts (owner's decision, 2026-11-16: rather a little more loading time than a settings page showing less than it should; "Needless back-offs made safe", "Preflight timing"). **(1) During the game's loading**, only when the patches finished before the main menu showed (a start slowed by many mods: a real session with ten Workshop mods resolved 617 entries there in 9.3 ms): every registered entry resolved (the patches' lazy allowed lists and sources: reflection on other mods' types) **and checked** (Harmony lookups, shape rules), in one go; the entries are read in rounds until none is new (a late batch registering during it is checked too). Usually the menu shows first (every test log: the remaining patches then apply at once on its frame) and this part is empty. Since P2 (2026-11-20) the patches are only all done once the menu shows, so part (1) no longer runs; parts (2) and (3) are unchanged. **(2) At the main menu**, whatever is left (each entry's resolution its own step, one that failed at load tried again) and then the note checks (they need every mod's own setup done: the list of note checks is read only when they start, so a late batch's note is in it), within 3 ms per frame (a run starting meanwhile gets the rest at once). **(3) On demand**: the settings page or `bdopt` opened before (2) ended runs the rest at once (`FinishStartup`, a one-off pause of tens of ms at most), then its usual synchronous pass: never a half-checked state shown. Logged once: `Preflight: N methods and notes checked; during loading S steps in X ms, longest Y ms (patch); at the main menu M steps, Z ms over F frames, longest step W ms (patch)[; finished on demand (by): K steps in V ms] (done T ms after the main menu): B back-offs, O notes (...)`, with `during loading nothing (the menu showed first)` or `at the main menu nothing` for an empty part (a step can't be split: a note's scan of every BaseLib-dependent assembly is one, its frame may go past the budget: measured there first, before splitting it). Needless cost removed first: another mod's patch method allowed by name is looked up in that mod's assembly only, when it is loaded (`PatchSafety.MethodIn`: SpeedX's postfix for CardFlightLimit, Limitless's ledger for CardDbScan, BaseLib's hook transpilers for HookSkip); `AccessTools.Method("Type:Method")` on a mod not loaded ended in Harmony's `TypeByName` fallback, every type of every loaded assembly listed and compared twice, in one main-menu step. The settings page (`SetupConfigUI`, before its texts) and `bdopt` run it again synchronously (cached answers reused). Nothing before every patch applied (allowed lists are built once mods loaded) or when the mod started off.
- **Notes** (`PatchSafety.Note`/`ClearNote`, checks registered with `WatchNote`, run by the preflight only): silent back-offs, with the mods responsible. BaseLib card modifier types (FastListeners' subscriber kept, ListenerCache's cross-scope reuse, LimitlessReserialize's card modifier save; the mods defining them, `BaseLibTypes.DefiningMods`), BaseLib type line modifiers (TypePlaqueSkip), SavedSpireFields on cards or enchantments (LimitlessReserialize's plans, SnapshotCardReuse's memo, LimitlessRecalcSkip's plain types; the mod is the code behind the field's default value, else its target type's mod), other mods' BaseLib card/enchantment saves (no memo: SnapshotCardReuse, LimitlessReserialize) and relic saves (RelicStateReuse off; the mod is each save's getter code), and other mods' types met in combats that leave a sort (CardSortKeys: own sort order, cost hooks not known to only read) or the pure reads (SnapshotCardReuse) to the game.
- **Display**: `Settings.ConflictingMods`/`Conflict` include the notes' mods ("other mods" when a note names none, key `other_mods`); the settings page shows a line under the co-op note when anything is recorded (`Settings.ConflictSummary`, key `conflict_summary`: each mod once, by name only since d113d41 (the owner's choice; most back-offs first, a mod's hidden IL hooks under its own name), the counts staying in `bdopt why` (a method watched under several patch names or tolerances counted once there); only the parts that run count: an option switched off, faulted or not applied, or the whole mod off, shows nothing); `bdopt why` (`Settings.WhyReport`) lists them; `bdperf conflicts` prints the same with the preflight counters (Debug).
- Not proved in a game yet: see the roadmap's checks for it.

### Startup P1 and P2 (2026-11-20)

Commit 735ba02 (Bench: 7bc0671, `--bdstartprof`). **Before** (main f60fec7): with the intro logo the main menu froze once for
100-155 ms about 0.37 s after it showed, on every configuration: RelicFlashMerge's note step building its three code sets
(`CodeSet.Compute` x3, `LazyGuard.Find`), then a GC; CardFlightLimit's sets added 20-50 ms on the menu. Without the logo,
InlineGuard took its quarter-frame share of the 1.3 s frame that loaded the menu: 320-340 ms of rebuilds in the menu's first frame.
With the logo, InlineGuard's steps and the preflight's loading part were the logo's remaining 30-60 ms hitches.

- **P1: the code sets are built on a background thread while the game loads** (`Patches/BackgroundBuild.cs`, below-normal
  priority, as LimitlessCloneMemo's closures worker). Its result is published whole (`Volatile`); the main thread never waits:
  `RelicFlashMerge.Sets()` and `CardFlightLimit.FlightSet()` take it if done, else build the sets themselves as before, through
  the same functions (`BuildSets`, `BuildFlightSets`). Only the game's IL and types go in (no patch state); every answer that
  depends on other mods (`CodeSet.AnyPatched`, `AnyHasPatches`, `ForeignOverrides`, `LazyGuard.Shut`, `LatchedShut`, the strict and
  note checks) is still asked at use time on the main thread. Thread safety reviewed (`MethodFingerprint`'s concurrent state, as
  the closures worker already used it; Bench's `LoadTicks[0]` now an atomic add). A failed background build logs one line and
  leaves the build to the main thread. Background build 120-180 ms (RelicFlashMerge) and 40-90 ms (CardFlightLimit) of wall
  clock, done before the menu in every rendered session; `startsets` found both parts' sets from the background build.
- **P2: InlineGuard's rebuilds run on the main menu, 4 ms a frame** (`ModEntry.RecompileOverFrames`): the patched methods are
  listed when every patch of the start is in, as before; the steps wait for the main menu (latched; 30 s at most) and for
  HookSkip's queued hooks (`HookSkip.HooksPending`), then run within `ApplyBudgetMs` a frame (the share of the previous frame is
  gone). A run under way gets the rest at once in its first frame (logged), before its first combat: the guarantee is kept
  (`startup-early-on/off`). The preflight starts after the rebuilds, so all of it runs on the menu at its 3 ms a frame.

**Effects** (rendered, one game at a time, base 7bc0671 vs 735ba02, 2 runs each; PresentMon on Release, `--bdstartprof` on
Debug). Longest frame of the menu's first second (ms, v0.111.1 / v0.113.1): alone 145 -> 25 / 127 -> 33, BaseLib+Limitless
117 -> 34 / 112 -> 30, RitsuLib 111 -> 27 / 137 -> 29, BaseLib+Limitless with the logo skipped - / 65 -> 30; 15 mods without
the logo (in-game, the menu's own first frame is 240-400 ms without this mod) 572 -> 235 / 625 -> 283, this mod's work in it
371 -> 14 ms. The RelicFlashMerge note step at the menu: 108-153 -> 4-66 ms (what is left grows with the back-offs). Frames over
33 ms in the menu's first 5 s: BaseLib+Limitless 1 -> 0, RitsuLib 2 -> 2 (one rebuild and one note step, both unsplittable),
15 mods 6-7 -> 4; frames over 16.7 ms rose from 2 to 4-7 (the rebuilds spread over the menu's first second). Logo frames over 33
ms: BaseLib+Limitless 2-4 -> 0-1, RitsuLib 2 -> 0-1; alone unchanged (ApplySome's spread calls, P3). Time to the main menu:
unchanged beyond noise. Tests: `startup-early` and `relicflash-ctl` pairs identical, `foreign-flights-controls`,
`foreign-parts-flights`, `preflight-check`, `audit-parts-visible`, `release-checks` no problem, `effects-check` all ran,
`bdopt why` line for line identical with 735ba02's base in four mod configurations, mutations 45/45 on both versions.

**The owner's two decisions (2026-11-20).**
- **InlineGuard's larger, stable rebuild set is kept.** Before, the steps ran beside HookSkip's batches: a replacement judged
  before a hook it reaches was patched (protected) wasn't rebuilt, one judged after was, so the set depended on how frames fell
  (RitsuLib on v0.113.1: 251 one launch, 252 the next). Now every replacement is judged after all hooks are protected: the old set
  plus `NCreature.OnPowerIncreased`, `Player.OnRelicFlashed`, `NCardFlyShuffleVfx.Create` (they reach
  `Hook.ShouldStopCombatFromEnding`) and `PotionRewardOdds.Roll` (`Hook.ShouldForcePotionReward`), the same at every launch
  (v0.111.1 / v0.113.1: BaseLib+Limitless 163 -> 166 / 166 -> 169, default test mods 172 -> 176 / 176 -> 180, RitsuLib 250 -> 250 /
  251-252 -> 252); never a method lost, about 3 ms each. Alone the list itself depends on the frames, before and after alike.
- **The preflight's part (1) now runs on the menu: accepted** (the patches finish only once the menu shows).

### Foreign patches judged by their shape (2026-11-15)

**Not released: audited (2026-11-16, the rules tightened below, "Audit of the shape rules"), still to be proven in game** (synthetic shapes A/B, `coop-foreign` with `parcoop -OnOff`, `BDTEST_EXTRA_MODS` with RitsuLib, Zone the Spire, SynergyMod and CardAscension on v0.107.1 and v0.113.1, solo and co-op).

The same player's mod pack (previous section) kept most combat optimizations vanilla through back-offs from patches that can't change what this mod relies on. No per-mod list and no fingerprint of another mod's code (each of their updates would need checking again): the rules are generic, decided from what Harmony says about each foreign patch (its kind; its method's return type and parameters) and, for type gates only, a strict IL shape. A patch matching no rule keeps today's back-off.

- **`PatchTolerance.SurroundingOnly`** (`Patches/PatchSafetyTolerance.cs`, `PatchSafety.IsPatchedByOthers(method, [allowed,] tolerance)`): only at call sites where the original always runs and this mod's code around it keeps its effect identical (no prefix of this mod skips it: HookSkip's transpiled `Hook` methods, the pile methods), or where every prefix of this mod that may skip it backs off from any other mod's postfix itself: on the five HookSkip rows whose `Hook` method HookFilter also mirrors with a skipping bool prefix (`ModifyEnergyCostInCombat`, `ModifyKeywordsInCombat`, `ShouldAllowHitting`, `ShouldPlay`, `ModifyDamageInternal`), a postfix is let stand only because HookFilter's `PrefixesOnly` check of the same method turns the mirror off then (the original runs): an invariant both call sites name in a comment (`HookSkip.FilterFor`, `FilteredHook.IsSafe`). Let stand: a **postfix** whose parameters are all by value except `__result` (the caller gets what it leaves, as without this mod) and `__state` (its own prefix's), not taking `__args` (Harmony's `RestoreArgumentArray` writes the array back into the argument slots) nor `__runOriginal` (it differs wherever a prefix of this mod may skip the original); a by-reference argument or instance is the replacement's own argument slot (`ldarga`), which this mod's finalizers read after every postfix (PileIndex, FastListeners, CardDbScan on `AddInternal`). Neither takes an instance field of a reference type other than string (`___name`, Harmony's field injection: the field's object itself, e.g. `List<CardModel> ____cards`, the pile's own list, which the patch could change behind the methods this mod tracks; a value-type or string field can only be read; re-audit 2026-11-16). A **prefix returning void** with the same parameter rules (only `__state` by reference: SynergyMod's `AddInternal` prefix; it may read `__runOriginal` by value, since sorted before every prefix of this mod it sees what it would without this mod), not taking `__args`, and sorted by Harmony (`PatchProcessor.GetSortedPatchMethods`: priority, before/after, registration) before **every** prefix of this mod on the method, each of its copies (`PatchInfo.Add` doesn't deduplicate: the last copy decides), except this mod's `[OrderFreePrefix]` prefixes (PileIndex's and CardDbScan's `WatchAdd`/`WatchRemove`: the pile count they take is only read when the tracking prefix didn't run; FastListeners' `TakeRunFilter` and HookSkip's `DropPendingFilter` since the fourth pass's delta: no argument, never skipping the original, a void prefix after them can only leave the walk unfiltered; the test tooling's clock prefixes). Before every prefix, not only the skipping ones: one sorted after a tracking prefix runs between this mod's bookkeeping and the list change (a walk it makes cached at the version the change is read at, a nested add recorded out of the pile's order); one after a skipping prefix runs after the mirror's computation, or not at all (Harmony skips a prefix that "affects the original", `MethodCreatorTools.AffectsOriginal`, once an earlier one returned false). The tracking prefixes on `AddInternal`/`RemoveInternal` are therefore late (`Priority.Last + 1` for FastListeners' bump and CardDbScan's `RecordAdd`, `Priority.Last` for PileIndex's update, their order among themselves unchanged): another mod's prefix at the default priority runs before them. Not let stand anywhere: bool prefixes, by-ref prefixes, transpilers, finalizers, inner prefixes and postfixes, factory patch methods (returning the patch to use: Harmony runs them, and its sorter would too), patches with a `HarmonyArgument` on the method, a parameter or the class (Harmony binds the real name, `InjectedParameter.CalculateRealName`: a harmless-looking parameter can be `__args`, `__state` or an argument), a patch whose method can't be found or classified.
- **`PatchTolerance.PrefixesOnly`**: for a call site where a prefix of this mod answers for the original and skips it (HookFilter's mirrored hooks and cost color helpers): only the void prefixes SurroundingOnly lets stand. No postfix: behind a skipping prefix Harmony still runs every postfix, where the game would not always (a listener exception the mirror passes on, `PassOn`/`RethrowFinalizer`: the game runs no postfix after a throwing original; a postfix there could also call another mirrored hook whose finalizer throws the outer exception and a `try` in the postfix swallow it), and a postfix reading `__runOriginal` sees false there.
- **`PatchTolerance.TypeGated`** (`PatchSafety.IsPatchedByOthers(method, out Type[] gates)`): only for an `AbstractModel` default whose calls this mod skips for listener types keeping it. A postfix there would miss the skipped calls, so only a prefix or postfix that provably does nothing unless `__instance` is of some types stands, and those types are reported. `PatchSafety.TypeGates` (analysed once per patch method, `MethodFingerprint.Instructions` decodes the IL): from the first instruction, nops aside, only tests `ldarg __instance; isinst T; [stloc n; ldloc n;] brfalse|brtrue L` along the path a non-matching instance takes (isinst gives null: brfalse taken, brtrue falls through), unconditional branches, then the no-op exit: `ret` for a void patch, `ldc.i4.1; ret` for a bool prefix. Nothing else on that path: no call, no field or static write, no other argument read; or-ed tests (`is A || is B`, a `switch` on the type) chain; a postfix returning a value never matches; T a reference type (class or interface), not a value type nor an open generic (a closed generic is accepted, matched with `IsAssignableFrom` like `isinst`, variance included). Also refused: a patch whose own method is patched by anyone (the code that runs is its replacement, not the IL read; asked at each recheck, not cached), one whose class has a static constructor (`TypeInitializer`: its first call would run it at another time, or never; none of SynergyMod's gated classes has one), one renaming its parameters (`__instance` found by its declared name only once no `HarmonyArgument` can rebind it). A detour made without Harmony isn't seen. Checked offline on Release-compiled copies of the report's shapes (`if (__instance is Stomp stomp && ...)`, `if (!(__instance is Stomp stomp) || ...) return true;`, `is A || is B`, a type switch, an early return) and near misses (a static write or a call before the test, another argument tested, the negated test doing work, a bool prefix returning false, `GetType() ==`, an empty patch): all as intended.
- Every answer shares PatchSafety's 2 s cache; a check with a tolerance first reads the plain check's answer, so with no other mod's patch the cost is the plain check's. With one let stand, a cached answer costs the tolerant dictionary lookup and, for the few runtime call sites sharing it (PileIndex, FastListeners and CardDbScan on `AddInternal`), reference compares instead of a logging lookup (`TolerantAnswer.LoggedFor`, four slots for runtime sites only: the preflight's patch names, which share the same answer, always take the logging lookup, so they never push a hot site out). What a patch method's own code decides (factory, renamed parameters through attribute data, no attribute constructor run; return type; parameters) is worked out once per method, kind and tolerance (`ShapeRefusal`); only the prefix order and whether the patch method is itself patched are looked at again at each 2 s recheck. `PatchSafety.ToleratedChecks` (Debug) counts runtime checks only, not the preflight's. The prefix order is Harmony's sorter run once per change of a method's prefix list (`Precedence`, keyed by owners, indexes, priorities, before/after and methods), with each patch's method set from `PatchMethodOf`'s cache first (the sorter otherwise resolves each one through `AccessTools.GetMethodByModuleAndToken`, a search of every loaded module), never with a factory in the list (the sorter would run it). A back-off from a tolerant check is recorded and logged "(postfixes and void prefixes not enough)" / "(void prefixes not enough)" / "(patches gated on the listener's type not enough)", keyed by tolerance too (a patch checking one method strictly for one part and tolerantly for another doesn't flip its record at each call); patches let stand are logged once per patch, method and mods ("... (postfixes and void prefixes: run around this mod's code as without it): kept", gated ones with "X treated as overriding it"). Every changed check registers its tolerance with `PatchSafety.Watch` (same arguments), so the preflight shows the same answers. Test builds count `PatchSafety.ToleratedChecks`, `GateAnalyses`, `GatedMethods`, and per runtime call site (the preflight passes a patch name and isn't counted) the checks kept and backed off (`PatchSafety.RuntimeReport`), and HookSkip's walks filtered with gates per hook (`HookSkip.GatedWalkReport`); `bdperf conflicts` lists what was let stand (`PatchSafety.ToleratedReport`).

Where applied, and why it's exact there:

- **HookSkip, the `Hook` method itself** (SurroundingOnly, allowed BaseLib patches kept; other mods' transpilers there or on its state machine judged by their IL since 2026-11-16: "Foreign transpilers judged by IL"): the transpiled body calls the same listeners with the same effects, only no-op calls left out, so a postfix (RitsuLib's ~53 lifecycle postfixes wrapping the returned task, `ref Task __result` and arguments by value; Zone the Spire's, CardAscension's) or an earlier void prefix sees the state it sees without this mod. On a hook that also carries a HookFilter mirror (a bool prefix of this mod), a void prefix must precede it here too. **Not** on an async hook's state machine (`MoveNext`): a patch there can read the machine's fields, the walk's enumerator among them, which the redirect makes another object. Left as is: a postfix of an async hook runs once the hook first awaits, the walk suspended, and a subscription it made then to a later dropped listener's `ExecutionFinished` isn't signalled (`Entry.Subscribed`'s accepted gap, which a listener's own code already had; no mod read subscribes).
- **HookSkip, the `AbstractModel` defaults** (TypeGated): `FilterFor` gives a filter in which types assignable to a gate also react (`PileFilter.WithGates`: a new filter with its own caches, segments and kind verdicts, kept while the gates stay the same; stacked Limitless enchantments are never dropped there, their inner calls reach the patched default and LimitlessHookSkip's inner check doesn't know the gates). Gate types keep going through the game's call, the patch running as without this mod; every other type's call would have done nothing (the patch's no-op path, then the default). Several gated defaults of one hook: their gates combined once per set of PatchSafety answers (`Entry.LastCombined`, compared by reference: no allocation per walk, and the same array for `WithGates`).
- **HookFilter, the mirrored hooks and the two cost color helpers** (PrefixesOnly): the mirror's prefix answers for the original and skips it, so only void prefixes Harmony runs before it stand (as before the original without this mod; one at the same priority registered later runs after the mirror and backs off, `foreign-controls`); every postfix backs off (PrefixesOnly above). `_passOn` isn't cleared at a mirror's entry: with no other mod's postfix or finalizer there, nothing runs another mirrored hook between `PassOn` and `RethrowFinalizer`, and clearing it would let a nested mirror erase an outer pending exception. A caller using these lists to skip the hook call altogether keeps the strict check on the hook (`HookFilter.HookMethodOf`): SnapshotCardReuse's fast reads (local keywords and cost, reused pile states) check `Hook.ModifyKeywordsInCombat` and `Hook.ModifyEnergyCostInCombat` with no tolerance, since a skipped call skips even a void prefix.
- **PileIndex, `CardPile.AddInternal`/`RemoveInternal`** (the self-check that also makes `PlacesIn` unsure, SurroundingOnly): a tolerated patch can't skip the original or change its arguments (nor the argument slots the finalizer reads afterwards: postfixes by value), so `BeforeAdd`/`BeforeRemove` (Priority.Last, void) always run and the finalizer judges throws from the list (`Judge`); a card a postfix moves goes through these methods again and is counted there. The in-flight check (`_moveOthers`, a card mid-move gets the game's scan) still sees any patch, without recording a back-off (`PatchSafety.HasOtherPatches`): it isn't one, the index keeps answering. Clear, the moves and the shuffle keep no tolerance. Under the self-check (a changed list method), a postfix's nested add to the same pile makes the count check fail: the index faults, the game's scan from then on.
- **FastListeners' tracked code, `AddInternal`/`RemoveInternal`** (SurroundingOnly): they were listed because an earlier prefix returning false makes Harmony skip the tracking prefix (it takes the card); a tolerated patch can't. A tolerated void prefix runs before `BeforeAdd`/`BeforeRemove` (now `Priority.Last + 1`): a walk it makes is cached at the version before the bump, never at the bumped version with the pile not yet changed. A card moved by a postfix bumps `PileVersion` twice more inside the outer call, whose finalizer then sees `PileVersion != From + 2` and records no move: the segments are rebuilt. The getters (`Cards`, `Enchantment`, `Affliction`) and the other writers keep no tolerance.
- **CardDbScan, `AddInternal`** (SurroundingOnly, both at combat start and per walk): `RecordAdd` (a prefix taking the card) always runs; a card a patch puts in a pile goes through `AddInternal` again and is recorded, its own `ContentsChanged` walks it; a tolerated void prefix runs before `RecordAdd` (now `Priority.Last + 1`), so a card it adds to the same pile is recorded first, in the pile's order; the 1-in-64 whole-pile check stays. The walk and `IdCardIfNecessary` keep no tolerance (the walk is skipped, a patch there or on the calls it makes would miss them).
- **LimitlessCloneMemo and LimitlessHookSkip, the clone chain's card methods one level down** (`CardModel.AddKeyword`, `RemoveKeyword`, `get_MaxUpgradeLevel`; TypeGated, re-audit's second pass): a memo hit or a dropped stacked enchantment skips the whole build of a clone's inner card, these calls included, and every one of them runs on that inner card (the enchantments' `OnEnchant` call `base.Card`'s, the upgrade cap is the card's own). A patch there that provably does nothing unless the card is of a gate type changes nothing for a card of another type: the chain's answer keeps the gated methods with their gates (`ChainPatchedFor(cardType)`), the memo checks them against each saved card's type and LimitlessHookSkip against Limitless's storage card (every inner card); a card of a gate type gets the strict check of that method (its back-off recorded). CardAscension's `AddKeyword` bool prefix, gated on its `ShreddedSoul`, no longer turns both off (it did since the chain went one level down on this branch). RitsuLib's `NCard.FindOnTable` prefix stays a back-off (not gated), and RitsuLib already patched three methods of the older chain strictly (`AbstractModel.MutableClone`, `SavedProperties.FillInternal`, `DynamicVar.Clone` postfixes): both parts were already off with RitsuLib in v1.0.3. The other chain methods keep no tolerance.
- **CardSortKeys, the cost hooks' defaults** (TypeGated): a sort runs the cost hook once per card instead of per comparison; a gated patch stands while no listener of the combat is of a gate type (then each of its calls does nothing, however many); a gate-type listener refuses that sort ("... gated on it").

Not applied, and why (each call site says so in a comment): HookSkip's choice-context helpers without a listener argument and every helper's state machine (a dropped listener skips its calls to them; since 2026-11-16 per hook only its own helpers, and those taking the listener let patches gated on it stand: "Needless back-offs made safe", class 4), BaseLib's hand size/heal/downgrade methods (a void prefix after HookSkip's would take the pending filter for its own walk), the walk code (`IterateHookListeners` itself since 2026-11-16 only for the walks served directly, "Needless back-offs made safe"; its iterators, `Contains`, ModHelper's subscriber walks, `Hook.IterateCombatHookListeners` and the getters it reads: a postfix changes what the mirrored or replayed walk yields), every getter this mod answers from its own state or reads around (`CardModel.Pile` and `Player.Piles` until 2026-11-16, "Needless back-offs made safe", `CardPile.Cards`, `Enchantment`, `Affliction`, keywords, `GetWithModifiers`, DeckCountMemo's getters), the serializers SnapshotCardReuse, RelicStateReuse and LimitlessReserialize reuse output of, every method whose call this mod skips or merges (HookFilter's defaults, SnapshotCardReuse's pure overrides, DeckCountMemo, CardFlightLimit, HoverTipReuse, IdleButtonInput, LimitlessCloneMemo except its three gated card methods above, LimitlessRecalcSkip, LimitlessHookSkip, ReplayStateSkip, RelicFlashMerge, TypePlaqueSkip, PileCounterBatch, RichTextEnvRelease, ExtraScopes' first-turn query lambdas and BetterSpire2 part (its `SetupPlayerTurn` state machine lets other mods' prefixes, postfixes, finalizers and transpilers sorted before its own `Priority.Last` one stand, their code judged by `TranspilerJudge.FeedsUnchanged`: "Fourth pass (delta since f7e4f3d)"), PoolSignalScan, EnchantmentTabReuse), PileIndex's `RunState.ContainsCard` and Clear/moves. HookFilter's defaults keep no tolerance at all, gates included: its per-type override masks are cached for the session and its lists per scope, SnapshotCardReuse's pile trust reads those lists (a gate-type listener would be trusted as pure while the gated patch runs at each cost read), so gates there need both keyed by the gate set first. SnapshotCardReuse has no default check of its own (its pure listeners come from HookFilter), and no tolerance on the keyword and cost hooks it skips. Every SurroundingOnly site relies on "no code but the game's writers changes `CardPile._cards`" (`RequireFieldWriters`, game code only): a tolerated postfix writing that list directly (reflection, a field reference) isn't told apart from its shape; a mod doing it from any other code (a `ContentsChanged` handler) was never seen either. PileIndex's count check stays off with tolerated patches (on, a nested add through `AddInternal` would fault it); CardDbScan's sampled whole-pile check stays.

Real cases. SynergyMod from its shipped DLL (`ilspycmd -il`; the first count came from the decompiled C#, which hides a copy of an argument into a local before the type test): 88 patches on `AbstractModel` defaults, **75 gated** (the auditor's replica of `AnalyseGates` over the IL), 13 not: 5 postfixes on `ModifyCardPlayCount`, 2 patches on `BeforeCardPlayed` wrapped in `try` or its `SynergyPatchSafety.Run`, and 6 starting `ldarg.k; stloc.0` before `ldarg.0; isinst` (StompZeroCostPlayedPatch and RebootPlayableCheckPatch on `AfterCardPlayed`, StompReturnNextTurnPatch on `AfterPlayerTurnStart`, HappyFlowerPotionAdvancePatch on `AfterPotionUsed`, RebootExtraTurnConsumedPatch on `AfterTakingExtraTurn`, RebootExtraTurnRequestPatch on `ShouldTakeExtraTurn`): those hooks stay vanilla, `AfterCardPlayed` among them (accepting such a pure copy prologue would recover them; not done, the rule kept as strict as checked). None of its gated classes has a static constructor or a `HarmonyArgument`. Its `CardPile.AddInternal` void prefix (default priority, `ref __state`, card by value) now precedes this mod's tracking prefixes and its postfixes (by value) and `RemoveInternal` postfix are let stand; its 8 `Hook` patches (postfixes, void prefixes taking `CardPlay`/`CardModel` by value) are let stand by HookSkip except a default-priority void prefix on a hook carrying a HookFilter mirror. CardAscension patches no `AbstractModel` default (its ShreddedSoul gates test a `card` argument): 0 type-gated matches; its 4 `Hook` postfixes (`ModifyKeywordsInCombat`, `ModifyCardPlayResultLocation`, `ModifyPowerAmountGiven`, `BeforeCombatStart`) are let stand by HookSkip, while HookFilter's keyword mirror and SnapshotCardReuse's keyword reads now back off from the first; its bool prefixes elsewhere still back off. Zone the Spire's 6 `Hook` postfixes and RitsuLib's lifecycle postfixes (`ref Task __result`, arguments by value): let stand by HookSkip; HookFilter backs off from any postfix on a mirrored hook (Zone's `ShouldAllowHitting`). **Expected** for that player (not measured): the pile index, the cached filtered walks and the card id walks back on with SynergyMod; most hooks filtered again, but not `ModifyCardPlayCount`, `BeforeCardPlayed`, `AfterCardPlayed`, `AfterPlayerTurnStart`, `AfterPotionUsed`, `AfterTakingExtraTurn`, `ShouldTakeExtraTurn` and those another of their patches keeps vanilla; HookFilter's damage and energy cost mirrors stay vanilla inside refresh scopes (SynergyMod patches their defaults), its keyword and hitting mirrors too (CardAscension's, Zone's postfixes); CardSortKeys still the game's (CardAscension's `GetWithModifiers` prefix).

Less for CrossVersionCompat to rewrite: `tools/compat` on the Release DLL listed, as BDO's only references to game members missing on v0.113.0/v0.113.1, FastListeners' two `[PatchPart("scaling")]` tails (`CombatState`/`RunState.MultiplayerScalingModel`). They now read the property through compiled getters made only on v0.107.1/v0.111.0 (`Getter<>`, as v0.113's run models and dominion), the same object (an auto-property; the combat tail reads it once instead of twice); the walk fingerprints choosing the tail are unchanged. `tools/compat`: 0 problem and no unresolved part on v0.107.1, v0.111.0, v0.113.0 and v0.113.1. This came in with 08b0c48 but doesn't depend on the shape rules: it stays when they are switched off (`PatchSafety.ShapeRules`) or their commits reverted (cherry-pick it then); not a player-facing change, so no CHANGELOG item.

Tests (`Bench/ForeignPatches.cs`; script `foreignpatch`, console `bdperf foreign`; Harmony ids `bdtest.foreign.<shape>`; each install logs `FOREIGN installed` and `PatchSafety.Describe`'s verdict on every other mod's patch of the method, `FOREIGN classify ...: SurroundingOnly tolerated|backs off (why); PrefixesOnly ...; TypeGated ...`; `foreignpatch report` adds, Debug only, `FOREIGN runtime <Patch> <Type.Method> (<tolerance>) kept N backed off M` from the runtime checks alone and `FOREIGN gated walks Hook.X N`):

- Tolerated: `noop-postfix`, `noop-prefix [priority N]`, `count-postfix`, `count-prefix` (calls counted per target), `nested-postfix` (on `CardPile:AddInternal`: a card added to a discard pile takes the draw pile's last card along through `RemoveInternal` + `AddInternal`, the game's own methods), with effects the checksums see: `keyword-prefix squeeze` (void, priority 800, on `Hook:ModifyKeywordsInCombat`: Squeeze gets Retain; HookFilter's mirror and HookSkip let it stand, SnapshotCardReuse's local keyword reads must back off or the checksums differ), `cost-postfix squeeze` (`ref __result` on `Hook:ModifyEnergyCostInCombat`: Squeeze costs 1 more; HookSkip lets it stand, HookFilter and SnapshotCardReuse back off), `probe-prefix`/`probe-postfix` (the added card's cost before and after each `CardPile:AddInternal`, at the default priority: a hook walk over the cached piles while a card moves, summed as STATE lines), `state-prefix` (SynergyMod's `AddInternal` shape: a void prefix taking `ref __state`, its postfix summing the pile's growth), `count-prefix` also on the async `Hook:BeforeCardPlayed` (RitsuLib's `Before*` shape), `gated-postfix squeeze|relic`, `gated-prefix squeeze`, `gated-or-postfix squeeze-relic` (Squeeze overrides no hook; gated calls counted per target; two gated defaults of `AfterCardPlayed`, three of `AfterPlayerTurnStart`: combined gates). The gated shapes live in a class without a static constructor (`ForeignPatches.Gates`). `foreignpatch report <label>` prints the counts as STATE lines (compare.py compares them), then the lines above; `foreignpatch require tolerated` fails unless a check let one stand.
- Must back off: `bool-prefix` (returns true), `ref-prefix` (`ref __result`, or `ref __runOriginal` on a void method), `identity-transpiler`, `noop-prefix` at the default priority on a HookFilter mirror (sorted after it) or at priority 0 on `RemoveInternal` (after the tracking prefixes), `noop-postfix` on a HookFilter mirror (HookFilter backs off, HookSkip keeps it), `runoriginal-postfix`, `byref-postfix` (`ref CardModel card`), `argsrename-prefix` (`[HarmonyArgument("__args")]`), `factory-postfix`, `dup-prefix` (one method registered at priority 800 and again at the default: the later copy decides), near misses of a type gate: `nearmiss-postfix squeeze` (a static write before the test; also in the A/B, every call counted as a STATE line, so a near miss taken as gated would change a count), `renamed-gated-postfix squeeze` (`[HarmonyArgument("card")] __instance`), `gated-patched-postfix squeeze` (its own method patched), `cctor-gated-postfix squeeze` (a static constructor); `inner-postfix` (classified only: Harmony 2.4.2 can register one, `PatchProcessor.AddInnerPostfix`, but nothing gives it its inner method, so Harmony can't build it; a synthetic `HarmonyLib.Patches` goes through `PatchSafety.Describe(method, patches)`, the listing of every kind's list a real method gets). `foreignpatch classify <Type:Method>`, `foreignpatch remove`.
- Scenarios: `foreign-shapes-2k-on`/`-off` (ab-2k with the tolerated shapes and the near miss installed after the imports: CHECKSUM and STATE lines, the foreign counts and sums included, must match line for line; the on side expects the "kept" and back-off lines, positive counts for every gated shape, the probes and the near miss, `FOREIGN runtime` lines for each site (kept, or backed off where the rule says) and `FOREIGN gated walks` on the gated hooks, `effectcheck` HookSkip, PileIndex, FastListeners.Cache and HookFilter grew, `foreignpatch require tolerated`; the off side alone runs `bdperf checks off`: no counting shape on the cost color helpers, which the Debug re-check calls again), `foreign-controls` (the shapes that must back off, each on its own hook so a default's runtime check isn't cut short by a control on its hook: classification lines and `FOREIGN runtime ... kept 0 backed off N` lines the preflight can't produce), `coop-foreign-host`/`-client` (coop with tolerated shapes on both peers, `parcoop -OnOff`: the mod-off twins keep the `foreignpatch` lines, only `foreignpatch require` goes; the shapes installed before the imports and any combat, then a barrier, so every peer and its twin count the same calls; the reports after a barrier; parcoop compares each peer's `STATE foreign` lines with its twin's, besides the turn and run states; a `foreignpatch` shape changing state is refused once a co-op combat is set up, and from the console in co-op). `field-postfix` (a `List<CardModel> ____cards` postfix on `RemoveInternal`) is one of the controls that must back off. `fakeconflict <Type:Method> [boolprefix]`: `conflict-check` puts a bool prefix on the cost color helper (a postfix there backs off too now).


#### Audit of the shape rules (2026-11-16)

An adversarial audit (four parts: Harmony semantics, call sites, gates, tests; 31 findings, each confirmed by a verifier) of 08b0c48. The owner's stance: no per-mod list, generic rules; where equivalence with the game running those mods without this mod isn't provable at a call site, tighten the rule there rather than add machinery. What changed:

- **HookFilter's mirrors and cost color helpers: no postfix** (`PrefixesOnly`). Behind a skipping prefix Harmony runs every postfix, also on the listener-exception path where the game runs none (and a postfix calling another mirrored hook could get the outer exception thrown at it and swallow it), and hands them `__runOriginal` false. Only void prefixes sorted before every prefix of this mod stand there.
- **SnapshotCardReuse keeps the strict meaning**: its fast reads skip `Hook.ModifyKeywordsInCombat`/`ModifyEnergyCostInCombat` altogether, so it checks them with no tolerance itself (it had inherited HookFilter's: a keyword or cost postfix would have been missing from the co-op checksum snapshot, CardAscension's shape).
- **Prefix order against every prefix of this mod** on the method, not only the skipping ones (a void prefix after FastListeners' bump cached the pile before the change at the version of the change), every copy of a patch registered twice, `[OrderFreePrefix]` watches aside; the tracking prefixes on `AddInternal`/`RemoveInternal` made late so other mods' default-priority prefixes still precede them. The order is computed once per change of the prefix list, without Harmony's module search, never running a factory.
- **Postfix parameters** (SurroundingOnly): by value only, `__result` and `__state` aside; no `__args`, no `__runOriginal` (the finalizers read the argument slots after the postfixes).
- **Real names**: any `HarmonyArgument` on the patch method, a parameter or its class backs off (all tolerances, the gates' `__instance` included).
- **Gates**: refused when the patch method is itself patched (checked at each recheck), when its class has a static constructor, when it renames parameters; SynergyMod's count corrected from the shipped DLL (75 gated, 6 more hooks vanilla). Combined gates cached per set of answers (no allocation per walk).
- **Factory patch methods** back off everywhere.
- **Tests** can now fail when a rule is wrong: shapes with effects the checksums see on each tolerated site, a near miss counted in the A/B, controls each on its own hook with runtime-only `FOREIGN runtime` lines, `inner-postfix` through PatchSafety's own listing, a co-op pair whose mod-off twins keep the foreign patches.

What remains, accepted and documented at the call sites: a postfix of an async `Hook` method subscribing to a later dropped listener's `ExecutionFinished` during the suspended walk (`Entry.Subscribed`'s gap, which listeners' own code already had); a tolerated patch writing `CardPile._cards` through `__instance.Cards` cast to its list, reflection or code outside a patch (no shape rule sees it; the same as any other mod code doing it; a `___cards` field parameter is refused since the re-audit); PileIndex under its self-check (a list method patched or changed): a tolerated postfix or a game event handler moving another card into the same pile during an add, or a tolerated void prefix that moved one and then threw, faults the index (the count check expects only the call's own card: conservative, the game's scan from then on; not a regression: before the shape rules any `AddInternal` patch turned the self-check on); a detour made without Harmony on a gated patch method; SynergyMod's six `ldarg; stloc` prologue gates left vanilla. Not tested: a throwing listener under a mirrored hook with a foreign patch (no postfix stands there any more; a void prefix runs before the mirror as before the original), interface and enchantment gates (Limitless's `_noMultiDrop` path), the cost color helpers' runtime lines (the helpers run for the UI). None of the scenarios has been run yet.

#### Re-audit of the fourth pass (2026-11-16)

A read-only re-audit of every change on the branch (eight parts: Harmony shape rules, their call sites, back-off visibility, co-op, walks and caches, the other patches, the test tooling, the docs; each finding confirmed, refuted or left uncertain by a verifier), then the fixes. Code:

- **Co-op both ways**: turning the whole mod **on** in a co-op game needs `bdopt on yes` too, the menu's master switch is locked both ways during a co-op run, and `bdopt` with the mod off there gives a neutral line (all on or all off), not "the others start fast". The co-op save's load lobby (`LoadRunLobby`) counts as a co-op lobby. v0.111+ keeps the join list from when hosting or joining started: `CoopModeTag.Refresh` retags the lists handed out at every switch, so the next handshake compares the current state ("Console commands", the `CoopModeTag` row). The confirmations say a player who drops can only rejoin once everyone has the same state.
- **Shape rules**: a reference-type instance field injected by Harmony (`___field`, e.g. `____cards`) is refused under SurroundingOnly and PrefixesOnly; what a patch method's code decides is cached per method, kind and tolerance, its attributes read as data (no other mod's attribute constructor runs at each 2 s recheck); the logged-call-site slot holds four sites (PileIndex, FastListeners and CardDbScan share `AddInternal`'s answer); `ToleratedChecks` counts runtime checks only. Docs only: a void prefix may read `__runOriginal`; `[OrderFreePrefix]`'s count is also read after a prefix threw (the index then faults if the pile moved by another card: kept strict, the message says "threw"); PileIndex's tolerance comment names every prefix of this mod and its self-check's limit.
- **Back-off visibility**: the strict checks' runtime back-offs are counted apart from the preflight (Debug `CountRuntime`), and the back-off scenarios assert them (`runtimebackoff`); `preflight-check` is the preflight's own control. The per-mod counts count each method once, whatever patch names watch it; `bdopt why` and the menu line leave out parts switched off, faulted or not applied, and say when the whole mod is off. The conflict sentence is passive in every language (it agreed with no list: "d'autres mods modifie"); `bdopt` says "partly vanilla because of". An interface-keyed SavedSpireField counts for the cards' note. HookSkip asks once whether BaseLib is loaded for the preflight (it scanned every assembly's name per watched hook without BaseLib). The preflight logs its longest step (a note's scan can't be split: measured first).
- **Walks and caches**: the CombatStateChanged dispatch scope requires the fingerprints of `NPlayerHand.OnCombatStateChanged`, `RevalidateSelectionAfterStateChange` and `SelectCards`' body (the code its suspension relies on), else the whole scope stays vanilla; the v0.107.1 scaling getters bind to the property getter (no expression compiled at load); pile bits run only once their install check passed and while the transpiler still replaces exactly one call; CardFlightLimit re-queues a lost flush from the next soul made, kept or skipped.
- **Test tooling**: a mod-off co-op control is launched off (`launchoff` line, `--bdoff`: the whole mod off for the session in a prefix on `NMainMenu.CheckCommandLineArgs`, before the `--fastmp` lobby exists; `ModEntry.StartedOff` now reads the saved choice, `MasterChoice`), since `bdopt off` in that lobby asks for `yes` and v0.111+ built its join list with the mod on; parcoop's `-autooff` twins and the hand-written off scenarios do so and read the join list back (`coopmodetag off`); `coop-modetag.ps1` runs a mixed pair (refused) and an off pair (joined). The co-op scenarios switching the mod back on mid-run say `bdopt on yes`. `coop-foreign` installs its shapes before the imports, with barriers, and parcoop compares the `STATE foreign` counts; `foreignpatch` refuses a state-changing shape after a co-op combat is set up, `bdperf foreign` refuses it in co-op. The no-Steam-deploy hook takes a repeated property's last value and refuses `AllowSteamDeploy=true`.

Refuted by the verifiers (no change): HookFilter's mirrors at the default priority (mods patch at load, before this mod's deferred batches), a postfix on an async hook subscribing mid-walk (already documented, and only pile entries are dropped there), ListenerCache's warm-up during mod loading (it runs on a later frame). Left as they are, documented: PileIndex's self-check limit (above), the suspension's in-game proof (a hand selection ended by a state change: the tests' fights answer selections automatically; since the second pass `handselect` holds one open, below).

Proof: pending, with the fourth pass's ("Audit of 2026-11-15, fourth pass"), plus `coop-modetag.ps1`, `preflight-check`, the off controls (`coopoff`, `coopclonevan`, `coopleakoff`, `outside-coopoff`, `parcoop -OnOff`) launched off on both versions, and `foreign-controls`' `field-postfix`.

**Second pass** (2026-11-16): the same eight parts re-read on 586bb44, 31 findings (30 confirmed, 1 uncertain), then the fixes. Code:

- **LimitlessCloneMemo / LimitlessHookSkip, a regression of this branch**: the clone chain's new deeper entries were checked strictly, so CardAscension's `CardModel.AddKeyword` bool prefix (gated on its `ShreddedSoul`) turned both parts off with Limitless + BaseLib + CardAscension. The card's own `AddKeyword`, `RemoveKeyword` and `get_MaxUpgradeLevel` are now checked type-gated (`IsPatchedByOthers(method, allowed, out gates)`, a new overload): a gated patch stands for cards of other types, the memo checks the gates against each saved card's type, LimitlessHookSkip against Limitless's storage card; a card of a gate type gets the method's strict check ("Foreign patches judged by their shape", where applied). The RitsuLib half of the finding was refuted by its verifier: RitsuLib already patched three methods of v1.0.3's chain, both parts were already off with it. The chain's answer is one published object (another thread may ask). The preflight registers the gated entries with their tolerance (`WatchPairs` takes one now).
- **Test builds judged like Release** (the prefix order rule): the test tooling's read-only prefixes (CombatStartTimer's on `Hook.AfterRoomEntered`/`BeforeCombatStart` and the combat room, PerfProbe's) only read the clock: `[OrderFreePrefix]` (its doc now covers them), so another mod's default-priority void prefix there is let stand in Debug as in Release. `foreign-shapes-2k-on/off` add such a prefix on both hooks and expect "kept" and `FOREIGN runtime HookSkip Hook.BeforeCombatStart (SurroundingOnly) kept`.
- **FastListeners' filtered walk**: on an exception it now refills the walk unfiltered first and turns itself off only when that succeeds; an exception the unfiltered walk throws too (the game's or a content mod's getter) reaches the hook as vanilla's, FastListeners staying on (before, it went off for the session and the log blamed this mod).
- **The co-op lobby check** (`Settings.InCoopLobby`): a node type of another mod with a field typed from an assembly not loaded threw on `FieldType` and aborted the whole walk (no warning in a lobby). Each field and node is now read in its own try (the readable fields cached per type, one log line), the rest of the tree still walked.
- **Startup cost (VIS-1, and the owner's decision on where the preflight runs)**: other mods' allowed patch methods are looked up in their own assembly only, when loaded (`PatchSafety.MethodIn`; SpeedX's for CardFlightLimit, Limitless's ledger for CardDbScan, BaseLib's hook transpilers for HookSkip, also on the runtime path): `AccessTools.Method("Type:Method")` on a mod not loaded listed every type of every loaded assembly, in one main-menu step. The startup preflight resolves every entry at load when the patches finished before the menu (otherwise at the menu, each its own step: third pass) and runs the checks on the menu's frames; the `Preflight:` line logs both parts' times and longest steps ("Back-offs visible before they happen").
- **`bdperf opt` both ways**: `bdperf opt on` in a co-op run needs `yes` too, as `bdopt on` (not after a start with the mod off).
- **`bdperf sortkeys`' Veilpiercer case**: it matched the refusal's text by a suffix the text never has, so a keyword-listener refusal (by design) failed the bench; CardSortKeys now records the refusal's kind (`LastRefusalKeywordListener`, an unknown keyword override or a known adder another mod patches) and the bench tests that.
- **Visibility**: LimitlessHookSkip registers the clone chain and the storage card's overrides under its own name too, so `bdopt why` and the menu still name the mods when LimitlessCloneMemo is skipped or faulted; back-offs are told apart per method, not per `Type.Method` name (overloads and generic instantiations counted apart, printed with their signatures when two would print the same); the menu hover shows both the parts-off and the conflict sentences when both hold; SnapshotCardReuse registers its pile method only once the pile part applied, and HookSkip forgets a hook's registration when its transpiler fails (`PatchSafety.Unwatch`: the hook and its state machine; since the third pass also its `AbstractModel` defaults no other filtered hook keeps, the version's shared helpers staying). French `why.coop_run_on` ("les autres ont rejoint la partie", not "vous ont rejoint": wrong for a client).
- **A started-off game** takes part in the co-op check (`CoopModeTag`): the menu hover (`enabled.desc`), `why.started_off` (14 languages), `bdopt`'s status and the load log line say so.
- **`TolerantAnswer.LoggedFor`**: the preflight's patch names, which share `AddInternal`'s answer with the three runtime sites, take no slot (they pushed a hot site out).
- **A kill switch for the shape rules** (`PatchSafety.ShapeRules`): false makes every tolerant check the strict one, so a release can leave them out until their proof (docs/RELEASE.md says so; the CHANGELOG comment too).
- **Test tooling**: `coop-modetag.ps1` matched the client's own `COOPMODETAG` line as "the refusal names the -off entry": it now reads the game's detail lines (v0.107.1 `JoinFlow`, v0.111+ `HandshakeManager`), the `-off` entry on the client's side and the host's untagged one on the other; `audit-cooptoggle-host/-client` read the join list back (`coopmodetag`) after each whole-mod switch (the retag `CoopModeTag.Refresh` does on v0.111+, untested before).
- **Owner-required items of the first pass**: `handselect <ID> [hold <s>] [free]` (AutoTest, solo) plays a hand card through the game's own hand choice (`NPlayerHand.SelectCards`: the tooling's selectors are off for that choice), holds it open while the play's own changes dispatch `CombatStateChanged` (each runs `RevalidateSelectionAfterStateChange`), then removes every hand card its filter accepts (`CardPileCmd.RemoveFromCombat`, as `remove_card`): the game's revalidation ends the selection with no card (`TrySetResult`) and the card's play goes on inline inside the dispatch, the case ListenerCache suspends its lists for. The line fails if the selection never opens or the game doesn't end it within 15 s, and never leaves a choice open (a leftover selection is ended with no card, the selectors put back). `handselect-on`/`-off` (Dual Wield, two held selections, CHECKSUM and STATE line for line; the on side requires `ListenerCache.Suspended` to grow), and `effects-check` holds one: `ListenerCache.SuspendedRevalidations` is an effect (`ListenerCache.Suspended`), listed only in sessions whose script has a `handselect` line. PileIndex's self-check limit gets its control: in test builds each add and remove keeps a per-thread frame (the pile's count at the start, the net change of the calls nested in it), and a self-check fault explained exactly by a nested move into the same pile is counted (`PileIndex.NestedMoveFaults`, logged as "self-check limit", in `counters`); `pileindex-nested-limit` (a strict foreign patch on `CardPile.Clear` turns the self-check on, `nested-postfix` on `AddInternal`) expects the fault, the limit line and a count of 1 or more, then PileIndex answering nothing (`effectcheck PileIndex same`). Release unchanged. SC-2 of the first pass (the card-count comparison kept on purpose): nothing in this pass changes that decision.

Docs: the conflict tooltip and `bdopt` wording quoted in "Conflicts with other mods", `-WithMods`' test mods (here and coop.ps1), CLAUDE.md's status, the CHANGELOG (the mods screen with the mod off shows no image, not the game's; the cross-version item dropped: it comes from 08b0c48 and isn't player-facing, kept if the shape rules are switched off), the lobby walk's cost ("or shows the state while it is off"), `bdperf opt`, the CardDbScan row (SurroundingOnly on `AddInternal`), the SurroundingOnly definition (the five mirrored hooks: an invariant with HookFilter's PrefixesOnly, named at both call sites), gate types (closed generics accepted), README's build line (the csproj refuses a Steam deploy), InlineGuard's ~300 ms (measured at depth 2).

Refuted or left as is: OP-1's RitsuLib half (above). OP-4, uncertain (InlineGuard at callee depth 4, recording without `Available`): kept; the rebuild set is bounded by the patched methods, each rebuilt once and guarded by HarmonyRebuild, and the deeper search finds inlined writes depth 2 missed; its count and time are in the `InlineGuard: N patched methods ... compiled again (X ms)` line, to be measured against the depth-2 baseline (115-144 methods, ~300 ms) before restricting it. HookSkip's registration stays at Apply (registering after each batch would hide the hooks still queued from the startup preflight); only a failed hook is forgotten. RelicStateReuse's registrations while BaseLib relic saves short-circuit its checks: real back-offs once those saves go, the note already explains.

Proof (second pass): with the above, `foreign-shapes-2k-on/off` (the two new default-priority prefixes kept), `coop-modetag.ps1` (the detail lines), `audit-cooptoggle` (`coopmodetag` after each switch, on v0.113.1 the retagged handshake list), `handselect-on/off` and `effects-check` (`ListenerCache.Suspended` > 0), `pileindex-nested-limit`, `bdperf sortkeys` in a Veilpiercer combat on v0.113.1, Limitless + BaseLib + CardAscension through `BDTEST_EXTRA_MODS` (`LimitlessCloneMemo` and `LimitlessHookSkip` running, the gated `AddKeyword` logged "kept"), the `Preflight:` line's two parts and longest steps with the usual mods and a big pack on both versions, `InlineGuard`'s count and time.

**Third pass** (2026-11-16): the eight parts re-read on f7e4f3d, 26 findings (4 confirmed by verifiers, the rest re-read and judged here), then the fixes, and two owner requests. Code:

- **Startup preflight (SH-2)**: the load part runs only when the patches finish before the main menu, which no test log shows (the menu first, the remaining patches applied at once on its frame) but a real start with ten Workshop mods did (617 entries in 9.3 ms, longest 5.4 ms). Kept, documented as it runs ("Back-offs visible before they happen"); when the menu came first, each entry's resolution is now its own step of the menu part (a lazy allowed list or source could take a few ms in the same step as its first check: the menu steps reached 4.0-4.3 ms for a 3 ms budget), an entry that failed at load is tried again there, and the `Preflight:` line names the patch of the longest load entry and of the longest menu step, to split further what the next session shows.
- **`ShapeRules = false` (SH-1)**: refuted as a regression of the switch, fixed as docs. The switch gives exactly the strict checks of before the shape rules (the one switch point, `CheckTolerant`; gates empty, so `ChainPatchedFor` is `ChainPatched`); "before" is the fourth pass's state (a501424), whose deeper clone chain already checked `CardModel.AddKeyword`, `RemoveKeyword`, `get_MaxUpgradeLevel` strictly: with CardAscension both Limitless parts back off then, as they did, while v1.0.3 never checked those methods. `PatchSafety.ShapeRules`' doc and docs/RELEASE.md say so; TypeGated's doc covers its second use (the clone chain's card methods, per saved card type).
- **FastListeners' filtered walk (WC-1)**: refuted. The pile entry caches (snapshots, catch-ups, segments) are only written inside `GuardedPiles`/`GuardedDeck`, which catch any exception there, a getter's included, and fault FastListeners for the session before anything reads them again; what reaches `AddCombatGuarded`/`AddRunGuarded`'s catch comes from outside those caches (kinds, relics, potions, the tails), which keep no state across walks. Said at the catch.
- **The lobby walk (COOP-4)**: `InCoopGame` walks the scene tree only when no run exists (a run frees the main menu and its lobby screens), so `bdopt` in a solo run no longer walks it.
- **Unwatch against a preflight under way (V2)**: an entry unwatched after the preflight took its snapshot (HookSkip's hook failing in a late batch) is skipped by that preflight's later steps (`Watched.Removed`), so what Unwatch forgot isn't recorded again. **HookSkip's failed hook (WC-4, SCS-3)** also unwatches its `AbstractModel` defaults no other filtered hook keeps.
- **Per-mod counts (V3)**: one method reached through two reflected types counts once (keyed by method and declaring type handles, generic instantiations apart).
- **`LoggedFor` (SH-3)**: the cached tolerant answer's call-site slots are compared by reference first; the `.cs` test runs only before taking a slot.
- **SnapshotCardReuse's pile part (WC-5, SCS-4)**: the release postfix is patched before the pile prefix, and the part counts as applied (shown by the preflight) as soon as the prefix is in place.
- **Co-op texts (COOP-1, COOP-3)**: the warnings and the menu's lock reasons no longer claim how the others joined (a player undoing a mistaken switch was told the undo would desync): every player must have the same state, switch only to the state every other player has (`why.coop_run`, `why.coop_run_on` in 14 languages). `CoopModeTag.Working` (applied, and no list met without this mod's entry): when false, `bdopt` says the game can't tell on from off here instead of promising the join check (`Settings.CoopCheckMissing`).
- **The menu in a co-op run (COOP-2)**: BaseLib 3.4.8 opens its mod configuration from the main menu only, so the locked master switch can't be seen in a run: the CHANGELOG no longer promises it, DESIGN's menu row says it is a guard for a BaseLib that opens there.
- **Docs and tooling**: `package.py` refuses a CHANGELOG section with an HTML comment or a pending mark (the Workshop change note is plain text), docs/RELEASE.md says to remove the comment; the autotest README's mod-off co-op recipe (`launchoff`, not `bdopt off`) and its stray parenthesis; DESIGN's `conflict-check` (a bool prefix), `-WithMods`' folder (removed with the last series), `bdopt`'s pointer to `bdopt why` (mod on, a running part concerned); CLAUDE.md's status; LimitlessHookSkip's doc comments in place; `handselect` puts the tooling's selectors back in stack order (T1).
- **Owner request: other mods named by their mod**: a Harmony owner id is mapped to the loaded mod whose assemblies hold the patch method, now read raw (`Type.GetField`, only the runtime's own `FieldInfo` of the game's `Mod`) with `Mod.assemblies` first: CrossVersionCompat patches `AccessTools.Field` and answered the missing `Mod.assembly` with a stand-in giving the first of `Mod.assemblies` only, which lost RitsuLib's name (its modules are separate assemblies). Then the mod whose folder holds the assembly's file (a framework loading its own per-version modules), then the mod whose manifest id is a dot-separated part of the Harmony id (a patch method in a dynamic assembly), else the id. No per-mod list. RitsuLib's five `com.ritsukage.sts2-RitsuLib.*` ids read "RitsuLib" once, its methods counted once, in the menu's summary, the hovers and `bdopt why`.
- **Owner request: coloured titles** (described in the `Settings` row of "Implemented patches"): one state per optimization (`Settings.PartState`: Healthy, Partly, Inactive; `StateOf`), used by the menu, `bdopt` ("(inactive: nothing of it runs now)") and `bdopt why` (the lists of inactive and partly inactive optimizations). A back-off keeps a whole part vanilla where the patch says so (display only): `PatchSafety.WholePatch` for patches with one gate in front of all they do (CardDbScan, EnchantmentTabReuse, HoverTipReuse, IdleButtonInput, ListenerCache, PileCounterBatch, PoolSignalScan, ReplayStateSkip, RichTextEnvRelease, TypePlaqueSkip), `WholeMethods` for some methods only (CardFlightLimit's `Create`, `_Ready` and the skipped callees; its pile buttons' `AddCard` only merges counts), whole notes (ListenerCache's reuse with card modifiers, RelicStateReuse's relic saves, TypePlaqueSkip's type line modifiers). Patches checking per hook or per type (HookSkip, HookFilter, FastListeners, SnapshotCardReuse...) never count as whole: their back-offs give orange. New texts `inactive`, `inactive_mods` (13 languages).

Kept as is: SCS-2's gated chain had no scenario: `limitless-gated-2k-off`/`-on` (a gated prefix on `CardModel.AddKeyword` before three combat starts; `LimitlessCloneMemo` and `LimitlessHookSkip` grow, the runtime check `TypeGated kept`). COOP-3's other promises (the menu's `coop_note`, `why.started_off`, the load line) stay: the menu's note can't know the join check's state before a game, and a failing check is logged.

Proof (third pass): with the above, the `Preflight:` line's two parts and their named longest steps (a normal start and one with a big pack), the menu with RitsuLib (one "RitsuLib (N)" entry, the names in hovers and `bdopt why`), a title colour of each kind (orange with RitsuLib or Zone the Spire; red with a fake whole conflict, `fakeconflict` on `NCardFlyShuffleVfx.Create`; normal with an option switched off), the page refreshing while open (a back-off recorded by a combat behind it is not reachable: the menu opens from the main menu only; the startup preflight finishing while the page is open is), `limitless-gated-2k-off/on` with Limitless and BaseLib, `bdopt` in a solo run (no lobby walk) and in a co-op lobby (its warning).

**Fourth pass (delta since f7e4f3d)** (2026-11-16): the eight parts re-read on 726bd56 (the changes since the third pass: reflection interception, needless back-offs, transpilers judged by IL), 28 findings (6 confirmed by verifiers, the rest re-read here), then the fixes. Code:

- **ExtraScopes' first turn with RitsuLib, a regression of 0177e77 (SCS-1)**: the first-turn scope checked `SetupPlayerTurn`'s state machine strictly, and RitsuLib always transpiles it (its hand size constants, at its deferred init): the 6.5 s to 12 ms first-turn fix never ran with RitsuLib. Now generic, no list of mods: the query lambdas stay strict; on the state machine other mods' prefixes, postfixes and finalizers stand (they run before or after a `MoveNext` call, never inside the scoped `ToList` calls), and so do transpilers Harmony runs before this mod's, whose transpiler is now `Priority.Last` and judges the code it is handed (`TranspilerJudge.FeedsUnchanged`): each `ToList` call and the code feeding it (found backwards by stack depth: the query, its cached delegates, the pile and list locals), and every store of a local or field that code reads (followed in turn), must be the game's instruction for instruction, with no branch in or out and no address taken; otherwise the calls stay the game's (logged, note `first_turn_code`). A transpiler sorted after this mod's, inner patches or a factory: a back-off as before. Fields stored by other methods (the kickoff, a lambda class's constructor) aren't followed. Self-test cases (`foreignpatch iljudge selftest`, 50 on both versions offline): unchanged, an observer at the entry and RitsuLib's shape (the hand size read replaced) accepted; an observer inside a query, a swapped predicate, the pile stored again, a jump into a query, a query dropped refused. `foreign-il-2k-on/off` install `il-firstturn-outside` (code at the state machine's entry) and the on side expects the first-turn filter `(in a scope)` and the `kept` line. RitsuLib's expectations ("Needless back-offs made safe", proof) gain ExtraScopes: not listed by `bdopt why`, its first-turn filter in a scope.
- **HarmonyWatch (SH-1)**: `Changed` read `Harmony.GetPatchInfo` inside `UpdateWrapper`, which Harmony 2.4 serves from the list before that call (it stores the new one after `UpdateWrapper` returns): a hidden IL hook installed after load (a first patch on `MethodCreator.CreateReplacement`) was never noted, nor a first foreign patch on this mod's own code. It now reads the list being built (`UpdateWrapper`'s second argument, injected as `__1`). `foreign-controls` installs a no-op postfix on `MethodCreator.CreateReplacement` then one on `Hook.AfterPotionUsed` and expects both HarmonyWatch lines.
- **Kind watch (WC-D1)**: a relic, potion or orb type met before the first scan (a saved run's relics join before any walk) or after a failed scan was dropped; it is now kept for the next scan (no log, no stale mark).
- **ListenerCache's augmented scopes (WC-D2)**: a mirror declining mid-scope (another mod patched the walk code since the scope opened) threw and faulted ListenerCache for the session; the prefixes now give the game's call.
- **CoopModeTag (COOP-D1, D2, D7)**: the postfix order check runs after the tag and its bookkeeping, in its own try (a failure there could leave the first join list, kept for the whole net service on v0.111+, untagged); it reads Harmony's own sort (before/after ids), priority and registration only with a factory; `Working` is false once the part is faulted.
- **Shape rules (SH-2, SCS-2)**: a postfix let stand because its IL only reads through a by-reference argument is refused while its own method is patched (asked at each recheck, as TypeGated's gates). `TakeRunFilter` and `DropPendingFilter` are `[OrderFreePrefix]` (no argument, never skipping the original; a void prefix after them can only leave the walk unfiltered): a default-priority void prefix another mod puts on the walks at its load (before this mod's batches: sorted before ListenerCache's default-priority prefixes and FastListeners' `Priority.Last` ones) no longer backs FastListeners' served walks and ListenerCache off.
- **Display (SCS-4, SH-4, VIS-M1)**: HookFilter registers the walk code ListenerCache's scopes require (both state machines, `Contains`, ModHelper's subscriber walks) under its own name, whole (lazily: `WholeMethods` with a source), so it no longer shows healthy while none of it runs. The startup preflight reads the registered entries in rounds until none is new and the note checks only when they start (a late batch's entries and notes are in it). The menu's greying only adds on a refresh: nothing that greys a row clears while the page is open (comment).
- **GridSortCache (SCS-3)**: the comparer's patch check is one reference compare per comparison (the last delegate and its answer kept per scope); the scope's postfix runs at `int.MaxValue`.
- **FastCombatStart (COOP-D3)**: a fault in a co-op run logs its own sentence (no switch was changed; this player now starts combats at the game's pace; restart), not the switch warning.
- **Test tooling**: `foreignpatch iljudge sweep` fails when a hook other than the three `AfterPreventing*` (their walk goes to `Contains`) isn't understood, or one of those becomes understood (T1). `bdperf states` (VIS-T1): each option's `StateOf`, the patches registered whole, `StatesChanged` raised since the first call and its listeners; `conflict-check` adds a no-op postfix on `NetCombatCardDb.OnPileContentsChanged` and expects `card_ids` Inactive, `hook_filter` Partly, `CardDbScan` whole, events raised, the menu's `QueueRefresh` listening, and the red hover of `CARD_IDS`. `bdperf roomleft` (COOP-D6): a window whose room the run left closes at its first wait (`fast-start-check`). `fast-start-check`'s mod-off fight runs on the deck trimmed to 500 cards (`bigdeck trim`, with the mod on): at ~6,000 cards the game's own combat start took ~23 minutes on v0.113.1 (its quadratic shuffle sort), past `par.ps1`'s stall limit. ExtraScopes' Debug line says `(in a scope)` or `(without a scope)`. ForeignLaunch's stand-in check reads `Mod.assemblies` at any visibility (T2); `byref-out-postfix` counts a collection without enumerating it (T3).

Docs: the `+off` marker (RELEASE.md, the autotest README, `coopmixed-client`, `coop-modetag.ps1`, AutoTest), RELEASE.md's two pending CHANGELOG items under `ShapeRules` and their scenarios in its gate, the menu lock RELEASE.md promised to see in a run (BaseLib 3.4.8 opens its settings from the main menu only), the language counts (14 with English), the preflight bullet ("Back-offs visible before they happen": three parts, the log line as it is), "co-op game" with the connected net session (Console commands, the fourth pass, `bdopt`'s and `InCoopGame`'s docs), the Implemented-patches rows of ListenerCache (the `InNoPile` exception, v0.113's `RunModels` setters, augmented scopes), PileIndex (`InNoPile` for ListenerCache), FastListeners kinds (the kind watch), ExtraScopes, HarmonyWatch and GridSortCache. Refuted: D2 (the CHANGELOG left the menu lock out on purpose: the third pass's COOP-2). The second and third passes' entries above describe the state then.

**Fifth pass (delta since 726bd56)** (2026-11-16): the same parts re-read on the fourth pass's fixes, 16 findings (4 confirmed by verifiers, all test gaps), all fixed or answered. Code:

- **Preflight rounds (VIS-D1)**: the startup preflight's part (1) stops before the notes during the loading and part (2) resumes on the main menu's frames, yet the entries were read only before that stop: a late batch registering in between (a mod's assembly loading meanwhile) was never method-checked by the preflight. The rounds (`PreflightRounds`, until a round finds nothing new, at most 64) now run again after that stop, just before the notes are read. The unused `resolved` argument of `PreflightSteps` and its doc are gone (SHH-D3: nothing is carried over between preflights).
- **HarmonyWatch (SHH-D1)**: `Changed` reads the list being built (`__1`) only when `UpdateWrapper` succeeded; on a throw Harmony stores nothing and installs no detour, so the list it still holds is the one in place. A failed patch on `MethodCreator.CreateReplacement` no longer turns hidden-hook mode on (every rebuilt method then treated as patched by hidden hooks), nor a failed patch on this mod's code logs "another mod patches this mod's own".
- **ListenerCache's augmented scopes (WC-E1, SCS-D3)**: once FastListeners' unpatched mirror declined in a scope (another mod patched the walk code mid-scope), each later walk of that scope threw and caught `UnpatchedWalkMissing` again (hundreds in a big refresh). The decline is remembered for the scope (`_mirrorDeclined`, thread-static, cleared at the next outermost scope): the rest takes the game's call without an exception. Results unchanged (the game's call either way).
- **FastCombatStart (COOP-E1)**: `bdperf roomleft` logged a second, false "combat start skipped N animation waits" line (the last fight's counters): the bench's windows log no summary line.

Test tooling (the four confirmed gaps first):

- **COOP-E2**: `bdperf roomleft` now checks a live room the run doesn't hold (an uninitialized `CombatRoom`: the reference compare a mod's quick restart reaches) and a dead one (the weak reference), and, when the run's room is a combat room, that it is recognised (a compare always false would close every window). `fast-start-check` expects the line naming both rooms.
- **COOP-E3**: `bdperf modorder [none|<owner>]` runs CoopModeTag's order check again (once per session otherwise) and fails when it disagrees. New scenario `coop-modorder` (no run): a foreign no-op postfix at priority 0 on `GetGameplayRelevantModNameList` (registered after this mod's `Priority.Last` one: runs after it) must be listed and warned about, the same at priority 500 must not (control).
- **SHH-D2**: `bdperf states` marks a patch with fixed whole methods and lazily registered ones `(N methods + lazy)`. New scenario `walkcode-check`: a bool prefix on `CombatState.Contains` alone (walk code, neither `IterateHookListeners` patched) must show HookFilter `+ lazy`, `hook_filter` Inactive and `bdopt why`'s `(all of that part)` on that back-off.
- **SCS-D1**: new scenario `foreign-firstturn-controls`, the negative controls of ExtraScopes' allowed list: `il-firstturn-outside` at priority 0 (sorted after this mod's transpiler, which then judged the game's code, not the other's output) must back off (the state machine's back-off line, both first-turn filters `(without a scope)`, no ExtraScopes effect). Inner patches: Harmony 2.4.2 can't build one, so `bdperf firstturn inner` puts a synthetic patch list (another mod's prefix, postfix, inner postfix) through `FirstTurnAllowed` and the same filter as `IsPatchedByOthers`: the prefix and postfix stand, the inner postfix is left over.
- **T2, DOC-1**: `coop-modetag.ps1` waits only for the games the mixed pair started (the PIDs not running before it), 60 s at most; one still there is a PROBLEM line and the off pair isn't run (coop.ps1 would refuse, an earlier session's logs would be judged).
- **T1**: answered: the sweep's `NotUnderstood` list is v0.107.1's and v0.113.1's (the supported versions); its doc and its failure message say so, so a failure on another version is read as that version's hooks first.

Docs: the Workshop text's orange and red (COOP-E4: orange is part of an option on the normal game, from another mod or from a game update or an error; red is none of it running; "hover it to see why", not "which mod"); FastListeners' `_met` summary (WC-E2: also every type met while no scan was published); the shape section's `[OrderFreePrefix]` set (TakeRunFilter, DropPendingFilter, the tooling's clock prefixes) and ExtraScopes' place in the "Not applied" list (its first-turn lambdas and BetterSpire2 part strict, its state machine on the allowed list) (SCS-D2).
